# Backlog

Each item is a self-contained prompt for a new thread. Ordered by suggested priority; reorder freely. When an item is done, tick it and move it to the Completed section at the bottom, under the matching heading, so follow-ups can still be traced back to it.

## Next up

- [ ] **Donation receipts: a form for the Director to issue a receipt, saved to Drive and ready to send to the donor.** Lutan, 2026-10-07. An internal form (not public yet; it will later join the Donate page and the donation-recording items below) where the Director enters a donation and the system produces a receipt. **Moved to the top of the backlog by Lutan, 2026-10-07.** **The template has arrived** (a past receipt the Director issued, `101-Global-Tiger.pdf`, in Lutan's OneDrive `Desktop\LCA`; **keep it out of git**, it names a real donor, and attach it to the PR description instead). Build the layout from it: A4 portrait, white; **RECEIPT** large at the top left in a tall condensed navy typeface (it looks like Bebas Neue, available from Google Fonts, so it can be embedded; the PDF also needs the Thai font the archive uses for Thai names); **the foundation's round seal logo** top right (the Thai-and-English "Lanna Care for Animals Foundation" seal, which is **not** the square `public/lca-logo.jpg`; get the file from the Director or Lutan); under the title, **Lanna Care for Animals Foundation** in bold and **Chiang Mai, Thailand** (confirm the address line with the Director, and whether a registration or tax number should appear: the sample has none); a **BILL TO** block with the donor's name on the left, and **RECEIPT #** and **RECEIPT DATE** (dd/mm/yyyy) on the right, labels in navy; a table with navy **DESCRIPTION** and **AMOUNT** headings between thin red rules, one row per line (the sample: "Donation to charity. Sponsorship of two packs (40kg) of dry dog food. (15 dumped dogs group)", 1,200.00), so the form takes one or more description lines each with an amount; **TOTAL ฿1,200.00** in navy, right-aligned; and at the foot, **Thank you** in a navy script typeface beside a short rule and **TERMS & CONDITIONS** in red with "N/A" (keep it, or make it a setting the Director can fill in). The sample is English only: ask whether a Thai version or Thai text is wanted, rather than translating it unasked. **A new numbering sequence (Lutan, 2026-10-07):** receipts are numbered **`LCA` followed by seven digits**, e.g. **`LCA0009000`**, not continuing her old #101 series. Confirm the first number with Lutan (the example suggests starting at `LCA0009000`), then each receipt takes the next one, never repeated or skipped; a voided receipt keeps its number. **File name** `<receipt number>-<Donor-Name>.pdf` (e.g. `LCA0009000-Global-Tiger.pdf`), on Drive and when downloaded. **The form** (Management, the Director's; on a phone as well as her PC at home): donor name, how to reach them (email, phone or LINE), amount in baht, date received, how it was given (bank transfer, PromptPay, cash, DonorBox, in kind), what it is for if designated (general, a resident, a project, an appeal), an optional note, and for an in-kind gift a description instead of an amount. Pick an existing contact or add one, so a repeat donor builds a history (contacts, Fundraising "Make `/donate` ask for money" step (b) is the same record; build the **donation record** that item describes now, and the receipt is printed from it, rather than a receipt with no donation behind it). **The receipt:** a PDF in the template's layout (the PDF tooling and Thai fonts the resident archive uses, `@react-pdf/renderer`, `src/lib/archive/fonts/`), English and Thai as the template has them, with a **receipt number** from a sequence that never repeats or skips (a receipt register is something an accountant or the Revenue Department may ask to see; a cancelled receipt is marked void and kept, never deleted or reused), the shelter's name, address and registration details as the template shows them, and who issued it. **Saved to Drive:** each PDF filed under a restricted folder (e.g. `Admin/Donations/Receipts/<year>/`, shared with the Director only, like `Admin/Backups`), named with the number and donor, and its file id kept on the donation record. If Drive is down (a lapsed token has broken uploads before), the receipt must still be produced and offered to the Director, with the upload retried and the record saying "not yet saved to Drive", never lost. **Sending it:** after saving, the receipt opens for her to send herself: on a phone, the share sheet with the PDF attached (Web Share API with files) so she can pick Gmail, LINE or anything else; on the PC, download plus an email draft (`mailto:` with the donor's address, subject and a short message; attach the PDF by hand, since a `mailto:` link cannot attach). The app does not send it for her (the shelter's sending setup sends only to verified addresses, and a receipt should come from her). Mark the record "sent" when she confirms. **Also:** re-print or re-send a past receipt from the donation record, a list of receipts by date with totals (the start of the donations ledger the Cashflow forecast lacks), and correcting a mistake by voiding and re-issuing. **Privacy and tax:** no Thai ID number unless the template or the Revenue Department's e-Donation scheme needs it for a tax deduction; if it does, collect it only when the donor asks for a deductible receipt, store it apart and decide retention first (the per-donation QR item in this section already raised this). Who: the Director (Admin) by default, a new activity in the permissions catalogue ("Issue a donation receipt") so it can be delegated later. Schema PR first (donations, receipt numbering). Manual, both dictionaries, a decision file (numbering, voiding, storage), a releases line, and a test plan including a receipt with Thai text, an in-kind gift, a void, a Drive failure and sharing from a phone. **Thai tax rules, researched 2026-10-07 (to confirm with the foundation's accountant before building; this is not tax advice):** (a) **Only some charities' donors can claim.** A donation is deductible in Thailand only if the foundation has been **announced as a public charitable organisation (องค์การสาธารณกุศล) by the Ministry of Finance in the Royal Gazette**. Find out from the Director whether Lanna Care for Animals Foundation is on that list; if it is not, the receipt must **not** suggest the gift is tax-deductible, and getting listed is a separate job for her and the accountant. (b) **Since 1 January 2026 a paper or PDF receipt is no longer enough to claim.** A Thai donor can deduct a donation only if it is recorded in the Revenue Department's **e-Donation** system: the foundation records the donor's **name and 13-digit tax ID** (a person's Thai ID number, or a company's tax number), the **amount** and the **date**, or the donor pays by the foundation's e-Donation QR code. The figure then flows into the donor's tax return by itself. So the receipt this item builds is the **thank-you and the foundation's own record**; the **e-Donation entry is what lets a donor claim**. (c) **Limits, for the donor's information:** an individual can deduct donations up to 10% of net income, a company up to 2% of net profit; double deductions apply to some e-Donation gifts. **What that adds to the build:** an optional *Donor wants a tax deduction* tick on the form, which then asks for the 13-digit ID (checked digit-by-digit with the Thai ID checksum) and shows a reminder **"Record this in e-Donation"** with a link to the Revenue Department site, plus a *Recorded in e-Donation on <date>* tick on the donation record and a list of donations still waiting to be recorded; if the e-Donation system can import a file, an export in its format. The receipt then shows the foundation's own tax ID / registration number, and the donor's ID **masked** (last four digits) if shown at all. **Overseas donors** (the sample's donor is a foreign company) get the same receipt; Thai tax rules do not affect them. **Privacy:** the 13-digit ID is sensitive personal data under the PDPA: collect it only when the donor asks for a deduction, store it separately from the receipt, readable only by whoever issues receipts, and decide how long it is kept. Sources: Revenue Department guides (`rd.go.th`, donations by individuals and by companies), The Nation (2026, e-Donation mandatory from 1 January 2026), Forvis Mazars (e-Donation system).

- [ ] **Does `has_permission()` need a statement-level cache? (measure; found by `perm-convert-orphans`, 2026-10-05).** Each converted table in a statement plans one `(select has_permission(…))` init-plan: about 0.3 to 0.5 ms and about 150 buffers a table on a cold call. `0145` measured it on the residents list (staff, 92 rows): 4.5 ms and 523 buffers to 5.7 ms and 839, from `enclosures` and `zones` joining the converted `residents`. Fine today; it grows with the number of converted tables in one query. A recurring task linked to a page (the weekly stocktake is the case that matters, P4) cannot be assigned to the 2IC from Management → Recurring jobs, and loses its link on her `/my`, because `app_users.role` is the legacy enum (`volunteer` for all three configured roles) and `canDoJob` / `ASSIGNABLE_ROLES` / `loadEligibility` ask `role_can()` about that enum value, and a volunteer may not open `/stocktake`. It works for a staff login, which is the real price of giving her the volunteer floor (`docs/decisions/2026-10-04-2ic-role.md` §1, §5). **Shape:** `app_users` exposes the role key; `ASSIGNABLE_ROLES` and the picker work in keys (every non-vet role that opens the app), `/my` passes the reader's own key, and `scripts/check-recurring-job-eligibility.mjs` gains a configured role. Belongs with R5's recurring-jobs-on-a-phone work. Marking a task done already works for her. **Also found, not fixed:** the Head of Medical's medication label photo probably fails the same way as the 2IC's did (the photo proxy asks the `medication` table; her list reads `medication_list_medications`), and the maintenance board still offers photos and Delete job to a role that holds neither (`maintenance.photos`; delete has no activity of its own).
- [x] **A home screen belongs to a role, not to the string `management` (`tiles.ts`).** Found by `management-role` (2026-10-05). The Director's four-tile phone home is `managementDay`, chosen by `perms.role.key === "management"`; a shelter that makes its own manager role in Settings, even with Management's exact cells, gets the generic one-tile-per-page home. Decide whether a home is a property of the role (`roles.home_path` already exists and a vet uses it) or of a job list, before the "roles a shelter adds" stream. `docs/decisions/2026-10-05-management-role.md` §4. **Decided 2026-10-06 (`home-screen-belongs-to-a-role`): neither; the derived home is right for a shelter-made role until it is curated; `home_path` is a redirect and the vet does not use it. `docs/decisions/2026-10-06-home-screen-belongs-to-a-role.md`.**


- [ ] **The Pi is a single point of failure for every write, on both environments, and the Worker fallback only covers reads.** Found live on 2026-10-02: the Pi went off the network mid-release and **nobody could sign in or save anything on production**, while every public page still rendered. Three paths confirmed it was the Pi and not name resolution — mDNS (`lanna-pi.local`, `lanna-pi`) silent, the cached LAN address `192.168.68.102` (a Raspberry Pi Foundation MAC) answering neither ping nor port 22, and `POST https://lannacare.org/login` returning `503 x-lanna-served-by: pi-timeout` while `GET` returned 200. The Supabase auth config was checked and was correct, so this was not an auth fault: **"Continue with Google" is a server action, so a POST** — the flow dies before the redirect to Google is issued and the user sees a bare `503` body ("The shelter's server did not answer in time"), which reads as a dead page. Email-and-password sign-in fails identically. **Since this release, test is on the same Pi** (`test-pi.lannacare.org`, same box, same tunnel), so one machine going down now takes writes out on **production and test at once — including the environment you would use to verify a fix.** The cloudflared config anticipates this (`# A second Pi later: run cloudflared there with this same tunnel's credentials and Cloudflare balances between the two`), but there is one Pi. **Three separable pieces, smallest first.** (a) **Distinguish a connect-time failure from an in-flight one in `worker/origin.mjs`.** It splits on method alone — `const idempotent = IDEMPOTENT_METHODS.has(request.method)` — and every failure path returns `idempotent ? null : writeUnanswered()`, so a write is refused whether the Pi timed out mid-request or the fetch never left the Worker. **Keep the refusal where it is right:** a 502/504 can be cloudflared losing the origin *after* the Pi has acted, and replaying would record a second intake (#250, and that file's own comment). But a connection refused or a DNS failure means the request provably never arrived, so replaying locally cannot duplicate anything. Today's outage was entirely the second kind, and **one condition would have meant no outage at all** — writes would have fallen through to the Worker exactly as reads did. Check what the Workers runtime actually surfaces for a connect failure versus a timeout before relying on the distinction; if `fetch` throws indistinguishably, a short pre-flight (a `HEAD` with a one-second timeout, or the status the `scheduled` handler already collects) is the fallback plan. (b) **Nothing said the Pi was down.** It was found because a person could not sign in. Settings → System status has a Pi tile — behind a login that stops working when the Pi does, so the one place that would say so is unreachable exactly when it matters. The Worker stays up in that state and already runs a cron every fifteen minutes (`wrangler.jsonc`, status alerts), so it is the natural place to notice and send the mail `0098` built. (c) **The weekly backup runs on the Pi**, so a Pi down over a Sunday means no backup that week, silently — a larger exposure than the outage. Either run it somewhere that is not the same single point of failure, or alert when a run is missed. **Also worth deciding, separately:** whether emptying `ORIGIN_HOST` should be a documented emergency lever. Setting it to `""` in an environment's block and deploying makes the Worker serve everything including writes, restoring sign-in within a deploy, at the cost of the CPU limits the Pi was adopted to avoid (`Error 1102`). It worked that way until 2026-09-30 and is currently folklore rather than a runbook step. **Status 2026-10-02: (a) is done** (`claude/origin-write-fallback`; docs/decisions/2026-10-02-origin-write-fallback.md). A thrown fetch turned out not to distinguish the two cases — workerd words a refused connect and a connection dropped after the write arrived identically — so the split is on the edge's status instead: a write is rendered by the Worker on a **530** (no tunnel connector, error 1033) and refused as before on everything else. **Status, `claude/pi-down-alert`: (b) is done** — the alert run now treats a dead Pi as red (the tile stays amber), so the existing two-consecutive-runs mail and recovery mail fire for it, with what to check in the body (docs/decisions/2026-10-02-pi-down-alert.md). **Still open: (c) (the existing backup tile alerts only after two missed Sundays; one constant changes that), the `ORIGIN_HOST` lever, and the drill that proves (a) on the real tunnel** (stop `cloudflared` on the Pi, sign in).

- [ ] **Rethink Pi failover: today a power cut still refuses writes. Make the fallback cover the outages that actually happen — options paper, then build.** Lutan, 2026-10-02: not happy with where the write fallback (`claude/origin-write-fallback`, docs/decisions/2026-10-02-origin-write-fallback.md) leaves us. The 2026-10-02 production release notes set it out: **`cloudflared` stopped → 530 at once → writes fall back and work**; **power cut, first seconds → 502/504 or a 20 s timeout → writes refused**; **power cut, once the edge notices → 530 → fall back**; **Pi up but sick, or `lanna-care` stopped → 502/503 from cloudflared → refused**. So the one case that falls back cleanly is the drill, and a power cut (the likeliest outage for a box in a house) starts with a window of refused sign-ins and saves, and a sick-but-up Pi never falls back at all. "If it's not going to fail over properly, we need a different solution." **Why it is built this way, so the replacement keeps the guarantee:** a 502/504 can arrive *after* the Pi has written, so replaying it on the Worker could record a second intake (#250), and a thrown fetch cannot tell "never arrived" from "arrived, then dropped" (the decision file's probe table). Any new design must still never apply a write twice. **The key fact to build on: the data is not on the Pi.** Both the Pi and the Worker write to the same Supabase database, so the duplicate risk can be closed *at the database* rather than by guessing at the edge, after which retrying on the Worker is always safe. **Options to weigh, not mutually exclusive:** (1) **Idempotent writes.** Every server action carries a client-generated request id, and the database records it under a unique key in the same transaction as the write (one shared wrapper around `runAction()` plus a `write_requests` table, or a parameter on the RPCs that matter: intake, placements, weights, deliveries, stocktake, uploads' attachment rows). A replay then returns the first result instead of writing again, and **every** failure (502, 504, timeout, thrown fetch) can fall back to the Worker. It removes the "refused by design" rows outright, but it touches every write path, so it needs its own schema PR and a sweep like #441's. (2) **Route on health, not on each request's outcome.** The Pi sends a heartbeat every few seconds (or the Worker's cron probes it) into Workers KV or a Durable Object; when it is stale the Worker serves *everything* itself before trying the Pi, so a new request after a power cut never waits for the edge to notice. Covers the "Pi up but sick" row too if the heartbeat comes from the app rather than the OS. It does not cover a request already in flight when the power goes, which is what (1) is for. (3) **Keep the power on.** A UPS HAT or small UPS for the Pi *and* the router/ONT (the tunnel needs both), sized for a typical Suphan Buri outage, with a clean shutdown on low battery. Cheapest and most direct for the power-cut row; does nothing for a crash or a broken deploy. (4) **A second connector on the same tunnel**, off-site: cloudflared already supports replicas ("A second Pi later" in `scripts/pi/cloudflared-config.yml`), and a free-tier cloud VM (e.g. an always-free ARM instance) running the same build would keep the origin up when the house goes dark. Real redundancy at near-zero cost, but a second machine to deploy to, patch and keep the secrets on, and each replica serves a share of traffic, not only the failover. (5) **Flip it: writes always on the Worker, the Pi only for page renders.** The 1102s came from rendering, not from server actions, so writes might fit the Worker's CPU budget. Then a Pi outage costs reads (which already fall back) and never writes. It hinges on a Pi-rendered form posting to a Worker build, which needs the same server-action ids and a shared `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (the open Deployment item), so verify that first. Paid Cloudflare Load Balancing was considered and is out: it costs every month and cannot target a Worker. **Deliverable:** a short paper (`docs/pi-failover.md`) that measures before it argues: on the real tunnel, time how long a hard power-off takes to become a 530 (pull the plug, POST every second), and what a stopped `lanna-care` returns. Then a recommendation of the cheapest combination that makes every row of the table "falls back and works" without ever risking a double write. My expectation is (1) + (2), with (3) as cheap insurance, but let the measurements decide. Agree it with Lutan, record it in `docs/decisions/`, then build (schema PR first if (1)). **Done when** the drill passes for all four rows: stop `cloudflared`, pull the power, stop `lanna-care`, and wedge the app, and in each case a sign-in and a save made during the outage succeed exactly once. Supersedes the `ORIGIN_HOST=""` emergency-lever question in the item above if (2) lands, and pairs with that item's (b), the alert when the Pi goes down. **Status 2026-10-03: the options paper is written and proposed ([#310](https://github.com/lutanbennett/Animal_Shelter_App/pull/310), `docs/pi-failover.md`); it is not agreed and no drill has been run. Remaining: Lutan reads it and answers its two questions; the drill (recipe in the paper) is run on the Pi; option 5 waits only on a CPU measurement (the form-key question is answered: a shared key is required and works on both the Pi’s and the Worker’s build, per `server-actions-encryption-key`, 2026-10-03, and it is live only once #312 is merged and both builds are redeployed); then the agreed recommendation goes in `docs/decisions/` and the build is planned.**

Pass 1 was never reached, so **the Vet pass still has no completed run behind it** — the 2026-09-27 record remains the only walkthrough evidence the app has. This was the **second** walkthrough killed by 1102 and it got *less* far than the first: on 2026-09-27 `/admin` loaded at all. **It was not the Pi failing and not an app defect.** At the time the Pi served production only — `ORIGIN_HOST` was `pi.lannacare.org` for `production` and `""` for `test` and `uat`, and that variable is the only thing `worker/index.mjs` consults — so test still rendered every request in the CPU-limited Worker, and `/admin` is close to the heaviest authenticated render in the app. **Do not be reassured by an unauthenticated probe:** one returned 200 throughout the blocked run, because the public pages are cheap and edge-cached; it says nothing about the authenticated render that actually failed. **The cause is already fixed** — see the ticked item under Deployment: #293 set `ORIGIN_HOST: "test-pi.lannacare.org"` in `0.14.0`. **Verified 2026-10-03:** `test.lannacare.org` answers `x-lanna-served-by: pi` and `/admin` returns a normal `307` to the sign-in page, not a 1102. So the blocker is gone and the only thing outstanding is **running the walkthrough**, which has now been owed twice. Script is `docs/role-walkthrough.md`; the 2026-09-27 record is `docs/uat/2026-09-27.md`. **The blocked run's own record is `docs/uat/2026-10-02.md`, which exists only on the branch `claude/uat-vet-pass`** — it was never merged, so it disappears if that branch is deleted. It is 466 lines and its value is the diagnosis above plus the per-step state of Pass 0; if the branch has since gone, this item is what survives of it. **CHECKED 2026-10-07 (staleness sweep) — still open, but one claim above is stale.** The blocked run's record **is on `main`**: `cea3cdaf` (2026-10-03, *Preserve the 2026-10-02 role walkthrough record on main*) committed `docs/uat/2026-10-02.md`, so it no longer depends on `claude/uat-vet-pass` surviving. No later walkthrough record exists in `docs/uat/` (only `2026-09-27`, `2026-10-02` and the staff dry run of 2026-10-03), so the re-run is still owed. Worth knowing before it is run: vets are now deliberately on hold (microchip only, the Director's draft, confirmed 2026-10-07), and three configured roles have been added since the script was last patched, so the Vet pass and the role list in `docs/role-walkthrough.md` need reading against today first.

**Role walkthrough, Pass 1 (Vet) — 2026-09-27. Work these first, then re-run the
walkthrough from the top** (Lutan). The twelve items live under their own headings
rather than being copied here; this is the order to take them in and what blocks
what. Record is `docs/uat/2026-09-27.md`; the script is `docs/role-walkthrough.md`.

| Order | Item | Where | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A refused page and a booked vet visit dump you on the public website | Quick wins | Smallest of the twelve, and it blocks re-testing: it ejects the tester mid-checklist on every refused route. Also cuts load on the CPU path the 1102s are hitting |
| 2 | Doctors belong to a vet — managed list rather than free text | Medical records | **The real gate.** A vet account has no link to a clinic at all today, so items 4 and 8 cannot be built until this exists. Schema PR of its own, ahead of both |
| 3 | Cut the vet's world down to residents and their own tasks | Auth | Independent of the schema, and it is most of what Lutan wants the vet role to feel like. Menu is easy; the five unguarded route trees are the work |
| 4 | A vet should see only the residents their own clinic treats | Auth | Largest item from Pass 1. Needs 2 first, and needs the rule **decided with Lutan before anyone builds it** — a scope that hides a resident from the vet about to treat them is worse than one that shows too much |
| 5 | Recurring jobs: only offer people who can actually do the job | Management | Independent. Stops the Pass 0 situation arising rather than handling it |
| 6 | Prescription should not attach to a future vet visit | Medical records | Small, independent, correctness |
| 7 | One weight per vet visit | Medical records | Small, independent, correctness |
| 8 | Vet visit form: clinic picker and the Status dropdown | Medical records | The clinic half needs 2; the Status question is a decision, not code |
| 9 | A vet's photo upload limited to the Medical folder | Medical records | **Do with the Medical-folder item already at the top of this section** — both are about what Medical means as a destination, and splitting them means touching the same upload path twice |
| 10 | A date field cannot be cleared once set, on mobile | Mobile | Small, and it affects every optional date in the app rather than the one it was found on |
| 11 | A role-based manual | Documentation | Pairs with 3: as the vet's menu shrinks, a manual still explaining Enclosures is actively wrong |
| 12 | File-type icon for non-images; release notes by role | Quick wins, Documentation | Nice to haves. Last |

**Fold the schema into one PR first, or the migration rule will be the bottleneck.**
Four of the twelve want a migration — 2 (a `vet_doctors` table, and the link from a
vet *account* to a clinic that does not exist today), 4 (the RLS policies that scope
residents to a clinic, which need that same link), 7 (a unique index behind one
weight per visit) and 6 (a check against the appointment date). CLAUDE.md allows
**only one in-flight branch carrying a migration at a time**, and says that where two
streams both need schema their migrations go in one PR. Done separately these four
serialise behind each other no matter how many worktrees are open; done as a single
additive schema PR up front, every feature half that follows can run in parallel.
2 and 4 need the same link anyway, so they are not really two decisions.

**Item 4 is gated on a decision, not on throughput.** It is the one thing here that
should not be picked up by a session and built: the rule for what a vet may see
wants settling with Lutan first, because a scope that hides a resident from the vet
about to treat them is worse than the current one that shows too much.
Not on this list and not forgotten: **Cloudflare 1102 on the test site**, expected
to clear when the Pi becomes the origin (see Deployment), now deferred to
Friday 2026-10-02, so expect it to persist until then. It is the environment,
not a defect in the app.

The 0.8.0 note stays; an `unreleased` line says the page was replaced. Lutan, 2026-09-27. **In Next up because it must land before the next production release:** the relocation page is merged (#171) but still only in `unreleased` in `src/lib/releases.ts`, so it has not reached lannacare.org yet — keep it that way. **Why:** the "Pet relocation" item was a mistake. Relocation is the Director's own private work, not something the shelter offers, so it does not belong on the shelter's site. What the shelter *does* do is **international adoptions** — adopters abroad taking one of its animals home. **1. Hide relocation.** Take `/relocation` off the site: the route (`src/app/relocation/page.tsx` — a redirect to the new International adoptions page is kinder than a 404 if the link was shared), the **Services** menu entry (`PublicHeader.tsx` / `PublicNav.tsx`) and footer link (`PublicFooter.tsx`), the "Adopting from abroad? We can arrange the journey" line on `/adopt` (`src/app/adopt/page.tsx`; repoint it, see 2), the relocation editor on Settings → Website (`src/app/admin/website/page.tsx`, `SitePageForm.tsx` starter note), the translation-queue label (`src/app/management/translations/actions.ts`), `sitePages.relocationStarter` in both dictionaries, the public path in `src/lib/public-paths.ts` and `worker/index.mjs`, and any manual text. **Remove the relocation line from `unreleased`** rather than adding a "removed" line — no user ever saw it. Search for `relocation` across `src/`, `worker/` and `src/lib/assistant/` (the assistant's `move.ts` hit is about moving residents between enclosures — check, don't touch). The `relocation` row in `site_pages` (0099) is not dropped: step 2 renames it. Before the rename, check whether an admin saved a body on dev or production; if it is relocation text, clear it so the new page starts from its own starter text. With relocation gone, **Services has no entries** — hide the group until desexing drives (Public website) gives it one, rather than showing an empty menu. **2. An International adoptions page — repurpose the relocation page (decided by Lutan, 2026-09-27).** **Repurpose it** — rename the slug `relocation` → e.g. `international-adoption` in a tiny schema PR (update the row and the `site_pages.slug` check constraint, re-runnable), route at `/adopt/international` (or `/international-adoption`), reuse the `SitePageView` + starter-text plumbing already built. The plumbing (starter text from the dictionaries, admin editor prefill, translation queue) is exactly what the new page needs and only the words change. A new page with a fresh slug was the alternative and was turned down. The schema PR comes first. **Where it links from:** under **Adopt** in the header and footer (it is adoption, not a service), and the `/adopt` listing's line for adopters abroad. **Content:** generic starter text in English and Thai in our own words, same rule as relocation: what an international adoption involves in outline (meeting the animal, the paperwork and health requirements an overseas home usually needs, travel being arranged with the adopter) — **no fees, timelines, countries served, partner names or licence claims**; those come from the shelter. `generateMetadata` with OG tags, a releases line for the new page. **3. The illustration: sketch C, "Over the globe" — with the pyjamas.** Of the three caped-puppy sketches drawn for relocation (A straight-out flight, B carrying a crate — the one used, C over the globe), Lutan chose **C** for International adoptions (confirmed 2026-09-27): the puppy flying up and out over the curve of the planet, a dotted route trailing back to a pin. **But always in its orange pyjamas** — sketch C was drawn in plain fur; use the pyjama body from sketch A / the current `PuppyFlying`. The sketch lived only in the relocation session's scratchpad, so its globe is recorded here: a 240×160 viewBox, a planet `circle cx=120 cy=330 r=190` in soft forest green with two darker land shapes clipped to it (`M20 150 q20 -14 44 -6 q18 6 12 20 h-60z` and `M130 142 q24 -8 40 2 q14 10 34 4 l10 20 h-90z`), a terracotta pin at (44, 147), a dashed terracotta route `M44 146 C40 120 52 96 70 84` (dash `5 3`), a cloud top right, and the puppy at `translate(40 -8) scale(.82)`; in C the cape was forest green with a terracotta collar. Add it to `src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx` beside `PuppyFlying` (e.g. `PuppyOverGlobe`), same approach: plain SVG, colours from `--site-action` / `--site-accent` so it follows the dev recolour, animated in `globals.css` (cape ripple, gentle bob, and the route could draw itself in) with a still pose under `prefers-reduced-motion`, decorative (`aria-hidden`). Show Lutan the pyjama version before wiring it in. Decide what happens to `PuppyFlying` (the crate): keep it if anything else uses it, otherwise delete it with the page. Record the reversal in `docs/decisions.md` (the relocation entry of 2026-09-27 stays as history), and update the Settings → Website tabs item (Admin), which still mentions a Pet relocation page.

Release line and manual updated. — the follow-up `claude/medical-photos-profile` was told to flag rather than fold in, since `0102` held the batch's migration slot. The app now refuses *choosing* a Medical photo as the profile photo, but `record_attachment` still makes a resident's first upload the profile photo whatever its folder and `delete_resident_photo` falls back to the oldest — on dev 7 of the 8 residents with a Medical profile photo have no photo in any other folder, and Markey is on the website. Redefine `public_resident_profiles`, `public_recent_adoptions` and `public_resident_cards` so `profile_photo_drive_file_id` is null when that file is a Medical attachment (`lower(btrim(coalesce(sub_folder, ''))) = 'medical'`, the test `0101` uses), same column lists so grants carry over; `is_public_drive_file()` then stops serving it to a signed-out visitor too. The photo stays the profile photo *inside* the app, where a Medical photo is fine. Watch the `private.resident_current_state` trap `0101` documents. Consider also teaching `record_attachment` / `delete_resident_photo` to skip Medical when choosing automatically. `node scripts/check-medical-photos.mjs` lists the residents affected; the test is that Markey's `/adopt` card shows no photo and `/api/photos/<his profile file>` is 404 signed out. Then drop the "that is being fixed next" clause from the release line.
- [x] **Bug: uploading a Shelter Friend logo fails [token fixed and retested 2026-09-25; code follow-ups done 2026-09-26] with "Google OAuth token refresh failed: invalid_grant: Token has been expired or revoked."** Reported by Lutan 2026-09-25 with a screenshot of the Shelter Friend card on a contact (Save pressed after choosing a logo; the address/map opt-ins visible above). **Environment: UAT, lannacare.org** (confirmed by Lutan). **Urgent: uploads on the site the customer is testing are down.** lannacare.org is served by the `production` block in `wrangler.jsonc`, whose Drive is still the dev Google account (lannaanimalfoundationbwm@gmail.com), so that account's Google Cloud project is where to look. Checked 2026-09-25: the local `.env.local` / `.dev.vars` credentials now fail with `invalid_client` ("The OAuth client was not found"), so they can't stand in for UAT's, and a new token has to be minted. **Cause is not the logo code:** the message comes from `src/lib/google/drive.ts` (token refresh, ~line 105): Google refused `GOOGLE_OAUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN` for that environment. So **every Drive upload on that environment is failing** (resident photos, hero, logos, project photos, backups), not just this one. Different from the dev-only `invalid_client` seen 2026-09-24 (a dead client); `invalid_grant` means the refresh token itself was revoked or expired. Usual reasons: the OAuth consent screen of the Drive client's project is in *Testing* (refresh tokens die after 7 days), the Google account's password was changed or access removed, or a newer refresh token was issued for the same client and account (Google keeps a limited number). **Fix:** (1) find the reason in the Drive client's Google Cloud project (under the account that owns the Drive; see the 90-day-token item too), publish the consent screen if it is in Testing, (2) mint a new refresh token and set it as the Worker secret for the affected environment (and `.dev.vars` / `.env.local` locally), (3) redeploy and retry the logo upload. **Code follow-ups, same PR or next:** show staff a plain message ("Photo storage is not connected — tell an admin") instead of Google's raw text, and make sure a failed logo upload doesn't throw away the rest of the Shelter Friend form; report it on the release/health check (e.g. a Drive token check in `scripts/` or the admin page) so an expired token shows up before a user hits it. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (token):** cause confirmed. The Drive client "Lanna Care Drive Access" (Desktop, created 2026-09-18) lives in the lanna account's project **Lanna Care - Dev** (`lanna-care-dev`), which was still in *Testing*, so its refresh token expired 7 days later, on 2026-09-25. The v0.5.0 deploy was not involved (no secret changes in `wrangler deployments list`). Published the project (logo removed, since a logo forces verification; home page `https://lannacare.org` and privacy `https://lannacare.org/privacy` added; `lannacare.org` added as an authorised domain), minted a new token, and set it in `.env.local` and as `GOOGLE_OAUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN` on the `production` and `test` Workers. Also removed a stray space after `=` on the Google keys in `.env.local`. Lutan retested the Shelter Friend logo upload on lannacare.org 2026-09-25: works. **Code follow-ups done 2026-09-26** (`claude/drive-upload-errors`): a dead token or client now raises `DriveNotConnectedError`, and every upload (logo, Website photos, resident/project/maintenance photos, attachments) and Drive folder sync says "Photo storage is not connected — tell an admin.", with Google's text going to the server log. The form itself was never lost (the logo uploads on its own, apart from Save, and the draft survives), but its message showed below Save, out of sight; it now sits by the logo. Settings (`/admin`) shows whether Drive is connected, and `scripts/check-drive-token.mjs` checks an env file.

- [x] **Give the public site some life: spring motion on text and content blocks.** Lutan, 2026-09-26: some public websites have a "springy" feel to their text blocks, and the public pages should feel less static. **What it means here:** headings, text blocks and cards ease into place as they scroll into view (a short rise and fade with a slight spring overshoot, staggered within a group), and interactive cards (the "How you can help" cards, resident cards, Shelter Friend logos, buttons) give a small springy lift on hover and press. Subtle, not a show: short distances, around 300 to 600 ms, each block animates once. **How:** prefer plain CSS with the `linear()` easing function (it can express a real spring curve) and an `IntersectionObserver` that adds a class, so there is no animation library and almost no bundle cost. Reach for a library (e.g. `motion`) only if something needs physics CSS cannot fake, and say why in `docs/decisions.md`. **Must:** respect `prefers-reduced-motion` (no movement at all, content simply present), never hide content from anyone without JavaScript or from search engines (start visible and animate only once JS has run, or make the no-JS state the final one), no layout shift, and keep the staff app itself calm (public pages only). **When:** build it on the redesigned components, so after `public-site-shell` (batch 2 of the 2026-09-25 plan). It can ride with or follow `public-site-home`. First, agree the feel with Lutan on one section (e.g. the "How you can help" cards) with two or three variants in a preview before rolling it across the site. Reference sites Lutan likes would help; ask for a link.
- [x] **A puppy-at-a-laptop loading animation for page changes and slow loads.** [done 2026-09-26: Lutan chose sketch A (side-on, nose to the screen) for the public site and the one-colour glyph C for the staff app; `src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx`, root `src/app/loading.tsx`] Lutan, 2026-09-26: the RSPCA site shows a little running dog while a page changes or is waiting to load; the shelter's site should have its own. **Artwork must be our own.** Don't copy RSPCA's animation. Draw an original dog (ideally the shelter's logo dog, or a dog and a cat taking turns) as a looping run cycle. **Format:** an animated SVG (CSS keyframes on a few shapes) or a Lottie file rather than a GIF, so it is tiny, crisp at any size, recolourable to the redesign palette, and can be paused. **Where it shows:** the public pages' `loading.tsx` (Next's route-level loading UI) and client-side route changes, plus any wait over about a second such as a form submit or a photo upload. The staff app gets it too (Lutan, 2026-09-26): a small inline version for uploads and slow pages, not a full-screen overlay. **Behaviour:** appear only after a short delay (about 300 ms) so fast navigations don't flash it, and have an accessible label ("Loading", en and th) with `role="status"`. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` show a still dog with the label instead of the run cycle. **When:** with or after `public-site-shell` (redesign part 1), so it uses the new palette; it pairs naturally with the spring-motion item above. **Reference and concept (Lutan, 2026-09-26):** not a running dog after all, but **a puppy working on a laptop**, drawn from Lutan's own video (`C:UsersLeidosOneDrivePicturesIMG_0004 (2).MOV`, 2.6 s, portrait): a fluffy apricot poodle-cross in orange patterned pyjamas, leaning in and peering at an open laptop, nose close to the screen. Loop idea: the puppy leans in, looks at the screen, taps or paws at the keyboard, head bobs, with a small progress bar or dots on the laptop screen as the "loading" signal. Keep it a simple, flat illustration in the redesign palette, not a traced photo. Start from that, agree the drawing with him (two or three sketches or frames in a preview), then build.

## Security

**Moved above Deployment on 2026-09-30 (Lutan): severity governs here, not
file position.** The review appended this section at the end of the file,
which by this file's own convention ("ordered by suggested priority") read as
the lowest priority in the backlog while it held the only High finding and a
live open redirect. That was an artifact of how it was written, not a
judgement — so the section was moved rather than left to mislead the next
planner.

From the 2026-09-30 security assessment (`docs/security/security-assessment-2026-09-30.docx`, `.pdf`; commit d26103b, release 0.10.0). Finding IDs (DB-n, CODE-n, WEB-n) are the report's. No Critical finding; one High. Items are grouped so each is one stream; schema items are `claude/<name>-schema` PRs first, per the migrations rules. Effort is the report's. None of these makes the app harder for field staff.

- [x] **Next 16.3.5 carries a Critical RCE advisory; the fix is a patch bump that is "outside the stated dependency range" (Critical).** Surfaced 2026-10-01 by the new `audit` job on PR #265, which is — as far as the run history shows — the first PR to execute it: `next 16.2.0 - 16.3.5`, **critical**, *"Next.js: Remote Code Execution in next/og ImageResponse"*, [GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j), `fix available via npm audit fix --force`, `Will install next@16.3.8, which is outside the stated dependency range`. `package.json` pins `"next": "16.3.5"`, so **`main`, test and production are all affected equally** — this is not a property of the PR that found it. **Worth putting beside the 2026-09-30 assessment, which recorded "No Critical findings":** that was true of the code, and the first Critical to appear came from the supply chain instead, caught by a job the same security batch added. The chain is what the review could not see by reading the repo. **The mitigating fact, and it is a real one:** `next/og` and `ImageResponse` appear **nowhere** in `src/` or `worker/` (grepped 2026-10-01), so the vulnerable entry point is not reachable in this app — the dependency is vulnerable, the code path is unused. That is the difference between "patch on the next convenient day" and "stop and patch now", and it is why this is a backlog item rather than a hotfix. Do not treat it as safe indefinitely, though: a future feature that wants an Open Graph image would reach for exactly that API, and the person adding it will not know this. **Why it is not a one-line bump:** `AGENTS.md` opens by saying this is a modified Next whose APIs, conventions and file structure may differ from training data, and npm itself reports `16.3.8` as outside the declared range. So it needs its own PR: read `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` and the upstream notes for 16.3.6–16.3.8, bump, run the full gates, and **check the two things this project has already been bitten by around builds** — that `strip-baked-env` still finds and empties `next-env.mjs` (its shape is an OpenNext implementation detail), and that `getSiteOrigin()`'s header reading still behaves on the Pi behind cloudflared, since a sign-in outage on 30 September came from exactly that area. **Also fix the reporting gap this exposed:** `audit` is `if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'`, so a push to `main` skips it and nothing reports a new advisory between PRs — an advisory published on a quiet day is invisible until someone happens to open a PR. Its `continue-on-error: true` is correct and should stay (the job's own comment argues the case well, and contrasts it with `test-plan`), but "reported, not enforced" only works if it is actually reported: consider running it on a schedule as well, so a Critical lands somewhere a person sees without a PR to carry it. **Status 2026-10-02:** the bump landed (#268, Next 16.3.8) and the reporting gap is closed by `.github/workflows/advisories.yml` (daily, fails on purpose; docs/decisions/2026-10-02-scheduled-audit-fails-on-purpose.md). **Still open, so not ticked:** `getSiteOrigin()`/sign-in on the Pi behind cloudflared has not been confirmed on 16.3.8 (#268 verified sign-in on a local production build only, and was not deployed). **Status 2026-10-06 (verified by `/plan-day`, not ticked): the Critical is RESOLVED.** `48b1a27a` *"Bump next and eslint-config-next to 16.3.8 for GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j"* landed the fix, so `package.json` now pins **`next: 16.3.8`** — the version the advisory names — and `npm audit --audit-level=critical` reports **no critical vulnerabilities**. `next/og` and `ImageResponse` are still absent from `src/` and `worker/`, so the re-grep the item asked for still holds. **The 5 high-severity advisories that remain are dev-only:** `npm audit --omit=dev` finds **0 vulnerabilities**; the highs are `fast-glob` reached through `@next/eslint-plugin-next` → `eslint-config-next`, i.e. the lint toolchain, not anything shipped. **Left unticked deliberately** — the item also carries the standing warning that a future Open Graph feature would reach for exactly that API and the person adding it will not know this, so whoever owns Security should decide whether that warning belongs somewhere more durable than a closed backlog item before ticking. **Closed 2026-10-07 (claude/next-og-warning-durable):** the standing warning now has a durable home — `scripts/check-next-og.mjs`, run by `npm run lint`, fails if `next/og`/`ImageResponse`/an OG convention file appears while Next is below 16.3.8 (docs/decisions/2026-10-07-next-og-warning-lives-in-lint.md). Re-verified: `npm audit --omit=dev` 0 vulnerabilities, no `next/og` in `src/` or `worker/`. Not confirmed: `getSiteOrigin()` on the Pi behind cloudflared on 16.3.8 — file separately if still wanted.

- [x] **Encrypt the weekly backup and keep session tables out of it (DB-1, High).** **Done 2026-10-02** (rollout in `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-backup-encryption-rollout.md`, code in #243): key pair made by Lutan, private key in two places only; first encrypted production run on the Pi (`.dump.age`, link not logged); a decrypt rehearsal gave a valid archive with the session and MFA tables absent; old plaintext dumps deleted on the Pi and in Drive, `backup.log` truncated; `Backups/` moved to `Admin/Backups`, shared with Lutan only; every production session and refresh token rotated. Left over, split out as its own item below: clearing the MFA factors. The restore itself belongs to DB-2 and the Deployment restore item. `scripts/backup.mjs:44` dumps `public` and `auth` with `pg_dump` and uploads the file to the shelter's Drive as-is; `auth` holds password hashes, TOTP secrets in plain text and live refresh tokens, and line 104 logs the shareable link to `backup.log`. Encrypt before upload with a key kept only by the administrator (age, gpg or `openssl enc -aes-256-cbc -pbkdf2`; key in a password manager, never the repo or `.env`), add `--exclude-table=auth.refresh_tokens --exclude-table=auth.sessions --exclude-table='auth.mfa_*'`, put `Backups/` in a folder shared with the admin only rather than the application root, and stop logging the web link. Then rehearse a restore. Half a day. **Do first.** **Update 2026-09-30:** the weekly backup now runs on the Raspberry Pi as well as the laptop (`--local-copy`, cron Sundays 03:00), so **the Pi's SSD also holds plaintext dumps**, and its `~/backups/backup.log` collects the Drive link every week. Encrypt with `age` public-key encryption so the decrypting key never sits on the Pi, and encrypt before both the upload and the local copy. **Status 2026-09-30:** code done in PR #243 (age public key, exclusions, link no longer logged, tile reads `.dump.age`); **left open until Lutan** generates the key pair, installs the public key on the Pi and laptop, does the first run and the restore rehearsal, deletes the old plaintext dumps and the Pi's `backup.log`, re-shares `Backups/` and rotates (test plan manual table).
- [ ] **Clear the production MFA factors left over from DB-1, and re-enrol.** From the DB-1 rollout (2026-10-02): Lutan's authenticator (TOTP) secret was in the plaintext dumps that were exposed before encryption, and the factors were not cleared when sessions were rotated. Lutan is the only person with one. In the production SQL editor: `delete from auth.mfa_factors;`, then sign in, open Settings → Security and enrol the authenticator app again. A few minutes, Lutan's to run.
- [x] **Backups from an always-on machine, and a rehearsed restore (DB-2).** **Always-on half done 2026-09-30, closed 2026-10-04:** the weekly backup runs from cron on the Pi (Sundays 03:00, `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-one-pi-hosting-and-backup.md`), and a red Weekly backup tile already emails admins (`src/lib/status/alerts.ts`). **The restore rehearsal was not done** and is carried by "Rehearse a restore from a production backup" (Deployment) rather than kept open in two places; retiring the laptop schedule is the "Confirm the Pi's first scheduled backup" item. The scheduled task runs only while the developer's Windows account is logged on (`scripts/backup-schedule.ps1:43-46`); `backup.log` shows two manual runs (21 and 26 Sep), and the restore procedure has never been tested. Move the schedule to a GitHub Actions workflow or the Pi in `docs/pi-hosting.md`, restore once into a scratch Supabase project and write the steps down, repeat quarterly, and have the "Weekly backup" status tile alert admins when it goes red. A day, including the rehearsal. Do together with the item above.
- [x] **HTTP security headers and Secure cookies (WEB-2).** *Done in the Worker; the CSP is report-only, tightening owed (see docs/decisions/2026-09-30-security-headers-in-the-worker.md).* Nothing sets CSP, X-Frame-Options, HSTS, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy or nosniff, and the Supabase cookie is created without `secure`. Add `headers()` in `next.config.ts` (or the Worker): `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy: camera=(self), geolocation=()`, HSTS if the Cloudflare zone does not already send it; CSP in report-only first (`default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; frame-src https://www.google.com`), tighten after a quiet week; `cookieOptions: { secure: true }` in production on both Supabase client factories. Camera must stay allowed for photos and QR scanning. Also covers CODE-7's proxy half (`nosniff` and `Content-Disposition` on `src/app/api/photos/[fileId]/route.ts`).
- [x] **Tighten the CSP (WEB-2 follow-up).** *Collection is done (2026-10-01, `claude/csp-tighten-and-collect`): the Worker answers `POST /api/csp-report` and logs a bounded one-line summary per violation, readable in Workers Logs (search `csp-report`); `docs/decisions/2026-10-01-csp-report-collection.md`. Enforcement is deliberately not on, because no reports existed before that change.* **Gate: after the collector has been deployed to production for a week**, read every `csp-report` line and explain or fix each distinct (directive, blocked) pair; if the week is thin or has unexplained hits, wait longer. Then: switch `content-security-policy-report-only` to `content-security-policy` in `worker/security-headers.mjs` (keep `frame-src https://www.google.com`, `img-src 'self' data: blob:` and `camera=(self)`); drop `'unsafe-inline'` from `script-src` with nonces only if they can reach every inline script on **both** render paths (the Pi's `next start` and the Worker's OpenNext render), otherwise leave it and say why; consider raising HSTS above six months once stable. A blocking CSP breaks public pages for visitors, so check `/`, `/adopt`, `/our-work`, a Shelter Friend page and the camera on a phone before merging. **CHECKED 2026-10-07 (staleness sweep) — built and merged today; not ticked because what is left is a person looking.** `59238341` (#431, `claude/csp-enforce`, 2026-10-07): `worker/security-headers.mjs` sends `content-security-policy` (line 46). Separately `deleteMaintenanceJob` (`maintenance/actions.ts:308-323`) deletes the job's attachment rows before it learns the caller may not delete the job, so any volunteer can strip a job's photo records: delete the job first and let a foreign-key cascade take the attachments, or check the role up front as `deceased/actions.ts:92-95` does. - [ ] **Retention period for what people typed at the assistant (DB-11) — decision first.** Split out of "Defence in depth and hygiene" on 2026-10-01 because it needs a decision and a migration, and bundling it meant a stream would either stall on the decision or invent a policy. `assistant_actions.request_text` (`0070`) keeps the **raw text a user typed** at the assistant, indefinitely. The assessment suggests stating a retention period (it guessed 90 days) and adding a scheduled delete, **or** storing only the parsed intent and never keeping the raw text at all. **The period is Lutan's to set and he has deferred it (2026-10-01): do not build against a guessed number.** The trade-off to put to him: keeping the raw text is what lets anyone see *why* the assistant misread a request, which is the main way the keyword assistant gets better; dropping it removes the retention question entirely but loses that. Once a period is set this is a small schema PR (a scheduled delete) plus a line in the privacy page if the text is user-supplied content being kept for a stated time.

(a) **Turn off open sign-up, or restrict Google sign-in to the shelter's domain** (WEB-3) — on **dev and production**; written in `signup-bounds-and-mfa` 2026-10-01 and again in `signup-bounds-two-step` 2026-10-02, and done neither time. (c) **Authentication → Sessions → inactivity timeout = 60 days** (`session-inactivity-timeout`, 2026-10-01, WEB-11). Lutan's console, not a session's: nothing in the repo can set or read these. The one way to tell they are done is the console itself, so tick this item with the date rather than leaving it to be inferred. **PARTLY DONE 2026-10-07 — and the item's framing was wrong twice.** **(1) "Nothing in the repo can set or read these" is false.** The Supabase **Management API** does both: `GET`/`PATCH https://api.supabase.com/v1/projects/<ref>/config/auth` with `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` from `.env.local` — the same token and endpoint `apply-migrations.mjs` already uses. The three settings are `disable_signup`, `password_hibp_enabled` and `sessions_inactivity_timeout`. **So they can be verified from outside the console**, which is what this item said was impossible, and a future session should check rather than ask. **(2) Two of the three are NOT clickable on the current plan**, which is the likely reason this sat open from 2026-10-01 being treated as three forgotten toggles: **`password_hibp_enabled`** → `402 Configuring leaked password protection via HaveIBeenPwned.org is available on Pro Plans and up`; **`sessions_inactivity_timeout`** → `402 User sessions can only be configured on Pro Plans and up`. **Both are a plan decision, not a task.** **DONE: (a) open sign-up is off on BOTH projects.** `disable_signup: true` on production `dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu` and dev/test `qxkmhwybjggxvsfxsxbd`, set and read back on 2026-10-07; both sites answered 200 on `/` and `/login` afterwards. **Safe because staff are created through Settings → Security** with the service role, and the `check-*` harnesses make their disposable logins with `service.auth.admin.createUser()`, which bypasses the sign-up setting — checked before changing dev. **STILL OPEN, and now a question for Lutan rather than a chore:** (b) and (c) need **Supabase Pro**. Worth weighing against what they buy: **(b)** only matters where passwords are used at all, and staff sign in with Google; **(c)** was WEB-11, severity *Info*, and the 400-day cookie is deliberate. **Free partial substitutes if Pro is not wanted:** `password_min_length` is **6** on both projects and is settable on the free plan — raising it is a real improvement for the email-and-password accounts that do exist; and an app-level idle sign-out would cover (c) without the plan, at the cost of building it. **Do not tick this item until (b) and (c) are either bought or consciously declined.** **ALSO DONE 2026-10-07, both substitutes applied on Lutan's go:** **`password_min_length` raised from 6 to 10** on production and dev/test (set and read back). **Checked first that it breaks no tooling:** every `check-*` harness generates its disposable login's password with `randomBytes(18)` (~24 chars) or `randomBytes(12)` (~16), and the nine `randomBytes(4)` uses are email tags, not passwords — so the shortest generated password is well clear of 10. It applies only when a password is **set or changed**, so no existing account is locked out. And the **app-level idle sign-out** is filed as its own item above, as the free substitute for (c). **CLOSED 2026-10-07 (Lutan): (a) done, (b) and (c) DECLINED. Do not reopen without a reason that is new.** His decision, in his words: *"it is not worth keeping on the list and the site is a Not for Profit shelter site so risks are low and data that could be stolen is of no real consequence."* **One correction to that reasoning, recorded so the file is accurate rather than flattering:** the data is not quite inconsequential — the system holds **carers' home addresses and phone numbers** (the subject of the contacts decision the same day), staff emails, and the **backups contain password hashes and TOTP secrets**, which is why DB-1 existed and why the backups are now `age`-encrypted. So "nothing worth stealing" is not the ground to stand on. **But the decision is right anyway, on stronger ground, and this is the reason to keep:** **(b)** buys little because staff sign in with **Google**, and the few email-and-password accounts are now covered by `password_min_length` **10**; **(c)** is severity **Info**, the 400-day cookie is deliberate by design, and the server-side substitute is a migration plus a change to the request path. **Both were also Pro-plan only**, so the choice was buy a subscription or accept the risk — and for two low-value controls, accepting it is reasonable. **What is actually in place:** open sign-up **off** on both projects, minimum password length **10** on both, TOTP two-step available and enrolled, and backups encrypted.

Until this is done the encryption protects the backups from everyone including the shelter. The README restore recipe has never been exercised either, which is the natural second half of this item. **Status 2026-10-05 (Lutan, in chat): the custody half is DONE.** The key pair exists and the private half is held in two places — an encrypted Kangaroo drive and his Apple Keychain — which is what this item asked for (generate on his own machine, keep the whole file in a password manager, not on the Pi, not in chat). `scripts/backup.mjs` **refuses to run** without `BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT` or `--age-recipient`, so it cannot write a plaintext dump: *"the backups are still plaintext"* was carried in planning notes after it had stopped being true, and should not be repeated. **What remains is the restore rehearsal** — the README recipe has still never been exercised — which is also the separate *Rehearse a restore from a production backup* item under Deployment, now carrying DB-2's restore half. **One thing worth confirming once, not checked here:** `BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT` is absent from this laptop's `.env.local` and empty in `.env.example`, so a backup run from the laptop would refuse; whether it is set on the Pi, where the cron actually runs, is unverified.

(b) **`GOOGLE_SIGNIN_CLIENT_SECRET` needs rotating in the Google Cloud console** — the QA session exposed its value in a transcript on 2026-10-01 and reported it. That one lived only in chat until now, which is exactly how it would have been lost. Both are Lutan's; neither is something a session should do. **STOCKTAKE FIRST — Lutan's instruction, 2026-10-07, after an attempt at the secret rotation went wrong three times:** *"next time let's start with a full stocktake of all Google accounts and clients first."* **Do that before touching anything, and do not plan a click-path out of `docs/google-cloud-inventory.md` until it has been done** — that doc marks most of its Google facts `[CONFIRM]`, meaning **not verified**, and a session read them as settled and sent Lutan to the wrong client. **What the stocktake should produce, per account:** every Google account that owns anything (at least `lutan.bennett2@gmail.com` and `lannaanimalfoundationbwm@gmail.com`); every project in each, with id and **project number**; and for every OAuth client: **name, type, client ID, and its authorised redirect URIs**. **The redirect URI is the only reliable identifier** — client names are Google defaults (`Web Client 1`) and say nothing about purpose. **What was actually established on 2026-10-07, and is worth keeping:** (1) **LCA App's project number is `104841707674`** (the doc had it as `[CONFIRM]`, not in the repo); (2) **Supabase production (`dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`) authenticates against client `104841707674-bjgla5kd0i4rbifi5buuekea7jqd74dp`**, read live from the Management API — a plain identifier, not a secret; (3) **dev/test (`qxkmhwybjggxvsfxsxbd`) uses `1036347359893-tk4qklicq40o496onnqan8j8mf1od331`**, a different client in the lanna account, so **rotating production's secret cannot break test**; (4) **a second client `104841707674-drg7qtiia19jbq…` exists in LCA App** and Lutan believed it was production's — **unexplained, and the stocktake should settle what it is**; (5) **the `GOOGLE_SIGNIN_CLIENT_SECRET=` line in `.env.deploy.production` is malformed** — 131 characters, starting `GOCSPX-` *and* containing `apps.googleusercontent.com`, i.e. a secret and a client ID concatenated with no separator. **Splitting it would reveal which client the recorded secret belongs to.** (6) **Supabase's Management API does not return the Google secret in plaintext** (64 url-safe chars beginning `018`), so **do not compare it against a `GOCSPX-` value** — a session did and drew a false conclusion. **Also still true and useful:** `scripts/deploy.mjs` never pushes `GOOGLE_SIGNIN_CLIENT_SECRET` (`RUNTIME_SECRETS` carries the Drive one instead) and no file under `src/` or `worker/` reads it, **so a rotation needs no deploy**; and rotating does not sign anyone out, because the secret is only used during the sign-in exchange.

(b) **Who opens 2-step set-up for a real admin who has no app yet** — `node scripts/bootstrap-admin.mjs --env production --allow-2step-setup <email>`, or another admin's **Allow set-up**.
