Admins enrolled before that date are grandfathered and unaffected. **DECIDED 2026-10-07 (Lutan, decision brief part 2 q13): **(a) YES — refuse the admin role until the person has enrolled in 2-step.** Option A, after nine releases open. **The lockout risk is real and the brief stated it**, so treat this as the precondition rather than a footnote: **a second admin account must exist and be enrolled BEFORE this ships**, or losing one phone locks the shelter out of its own Settings. Build order therefore: create and enrol the second admin (Lutan's, on Settings → Security), confirm both can sign in, *then* turn on the refusal. **Do not ship the refusal and the second account in the same change** — if the account creation is what breaks, the refusal must not already be live. The other half of this item (the 2-step set-up decision) is answered by the same choice; check whether anything else in it is still open before ticking.
- [x] **A high advisory in `sharp` reaches the production tree through the Cloudflare build chain, and the only fix npm offers is a breaking `wrangler` change.** Found 2026-10-07 on PR #405's `audit` job, one day after the `source-map-js` high was closed by #398. **The advisory:** `sharp <0.35.5`, **high**, [GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w) — *Vulnerability in librsvg dependency*, CVE-2026-96889. **How it reaches production:** `@opennextjs/cloudflare@1.20.6` is a **`dependencies`** entry (wrangler itself is only a devDependency, which is why this is easy to misread) → `wrangler@4.135.0` → `miniflare@5.x-alpha` → `sharp@0.35.4`; **`next` also pulls `sharp@0.35.4` directly**. So `npm audit --omit=dev` sees it and `npm run audit:prod` fails. **Why it is not a one-liner:** `npm audit fix --force` reports *"Will install wrangler@4.15.2, which is a breaking change"* — a major move of the Cloudflare build chain, which is how the app is built and deployed. **Do not take that fix inside an unrelated PR**, and weigh a narrower route first: a newer `@opennextjs/cloudflare` that depends on a patched wrangler, a `sharp` bump on its own, or an `overrides` entry pinning `sharp >= 0.35.5` (noting an override pins a transitive dependency behind the maintainer's back and needs removing when upstream catches up). **Reachability is worth establishing before urgency:** the flaw is in **librsvg**, i.e. SVG rendering inside `sharp`. Next only processes SVGs through its image optimiser when `dangerouslyAllowSVG` is set — **check whether it is** — and `miniflare` is the local Worker simulator, which does not run in production. If no untrusted SVG reaches `sharp`, this is *"patch on the next convenient day"* rather than a hotfix, which is exactly the judgement the Next RCE item recorded for `next/og`. **Expect `advisories.yml` to be red daily until it is settled** — that is the design (it fails on purpose so a human is emailed), and **the PR `audit` job will be red on every open PR meanwhile, which does not block merging** (`continue-on-error: true`, deliberate; `ci.yml` says *"Do not 'tidy' the two to match"*). **Say so on any PR that meets it**, so nobody re-derives it. **DONE 2026-10-07 (`claude/sharp-override`):** the narrower route this item asked to weigh first was the right one — `"overrides": { "sharp": "^0.35.5" }` in `package.json` plus the lockfile, and `npm run audit:prod` goes from **4 high to 0 vulnerabilities**. The breaking `wrangler` downgrade was **not** taken, and the reason is worth keeping: there is no newer release to move to — `miniflare` pins `sharp` at exactly `0.35.4` and every current `wrangler` pins that `miniflare` — so npm's only route was backwards, and a major regression of the chain that builds and deploys the app is the larger risk by a wide margin. **Reachability was established before urgency, and it is nil:** `next.config.ts` sets `images: { unoptimized: true }` and `dangerouslyAllowSVG` appears nowhere in the repo, so the `sharp`-backed image optimiser is never invoked, and `miniflare` is the local Worker simulator, which does not run in production. Hygiene, on the same reading the `next/og` item recorded. `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-sharp-override.md` has the argument and the condition under which it expires. Verified with `npm run opennext:build` as well as the three gates, because `next build` never loads `wrangler` or `miniflare` and so cannot exercise the pin that was overridden. **What the red cost, now addressed in CLAUDE.md:** it stalled the production release manager for about 45 minutes on 2026-10-07 working out whether the release could proceed, and it had been red on every PR for two days with nobody asking why. CLAUDE.md said nothing about the `audit` job while documenting a red `test-plan` at length, so it now carries a section stating that a red `audit` cannot fail a run or block a merge (measured across the last 30 pull-request runs: not one concluded failure because of `audit`), what a red does ask for, and the two standing do-nots. **Follow-up on the `backlog` branch, not here:** delete the override once `wrangler` and `miniflare` ship a `sharp` past 0.35.5. → *Security*
- [ ] **Delete the `sharp` override once `wrangler` and `miniflare` ship a `sharp` past 0.35.5.** Added 2026-10-07 by `claude/sharp-override`, which put `"overrides": { "sharp": "^0.35.5" }` in `package.json` to clear [GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w) (`sharp <0.35.5`, high, a flaw in the librsvg copy `sharp` bundles) out of the production tree. **Why it is a follow-up and not a defect:** an `overrides` entry pins a transitive dependency behind its maintainer's back — `miniflare` asked for exactly `0.35.4` and is getting `0.35.5` anyway. That is sound for a patch bump and nothing more, and it is not a licence to hold the pin for good: left in place it will one day silently hold `sharp` *back* from a version the chain actually wants, and that failure is much harder to read than the advisory it fixed. **The check:** when `npm ls sharp --omit=dev` shows the chain resolving to 0.35.5 or later on its own, delete the `overrides` block, run `npm install`, and confirm `npm run audit:prod` is still clean. `npm run opennext:build` is the gate that matters, not just `npm run build` — `next build` never loads `wrangler` or `miniflare`, so it cannot exercise the pin. **Not urgent, and do not chase it:** the advisory is not reachable in this app (`images: { unoptimized: true }`, no `dangerouslyAllowSVG`, and `miniflare` is the local simulator), so nothing is at risk either way; this is tidying a deliberate temporary measure. `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-sharp-override.md` has the full argument. → *Security*
- [x] **Two `public-views` failures overnight on 2026-10-07 that nothing explains.** **Explained 2026-10-07 (`public-views-overnight`):** both failed only on `is_admin(): anon EXECUTE is refused — HTTP 200: false`; 0153's first draft granted `is_admin()` to `anon`, was applied to dev at 00:19:57Z and corrected by hand by ~00:36Z. A real grant on dev, not a flaky check; production never had it. `apply-migrations.mjs` now refuses a file `check-migration-grants` rejects, and `check-public-views` repeats its failures at the end. `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-public-views-overnight.md`. Found while fixing the daytime flood (PR #420), and deliberately **not** folded into it — recorded as open defect 2 in `docs/test-plans/public-views-impact-figures.md`. **The runs:** `37552423077` (00:31Z) and `37551809878` (00:24Z), both `pull_request`, on `claude/worktree-held-reason` and `claude/photo-routes-off-predicate` respectively; `public-views` failed on both and the job was green again by morning. **Why the obvious answer is wrong:** the daytime failures were `0156` granting `public_impact_figures` to `anon` without declaring it in the allowlist, but `0156` only merged at 06:56 on 2026-10-07, hours *after* these. So this is a second, unidentified cause. **Why it is worth an hour:** `public-views` is the check that the anonymous tier of the database can read the `public_*` views and nothing else — it is not `continue-on-error`, it fails the run, and it is the one check whose red could mean real exposure. A failure that comes and goes on its own is exactly the shape nobody chases, and it is also the shape a genuine intermittent leak would have. **Where to start:** read the failed step's log on both runs (`gh run view <id> --log-failed`), which says which object or probe failed; then whether anything was applied to dev around 00:20–00:35 (`apply-migrations.mjs --status`, and the two branches' own migrations), and whether the two branches shared a migration. Rule out the mundane explanations first: Supabase rate-limiting or a cold start answering a probe with a 5xx the script reads as a failure, and the secrets being momentarily absent (the job exits 0 and warns in that case, so it should *not* present as a failure — if it did, that is itself the bug). **If it turns out to be flakiness in the script**, make it distinguish a failed assertion from a failed request, because today they are the same red. → *Security*
- [ ] **The next view a migration creates will again be writable by every signed-in login.** Found 2026-10-07 by `resident-view-grants-0160` (`0160`, `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-view-write-grants.md`). The project's default privileges give `authenticated` insert, update, delete, truncate, references and trigger on every new object, views included; `0160` revoked them from the six views that had them (five inert, one writable but no wider than its table). Nothing stops the seventh. **Fix, cheapest first:** teach `scripts/check-migration-grants.mjs` that a file creating a view must also `revoke insert, update, delete, truncate, references, trigger on <view> from authenticated, anon`, and say so in its header; or change the default privileges for views only, which Postgres cannot do (one setting covers tables and views), so the lint is the realistic route. Low priority: a non-updatable view's grants do nothing, and a writable `security_invoker` one is bounded by its table's policies. → *Security*

## Deployment (before go-live)

- [x] **The Pi and the Worker are separate builds with no shared `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, so a form rendered by one and submitted to the other probably fails once.** Noticed 2026-10-02 while building the write fallback (`claude/origin-write-fallback`, docs/decisions/2026-10-02-origin-write-fallback.md): since that change a write falls through to the Worker when the tunnel answers 530, so a page the Pi rendered just before it dropped is submitted to the Worker's build — and the reverse when the Pi comes back. Next's self-hosting guide (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/self-hosting.md`, "Server Functions encryption key") says instances without a shared key answer "Failed to find Server Action". **Reasoned from that guide, not reproduced** — so step one is to see it: build twice locally, render a form with one build, post it to the other. If it is real, the fix is one secret set identically in `deploy.mjs`'s build and `scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh`'s. The cost of leaving it: a reload fixes it for the user, and anyone who loads `/login` after the Pi is down is unaffected, which is why this is not part of that PR.
- [x] **Serve `test.lannacare.org` from the Pi too — it is the only environment still on the CPU-limited path, and it has now killed two role walkthroughs.** **Done 2026-10-02** with `0.14.0` (#293 set `ORIGIN_HOST: "test-pi.lannacare.org"`; the box side was completed the same day). Test now answers `x-lanna-served-by: pi` for **GET and POST**, and `/api/version` reports `0.14.0` with its sha, so the 1102s are gone and a walkthrough can be scheduled again. **The work was half-built for about an hour and test writes returned 503 in between** — the Worker was deployed with `ORIGIN_HOST` set while `test-pi.lannacare.org` had no DNS record and the Pi had no test service; reads fell back to the Worker, writes did not. The pieces, in the order `docs/pi-hosting.md` sets out: the test clone at `~/Animal_Shelter_App_test` and `cloudflared tunnel route dns … test-pi.lannacare.org` (Claude), `setup-test.sh` and the WAF rule (Lutan). **One real exposure was found and closed on the way:** with DNS live but the WAF rule still naming only `pi.lannacare.org`, `https://test-pi.lannacare.org/` answered **200 to the public internet** — the origin directly reachable, bypassing the Worker and its `x-origin-key` check. Production was checked at the same time and was correctly `403`. Extending the existing rule to `http.host in {"pi.lannacare.org" "test-pi.lannacare.org"}` closed it, verified four ways including that both Workers still reach their origins. **Lesson recorded in `docs/releases/2026-10-02.md`:** after any deploy where `ORIGIN_HOST` is set, check a POST, not a page — a GET and a version endpoint both looked healthy throughout. **Note the consequence for resilience**, now the first item under Next up: one Pi serves production and test, so a single box going down takes writes out on both at once.

- [ ] **The spare-clone timer was never installed on the Pi, so the USB-SSD spare does not exist.** Found 2026-10-02 while checking something else: `systemctl is-enabled spare-clone.timer` returns **`not-found`**, `is-active` is `inactive`, and `journalctl -u spare-clone.service` has **no entries at all**. #297 (`claude/pi-usb-ssd-spare`) merged the mechanism — `scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh`, `spare-clone.service`, `spare-clone.timer` — but the one-time `sudo scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh init /dev/disk/by-id/usb-…` that partitions the spare, takes the first clone and installs the timer has not been run. So the feature is in the repository and absent from the machine, which is the worst of both: it reads as done in the backlog and in `docs/pi-hosting.md`, and there is no spare. **Why it matters more since 2026-10-02:** the Pi now serves production *and* test, so it is the single point of failure for every write on both (see that item), and a bootable spare is the cheapest part of the answer — the box went off the network for about an hour today and the recovery was "Lutan restarted it", with no fallback had the SD or the USB SSD been the fault rather than the power. **Two things to do, in order:** run the `init` on the Pi against the spare's `by-id` path (not `/dev/sdX`, which moves between boots), then **rehearse the swap** — boot from the spare and confirm the app comes up, because an untested spare is a guess. **And fix the exec bit first** (see that item): `spare-clone.sh` is committed `100644` while `spare-clone.service` has `ExecStart=…/spare-clone.sh sync`, so systemd would refuse to run it even once the timer is installed. Installing the timer without that fix produces a timer that fires and fails silently, which is worse than no timer. **CHECKED 2026-10-07 (staleness sweep) — half of this is done, and the half left is a person at the Pi.** The exec-bit fix this item says to do first **landed the same day it was filed**: `38b4bac4` (2026-10-02, *Make setup-test.sh and spare-clone.sh executable*), and `scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh` is `100755` on `main`. So installing the timer will no longer give a timer that fires and fails. **Still open:** the one-time `spare-clone.sh init /dev/disk/by-id/usb-…` on the Pi and the boot-from-the-spare rehearsal — both need a drive in hand (the first was a counterfeit; see *A USB SSD on the Pi*, which is the same work and should be read with this one). Not checked: whether `init` has since been run on the Pi (`systemctl is-enabled spare-clone.timer`); nothing in git records it.



- [ ] **Let Claude run production deploys with fewer hand-offs: SSH from the laptop to the Pi, and a permission decision for the Worker — a guided session.** Lutan, 2026-10-02; facts from the release manager session the same day, which has run every release from 0.9.0 to 0.13.0. **Where each step of a production deploy stands:** (a) **Migrations** (`node scripts/apply-migrations.mjs --env production`) already run from a Claude session with no hand-off, using `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` from the gitignored `.env.deploy.production`. Leave them alone. (b) **The Worker** (`node scripts/deploy.mjs --env production >> deploy.log 2>&1`) is **not** blocked by credentials: wrangler is logged in through `npx wrangler login` (its own stored auth, `~/.wrangler/config/default.toml`), and the command would work. It is refused by Claude Code's auto-mode safety check (reason "Production Deploy"), consistently. **A token changes nothing here**; if Lutan wants Claude to run it, that is a Claude Code permission rule (an allow rule for that exact command in `.claude/settings.json`, via the `update-config` skill), and it is Lutan's decision, made in his own session, never at another session's request. Until then Lutan runs that one command himself. (c) **The Pi**, which is what users are served from (the Worker is the fallback, and both must be deployed or `/releases` shows the old version while the release mail announces the new one): `./scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh` must **run** on the Pi but can be **started** from the laptop over SSH, which is not set up. Tested 2026-10-02: `lanna-pi` / `lanna-pi.local` answer and are in `known_hosts`, but authentication fails because **there is no private key on this laptop** (`~/.ssh` holds only `known_hosts`) and no ssh-agent running. `pi.lannacare.org` cannot be used for SSH: it is the cloudflared HTTP ingress, so SSH has to go to the LAN name. **This is the part the session fixes:** Lutan runs `ssh-keygen -t ed25519` and then `ssh-copy-id lutan@lanna-pi.local` (it asks for his Pi password, so it is his to run), add a `Host pi` entry in `~/.ssh/config`, and decide whether to restrict that key on the Pi to the deploy script only (`command=` in `authorized_keys`). Then a laptop-side wrapper, e.g. `node scripts/deploy-pi-remote.mjs --ref <sha>`, runs the deploy over SSH and appends its output to `deploy.log`, so a Pi deploy is one command from any session. **A Cloudflare API token is optional, not the fix:** nothing in the repo uses `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` today, and swapping the OAuth login for a scoped, long-lived token kept in a file is a different risk, not simply an upgrade; ask Lutan whether he wants it (its only gain is not having to repeat `wrangler login` when that lapses). **Things to write down while there:** on this laptop PowerShell's execution policy blocks plain `npm`, so every command must be `node scripts/…` or `npm.cmd`; keep the `>> deploy.log 2>&1` redirect on deploy commands (release records depend on it) and warn that the terminal looks empty while it runs; and the 90-day token set on 2026-09-22 is still unlocated, so if auth fails around 2026-12-21 check that first. Record each credential's name, location, scope and expiry (never its value) in a `docs/decisions/` file and the README deploy section. **Done when:** from a fresh Claude session, a Pi deploy of a named SHA runs over SSH without a password prompt and lands in `deploy.log`, and Lutan has decided, in writing, whether Claude may run the Worker deploy. **CHECKED 2026-10-07 (staleness sweep) — the SSH half is in daily use; not ticked because the written decision on the Worker is not in a decision file.** Release records show Claude running the Pi build over SSH from 2026-10-02 (`docs/releases/2026-10-02.md`, *"Pi build over SSH"*), and the ticked *Confirm the new Pi allow rule* item (`852b80c3`, 2026-10-07) records the 0.20.1 release running it under `Bash(ssh lutan@lanna-pi.local *)` past `[Production Deploy]`. The Worker production deploy stays Lutan's by choice (same item: *"deliberately Lutan's own job"*), but this item's done-when asks for that **in writing** with each credential's name, location, scope and expiry in `docs/decisions/` and the README — no such file exists. That record is the whole of what remains.
- [x] **Write the missing release record for `0.8.0`.** Done 2026-09-28 by the release manager, commit `84a8a31`: `docs/releases/2026-09-27.md` gains a `0.8.0` section, appended to the deploy date beside `0.7.0` as a day log. **Less was lost than feared** — the `>>` redirect added after `0.6.1` meant the whole sequence survived in `deploy.log`, so the drift result, the three applies, `strip-baked-env`, the Worker version and the mail counts are quoted rather than inferred. It also settled what the cut plan could not: drift reported 104 files / 101 applied, so `0096`-`0101` were already on production, every dependency was met, and `0102`-`0104` applied cleanly first. **What is genuinely unrecoverable:** nobody ran `check-public-views.mjs` or `check-app-access-gate.mjs` against production, and this was the release that redefined two public views; the record lists them as not run rather than ticking them because the deploy succeeded. **How it got past the release step:** there is no release step to get past. `deploy.mjs` guards the cut and CI guards test plans on PRs; neither knows about the record, because the record is written *after* the deploy *from* its output. It has no trigger of its own, so it happens only when whoever deploys hands the output to whoever writes records - which held for `0.5.0`, `0.6.1` and `0.7.0` because the session was in the conversation at the time, and did not here. It is the only release artifact with no automated trigger and the only one that cannot be reconstructed later. Two options are in the record, neither built: a nudge at the end of `deploy.mjs` naming the record now owed (the better trade - it fires while the output is still on screen), or a script comparing `latestRelease.version` against `docs/releases/`. Passed to Lutan by the `release-notes-tidy` stream; his call. **Not covered by this tick:** the release that is due (`unreleased` holds five lines) is still outstanding. Not urgent, we are not ready to cut over, but it gates go-live, and the Pi origin item below is worth doing *after* it so the tunnel hostnames are created once, on the right zone. Order of work:
  1. **Host the zone on Cloudflare.** It was not registered there, so: add the site in the Cloudflare dashboard on the same account that holds `lannacare.org`, take the two assigned nameservers, set them in sav.com's domain manager, and wait for the zone to go Active (minutes to a couple of days, depending on the old TTLs). Nothing else can start until it is. Recreate the DNS that matters — the `google-site-verification` TXT record if Search Console is re-verified for the new domain (it is needed for the consent screen's brand verification), and Email Routing if the shelter wants `…@lannacareforanimals.org`.

Agreed 2026-09-23 and the right way round: the current production project (`dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`) already holds the real AppSheet migration — realistic volumes and real edge cases, which is exactly what UAT sign-off needs — while the live database should start clean and be loaded at cutover from a fresh export, with the 2026-09-22 import as the rehearsal. New project → `node scripts/apply-migrations.mjs --env production` applies every migration from zero (no baseline needed; dev was baselined at 0027 only because it predated the runner) → Google provider, redirect URLs and SMTP → seed Lutan as admin → re-run `import-appsheet.mjs` against it at cutover. Then `.env.deploy.production` takes the new project's values and the old ones move to `.env.deploy.uat`.

Recommended: put **production alone in its own organisation on the Pro plan**, which also buys the live system daily backups and PITR — worth having on its own merits, since `scripts/backup.mjs` exists precisely because the free tier has none, and it makes a UAT mistake unable to touch production's quota or settings. The free alternative is a second organisation for production; free projects pause after a week idle, which the live site never would be, but it leaves prod with no managed backups.

No application code changes for the hostname itself: `src/lib/site-origin.ts` derives the origin from the request, so copied tag and QR links (`src/lib/tags/origin.ts`, `CopyTagLink`) follow the domain on their own — but the tags and cards already printed carry `lannacare.org`, so either reprint them at the end of the parallel run or leave `lannacare.org` redirecting the tag paths to production.

Until that is done, UAT and production would share every photo and archive PDF by file ID, and a tester deleting one would delete the customer's copy.
  7. **Treat UAT as holding real personal data**, because it will: adopter and carer names and contact details, not just animals. Same RLS, same role gating, same care with who gets a login as production — a lower environment is not a reason to be looser. Decide too how UAT stays realistic over time: restoring a recent production backup into it before each sign-off round is the obvious answer, and it doubles as the restore rehearsal the item below asks for.
  8. **Then the docs:** README "Environments" and "Deploying to Cloudflare", `docs/pi-hosting.md`, `docs/data-migration.md`, the usage comments at the top of `scripts/deploy.mjs`, and a `docs/decisions.md` entry for why the old production database became UAT rather than the other way round.
- [ ] **Send all auth mail from one sender domain — Resend's free tier holds one, and seven shelters do not fit.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28, and it lands at **shelter #2, not shelter #7**. Resend was chosen on 2026-09-23 on the explicit constraint that it "must cost nothing", and the free tier holds **one verified domain**. The cutover item above already notes the near version of this: production on `lannacareforanimals.org` needs a second free account or the paid plan. Seven shelters on seven domains makes that seven sender domains for Supabase Auth's password-reset and invite mail. **The free fix is to stop tying the sender to the shelter's domain**: send every shelter's auth mail from one shared sender — `noreply@lannacareforanimals.org` or similar — whatever domain the shelter's own site is on. One Resend domain, seven shelters, no cost, and it is arguably better anyway because there is one DNS setup to keep working rather than seven. The alternative, a free Resend account per shelter, leans on the free tier in a way not worth building on and multiplies the ways mail can quietly stop. **Decide it at the cutover**, because that is when the production sender domain gets configured and `docs/email-sending.md` §2 gets rewritten — doing it then costs nothing, doing it after shelter #2 means re-verifying DNS. Check what the mail actually says while there: a reset mail from `lannacareforanimals.org` arriving for a user of a different shelter's site needs wording that does not look like phishing. Also confirm whether Supabase Auth allows a per-project sender only (it does) and therefore whether a pooled database forces one sender regardless — if so this is not a choice, it is a consequence, and the wording work is the whole item.

Once, into a scratch Supabase project — the org's free tier allows two, so this waits for a moment when the dev project can be recreated from a restore, or for a third project elsewhere: `pg_restore` the `public` schema, then `auth` data-only, then check `apply-migrations.mjs --status` and sign in. Correct the README recipe with whatever it took.
- [ ] **A USB SSD on the Pi as a bootable spare and a second backup copy.** **Status 2026-10-02 (PR #297 merged): script, timer and runbook are in (`scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh`, `docs/pi-hosting.md` "Spare boot drive"); NOT done until a clone has been booted from. The first drive tried was a counterfeit (2 TB label, 40-50 kB/s, USB 2, `048d:1234`) and was rejected. Needs a branded 500 GB-1 TB SSD, `f3probe`, then `spare-clone.sh init` and the boot test. Encryption decision is proposed (unencrypted clone, drive kept secure), awaiting Lutan: `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-spare-boot-drive-not-encrypted.md`.**  Lutan, 2026-10-02, who has a spare external USB SSD. **Worth doing, for fast recovery rather than for the code** (the code is already on GitHub and the laptop). **Main use, a bootable clone of the Pi's NVMe:** OS, packages, `/etc` and the things that are *not* in git (the two env files, the `cloudflared` tunnel credentials and config, the `lanna-care` systemd unit, Glances/monitoring config). Use `rpi-clone` again (`docs/pi-hosting.md` already uses it to copy SD → NVMe): `sudo rpi-clone sda` onto the USB drive, then on a timer (weekly, and before any OS upgrade), so the clone stays recent. The Pi's boot order `0xf146` tries NVMe first and USB next, so **verify** that with the NVMe removed or disabled the Pi boots from the USB clone and serves the site; that test is the point of the item. **Second use:** a copy of the weekly database dumps, which today sit on the NVMe beside everything else (plus Drive), e.g. `backup.mjs`'s planned `--local-copy` pointed at the USB mount. **Limits to write down:** same box, same house, so theft, fire, flood or a power surge take both drives; Drive and GitHub stay the off-site copies, this is the quick way back from a dead NVMe. Because the clone holds secrets and a USB drive is easy to walk off with, encrypt it (LUKS on the backup partition; a bootable clone needs either an unencrypted boot partition with the secrets on an encrypted one, or accept the risk and keep the drive physically secure; decide with Lutan). Check the Pi 5's power supply can drive the USB SSD alongside the NVMe HAT (the official 27 W supply), and that the drive shows up as the same device after a reboot (mount by UUID). Deliverable: the timer and script in `scripts/pi/`, a "Spare boot drive" section in `docs/pi-hosting.md` with the recovery steps, and a monitoring tile (clone age) in the Home Assistant item. **An SD card is an accepted interim spare, decided 2026-10-02, and it does only the bootable half.** Lutan has spare cards, and this closes the recovery gap without waiting for a purchase — which matters while the Pi is the single point of failure for every write on both environments and the last recovery was "Lutan restarted it". It genuinely works on this box: the Pi was first flashed to SD and `rpi-clone`d to the NVMe (`docs/pi-hosting.md:152-158`), and `BOOT_ORDER=0xf146` already reads 6 NVMe, 4 USB, 1 SD, so SD needs no eeprom change. **Three caveats.** (1) **Use a USB card reader, not the built-in slot.** `spare-clone.sh:102` tests `lsblk -dno TRAN` for `usb`, so `mmcblk0` is refused outright; a card in a reader reports `usb`, passes unchanged, and is tried earlier in the boot order too. No code change needed, and the check should stay as it is. (2) **Size.** `init` writes 512 MB boot plus `ROOT_SIZE`, default **64G**, so a 16-32 GB card needs `ROOT_SIZE` lowered to fit the NVMe’s *used* space — read `df -h /` on the Pi first. (3) **Still `f3probe` it.** SD is the most counterfeited storage there is and the drive tried on 2026-10-02 was a fake. Endurance is a moderate worry rather than a blocker, because `spare-clone.timer` runs `rpi-clone -u` incrementally and not a full weekly rewrite — but SD has no SMART and tends to fail silently or go read-only, on the one device you only reach for on the worst day. So **re-run the boot test periodically** and treat the card as interim, not as the answer. A branded SSD is still wanted: for the archive half (the item below) and for speed.
- [ ] **Keep a second copy of the deceased residents' archive on the Pi's USB SSD.** Lutan, 2026-10-02 (the USB SSD is now plugged into the Pi). Today a deceased resident's whole record lives only in Google Drive, under `Residents/Deceased/<Name> (<ID>)/`: the summary PDF, the offline `index.html` and the photos and files beside them (`src/lib/archive/archive-deceased-resident.ts`). This is the record the shelter keeps for good, so a copy that does not depend on Google is worth having. **What "offsite" means here:** the copy is independent of Google (an account lockout, a deleted folder, a Drive outage), but it sits in Lutan's house next to the Pi, so it is not offsite from the Pi; Drive stays the primary and this is the second copy. **Shape:** a pull-only mirror run by a timer on the Pi (nightly is plenty): walk `Residents/Deceased/` through the Drive API with the Pi's existing credentials, download new or changed files (compare Drive file id and `modifiedTime`, so the regenerated PDF and index after a bio or photo edit, `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()`, are picked up), and never write to Drive. The offline `index.html` was built to be opened without the app, so the mirror is browsable straight off the drive. **Deletions:** if a folder disappears from Drive, keep the local copy and log it rather than deleting it (the point is to survive a mistake in Drive); a death withdrawn in error (`undo_deceased_placement()` moves the folder back under `Residents/`) should be noted in the log too, not silently kept as if still deceased. **Sharing the drive with the spare boot clone:** give each its own partition (the clone needs the whole-disk layout `rpi-clone` writes; the archive needs a plain data partition mounted by UUID), or pick one job for this drive; decide alongside that item. **Privacy:** the archive holds medical history and may name carers and adopters, so encrypt the archive partition (LUKS) as the spare-drive item suggests for the clone. **Multi-shelter:** keep one folder per shelter on the drive, so it fits the per-shelter Drive item. **Done when:** the mirror script and timer are in `scripts/pi/`, a restore note in `docs/pi-hosting.md` (how to copy a folder back to Drive), a "last archive sync" tile in the Home Assistant item, and a test: mark a test resident deceased on dev, wait for the sync, edit its bio, sync again, and confirm the new PDF is on the drive. **This half needs the real SSD; the interim SD card does not cover it, decided 2026-10-02.** Capacity is the obvious reason — the clone takes the whole of a small card and leaves nothing over — but the stronger one is that the two jobs want opposite things from the drive. The clone is deliberately **unencrypted** (`docs/decisions/2026-10-02-spare-boot-drive-not-encrypted.md`: a Pi that must come back unattended after a power cut cannot wait for a LUKS passphrase), while this archive **must** be LUKS-encrypted, since it holds medical history and names carers and adopters. The clone is also rewritten weekly, so rot gets caught; an archive written once and then left on flash would quietly stop being a real copy, with no SMART to say so — the wrong medium for "the record the shelter keeps for good". Waiting costs little: Drive remains the primary copy, and the exposure this item addresses is an account lockout or a deleted folder, not a dead NVMe. This also settles the question the item raises above — *"give each its own partition ... or pick one job for this drive"* — as **two devices, one job each.**

- [ ] **Give production its own Drive tree on the shelter's account, and leave UAT on the current one.** Decided 2026-09-23, replacing the earlier "transfer ownership of the existing tree" plan: production gets a **copy** of the tree in the shelter's own Google account (`lannacareforanimals@gmail.com`), and the existing tree stays where it is on `lannaanimalfoundationBWM` for UAT, dev and test. The point is isolation — UAT is where a tester deletes a photo, re-runs an archive, or drops a resident to see what happens, and it must not be able to do any of that to the customer's real files. A copy is what buys that; an ownership *transfer* does not, because the old owner keeps edit access to what is now the customer's data, and removing that access breaks every legacy file ID in the UAT database. It also means the shelter owns its own files, which a developer's personal Drive account holding the only copy of the archive never did. Steps: copy `Residents/`, `Projects/` and the rest of the root into the new account (`files.copy` in a small script, structure and folder *names* preserved — the import matches on them); mint a refresh token for that account (`scripts/google-oauth-setup.mjs`); put the four `GOOGLE_*` values, including the new `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID`, in `.env.deploy.production`, and the current ones in `.env.deploy.uat`; `node scripts/deploy.mjs --env production --secrets`. **No remap script needed** — `scripts/import-appsheet.mjs` resolves attachments by walking the tree under `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID` and *recording* the file IDs it finds, so the cutover import against the copied tree records the copies' IDs by itself. **But rehearse it against the dev database first:** the 2026-09-22 import matched resident folders partly by the AppSheet id in their names, and those folders have since been renamed to R-codes, so the resolution that worked then may not work now. Two smaller things to check: the shelter's free 15 GB has to hold the tree and the archive PDFs it will keep generating, and the `1036…` Drive OAuth client lives in a project under the dev Google account — the new refresh token can keep using it, or it moves alongside.

If it is there, run `scriptsackup-schedule.ps1 -Remove` on the laptop and drop the laptop paragraph from README "Backups". Two schedulers until then is deliberate. A few minutes. **Done 2026-10-04.** The Pi’s cron fired at 03:00 Thailand and uploaded `lannacare-production-2026-10-03T2000Z.dump.age` (the timestamp is UTC; 20:00Z on the 3rd is 03:00 ICT on the 4th). Confirmed three ways: the file in Drive `Backups/`, the Pi’s own local copy in `~/backups/lannacare` at 03:00 (only the Pi keeps local copies, so that is what proves the Pi ran rather than the laptop), and a decrypt with the administrator’s key — `pg_restore --list` reads 1392 entries, 71 tables of data, and **no rows** for `auth.refresh_tokens`, `sessions`, `mfa_*`, `one_time_tokens` or `flow_state`, so DB-1’s exclusions hold in the artefact and not just in the arguments. **Drive holds two files for that morning** because the laptop’s task ran too, which was the deliberate overlap; its scheduled task has now been unregistered and README “Backups” updated. **Found on the way:** the laptop’s `backup.log` still held live Drive links to the unencrypted dumps of 21 and 26 September — the Pi’s log was truncated during the DB-1 rollout but the laptop’s was missed. Log truncated and the Drive trash emptied 2026-10-04. The Pi was re-checked and is clean: 0 links, no plaintext `*.dump`, folders `700`, dumps `600`.
- [ ] **Run the app on its own user on the Pi, separate from the backup.** From the Pi setup (2026-09-30): `lanna-care.service` and the backup cron both run as `lutan`, so a compromised app process could read the dumps and both `.env` files. A dedicated service user for `next start` (owning only what it needs) closes the gap the single-Pi decision records as not done (`docs/decisions/2026-09-30-one-pi-hosting-and-backup.md`). Do it with the security assessment's deployment-surface findings, and after DB-1, which shrinks what a read of the dumps would give.
- [x] **`setup-test.sh` run under sudo leaves the whole test clone root-owned, and every later deploy fails.** **Guard done 2026-10-06 (`claude/setup-test-sudo-guard`): `setup-test.sh`, `setup.sh` and `deploy-pi.sh` refuse as root. The Pi's existing test clone is NOT repaired by code: Lutan still has to run `sudo chown -R lutan:lutan ~/Animal_Shelter_App_test` on the Pi.** Found 2026-10-02 during the `0.15.0` release: `deploy-pi.sh --env test` died with `EACCES` writing `.env.production.local`, then with `EACCES` on `node_modules/.bin` from `npm ci`. `find ~/Animal_Shelter_App_test -user root` counted **57,249** paths; the production clone counted **0**, which is why production deploys have never needed a password. The script is written to run as your own user and uses `sudo` only for its three systemd lines (`tee`, `daemon-reload`, `enable`), so running the whole thing with `sudo` makes root the owner of `node_modules`, `.next`, `next-env.d.ts` and the env file. Recovery needs an interactive `sudo chown -R lutan:lutan ~/Animal_Shelter_App_test` — the husk cannot even be deleted without root, because the directories holding it are root's. Add a guard at the top of `scripts/pi/setup-test.sh` (and `setup.sh`, same shape): refuse when `EUID` is 0, saying it needs sudo only for the systemd steps. Probably worth the same guard in `deploy-pi.sh`. Note the exec bit on `setup-test.sh` was only added by #303 on the same day, so `sudo bash scripts/pi/setup-test.sh` was the natural way to run it before that.
- [x] **Confirm the new Pi allow rule actually overrides the auto-mode classifier — test it off the critical path, not during a release.** Added 2026-10-07. On that day the Production release manager's Pi deploy was refused (`Reason: [Production Deploy]`) and Lutan ran it himself from PowerShell; production is on 0.20.0 @ `d7f5dddc`. **The cause was that no rule had ever authorised it:** nothing in either settings file covered `ssh` or the Pi, so every previous success was the classifier choosing to allow it, which is not a decision that has to come out the same way twice. That is why it looked like a permission had been taken away when none had existed. **Now in place:** `Bash(ssh lutan@lanna-pi.local *)` in **`C:\Users\Leidos\.claude\settings.json`** — the *user* file deliberately, because each release runs from its own worktree and a rule in a checkout's `.claude/settings.local.json` would not apply there. Both files were confirmed valid JSON after the edit (a first attempt put a narrower duplicate in the project file and broke it with a missing comma, which silently stops *all* of that file's rules loading — worth remembering). **What is untested:** whether an allow rule beats a named classifier judgment. Precedent says yes — `Bash(node scripts/load-residents.mjs *)` unblocked the 2026-10-04 production loads — but it has not been proven for `[Production Deploy]`, which is a more cautious category. **So prove it deliberately:** have a session run the Pi **test** build (`ssh lutan@lanna-pi.local 'cd ~/Animal_Shelter_App_test && ./scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh --env test'`), which the same rule covers and which touches nothing live, and record the result here. **If it is still refused**, the rule is not the answer and the next release needs Lutan to run the Pi step by hand again — better to know that now than at release time. **Do not add a rule for `node scripts/deploy.mjs --env production`:** the Worker production deploy is deliberately Lutan's own job (his instruction, 2026-10-02, in `docs/release-procedure.md`) and it mails every admin. **ANSWERED 2026-10-07 by the `0.20.1` release: the rule works.** Not off the critical path as this item asked — the release exercised it first. Both Pi builds ran over `ssh` from the main checkout with no refusal: production `./scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh --ref 50631c85` and test `--env test`, both in the detached `setsid nohup` form. So **an allow rule does beat a named `[Production Deploy]` judgment**, as the `load-residents` precedent suggested. The other two `0.20.0` refusals also did not recur: `gh pr merge` and `apply-migrations.mjs --drift production` both ran, which closed the two gaps `0.20.0`'s record had to leave open. Net effect: Lutan did **exactly one job** on `0.20.1` (the Worker production deploy), which is what `docs/release-procedure.md` has specified since 2026-10-02 and had not been true since. The standing instruction not to add a rule for the Worker production deploy is unchanged. → *Deployment*

## Quick wins

- [x] **Home page impact band: drop the word "About" in front of the backfilled figures, and the estimate note under them.** Lutan, 2026-10-07: the home page shows "About 321 animals rehomed"; remove "About". It is added in `src/app/page.tsx` (around line 273: `stat.approximate && \`${t.home.stats.about} \``) for any figure that includes the backfilled baseline (the estimate of work done before the system existed), from `home.stats.about` ("About" / "ประมาณ") in both dictionaries. Remove the prefix in both languages and the now-unused `about` string; keep the `approximate` flag if anything else reads it. **Remove the note under the band too** (Lutan, 2026-10-07: "the disclaimer after the figures can also go"): the sentence saying the rehomed and village-sterilisation totals include an estimate of earlier work (`home.stats.estimateNote`, both dictionaries). The admin hint on Settings → Website that says the home page explains the figures are estimates must then be reworded so it no longer promises that. Check the band in English and Thai on a phone. No schema. A releases line (visitors see the change).
- [x] **Keep the header and the left-hand menu in place while a page scrolls (desktop).** Lutan, 2026-10-06: on a desktop, scrolling down a page scrolls the left-hand menu away with it, and the header row (LCA logo, sign-in / account) too, so getting to another page means scrolling back up first. Keep both fixed and let only the page content scroll. **This is the fix an earlier item deferred:** "The pinned nav footer group hides the last Admin links…" (Quick wins, closed 2026-09-23) named it as "bound the sidebar to the window and give it its own scrollbar (`md:sticky md:top-14 md:h-[calc(100dvh-3.5rem)] md:overflow-y-auto`), which needs `AppHeader` pinned at a fixed height and made sticky — a visible change on every page, so decide with the user first". Lutan has now decided. **Build:** (1) **The header** (`src/app/AppHeader.tsx`) becomes `sticky top-0` with a fixed height and the surface background, above the content (`z-index`), so it stays at the top while the page moves under it. Mind the safe-area inset on phones with a notch. (2) **The menu** (`src/app/NavLinks.tsx`, laid out in `src/app/layout.tsx`) from `md` up becomes `sticky` just under the header, exactly the window's height minus the header, with **its own scrollbar** when the menu is longer than the window (an admin's menu on a small laptop), and `overscroll-contain` so scrolling the menu never scrolls the page. The pinned footer group (User manual, Change password, Security) then sits at the bottom of that box naturally, which retires the `[@media(min-height:44rem)]:sticky` workaround and its comment. (3) **Phones are unchanged** (the menu is a drawer there), but check the sticky header on a phone: it costs vertical space on a small screen, so decide whether it stays pinned on phones too (useful for the language switch and the menu button) or only from `md` up; try both at 375 px. (4) **What has to keep working:** pages that already pin something themselves (the manual's contents list, `lg:sticky lg:top-6`, which must now sit below the header; the stocktake sheet's and Purchasing's sticky bars; the resident page's tabs); in-page anchors and deep links (`/manual#…`, `/releases#v…`, `?tab=`), which need `scroll-margin-top` equal to the header's height or they land hidden under it; print views (header and menu `print:hidden` as now); and the public website, which has its own header and is out of scope. Check the Dev / UAT colouring and badge still show in the pinned header. No schema. A releases line (everyone on a desktop will notice), and a check on a desktop at 1280×720 and 1920×1080, an admin's longest menu, the manual's deep links, and a phone.
- [x] **Purchasing: a never-counted item whose need is 0 is invisible on the phone.** Found building the fold (2026-10-06): `PurchasingPhone.tsx` lists only items to buy, and its "Not counted yet" box and the desk banner both count `needed > 0`, so a never-counted item whose sum is 0 shows on neither. The desk table now keeps it visible. Decide whether the phone should list it too (the 2IC is phone-only). No schema. [Done 2026-10-06 as `claude/purchasing-phone-never-counted`: the phone box now counts and names every never-counted item, and the desk banner counts them too; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-06-purchasing-phone-never-counted.md`.]

- [x] **Two shell scripts are committed without the executable bit, and one of them is invoked by systemd.** Found 2026-10-02 setting test up on the Pi: `bash: ./scripts/pi/setup-test.sh: Permission denied`. `git ls-files -s '*.sh'` shows `deploy-pi.sh` and `setup.sh` as `100755` but **`setup-test.sh` and `spare-clone.sh` as `100644`**, in the index, so every clone gets them non-executable. `docs/pi-hosting.md:122` tells you to run `./scripts/pi/setup-test.sh`, which cannot work as committed; the workaround is `bash scripts/pi/setup-test.sh`. **`spare-clone.sh` is the one that matters more**, because `scripts/pi/spare-clone.service` has `ExecStart=__REPO__/scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh sync` — systemd invokes the file directly and requires the bit, so the timer would fail with the same error, silently, somewhere nobody is watching. It has not bitten yet only because that timer is not installed (see the spare-clone item). **Fix:** `git update-index --chmod=+x scripts/pi/setup-test.sh scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh` and commit; two mode changes, no content. **Worth pairing with a guard**, since this is the second thing this week to reach `main` because nothing checks `scripts/` — a syntax error in `deploy.mjs` did the same on 2026-10-02 and CI was green on it (see that item). One CI step would catch both classes: `node --check` over `scripts/**/*.mjs`, and a check that any `*.sh` named in a `.service` file's `ExecStart` is mode `100755`. **Done 2026-10-02** (`claude/script-exec-bits-guard`): both files are `100755`, and CI job `script-integrity` (`scripts/check-script-integrity.mjs`) now parses every `scripts/**/*.mjs` and fails on a `.service` `ExecStart` `.sh` that is not `100755` in the index, printing the `git update-index --chmod=+x` fix. It states what it does not cover. Decision: `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-script-integrity-reads-the-index.md`.

- [x] **Replace the Lanna Care for Animals logo with Lutan's better-quality JPEG.** **Done 2026-10-02** (`claude/logo-replace`): the same artwork at 781 × 746 px, 33 KB, used as supplied (small enough already, and near-identical in shape, so every square `object-contain` box takes it unchanged); filename kept; favicon and Open Graph image left as they are. Lutan, 2026-10-02. Today's `public/lca-logo.jpg` is only **124 × 117 px** (8 KB), so it looks soft anywhere it is drawn larger than a thumbnail. It is one file used in 12 places: `AppHeader`, `PublicHeader`, `layout.tsx` (metadata), the sign-in, forgot-password and request-access pages, `LockedLanding` and `OfflineBanner`; `src/lib/public-paths.ts` lists `/lca-logo.jpg` as readable signed out (both lists), so **keep the filename** and nothing else changes. Get the new file from Lutan; resize it for the web (about 512 px on the long side is plenty for a 2× / 3× screen at today's display sizes), keep JPEG unless it has a transparent background (then PNG), compress it (aim well under 100 KB), and check the aspect ratio still suits each place it is drawn (the current one is near square, about 1.06 : 1; a different shape needs the `width`/`height` props adjusted so nothing stretches). While there, decide whether the favicon (`public/favicon.ico`) and the Open Graph image should come from the same artwork. Check the header, a sign-in page and the locked landing page on desktop and phone, light and dark, and that a hard refresh shows the new logo (browsers may hold the old one until their cache expires; a `?v=` query on the `src` is an easy fix if it lingers). A releases line (people will notice the sharper logo). No schema.
- [x] **26 check scripts replay a migration file to build their fixture; any that read a view or function a later migration changed carry the trap that broke `check-shelter-today.mjs`.** Found 2026-10-02 on `claude/check-shelter-today-fix`. `grep -l "supabase/migrations" scripts/check-*.mjs`. Most test one migration's own new objects, where replay is right; sweep for the ones that touch shared views (`public_*`, `resident_current_state`) and have them assert live instead. Done 2026-10-02: 36 scripts ran against dev, 14 were failing; 11 now assert live, 4 keep the replay with a stale-data fix, 12 were left alone (replayed objects not redefined) and 9 never replayed anything. Rule and per-script verdicts in `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-replay-or-assert-live.md`.
- [x] **`scripts/check-shelter-today.mjs` fails on dev: it replays `0073` and so restores a stale `public_shelter_stats`.** Found 2026-10-02 on `claude/schema-soft-delete`. The harness runs `0073_shelter_today.sql` inside its rollback transaction, which recreates `public_shelter_stats` reading the public `resident_current_state`; since `0108` anon cannot execute `current_vet_resident_ids()` behind that view, so the final anon step fails with `permission denied for function current_vet_resident_ids`. The migration is fine — the harness predates `0108`. Fix: have it assert against the live views instead of replaying `0073`, or replay only the functions it tests.
- [x] **Move the "Public website" link off the left nav and onto the header logo.** Done 2026-09-22: the roundel + wordmark in `AppHeader` is one `<Link href="/">` that opens in a new tab (`target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener"`), titled and labelled `t.nav.publicSite`; the nav entry is gone and the manual's navigation topic says so. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` lists `{ href: "/", label: t.nav.publicSite }` as an ordinary nav entry; the logo roundel + "LCA" wordmark in `src/app/AppHeader.tsx` is plain markup that links nowhere. Make the logo and wordmark one `<Link href="/">` (with `title`/`aria-label` from `t.nav.publicSite` so it is discoverable and screen-reader friendly), drop the nav entry, and check the phone drawer still has nothing referring to it. Consider opening the public site in a new tab (`target="_blank"`) since staff are mid-task when they click it; decide with the user. Update the manual page that mentions the Public website link if there is one (`src/lib/manual/`).
- [x] **Accordion the Management and Admin nav sections, collapsed by default.** Done 2026-09-22: a chevron button (`aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`) beside the parent link toggles the group; the label still navigates to the landing page. Collapsed on load, auto-opens whenever the route is inside the group, and the user's choice is kept in `sessionStorage` (`nav-expanded`); sidebar and drawer share the state. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. In `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` the two parent items with `children` (Management: dashboard, contacts, vets, medications, diets, translations; Admin: website, security, enclosures, zones, immunization/procedure/blood-test types) always render their children expanded, so an admin sees ~15 links before the fold. Make each parent a toggle (chevron, `aria-expanded`) that starts collapsed on app load, auto-expands when the current route is inside it (so a refresh on `/admin/zones` still shows where you are), and remembers the user's choice for the session (`sessionStorage`, wrapped in try/catch). Clicking the parent label should still navigate to its landing page (`/management`, `/admin`) — put the toggle on the chevron, or expand on first click and navigate on the second; decide with the user. Same behaviour in the phone drawer. Non-admin roles are unaffected (they never see these groups).
- [x] **Remove the Assistant link from the left nav — it lives in the header now.** Raised 2026-09-24 by Lutan. The header button (`AssistantPanel` in `src/app/AppHeader.tsx`, gated by `canUseAssistant`) opens the same conversation as a slide-over on every screen, so the sidebar entry (`src/app/NavLinks.tsx`, its own one-item group between the reference lists and Management/Settings) is a duplicate. Drop that group, the `t.nav.assistant` string if nothing else uses it, and `NAV_ICONS.assistant` likewise. **Decide with the user first:** the nav entry is the only link to the full-page `/assistant` — once it goes, that page can be reached only by typing the URL. Either keep the page as a deep link (and say so in the nav comment), add a "full page" link from the slide-over, or retire the route. Update the user manual topic that tells people to find the assistant in the left nav, and add a line to `unreleased` in `src/lib/releases.ts` — users will notice the entry disappear.
- [x] **A refused page and a booked vet visit both dump you onto the public website.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. `/` is the public home page, so every `redirect("/")` in the app throws a signed-in user out of the app entirely — no explanation, no way back except the menu they no longer have. Found when a vet tapped a stocktake recurring job. Four sites, and the last is the worst: `src/app/stocktake/page.tsx:37` and `src/app/deliveries/page.tsx:56` (role refusal), `src/lib/auth/require-management.ts` (every `/management/*` refusal), and **`src/app/vet-visits/new/actions.ts:71`, which is the success path** — *every* role that books a vet visit lands on the public website afterwards, not just vets. **Two different fixes.** The success path should go back to the resident or the visit it just created. The refusals should say "you do not have access to this" on a page inside the app, or fall back to `DEFAULT_SIGNED_IN_PATH` (`/my`) rather than `/` — `signedInLandingPath` in `src/lib/auth/app-access.ts` already knows the difference between an app user and a public viewer, and these guards do not use it. Worth a sweep for other `redirect("/")` calls added since. Small, and it makes every role-gating item below testable without the tester being ejected mid-pass.
- [x] **Show a file-type icon for attachments that are not images.** Done 2026-09-28 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `FileTypeIcon` (lucide `FileText` / `FileImage` / `File`, PDFs in red, an accessible name in both languages) replaces the 📄 glyph in procedures, blood tests, maintenance, project photos and the pending-files list; one `fileKind()` in `src/lib/uploads/file-kind.ts` replaces four copies of the image regex. Original item: Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough., noted while procedures passed. Uploading both an image and a PDF to a procedure worked, and both opened. The PDF just has nothing to look at in the list. A small type icon (PDF, doc, generic) where the thumbnail would be makes a list of mixed attachments readable at a glance. Nice to have, not blocking.

On a contact's page (`/contacts/[id]`), the Google Maps preview under the address overflows the screen width in desktop layout. The iframe is in `src/app/contacts/[id]/ContactHub.tsx` (`h-[200px] w-full`, inside a `min-w-0 flex-1` column beside the address icon) — find which ancestor lets it grow past the viewport (a flex/grid child missing `min-w-0`, or the iframe's intrinsic width), and give it a sensible cap (e.g. `max-w-md`/`max-w-lg`) so it sits under the address rather than spanning the card. Check phone width too, and both themes. [done 2026-10-06 — `claude/quick-wins`: no ancestor was missing `min-w-0` (nothing overflowed at 1440 or 375 px); the iframe simply filled a 1134 px card, so it now has `max-w-lg`]

Fix the line in `docs/release-smoke-test.md`, and check `docs/release-procedure.md` §8 says the same — it currently repeats the old wording too. A one-line docs change, deliberately kept out of the record's own PR so the record was not the vehicle for changing the rule it was subject to. [done 2026-10-06 — `claude/quick-wins`: fixed in `release-smoke-test.md` and `release-procedure.md` §8, and in `role-walkthrough.md`, which carried the same wording]

- [ ] **Rename the menu section "Shelter Operations" to just "Operations".** Lutan, 2026-10-08. The whole app is the shelter's, so "Shelter" adds length and nothing else. **English only:** the label is `nav.shelterOperations` and the landing's `title` in `src/lib/i18n/dictionaries/en.ts` (lines ~86 and ~1771); the Thai (`งานประจำวัน`, "daily work") never said "shelter", so `th.ts` stays as it is. Then the **manual** (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`, about 24 mentions, including the topic title "Shelter Operations: the daily work" and every `path: "Shelter Operations → …"`), and the code comments that name it (`NavLinks.tsx`, `operations/page.tsx`, `operations/medication-list/page.tsx`, `management/medication-list/page.tsx`, `enclosures/map/FacilityMap.tsx`) so a later search finds one name. A one-line `releases.ts` note ("The menu's Shelter Operations is now called Operations"), since people find pages by that word. Manual screenshots will show the old label until the planned full rerun; don't redo them here.

- [ ] **Residents: put the cursor in the Search box when the page opens, not in "Scan a chip".** Lutan, 2026-10-08. Today `src/app/residents/ScanChipBox.tsx` has `autoFocus` (chosen so a USB/Bluetooth reader could fire straight in), and the Search input in `src/app/residents/page.tsx` (`id="q"`, ~line 319) has none. **Move `autoFocus` to the Search input.** Nothing is lost for chip readers: both forms send the same `q`, and the page's exact-chip match (`chipFromSearch(q)`, line ~64) runs on whichever sent it, so a reader fired into Search still jumps to the resident or offers a new one; check that by typing a known 15-digit chip into Search, including with a place or zone filter set (the Search form carries those as hidden fields). Two things to check, not assume: (1) **phones**: autofocus on a phone opens the keyboard over the list; if it does on the Search box, focus only from `md:` up (a small client wrapper checking `matchMedia`) rather than on every device; (2) the Search form is **re-keyed** on every filter change (`key={...}` ~line 302), so `autoFocus` will re-fire after each filter click; that is probably what people want, but confirm it doesn't steal focus from a filter control mid-use. Update ScanChipBox's doc comment (it says "autofocused"), and the manual if it says where the cursor starts (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`, residents list topic).

## Mobile

- [x] **A 375 px overflow check, so a page that scrolls sideways fails before it ships.** Left over from F-06 (2026-10-03). The shared cause is fixed (a base-layer `.grid { grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) }` and `max-width: 100%` on form controls, `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-phone-width.md`), but nothing stops the next flex row or fixed-width box from overflowing again. A guard needs real rendering: `playwright-core` is already a dependency (`scripts/manual-screenshots.mjs`), but it needs a running server, a signed-in session per role and a page list with real data (an enclosure with a long name, a vet with a long clinic), so it is a project, not a one-liner, and was deliberately not half-built. **Shape:** a `scripts/check-phone-width.mjs` run by hand or in the release smoke test (not `npm run lint`) that opens each page at 375 px in English and Thai and asserts `scrollingElement.scrollWidth <= clientWidth`, printing the offending element. → *Mobile* **Done (claude/phone-width-check):** `scripts/check-phone-width.mjs`; six roles (admin, management, staff, vet, volunteer, head of medical), English and Thai, long-named seed rows, in the release smoke test and not the gates. First run found three overflowing pages, filed under *Mobile*. See `docs/decisions/2026-10-05-phone-width-check.md`.
- [x] **Three pages scroll sideways at 375 px, found by the first run of `scripts/check-phone-width.mjs` (2026-10-05).** Evidence for the parked *Mobile responsiveness sweep*; each is one element. (1) **Rehome / foster** (`/residents/{id}/rehome`, admin, management, staff, English and Thai): +244 px. The Carer `<select>` (`select#carerId`, inside `div.flex.flex-col.gap-1`) is 595 px wide because its longest option, "Lutan Bennett · 0624870160 …", sets the width of a flex column; the base-layer `max-width: 100%` does not cap it because the column itself is not shrinking (`min-w-0` on the column, or `w-full` on the select). (2) **`/management/medications` and (3) `/management/diets`** (admin, management): +88 px each, the same link: `a.inline-flex.shrink-0` "Stock between counts: compare stocktakes with the …" in a `flex-wrap` row is 377 px wide and `shrink-0` keeps it from wrapping its own text. Re-run `node scripts/check-phone-width.mjs` after fixing; it should end *No page scrolls sideways.* → *Mobile*
- [x] **Turn word-only action links into icon buttons, so actions are easier to find and tap.** *Done 2026-10-05 in four area PRs: resident hub (#361), Management (#367), `/admin` (#371) and everything else (`icon-buttons-rest`). Navigation links in sentences, lists and tables were left as text links on purpose: the ~27 `text-primary` links still under Management and ~17 under `/admin` are not a gap. Summary and final icon vocabulary: `docs/decisions/2026-10-05-icon-buttons-rest.md`.* Lutan, 2026-10-02: there are a lot of word-based action links, and actions would be easier to navigate with icons and buttons. **What is there today (counted 2026-10-02):** about **95** links styled as plain text (`text-primary` + `hover:underline`) across roughly 45 files under `src/app`, most in Management (16 files), then residents, maintenance, weight, vets, prescriptions and diets. The building blocks for the better version already exist but are barely used: `src/components/ActionLink.tsx` (icon + label, primary/secondary variants) appears in only 3 files, `RecordRowActions.tsx` handles per-row actions, and `src/components/hub-icons.ts` (`NAV_ICONS` and the hub's lucide icons) is the shared icon vocabulary. **Not every text link should change**, so start with an audit, one line per link: **navigation** in a sentence or a list ("see the vet's page", a resident's name in a table) stays a text link; an **action** (Add, Edit, Record, Book, Move, Remove, Download, Publish) becomes a button. Then three patterns, used the same way everywhere: (1) the page's **main action** is a filled button with an icon and its word (`ActionLink` primary, e.g. "+ Record a vet visit"); (2) **secondary actions** are outlined icon + word buttons; (3) **actions repeated on every row** of a table or list become icon buttons (pencil, bin, download and so on) through `RecordRowActions`, each with a tooltip and an `aria-label` that names the row ("Edit Panda's weight on 2 Oct"), because an icon alone means nothing to a screen reader. Destructive actions keep their confirm dialog and a distinct (danger) style. **Rules:** one icon per meaning across the whole app (an icon map in `hub-icons.ts`, extended rather than chosen per page); tap targets at least 44 px on phones; Thai labels are often longer, so check every button in Thai at 375 px for wrapping; keep the word beside the icon wherever there is room, since icon-only is only for repeated row actions; and do not change what any action does, only how it is shown. **Split into PRs by area** (Management, resident hub and its tabs, maintenance, the rest), each with before/after screenshots on desktop and phone, in English and Thai, so Lutan can approve the look on the first area before the rest follow. Read with the Mobile responsiveness sweep (same section) and the public redesign's 44 px touch-target rule. Manual screenshots will change; follow the deferred full re-run rather than per PR. A releases line per area.
  **Status 2026-10-04: area 1 of 4 (resident hub and its tabs) landed on `claude/icon-buttons`.** The audit is in `docs/decisions/2026-10-04-icon-buttons-resident-hub.md`. `hub-icons.ts` gained `ACTION_ICONS` (add, edit, delete, archive, restore, endToday, back, clear); new shared components `RowAction` (icon-only row actions with a row-naming accessible name) and `BackLink`. **Status 2026-10-05: area 2 (Management) landed on `claude/icon-buttons-management`.** Audit in `docs/decisions/2026-10-04-icon-buttons-management.md`. `ACTION_ICONS` gained count, merge, uploadImage, removeImage, moveUp, moveDown, makeStandard, deactivate, activate, pause, resume, publish, unpublish and handBack; `RowAction` gained a `hint` tooltip for why a button is disabled. Remaining: maintenance, `src/app/admin`, projects, vets and contacts pages outside Management, and the shared `TranslationPanel` (`icon-buttons-rest`, batch 50). **Status 2026-10-05: area 3 (`src/app/admin`) landed on `claude/icon-buttons-admin`.** Audit in `docs/decisions/2026-10-05-icon-buttons-admin.md`. `ACTION_ICONS` gained undo, moveLeft, moveRight, refresh, send, approve, deny, link, unlink, issuePassword, resetTwoStep and allowTwoStep; new shared `ActionButton` (icon + word `<button>`, the twin of `ActionLink`). No automated 375 px check had landed, so widths were measured by hand. Do not tick until all four are in.

No code changed; see decisions.md, 2026-09-24.* Found 2026-09-21 while building `/admin/procedure-types`: a server action called from a button (`startTransition(() => updateX(...))`, as every admin/management table's Save / Delete / Merge does) revalidates the path but does *not* refresh the client router on Next 16, so the row keeps showing the old name until the next navigation — only `<form action>` submissions refresh on their own. The fix is `refresh()` from `next/cache` after the `revalidatePath` calls in the action (procedure-types does this; see its `revalidateProcedureTypePages`). Apply the same one-liner to the immunization-types, zones, enclosures, security, contacts, vets and medications actions.

Splitting it into per-screen items and retiring the umbrella was considered and **turned down**; so was retiring it outright. It is not stalled and it is not a planning oversight — it is waiting for a batch Lutan clears for it, and he will say when. Until then, phone defects noticed in passing still get filed individually, as *drop the Resident ID column on phones* was (shipped #233). Go through every screen (residents list, resident hub, forms, admin pages, login) at phone width and fix layout, table overflow, and spacing so it feels professional in portrait. **Start from the admin-on-mobile list** (decisions.md, 2026-09-24, "Admin on mobile"): its field-needed rows (enclosure filters, resident edit, intake) and Security's users-table role column. **Known defect found there:** `/enclosures` scrolls the whole page sideways at 375px (`scrollWidth` 492 on dev data) — the per-zone card grids (`grid gap-3 sm:grid-cols-2 …`) have no column template below `sm`, so the single implicit column grows to the widest card's content (Main Zone section 468px). `grid-cols-1` (`minmax(0,1fr)`) is the likely fix, unverified; the `-mx-6` zone-chip strip also pushes its wrapper to 351px. **CHECKED 2026-10-07 (staleness sweep), not ticked (it is Lutan's to schedule):** the *known defect* above is fixed, by exactly the remedy it guessed. `bd898966` (2026-10-03) put `.grid { grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr); }` in `src/app/globals.css`'s base layer, so a one-column grid no longer grows to its widest card, and `/enclosures` is first in `scripts/check-phone-width.mjs`'s regression set (`docs/decisions/2026-10-05-phone-width-check.md`). So `/enclosures` at 375 px is no longer a reason to schedule this. What the sweep should now start from is the ~80 files with a raw `<button>` nobody has read (*Bare buttons under 44 px*, above) and the proposed `fieldset { min-width: 0 }` rule (`docs/decisions/2026-10-05-phone-width-fixes.md`). **2026-10-07 (`bare-buttons-lightbox`): those files have now been read and ruled** (`docs/decisions/2026-10-07-bare-buttons-lightbox.md`), so the buttons are no longer a starting point for the sweep; layout, tables and spacing remain.

- [x] **A date field cannot be cleared once it has been set, on mobile.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough., found on a diet's end date. Tapping an optional date field commits a value, and there is then no way to get back to empty — so an optional date becomes mandatory the moment it is touched by accident, and the only escape is abandoning the form. Likely affects **every optional date in the app**, not just the diet end date, so fix it in the shared date input rather than one form: a visible Clear control beside a populated optional field, and make sure the empty value actually reaches the server as null. Check the desktop behaviour too — the native picker may allow clearing there, which is exactly how this stays invisible to whoever built it.
