Below `md`, `/residents` still shows two data columns, the R-code and the name (plus the copy-link and edit icons), and the ID pushes long names (with their Thai and other names) into wrapping, so each row is taller than it needs to be. Hide the ID `<th>` and `<td>` below `md` the same way the other columns are (`hidden md:table-cell` in `src/app/residents/ResidentsTable.tsx`, around lines 136 and 181), so the name gets the width; desktop keeps the column. Update the comment above the table ("Phones show only ID and name") and the manual caption in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` (~line 282: "Phones show just the ID and name"; there is no Thai manual file yet). The R-code is still on the resident hub, and search by R-code (the adopted-residents search item) is unaffected. Check at 375px with a long bilingual name, and that tapping a row still opens the resident. A releases line (staff will notice the tidier list). No schema.
- [x] **The Notes box on Rehome / foster is too small to invite typing on a phone.** Lutan, **2026-10-07**, after looking at the form on his phone — the only design complaint from an otherwise clean pass (*"functionally the form works and fits on the screen and is useable"*). It is a `<textarea rows={3}>` with `field-sizing-content`, so it **grows as you type** — but three rows is small enough **before** you start that the box does not look like it wants a sentence, and trying to enlarge it is what surfaced the sideways scroll filed above. **Shape:** start it taller below `md` (more rows, or a `min-h`). **Look at the other Note boxes while there** — the same `textareaClass` is used on adoption updates, Record a death and Undo a death, and the phone-width work already recorded that *"Deliveries cuts its Note box"*, so this is likely a shared default rather than one form. Both dictionaries if any wording changes. → *Mobile*
- [x] **Clicking into a Notes box makes it grow wider than the screen, and the page then scrolls sideways.** Found by Lutan on **2026-10-07**, on his own phone, on Rehome / foster. **Reproduction, his words:** *"when I click in the notes field it resizes and then allows me to scroll left and right"* — so the trigger is **focus**, not a drag, and a refresh clears it. **Where:** every textarea in the app shares `textareaClass` = `inputClass` + **`field-sizing-content`** (`src/app/residents/[id]/rehome/RehomeForm.tsx` and the same constant in the adoption-update, Record a death and Undo a death forms), and that field has a **placeholder** (`notesPlaceholderFoster` / `notesPlaceholderAdopt`). **Likely cause, to confirm with a dev server:** `field-sizing: content` sizes the control to its content, and on focus the box is re-measured — with a long placeholder and nothing forcing it to wrap, the intrinsic width can exceed the column. The base layer already caps form controls at `max-width: 100%` (`src/app/globals.css`, the 2026-10-03 phone-width fix), **so something above it is still not shrinking** — which is the exact shape of the two fixes this form has already had: `min-w-0` on the column for the Carer select (`cbcfc1f2`, +244 px) and `min-width: min-content` on the fieldsets (`da3b9d68`). **Candidate fixes in order of cheapness:** `resize-y` so a drag cannot widen it either; `min-w-0` / `w-full` on whichever ancestor is still sizing to content; or drop `field-sizing-content` on phones and start the box taller instead (see the sibling item). **IMPORTANT — `scripts/check-phone-width.mjs` cannot see this, and that is a real gap.** It measures a freshly-loaded page and **focuses nothing**, so a defect that only appears once someone taps a field is invisible to it. **That is why a person looking found it and the guard did not**, and it is worth asking whether the check should focus each form control in turn — which would have caught this on any of the forms that share `textareaClass`. → *Mobile*
- [x] **Single-line inputs and selects are 14 px (`text-sm`), so iPhone Safari zooms the page in when one is tapped.** Found 2026-10-07 while fixing the Notes box (`notes-box-phone`, `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-notes-box-phone.md`): zoom-on-focus under 16 px is the probable cause of the sideways scroll, and only the textareas were fixed. **Shape:** one base-layer rule in `src/app/globals.css`, `input, select, textarea { font-size: 16px }` below `md`, rather than editing every form; then extend the textarea check in `scripts/check-phone-width.mjs` to inputs and selects. Look at a dense form (Intake) afterwards, since 16 px is wider than 14. → *Mobile*

## Resident operations

- [x] **Download the residents list as a spreadsheet, with more than the screen shows (prescriptions, next vet visit, sex, age, size).** Done 2026-10-06 (`claude/residents-spreadsheet`): `/residents/export`, one CSV through `csv.ts`, the current view or the ticked rows, English headings; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-06-residents-spreadsheet.md`. **Open with Lutan:** whether exporting is its own activity (until he says, it follows `resident.record`), and the final column list. Lutan, 2026-10-05. On `/residents`, let a user download the residents they are looking at: **the current view** (the place, zone and status filters, the search and the Show all toggle, exactly as on screen) or, where rows are ticked (`ResidentsTable.tsx` already has row selection for booking vet visits), **only the ticked ones**. **Columns, more than the table shows:** R-code, name, Thai name and other names, species, breed, **sex**, **age** (the same `formatAge()` wording as the hub, plus the estimated birth year so it sorts), **size**, colour, microchipped yes/no, zone and enclosure (or Hospital / Fostered / Adopted), status, intake date, ready for adoption, **current prescription count** (prescriptions running today, by the shelter's date, `todayIso()`), **next upcoming vet appointment date** (and the clinic), current diet, latest weight and its date. Agree the final list with Lutan; keep each column a plain value, one row per resident, so it filters and sorts in Excel or Google Sheets. **Format:** CSV through the existing writer in `src/lib/csv.ts` (Cashflow's), which already adds the UTF-8 BOM so Thai names open correctly in Excel, and already guards against spreadsheet formula injection (`csvField`, CODE-4). XLSX only if CSV turns out to be a problem for the Director. File name with the date and the view, e.g. `residents-on-site-2026-10-05.csv`. **Build it on the server** (a route or server action that re-runs the page's query with the same filters, plus the extra columns in one or two batched queries, never one query per resident), not by scraping the rendered table, so the file matches the screen's filters but not its column limits. **Permissions:** the download follows the person's permissions: a role that can read only who and where (the volunteer, `resident_who_and_where`) gets only those columns; prescription, vet and weight columns appear only for a role that can read them (the catalogue's medical activities); and decide with Lutan whether exporting is its own activity in the matrix (it moves a list of animals off the system, which is a different act from reading it) or follows `resident.record`. Deceased residents appear only when the view includes them. **Phones:** most users are phone-only, so the button must work on a phone (the file goes to Downloads / Files) and should say where it went. Manual (Residents topic), both dictionaries for the column headings (the header row in the reader's language, or English always; decide), a releases line, and a test plan with a filtered view, ticked rows, a Thai name, a resident with no prescriptions or visits, and a volunteer's export.

An adopted animal's hub gets an **Adoption updates** section: date, who sent it (the adopter contact from the Adopt placement's `carer_id`, preselected), how it came in (LINE, Facebook, email, visit), a note and photos. Photos go through the existing resident photo route into a `Residents/<Name> (<ID>)/Adoption updates/` folder. **Keep each photo tagged with its update** (Lutan, 2026-09-26): the photo's record links to the update, so wherever it appears (the update itself, the resident's Photos tab, a future public "Happy endings" card) it shows where it came from: sent by the adopter, on which date, via which channel (LINE, Facebook, email, visit). Photos taken by staff at the shelter stay untagged, so the two can be told apart and filtered. - [x] **Recurring jobs for staff, feeding My dashboard.** [done 2026-09-26: schema `0095` (#155), then Management → Recurring jobs (`src/app/management/recurring-jobs`) and the Recurring jobs section of My tasks (`src/lib/my-tasks/recurring.ts`), including the sick-week hand-over form; see docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26.] Lutan, 2026-09-25. Let management set up jobs that repeat on a calendar and are assigned to a person (or a team, like maintenance, 0063). Examples: "stocktake of on-hand medication, every Monday morning, staff member X", then "order and buy medication, every Monday after the stocktake is done, staff member Y". **Templates vs occurrences:** a `recurring_jobs` template (title, description, assignee(s), recurrence rule, time of day such as morning/afternoon, optional link to the screen the job is done on, active flag) produces *occurrences* that are transient. Each one shows on the assignee's My dashboard (the item above) for its day and is marked done or skipped, and only that result is kept (who, when, a note), so there is a record that the stocktake happened. Decide whether occurrences are generated ahead (a cron in `worker/`, or on first read of the day) or computed from the rule and stored only when acted on; the second avoids a job table filling with future rows. **Recurrence:** a small, explicit subset rather than full iCal RRULE: every N weeks on chosen weekday(s) (weekly, fortnightly with a start date as the anchor), monthly on day N, monthly on the Nth weekday ("first Monday"), with a start date and an optional end date. Dates are in Asia/Bangkok. **Order between jobs:** "after the stocktake" means an occurrence can depend on another template's occurrence the same day, and shows as "waiting for stocktake" until that one is done. **Missed jobs:** decide whether an undone occurrence stays overdue on the dashboard until done or skipped (suggest yes, with how many days late) or quietly expires. **Admin:** a Management page to create, edit, pause and reassign templates, with a preview of the next few dates, and reassignment when a login is archived. The stocktake example links naturally to the "Stock on hand" item below; the job can deep-link to its count screen, `/stocktake` (`?tab=diets` opens the Diets tab), which staff and volunteers can use (0091). Needs its own schema PR first. **Reassigning one week** (Lutan, 2026-09-26): if someone is sick, management can hand that date's occurrence to someone else without changing the job, and the next week goes back to the usual person; the schema for it is in `0095` (cover team per date, `reassign_recurring_job()`, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26), and the admin page needs the form, including "all of X's jobs for these dates → Y". Manual topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`.
- [x] **Deceased residents: confirm what happens to the archive when photos or the bio change after death.** Customer question 2026-09-22 — check the business logic and close the gaps. *How it works today:* the three edits still allowed on a deceased resident (0052: bio via `/residents/[id]/edit`, photo upload via `src/app/api/residents/[id]/photos/route.ts`, set-profile-photo and delete-photo in `src/app/residents/[id]/photos/actions.ts`) each call `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()` (`src/lib/archive/refresh-deceased-archive.ts`), which re-runs `archiveDeceasedResident()` — the folder move no-ops and the summary PDF and `index.html` in `Residents/Deceased/<Name> (<ID>)/` are replaced in place, so the archive follows the record. *Gaps to decide on:* (1) it is **best effort and silent** — every caller ignores the returned `{ error }`, so if Drive is down the edit saves but the PDF/index stay stale and nobody is told; either surface it (hub banner "Archive out of date — Retry", reusing the existing Retry button) or record a `deceased_archive_stale_at` on `residents` that the hub shows. (2) It only runs when `deceased_archived_at` is set: a resident whose first archive never completed does not get one from an edit — fine, but the hub should say so. (3) A multi-photo upload regenerates the PDF once **per file** (sequential uploads); cheap for a handful, but debounce or run once after the batch if it turns out slow. (4) A HEIC profile photo is never embedded in the PDF (`EMBEDDABLE_IMAGE_TYPES`), so choosing a phone photo as profile after death gives a PDF without a picture — convert on upload or note it in the manual. (5) Verify in dev end to end: mark a test resident deceased, add a photo, change the bio, set a different profile photo, delete a photo — open the PDF and `index.html` in Drive after each step and confirm the change is there; write the outcome into the manual's deceased topic. *Status 2026-10-02 (`claude/deceased-archive-followups`, decisions `2026-10-02-deceased-archive-followups`):* gaps 1, 2, 3 and 5 are closed or consciously declined (walked end to end in dev; a simulated Drive outage shows the warning; persistent flag and debouncing declined). **Still open: gap 4 — upload a real iPhone HEIC after death, choose it as profile, and confirm the PDF has the picture.** **GAP 4 IS CLOSED, and the "still open" note above was already wrong when it was written (corrected 2026-10-07).** `a1452515` (**2026-09-24**, *"Put the profile photo in the summary PDF even when it's HEIC or large"*) made `loadProfilePhoto()` try **Drive's JPEG thumbnail first** and fall back to the original — and Drive renders HEIC, so `EMBEDDABLE_IMAGE_TYPES` only ever gated the *fallback*. The 2026-10-02 status note carried gap 4 as open **eight days after it was fixed**. `f356e90b` (**2026-10-06**) then closed the silent-failure half: `archiveDeceasedResident()` returns `photoMissing`, surfaced on the upload response, the set-profile / delete / move actions (`archiveWarning`) and the edit redirect (`?archive=nophoto`) on the hub banner — see `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-deceased-archive-heic.md`, which also records why convert-on-upload was rejected (no native codec in the Worker, `sharp` cannot run there, and Drive already converts). **So the whole item is done.** `/plan-day` planned a stream on this gap on 2026-10-07; it found nothing to build and was right. **How it slipped: the item was checked by grepping `EMBEDDABLE_IMAGE_TYPES`, which agreed with the item, instead of asking whether a HEIC photo reaches the PDF — which it does, by another path.** `ls docs/decisions/ | grep -i deceased` and `git log -S downloadThumbnail` each settle it in a second. **Checking an item against the code means testing its claim, not finding a line that agrees with it.**
- [ ] **Regenerate the six production deceased-resident summary PDFs once the layout fix is live.** Filed 2026-09-24 from `claude/deceased-pdf`. Every archive PDF generated before that fix has the resident's name printed over the subtitle and no footer (name, ID, generated date, page N / M), and a deploy does not rewrite them — only a bio/photo edit on the deceased resident, or the hub's Retry for an archive that never completed, calls `archiveDeceasedResident()`. Six residents in production have one. Release-time work, after the deploy that carries the fix: regenerate each (re-saving the bio unchanged should do it via `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()` — confirm that a no-op save actually triggers it, or add an admin "Regenerate archive" action if not) and open each PDF in Drive to check.
- [ ] **Foster carer portal (future).** New RBAC role with access scoped to the residents currently fostered with that carer (`resident_current_state.current_carer_id`), allowing them to log weight, immunizations, vet visits and appointments. Needs a `user_id` on `contacts` (or a join table) to link a login to a carer, plus RLS policies keyed off it.
- [x] **Low priority: check the residents list's filter and search logic for adopted residents.** Done 2026-09-30 as a hardening, **not a reproduction**: the disappearance was not reproduced, and (a)–(c) are the code-explained causes; (d) was not checked against live data (see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-residents-list-adopted-search.md`). Search now matches the R-code, an adopted-matches pointer appears when a place, zone or enclosure filter hides them, and there is an Adopted chip. Lutan, 2026-09-26: a search for adopted Panda with Show all on once found nothing, but it could not be reproduced later the same day, so this is an investigation rather than a known bug. The code says adopted residents should appear. `resident_list_view` (0058) has no status filter, and `src/app/residents/page.tsx` hides only Deceased (`NOT_DECEASED`). Adopted is left out only by the On-site / Off-site place filter (`STATUSES_IN_PLACE` in `src/lib/residents/place.ts`: "Adopted and Deceased are history, not a place"). Likely causes to check: (a) the list was on **On-site** or **Off-site**, where adopted animals never show and the Show all toggle is ignored (`showAll` needs Everywhere), yet nothing says so; (b) a **zone chip** was selected, and adopted animals sit in the Lifecycle pseudo-zone; (c) the search was by **R-code**, but search only matches name, Thai name and other names; (d) RLS or data on the site used (the adopted row's `current_status`). **Improvements worth making anyway:** make search reach adopted (and deceased) animals from any place, the way a deceased name match already offers "show" (`deceasedMatchesHref`), e.g. "2 adopted residents match, show"; add R-code to the search; and consider an **Adopted** chip or "Past residents" view so staff can find them on purpose rather than by accident.

- [x] **Move a resident photo to a different folder when it was filed wrongly.** Lutan, 2026-09-27. A photo uploaded under the wrong category — typically recorded as **Shelter** when it is really a **Medical** photo — can today only be deleted and uploaded again, which loses its date taken and caption and is easy to skip. Add a **Move to folder** action in the photo viewer (`src/components/PhotoGallery.tsx`) offering the other `PHOTO_CATEGORIES` (Shelter / Medical / Foster / Adoption). **It matters for privacy, not just tidiness:** `0101` keeps Medical photos off the public site by `attachments.sub_folder`, and the migration's own note says it "cannot catch a medical photo filed in the wrong folder; that is for staff to refile" — this item is that refile. **Two halves, both needed:** update `attachments.sub_folder`, and move the Drive file to `Residents/<Name> (<ID>)/Photos/<Category>/<YYMM>/` (reuse `ensureResidentPhotosFolder` in `src/lib/google/drive.ts` with the photo's own date taken, then change the file's parents), so Drive and the database never disagree. Do the Drive move first and write the row only once it succeeds. **Rules to settle in the build:** moving a photo *into* Medical when it is the resident's profile photo must be refused or clear the profile photo, the same rule as `src/lib/residents/profile-photo.ts`; a role limited to one folder (a vet can only upload to Medical) should only be able to move photos into that folder, not out of it; adoption-update photos (dated folders, no category) are out of scope. Works for deceased residents too, since photos stay editable after death (decisions.md 2026-09-21) — check the archive path. Manual: a line in the photos topic in `en.ts` / `th.ts`; a releases line.
- [x] **Microchip number on a resident, searchable from a chip scanner.** Done 2026-09-30 across 0113, PR 221, 0116 and `claude/microchip-vet-feature` (0117); see the second-half item below and `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-microchip-second-half-vet-form-public-boolean.md`. Not built: a header search (the app has none) and the optional duplicate-scan box on the intake wizard's first step. Lutan, 2026-09-29. Two reasons: overseas adoptions need it (a microchip is the first thing an export permit and the destination country ask for, and `/adopt/international` already lists it), and the app should encourage shelters to chip their animals rather than treat it as optional. **Why now, when it isn't standard practice:** `0060_adoption_recommendation.sql` and `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-21) left the field out because microchipping isn't done as a matter of course in Thailand. That is still true (Lutan, 2026-09-29); the field is wanted anyway, to support overseas adoptions, to make chipping easy for the shelters that do it, and as groundwork for the public register item under Public website. Add a `docs/decisions.md` entry saying so, which extends rather than contradicts the 0060 one. "Microchipping" already exists as a procedure type (0031), so the act of chipping can already be logged, just not the number. **Schema PR first:** `residents.microchip_number text` (nullable), stored as digits only (strip spaces and dashes on save), **unique** across residents where set (a partial unique index, so a mistyped duplicate is caught), with a check that allows the ISO 11784/11785 standard 15 digits and, if the shelter has older chips, 9/10-digit ones too (ask Lutan whether any exist before tightening it). Optional `microchip_implanted_on date`. Locked with the rest of the record on death (the 0026 trigger's column list). **Where it is entered:** Edit resident and the intake wizard (Health step), and offered straight after a "Microchipping" procedure is logged ("Record the chip number?"). Show it on the hub's details card, in the deceased archive PDF and index, and on the vet-visit and procedure views where a vet needs it. **Scanner search:** handheld and USB/Bluetooth microchip readers act as a keyboard: they type the 15 digits and press Enter. So (1) the `/residents` search and the header search match `microchip_number` exactly (after stripping non-digits), alongside name and R-code; an exact chip match jumps straight to that resident's hub, whatever the place/zone filters or deceased toggle say; (2) a small **Scan a chip** box (autofocused input, e.g. on `/residents` and the intake wizard's first step) so a staff member can scan a stray at the gate: a known chip opens the resident, an unknown one offers "New resident with this chip" prefilled. Phones cannot read the common 134.2 kHz (FDX-B) chips over NFC, so do not promise phone scanning; test with a real reader Lutan has, or by typing into the box. **Privacy and public site:** the number stays staff-only. Do not add it to any `public_*` view; the public resident page may show "Microchipped: yes" (a boolean derived from the column), which is the flag 0060's "Desexed / Vaccinated" ticks were meant to have. Vets see it for residents in their scope. **Encouraging chipping:** a count on the management dashboard (residents without a chip), a filter on `/residents` ("No microchip"), and a gentle prompt on the hub for a resident marked ready for adoption with no chip. Keep it a nudge, not a block. Manual (intake, resident hub, a short "Scanning a microchip" topic), both dictionaries, a releases line. Read it beside the multi-shelter spike (Architecture): a chip number is globally unique, so the same animal turning up at two shelters is a real case to design for there. **Schema half done 2026-09-29 (0113, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-microchip-number-on-residents-15-digits-staff-only.md`):** `microchip_number` (exactly 15 digits, unique where set) and `microchip_implanted_on` on `residents`, locked on death, no public view touched; `check-public-views.mjs` now refuses anon both columns. **The check is 15-digit only** (Lutan, 2026-09-29: no 9/10-digit chips), so ignore the "9/10-digit" allowance above. **Entry, display and scanner search done 2026-09-29 (PR 221):** number and implant date on Edit resident and intake, shown on the hub with a no-chip nudge, and the Scan a chip box on `/residents` (exact match jumps to the hub, unknown chip offers a prefilled new resident). **Still to do:** see the follow-on item below (vet entry, procedure prompt, archive PDF, vet views, public boolean, dashboard count, No microchip filter).
- [x] **Microchip, second half: vet entry, procedure prompt, archive, public flag, dashboard nudge.** Done 2026-09-30 (0117, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-microchip-second-half-vet-form-public-boolean.md`): one Record chip / Correct form through `set_resident_microchip()` on the hub, the Vet appointments and Procedures tabs and a visit's Edit page, for admin, staff and vets in scope, with each refusal mapped to its own message; "Record the chip number?" after a Microchipping procedure; the chip in the deceased summary PDF and offline index; "Microchipped" on `/adopt/[id]` from `public_resident_profiles.is_microchipped` (boolean only); an in-care "no microchip" count on the management dashboard; a No microchip filter on `/residents`; the Scanning a microchip manual topic (English only; no Thai manual yet). The optional intake duplicate-scan box was not built. Lutan, 2026-09-29. The rest of the item above after PR 221. **Vet entry (asked for 2026-09-29):** a vet must be able to add or correct a chip from their own interface. Vets can read residents in their clinic's scope (0108) but have no update on `residents`, so this needs a **schema PR first** (`claude/microchip-vet-schema`, next free number after `0114`): a security-definer function, e.g. `set_resident_microchip(resident_id, number, implanted_on)`, that allows staff and a vet whose resident is in `current_vet_resident_ids()`, refuses a deceased resident, strips nothing (the app strips; the 15-digit check and unique index still apply) and does not widen any other column. Then the feature stream: a vet form on the resident hub and vet views, using that function. **Also:** offer "Record the chip number?" straight after a Microchipping procedure is logged; the chip in the deceased archive PDF and index; on the vet-visit and procedure views; **"Microchipped: yes" on `/adopt/[id]`** as a boolean derived from the column (a `public_*` view exposes the boolean only, never the number, and `check-public-views.mjs` must still refuse anon the number); a count on the management dashboard (residents with a chip vs without); a "No microchip" filter on `/residents`; the Scanning a microchip manual topic and Thai text if a Thai manual exists by then. The duplicate-scan box on the intake wizard's first step is optional. **Vet-entry schema done 2026-09-29 (0116, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-vet-microchip-write-via-definer-function.md`):** `set_resident_microchip(p_resident_id uuid, p_number text, p_implanted_on date default null) returns void`, security definer, for admin, staff and a vet whose resident is in `current_vet_resident_ids()`. It refuses a deceased resident (`restrict_violation`) and a vet outside scope (`insufficient_privilege`), and leaves the 0113 check and unique index to refuse malformed or duplicate numbers (`check_violation` / `unique_violation`), so the app must strip first. Null clears the chip, and the date is overwritten with what is passed, so the form should always send both. The vet form and the rest are the feature stream.
- [ ] **Record community and temple dogs helped — the system has no concept of them (design, then schema).** **Raised 2026-10-07 (the Director and Lutan, decision brief q10):** *"will need a backlog item to build functionality for point 3"* — point 3 being **temple and community dogs supported**, the one website figure with **nothing behind it at all**. Animals rehomed can be counted from `placement_history`, and sterilisations may exist as project data, but a dog the shelter helps **without taking in** is invisible to the app: it is not a resident, it has no intake, and it never appears in any list. **So this is a new idea, not a new column, and it needs a design conversation before any schema.** **Questions to settle with the Director first:** what counts as "helped" (fed, treated, sterilised, vaccinated, rehomed from a temple)? Is the unit a **dog** or a **visit**, and does the same dog helped twice count once or twice? Is it recorded per dog, per temple or village, or just as a monthly number? Who records it, and on a phone? Does it need photos? **Deliberately not designed here**, because guessing the unit is how this ends up counting the wrong thing — and once it is on the website as a public figure, changing the definition changes a published claim. **Until it exists**, the figure is baseline-only through the item above. **STATUS 2026-10-07: design half done** — options paper for the Director in `docs/design/community-dogs.md` (copy on Lutan’s Desktop), three options (starting number only / a note per outing / a card per dog), recommending the per-outing note, with eight questions for her (which option; temple vs community one count or two; what counts as helped; same dog twice; who records; photos; starting number and date; sterilisations overlap). **Waiting on her answer**, which becomes a `docs/decisions/` file; the schema PR follows. → *Resident operations*

## Medical records

- [ ] **Vet (clinic) page: every summary tile opens its details — starting with overdue and upcoming appointments.** Lutan, 2026-10-08: on a vet's page (`/vets/<id>`, e.g. Novel on test) the tiles are read-outs only: Novel's says **3 overdue** appointments, but nothing can be tapped to see which, and there is no list of past and upcoming appointments together. **What is there:** `src/app/vets/[id]/VetHub.tsx` draws seven `StatCard`s (Visits, Residents seen, Schedule, Spend, Procedures, Blood tests, Prescriptions), then Doctors, a Residents list and a Visits list for the chosen period. `StatCard` already takes an `href` ("a card with no href is a plain read-out"), so making a tile tappable is cheap; what is missing is somewhere for most of them to go. **Build:** (1) **A Schedule section** on the page: **overdue** first (a scheduled visit whose date has passed, in the danger colour, oldest first) then **upcoming** (soonest first), each row the resident (photo and name, linking to the resident), date and time, doctor, reason, and an *Open* link to the visit (`/vet-visits/<id>/edit`) so an overdue one can be marked done or cancelled straight from there. The Schedule tile links to it (`#schedule`) and says what it counts. (2) **Each other tile links to its list**, filtered: Visits → the Visits list (`#visits`); Residents seen → the Residents list; Procedures, Blood tests, Prescriptions → a list of those for this clinic in the period (a short section each, or the existing pages with a clinic filter if they have one); Spend → the visits that carry a cost. A tile with nothing to show stays a plain read-out. (3) **Past and upcoming together:** the Visits list gains a *Show upcoming too* option, or the Schedule section sits beside it, so the whole picture of a clinic is on one page; the period picker still governs past visits, and upcoming / overdue always look at everything (as the page's own comment says they do). (4) **The same for a doctor login:** the doctor's `/appointments` page already lists their appointments; check its overdue ones are as easy to find. Mind the clinic scope (`0108`): a doctor sees only their own clinics' rows. Phone-first (tiles that are big tap targets, the Schedule list readable at 375 px). Note the parked rename (Medical records: "Clinics and doctors: remove the word Vet"): build against today's names, and that item renames these too. No schema. Manual (the vet page), both dictionaries, a releases line, and a test plan with Novel's three overdue visits on test.
- [ ] **Pick the resident by scanning their name card: use the NFC name cards on the medical jobs (Add medical photos first).** Lutan, 2026-10-05: on `/medical/photos` (Add Medical Photos, the Head of Medical's job) staff may struggle to find the right dog in the picker without help. **The cards (Lutan, 2026-10-05):** every resident has its **own name card**: credit-card-sized plastic with the dog's photo and name on the front and an **NTAG215, 13.56 MHz NFC** chip inside, which every NFC phone can read without an app (iPhone XS or later, Android with NFC on). Each card is encoded with the resident's link, **`/r/<R-code>`** (`src/lib/tags/links.ts`, from 2026-09-22's "Copyable links for enclosure QR codes and resident RFID cards"), which normally opens the resident's page. The card belongs to the resident, not the enclosure, so it stays right when a resident moves. **The ask: take the resident out of the card's URL and use it in this search.** The medical jobs already accept a chosen resident (`/medical/photos?resident=<id>`; check `/medical/weight` and `/medical/diets` the same way), so the whole job is turning a scan into that parameter: (1) **Android, Chrome:** a *Scan card* button in the resident picker uses Web NFC (`NDEFReader`) to read the card's URL record, takes the R-code after `/r/`, looks it up (the same lookup `/r/[code]` does, `loadResidentCard`; an older card may hold a raw UUID, which `/r/` already accepts) and opens the form for that resident, without leaving the page. (2) **iPhone, Safari:** there is **no Web NFC**, and holding the phone to a card always opens its link in a new Safari tab, which the page cannot intercept. So make the link do the work: *Scan card* stores a short-lived "waiting for a card" marker (a cookie naming the job, e.g. medical photos, for about five minutes) and tells the user to hold the phone to the card; when `/r/<code>` opens with that marker present and the user signed in and allowed that job, it **redirects straight to `/medical/photos?resident=<id>`** and clears the marker. Without the marker, a tap behaves as it does today. (3) **Both:** the picker's search box also accepts the **R-code** printed on the card, or a pasted card link, and pulls the code out. **Make it work signed in (Lutan, 2026-10-05: a card normally takes a person to the *public* page):** `/r/<code>` goes on into the app only when the browser the card opened has a signed-in staff session; on an iPhone that browser is always **Safari**, on Android the default browser, so staff signed in elsewhere land on the public card (its *Sign in* link is small). So: make *I work here: sign in* a large button at the top of the public card, returning to the scan afterwards (and keeping the waiting marker); tell staff to sign in once in the browser the cards open and stay signed in (sessions are long-lived, 60-day inactivity timeout); and check a phone kept on site stays signed in there. A home-screen app would not help on iOS, since cards open Safari, not the app. **Also fix where a signed-in tap lands with no job waiting:** today it is the resident hub, which a Head of Medical, Head of Maintenance or volunteer may not be allowed to open (`resident.record`), so a tap can end on the no-access page. Instead show "Panda (R-0042), Enclosure 5" with the photo and big buttons for the jobs this person can do for that resident (*Add medical photos*, *Record weight*, *Diet*, *Open record* only for roles that hold it), drawn from their permissions; with one job, go straight to it. **Safety:** before anything is saved the form shows the resident's photo and name large enough to check against the dog, so a card picked up from the wrong kennel is caught before a photo lands on the wrong record. Never use the card to *write* anything by itself: it only chooses the resident. **Scope:** the medical jobs first (photos, weight, diets), then the same scan in any other per-resident form. Phone-only by definition (no PCs on site). Manual (a "Scanning a name card" topic, both phones), both dictionaries, a releases line, and a test plan with a real card on an iPhone and an Android phone: scan from the form on each, a scan when not signed in, a card for a resident the person may not open, a deceased resident, an unknown code, and a waiting marker that has expired.
- [x] **Head of Medical: record weight and add medical photos from a phone (the next two jobs).** [done 2026-10-04 (`claude/medical-jobs-app`): Record Weight (`/medical/weight`: pick a resident, a big number with the decimal keypad open, today's date, a second reading the same day replaces the first) and Add Medical Photos (`/medical/photos`: camera and gallery buttons, Medical folder only, through a sibling upload route). **The Head of Medical is complete at five jobs** (Administer Medication and its stock-room pick list, Record Weight, Add Medical Photos, Feed Special Diets); four home tiles, the pick list being a tab of the first.] Lutan, 2026-10-04, describing the Medical lead's day while `medical-role` was built. Two more jobs on the same configured role, each an expansion to activities and not a new mechanism (`src/lib/permissions/jobs.ts`). **(1) Record Weight:** `medical.weight` at Edit, and a weight page the role can reach, because a volunteer-based role cannot open a resident's record pages (only who and where, `0134`). Weight is what they check a prescribed amount against; adjusting a dose in the field is judgement and is not recorded (corrections show up as small count discrepancies and fix themselves at the next purchasing run). **(2) Add medical photos against a resident's profile:** an Edit activity for medical photos; `record_attachment()` dropped the volunteer in `0134`, so the function needs the role (or its activity), and `scope_photos = 'medical_only'` is enforced in app code. Both want a phone, and pictures over words (see the last item). **Status, 2026-10-04: the database is ready (`0140`, `claude/schema-medical-jobs`)**: the role holds `medical.weight` Edit and `photos.resident_add`; the weight needs no new policy (the cell is the grant, the picker is `resident_who_and_where`); `record_attachment()` now accepts the activity and the database itself keeps a `medical_only` role to the Medical folder; `medical_photo_residents` and `set_resident_drive_folder()` give the upload route what it reads of a resident. Not ticked: the Record Weight page, the Add Medical Photos page and both jobs and tiles are left (`docs/decisions/2026-10-04-medical-jobs-schema.md`).
- [x] **Head of Medical: feed special diets — a read-only list of the non-standard diets, per resident and enclosure.** [done 2026-10-04 (`claude/medical-jobs-app`): Feed Special Diets (`/medical/diets`): by zone then enclosure, for the meal the person chooses (Morning or Evening; the clock only pre-selects), amount per meal beside a bowl, the meals as the sunrise/moon strip; nothing is recorded as fed. **The Head of Medical is complete at five jobs.**] Lutan, 2026-10-04, adding to the Medical lead's day. The medical team also feeds the special diets, so they need to see which residents are on a non-standard diet, what it is and how much, grouped by zone and enclosure like the medication list, and phone-first with pictures (see the pictures-first item). A fourth job, "Feed Special Diets", on the same role: `medical.diet` Read plus who and where. `resident_diets` already answers `has_permission('medical.diet','read')` (`0135`) but `diet_types` still names roles (`-stock-and-lists`), so as with the medication list the screen reads a fixed-column view of the diet's name and amount, not the table. Nothing is recorded as fed, the same ruling as medication. Answered (Lutan, 2026-10-04): non-standard is any diet whose `diet_types.is_standard` is false (`0087`), so no new column. **Status, 2026-10-04: the database is ready (`0140`, `claude/schema-medical-jobs`)**: the role holds `medical.diet` Read, and the screen reads `special_diet_list`, a fixed-column view of every current non-standard diet with the resident's who and where, the diet's name, unit, amount (the resident's own, else the size default) and its rounds. Not ticked: the list screen, the job and its tile are left (`docs/decisions/2026-10-04-medical-jobs-schema.md`).

The round repeats up to three times a day, and the shelter wants "twice a day" to mean a morning and an evening dose. `frequency` holds a count per day and no clock times (§14 of the roles paper said so), so this is a schema change and a feature: the list would show each round's doses. - [ ] **Prescription schedule: three round ticks instead of a frequency list (design to settle first).** Lutan, 2026-10-04, from the whiteboard form (*Amoxicillin, 2 tablets, Morning / Lunch / Evening ticks, start, end*): perhaps the `frequency` table is not needed. Agreed shape: a prescription's pattern is one of three kinds. **Daily:** the ticks are the truth and `doses_per_day` is their count. **Every N days / weeks / months:** the interval lives on the prescription (a weekly tablet or monthly flea treatment has no honest tick pattern); it still holds a round (0138 defaults it to morning) and `doseDueState()` and `prescription_doses_between()` (0044) already work from interval plus start date. **As needed:** an explicit choice with no ticks, so an empty set is never confused with a mistake (the `none` vs `ok` status views). Edge cases to settle before building: (1) **more than three doses a day** (four- or six-hourly) cannot fit three rounds, so forbid it or add a fourth round (one `insert` into `rounds`); (2) **different amounts per round** (2 in the morning, 1 in the evening) cannot be expressed by one `dose_quantity`, and is likelier to come up than the others; (3) **stock forecast and usage-versus-plan** read `doses_per_day`, so they would read the tick count; (4) **existing prescriptions** all carry `frequency_id` and Management edits the frequency list today, so the migration needs a back-fill and that screen needs a replacement. Dropping `frequency_id` is a schema stream of its own (migration PR first, per CLAUDE.md). Not part of `medical-round-screens`, which reads `prescription_rounds` and `frequency` as separate sources and so is not blocked by this either way. Also decides where the add-prescription ticks live (Management, not the Head of Medical's screens). **ANSWERED by Lutan, 2026-10-04 — edge case (2): yes, a prescription can give different amounts per round.** Two in the morning and one in the evening is a real prescription, and the item already flagged this as *"likelier to come up than the others"*. **So `dose_quantity` moves from the prescription to the round**: the amount belongs on `prescription_rounds`, not on `prescriptions`. That is a schema change on top of `0138`, with a back-fill copying each prescription’s single amount onto every round it holds, and it reshapes the add-prescription form from one amount box to one per ticked round. **It also changes three readers:** the medication list and the pick list sum per round rather than dividing a daily figure, and edge case (3)’s stock forecast reads the per-round amounts summed rather than `doses_per_day` × one quantity. **None of this blocks what is already built** — `medical-round-screens` (#357) reads `prescription_rounds` and `frequency` as separate sources, so it keeps working until the amount moves. **Still to settle: (1)** more than three doses a day (four- or six-hourly) cannot fit three rounds — forbid it, or add a fourth round, which is one `insert` into `rounds`; and **(4)** the back-fill and the replacement for the frequency screen, since dropping `frequency_id` is *"a schema stream of its own"*.

Not done: a backup login for the Medical lead (a Management step; nothing assumes one user).] Lutan, 2026-10-04. The Medical lead's helpers cannot read either language and are supervised throughout by the Medical lead, who today is the only login. The medication list shows resident and label photos but the amount and frequency are text. A design pass for the phone: amount as a picture (a count of tablets or a syringe mark), frequency as sun and moon icons. Also: a backup login for the Medical lead may follow, so the role is not one person.
- [ ] **Clinics and doctors: remove the word "Vet" from the system — DECIDED 2026-10-03, now a rename (schema PR, then the sweep).** **PARKED 2026-10-03 (Lutan): do not start.** Lutan has further clarity from the Director and will raise it as a new item, which may change or replace parts of this one; read that item first and reconcile the two before building anything here. Lutan, 2026-10-03: "We seem to have a disconnect between Clinic, vets and Doctors." **The model, decided by Lutan the same day:** (1) **A Clinic is what the system used to call a Vet.** It is the place a resident is taken to and a visit is booked with. **"Vet" goes from the system entirely** (data, screens, menus, both languages, manual) so the word cannot mean two things. (2) **A Doctor is a person who works at a clinic.** Doctors and clinics are **many-to-many**: one or more doctors, each connected to one or more clinics where they work. (3) **A doctor may or may not have a login.** The login is attached to the doctor record (at most one login per doctor, at most one doctor per login), and most doctors will never have one. **How far today's system already matches:** the shape is mostly right and the names are wrong. `vet_doctors` are people, linked to clinics through `vet_doctor_clinics` (0125); a login is linked to a doctor by `vet_doctors.user_id` and gets its clinics from that doctor (0127); a visit points at a clinic and optionally a doctor, and the composite key keeps the doctor at that clinic. **What is wrong:** (a) the clinic table is called **`vets`**, and its rows carry both a `name` and a `clinic_name` (0001), a leftover from when a row could be a person; (b) the UI says "Vets" in the nav (`/vets`), Management → Vets and the booking form, but "clinics" in the doctor screens and Settings → Security; (c) `vet_doctors.vet_id` is still there, deprecated, as "first-listed clinic" (0125); (d) the **`vet` role** names a login after the old word. **The rename:** `vets` → `clinics` (fold `name` / `clinic_name` into one clinic name plus the contact fields; check every row on dev and production first and agree with Lutan any row where the two differ, rather than guessing which to keep); `vet_doctors` → `doctors`; `vet_doctor_clinics` → `doctor_clinics`; `vet_appointments` → `clinic_visits` or similar (choose with Lutan; it is the staff-facing "vet visit", so the screen label changes too); `vet_id` columns → `clinic_id`; drop `vet_doctors.vet_id`; functions `current_user_vet_ids()`, `current_vet_resident_ids()`, `merge_vet_doctors()`, `schedule_bulk_appointments()` and the vet-scoped `security definer` writers renamed or rewritten, with every RLS policy that names them; views (`vet_contacts` from 0126 and anything else with `vet` in it). **The `vet` role becomes `doctor`** (confirmed by Lutan, 2026-10-03: the vet login is the doctor's login): `alter type app_role rename value 'vet' to 'doctor'` keeps existing policies working, because they store the enum value, not the text, but every literal `'vet'` in `src/`, `worker/` and the check scripts has to change in the same release. Routes: `/vets` → `/clinics`, `/management/vets` → `/management/clinics`, `/vet-visits` → a clinic-visit route, each old URL kept as a redirect so bookmarks and printed links survive. **Order and safety:** it is a large rename across live tables, so do it as **one schema PR, re-runnable, with compatibility views** under the old table names for one release if anything outside the repo reads them (backups, `import-appsheet.mjs`, the assistant's lookups), dry-run on dev first, and land the app rename in the PR straight after, so `main` never carries schema the code does not know. Rewrite `scripts/check-doctor-multi-clinic.mjs`, `check-vet-resident-scope.mjs`, `check-vets-readonly.mjs`, `check-contact-visibility.mjs` and `check-public-views.mjs` with it. **Then the screens:** the nav and Management tile read **Clinics**; a clinic's page lists its doctors; booking asks for a clinic and offers its doctors; Settings → Security links a login to a doctor (as now) under the role name **Doctor**; the assistant's vet intent understands "clinic" and "doctor" (keep "vet" and "หมอ" as words people type, since users will still say them, but nothing the app shows says Vet). Manual (rename the Vets topics, Security, booking a visit), both dictionaries, the role walkthrough's Vet pass becomes the Doctor pass, release notes by role, and a releases line, since every staff member will see the labels change. **Mobile vets, decided by Lutan 2026-10-03:** a vet who works without premises is still running a business, and **the business is the clinic**. So they are entered as a clinic like any other, just with **no address or map link**. Every clinic field except the name is therefore optional, and the clinic page, booking form and any map or directions link must show nothing (no empty map, no broken link) when there is no address. **A visit's doctor stays optional, always** (Lutan, 2026-10-03): a clinic visit requires the clinic and never the doctor, so the rename must not add a not-null or a required field anywhere a doctor is chosen. **Decide while renaming: what a doctor login sees.** Lutan (2026-10-03) expected it to be every resident *that doctor* has cared for, whichever clinic it was at. **That is not what is built.** `current_vet_resident_ids()` (0108, last redefined in 0125, its clinic set from 0127) is **clinic-scoped**: a login sees every resident with a live visit, prescription, procedure or blood test at **any clinic its doctor currently works at**, whoever the doctor on that visit was, and nothing tied to a clinic the doctor has left (an inactive link drops it). It never looks at `doctor_id`. That was the rule Lutan chose on 2026-09-28 ("any clinical record, clinic-level", so a colleague covering a shift sees the same list), before doctors could have logins. Options: (a) keep clinic-scoped; (b) **doctor-scoped**: residents on visits where this doctor is the doctor, at any clinic, past clinics included; (c) both together (the union). (b) alone hides residents seen by a colleague at the same clinic, and a visit with no doctor recorded (optional, always) is then seen by no doctor login at all, so (c) is the likely answer. Also decide whether write rights follow the same rule (0110 limits writes to the login's own clinics). Ask Lutan, record it, and change the function in the rename's schema PR, with `check-vet-resident-scope.mjs` covering each case. **A doctor's own page already exists:** `/appointments` (#227) is the doctor login's landing page, so the rename only relabels it. **Still open, small:** for **staff**, is a list of doctors needed across all clinics (e.g. Management → Doctors, to find one by name and see every clinic they work at), or is each clinic's Doctors list enough? **Read with:** the per-shelter roles and permissions item (Architecture), which turns role names into data anyway, so the `doctor` rename should be done to fit it; and the walkthrough patch item (Documentation). A `docs/decisions/` file records the model in the three sentences above.

Stock on hand shipped in 0.5.0 (0083) as one editable cell per row on Management → Medications and → Diets (`updateMedicationStock` and its diet twin), so a stocktake of 100 medications means 100 separate edits. Add one count sheet, e.g. `/management/stocktake` with Medications and Diets tabs: every item in a list with its unit, the last count and when it was taken, and an input for today's count, plus search and a sort that follows how the cupboard is laid out if the shelter can tell us (name for now). **Save once:** one Save sends every changed row in a single server call that writes them in one transaction (an RPC), so a stocktake is all-or-nothing and every row gets the same counted-at time. Show a summary of what changed before saving (old → new, and the big differences highlighted). **Blank vs unchanged:** in today's cell a blank clears the count back to "not counted" and re-saving the same figure confirms it (0083). On the sheet, a blank input must mean "not counted this time, leave it alone", with a per-row "same as last time" tick to confirm an unchanged figure. Work out what was really used of each medication and diet between two stocktakes, and set it beside what the prescriptions and diets said would be used over the same dates, so big gaps stand out (waste, spillage, doses not given, theft, or a forecast that is simply wrong). **The gap in today's data:** `stock_on_hand` (0083) is one figure per item, overwritten at every count, so the previous count is gone. The formula `actual used = previous count + stock received − new count` needs two things that are not recorded: (1) **count history**, a `stock_counts` row per item per stocktake (item kind medication/diet, item id, counted quantity in the item's unit, `counted_at`, `counted_by`, stocktake id so one session's rows group together), written by the stocktake save call. `0088_record_stocktake.sql` has merged without it, and the stocktake sheet is in review (#143, with schema PR #140 for 0091). (2) **stock received**, because without deliveries every restock looks like negative usage: a `stock_receipts` row (item, quantity, received on, supplier contact optional, cost optional, note), entered from a "Record a delivery" action on Medications / Diets. Receipts that were forgotten will look like overuse, so the first line of any big gap should say "check deliveries were recorded". **Depends on** the Stocktake page (Medical records) and should follow it in the plan.
