- [x] **Units of measure: buy and count in one unit, feed or dose in another, with a conversion per item.** [done 2026-10-01 as `claude/units-of-measure-feature`: Units panel on Management → Medications / Diets, units on Record a delivery and the stocktake with the factor stamped server-side into `entered`, stock shown in the purchase unit, price per purchase unit; see docs/decisions/2026-10-01-units-of-measure-feature.md. The 2-decimal cost column refuses sub-1% precision, e.g. per-gram prices: follow-up on the backlog branch] From the backlog page's suggestion box (2026-09-26), confirmed by Lutan 2026-09-29. Dry kibble is bought in 20 kg bags but given to dogs by the cup; medicines arrive by the box or bottle but are given by the tablet or ml. **Today each item has exactly one unit**, used for everything: `diet_types.unit` (0051: "daily_qty_* and cost_per_unit are in this unit") and `medication.dose_unit` (0027). Stock on hand (0083), stock counts (0093) and deliveries (`stock_receipts`, 0096, which records the unit at receipt and expects "2 boxes of 50" to be converted by hand before saving) all use that same unit. So staff either count kibble in cups or feed in kilograms, and the forecast, stock and cost figures are only as right as that mental arithmetic. **Shape:** keep the item's existing unit as its **base unit** (the one feeding or dosing is recorded in, and what the forecasts already compute), and add a small per-item **conversion table**: other units it is bought, received or counted in, each with how many base units one of it holds (e.g. kibble: base `cup`; `kg` = 10 cups, `bag (20 kg)` = 200 cups; a tablet medicine: `box` = 50 tablets, `strip` = 10). Per item, not global, because a cup of one kibble weighs differently from another. Mark one conversion as the **purchase unit** and one as the **count unit** (can be the same) so forms default sensibly. **Where it is used:** "Record a delivery" takes a quantity in any of the item's units and stores both what was entered (quantity + unit) and the base-unit equivalent. The stocktake sheet (and the phone cards in the item above) counts in the count unit, e.g. "3 bags and 4 kg" if mixed entry is worth it, and saves base units through `record_stocktake()`. Stock on hand and days-of-stock can be shown in the purchase unit ("2.4 bags, about 11 days"). Cost moves to a **price per purchase unit** (what the receipt says: 850 baht a bag), with cost per base unit derived for the diet and medication forecasts and Cashflow. Doses, prescriptions, diets and forecasts stay in the base unit, so nothing there changes. **History must not shift:** existing counts and receipts are in today's single unit, which becomes the base unit, so they need no rewrite. Editing a conversion factor later must not silently re-value past rows, so each row keeps the base-unit amount it was saved with (0096 already snapshots the unit for the same reason). **Schema PR first** (a conversions table keyed on medication or diet type, like `stock_counts`' item-kind pair, plus the entered quantity/unit columns on `stock_receipts` and `stock_counts`); admin and management edit conversions on Management → Medications / Diets. Decide while building: whether a conversion factor can be approximate (a cup of kibble) and whether the page should say so; mixed-unit entry on the stocktake or one unit per line; and whether this settles the related open item "A single-cell stock edit writes no history". Check Stock between counts and Cashflow arithmetic end to end with a real kibble example. Manual (Medications, Diets, Stocktake, Deliveries topics), both dictionaries, a releases line and a `docs/decisions.md` entry for the base-unit rule. **Schema half done 2026-09-30 as `claude/schema-units-of-measure` (0118; see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-units-of-measure-schema.md`) — the feature half builds against this:** table `item_unit_conversions` (`item_kind` + `medication_id` / `diet_type_id` as in `stock_counts`; `unit`, `base_units_per` > 0, `is_purchase_unit`, `is_count_unit` with at most one of each per item, `note`; unique per item by lower-cased unit; admin and management write, admin / management / staff / volunteer read). The base unit is the item's existing `dose_unit` / `unit`, unchanged. **The point-in-time factor** is `entered jsonb` on both `stock_receipts` and `stock_counts`: `[{"quantity","unit","factor"}]`, a copy of the factors in force when the row was saved, so editing a conversion never re-values history; `quantity` / `counted_quantity` stay the base-unit total and a CHECK requires sum(quantity × factor) to equal it. Null `entered` = entered in the base unit (all existing rows). `record_stocktake(jsonb, jsonb)` keeps its signature and now accepts an optional `entered` per element beside `id` and `count` (count in base units). Deliveries are written straight to `stock_receipts`: set `quantity` (base) and `entered` together. No cost column: price per purchase unit ÷ `base_units_per`. Still to do in the feature half: the forms and pages, refusing a conversion named like the base unit, stamping `factor` from the live conversion at save time, and the open decisions above.
- [x] **Stock on hand and days-of-stock.** Neither medications nor diets record what's actually in the cupboard, so the forecasts say what will be used but not when it runs out. If the shelter wants that: a `stock_on_hand` (and maybe `reorder_at`) figure on `medication` and `diet_types`, edited in place on the two management pages, and a "days of stock" column computed from the 30-day rate — with a flag when it's inside the reorder lead time. Split out of the medication requirement page item when that closed (2026-09-21).

- [x] **Doctors belong to a vet: investigate a managed list rather than free text.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, during Pass 0 of the role walkthrough. A clinic has many doctors — Novel has around 20 on staff — and the concern raised was that the relationship is modelled the wrong way round. It is not: `doctor_name` sits on `vet_appointments`, not on `vets`, so one vet already has many doctors. What is missing is anywhere to **manage** them. The column is free text by Lutan's own call on 2026-09-24 (`0074`, `docs/decisions.md`), with the cost written down at the time — "Dr Somchai" and "Somchai" stay two strings, so counting visits per doctor means grouping by hand — and that migration closed with "Promoting it to a lookup is its own item if that is ever wanted". This is that item. Today the only affordance is `loadDoctorNamesByVet` (`src/lib/vets/doctors.ts`), which harvests the distinct names already typed against each vet and offers them as a `datalist` on the vet-visit form. That stops a name being typed three ways, but a doctor nobody has recorded yet is not suggested, a typo becomes a permanent suggestion, and there is no screen where a clinic's roster can be seen or corrected. **To investigate:** whether a `vet_doctors` table (vet_id, name, active) with a nullable `vet_appointments.doctor_id` beside the existing text column earns its keep; how the names already recorded would be reconciled into it; and whether the shelter will actually maintain a 20-name roster per clinic or would rather keep typing with better suggestions. Schema-first as its own PR if it goes ahead, and the free-text column stays for historical rows either way. Note for whoever picks this up: the walkthrough's Pass 0 line "Management → Vets has at least one vet, and at least one has a doctor name" was wrong — the vet record has no doctor field — and has been corrected in `docs/role-walkthrough.md`. **Decided 2026-09-27 (Lutan): build both.** Schema half is #176 (`0102_vet_doctors_and_vet_accounts.sql`, applied to dev): a `vet_doctors` list per clinic that fills itself from typed names by trigger, `doctor_name` kept and following the list, `merge_vet_doctors`, `p_doctor_name` on `schedule_bulk_appointments`, and `user_roles.vet_id` + `current_user_vet_id()` for the vet-account→clinic link items 4 and 8 need. **Feature half still to do:** the list on the clinic hub (view, rename, deactivate, merge), the booking form passing `p_doctor_name` instead of its second write, suggestions from the list instead of `loadDoctorNamesByVet`, choosing a vet account's clinic in `/admin/security`, manual and release-notes line; tick this item then. **Done 2026-09-28** (`claude/vet-doctors-roster-screen`): Management → Vets → Doctors lists each clinic's doctors with their visit counts, and renames (saying how many visits the new spelling reaches), merges (pointing out look-alike spellings such as "Somchai" / "Dr Somchai"), marks a doctor as left, and adds or deletes an unused one; the clinic hub shows the list read-only; booking passes `p_doctor_name` in the one RPC call; the visit forms suggest the clinic's active doctors. The `/admin/security` clinic choice had already shipped with the vet-scope work.

- [x] **A single-cell stock edit writes no history, but the `0093` back-fill treated past ones as counts.** [done 2026-09-29 as `claude/stock-edit-history`, migration `0112`: Lutan chose one way in — `record_stock_correction()` writes a `source = correction` row, kept out of usage intervals; see docs/decisions.md 2026-09-29] Found 2026-09-27 while clearing UAT's stock figures. `record_stocktake()` writes a `stock_counts` row per item (`0093`), and the stocktake sheet goes through it. `updateMedicationStock` and its diet twin (`src/app/management/medications/actions.ts`) do not: they `update` the column directly, so `0083`'s trigger stamps `stock_counted_at` and nothing lands in the history. The result is that **Stock between counts cannot see a figure typed on the Management page** — four such edits on UAT this morning left no trace in `stock_counts` at all. What makes it more than a design choice is that `0093`'s back-fill *did* capture exactly those edits, one synthetic stocktake each, because all it had to go on was `stock_on_hand` and `stock_counted_at`. So a cell edit made before `0093` is history and the same edit made after it is not, which is the kind of split nobody will reconstruct later from the data. **To decide:** either route the single-cell edit through `record_stocktake()` so there is one way in, or leave it out of history on purpose and say so on the page — and either way `0093`'s comment should stop implying the two agree. Worth settling before the first real count, since that is when the history starts being worth something. **The display half is now done too (2026-10-01, `claude/stock-count-source`):** Lutan chose Stock between counts only, so a row there says when a figure was typed by hand during the dates being compared, in the table and in the CSV. No figure changes — a correction is still never paired into an interval. Labelling back-fill rows so they stop passing as real stocktakes was considered and declined for now, and is noted as still open in `docs/decisions/2026-10-01-corrections-on-stock-between-counts.md`.

- [x] **A vet's photo upload should be limited to the Medical folder.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. **Done 2026-09-27** (`claude/medical-photos-profile`): no picker for a vet, and the upload route answers 403 to any other folder. Confirmed a vet can add photos from the resident hub, which is intended (`assertPhotoWriteAccess` allows every app role), but the folder picker offers all of them. A vet has no reason to file into the shelter's general or project folders, and a misfiled clinical photo is awkward to find later. Restrict the choice to **Medical** for the vet role — ideally by not showing the picker at all when there is only one valid destination, so it reads as "this goes in Medical" rather than a one-item dropdown. Enforce it on the server too, not only in the form: the upload route is the thing that has to refuse.
- [x] **Five more places take a UTC day for a shelter day.** Found 2026-09-28 by `claude/utc-visit-date`, grepping `slice(0, 10)` across `src/` after fixing the three visit forms. Each takes the leading date of a stored UTC timestamp as a shelter day, so it is a day early between 00:00 and 07:00 Bangkok. (1) `residents/[id]/ResidentHub.tsx` builds `today` from `now.slice(0, 10)` (`now` is `new Date().toISOString()` from the page), so a prescription that ended yesterday still counts as active on the hub until 07:00. (2)–(4) `hospital/return/ReturnFromHospitalForm.tsx`, `rehome/RehomeForm.tsx` and `rehome/return/ReturnToShelterForm.tsx` set the date input's `min` from `placement_history.start_date.slice(0, 10)`. That column is `timestamptz`, and `placementStartDate()` stores `now` for a same-day move, so a placement made at 03:00 has a `min` one day too early. The server still refuses the date, but with an error rather than a greyed-out day. (5, minor) `maintenance/MaintenanceBoard.tsx` falls back to `updated_at.slice(0, 10)` against a shelter-day cutoff. Fix with `todayIso(new Date(x))`, as `visitDate()` does. Checked and fine: `immunizations/new/actions.ts` (date arithmetic on a date), `lib/management/cashflow.ts` (a `date` month), `lib/status/usage.ts` (Cloudflare analytics days are UTC by design).
- [x] **Vet visit form: scope the clinic picker, and reconsider the Status dropdown.** **Done 2026-09-27** (`claude/vet-visit-form-scoping`, see `docs/decisions.md`): both halves. A vet account books and edits visits for its own clinic only, checked in the actions too; a vet account with no clinic is refused (Lutan). The clinic is set in Security, which gained a Clinic choice for vet accounts. Status stays a dropdown, because a past visit still Scheduled is how the app shows overdue, and cancelled comes from the deceased cascade; its options now say what they mean. Original: Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. Two things on the booking form. **(1)** The Vet dropdown lists every clinic. When a doctor is recording their own visit it should offer the clinic that doctor works in, not the shelter's whole list — which depends on the doctor/clinic hierarchy item, since today nothing links a doctor to a clinic or a vet account to either. **(2)** Status is a dropdown, but it looks derivable from the appointment date — a visit in the future is scheduled, one in the past happened. If it is genuinely automatic, it should be a **label showing the derived state**, not a field asking the user to agree with the calendar; if it is not automatic (a cancelled or missed visit is neither), then the dropdown is right and the options should say so. Settle which before changing it — turning a real field into a label loses the cancelled case.
- [x] **One weight per vet visit: hide visits that already have one.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. Recording a weight offers a linked vet visit, and the list includes visits a weight has already been recorded against, so the same visit can carry two. Filter the picker to visits with no weight yet, and back it with a constraint rather than only the form — a unique index on the linking column is the version that survives a second tab and an import. Decide what happens to any duplicates already recorded before adding the constraint, and whether correcting a weight means editing the existing row (it should) rather than adding a second. **Also one weight per DAY** (Lutan, 2026-09-27): a resident may have only one weight recorded on a given date, not just one per visit. Two independent constraints on `weight` (`0001`, plus `vet_appointment_id` from `0028`): unique on `vet_appointment_id` — **partial**, `where vet_appointment_id is not null`, or every unlinked weight collides — and unique on `(resident_id, date)`. The per-day rule is the stricter one and will have far more existing violations, so count those first. Check the intake path before adding it: `0029_intake_weight.sql` records a weight at intake, so a resident taken in and seen by a vet the same day is a legitimate-looking collision the per-day rule would refuse — decide whether intake wins, the visit wins, or it overwrites. **Done 2026-09-27:** duplicates counted first — none on dev (23 readings) or in the AppSheet snapshot (18); `0106` (#187) adds both indexes, unlinks extra visit links and refuses same-day pairs by name if it ever meets them. Intake: the visit's reading *corrects* the intake reading in place (new kg, linked to the visit) — `/weight/new` says so as soon as a taken day is picked and saves as a correction. Correcting any reading is `/weight/[id]/edit` (Edit on the Weight tab, Edit weight on its visit). Picker: `loadLinkableVisits` in `src/lib/vets/linkable.ts`, which also hides future visits (`notInFuture`) — for the prescription item below.
- [x] **A prescription should not be attachable to a future vet visit.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. The linked-visit dropdown on a prescription lists visits that have not happened yet. A prescription written against a visit that is still scheduled is either a mistake or a plan, and the record cannot tell which afterwards. Limit the picker to visits on or before today. Same question as the weight item about whether the rule belongs in the database as well as the form; a check against the appointment date would do it.
- [x] **Blood test, procedure and hospital forms default a visit's date from its UTC day.** Found 2026-09-28 by `claude/prescription-no-future-visit`. `BloodTestForm.tsx`, `ProcedureForm.tsx` and `residents/[id]/hospital/page.tsx` each take `appointment_date.slice(0, 10)` — the leading date of the stored UTC timestamp — as the visit's day. For a visit between 00:00 and 07:00 Bangkok that is the day before, so a blood test or procedure logged from such a visit defaults to the wrong date, and the hospital page's "visit is not in the future" test is a day early. `visitDate()` in `src/lib/vets/linkable.ts` now returns the shelter day (`todayIso(new Date(...))`), which the weight and prescription forms use; switch these three to it. Small, no schema; check each against a visit at 03:00 Bangkok.
- [x] **A doctor can work at more than one clinic.** Lutan, 2026-10-01. Today a person belongs to exactly one clinic in two places, and both need to allow several. **(1) The doctor roster:** `vet_doctors` (0102) has a single `vet_id`, so a doctor who also works at a second clinic has to be entered twice, as two unrelated rows, and nothing shows they are the same person. Make the doctor the person and add a link table, e.g. `vet_doctor_clinics (doctor_id, vet_id)`, keeping the name-key uniqueness per clinic. **Careful:** `vet_appointments` has a composite foreign key `(vet_id, doctor_id) → vet_doctors (vet_id, id)` that guarantees a visit's doctor works at the visit's clinic; that guarantee must move to the link table, not be dropped. Existing duplicate rows for the same person at two clinics can be merged afterwards from the clinic page (a "same person as…" merge), not guessed by name in the migration. **(2) Vet logins:** a vet account has one clinic, `user_roles.vet_id`, read through `current_user_vet_id()` by 18 places across `0102`, `0108` (which residents a vet sees) and `0110` (which clinic a vet may write for), plus `src/lib/vets/scope.ts`. Several clinics means a set: e.g. `user_vet_clinics (user_id, vet_id)` and a `current_user_vet_ids()` returning an array, with every policy changed from `= current_user_vet_id()` to `= any(current_user_vet_ids())`. A vet then sees the union of the residents their clinics treat; the vet-visit form's clinic, today fixed to their one clinic, becomes a choice among theirs (preselected when there is only one); Settings → Security gets a multi-select for a vet's clinics. Keep `current_user_vet_id()` working until nothing calls it, or replace all callers in the same PR. **Decided by Lutan, 2026-10-01: both,** the roster doctor and the vet login, **and a vet login is linked to its own doctor entry.** So: (3) a nullable, unique `user_id` on the doctor (one login is one doctor, and one doctor has at most one login), set in Settings → Security beside the vet's clinics (pick an existing doctor, or create one from the user's name). A linked vet's clinics should come from their doctor's clinics rather than be kept twice: decide while building whether `user_vet_clinics` is needed at all, or whether a vet's clinic set is simply the clinics of the doctor their login is linked to (simpler, one source of truth; an unlinked vet login then has no clinics and sees nothing, as today's login with no clinic does). When a linked vet records a visit, the Doctor field is themselves, prefilled and locked to their own entry; staff and admins still choose any doctor at the visit's clinic. A doctor removed from a clinic keeps their past visits there. **Most doctors will have no login (Lutan, 2026-10-01):** many of the vets residents are taken to never use the system, so adding a doctor must need only a name and their clinic(s), with **no email, account or invitation**. That stays possible from the clinic page and from the vet-visit form (typing a new name, as today). A login is linked to a doctor afterwards, only if that doctor is ever given one, and unlinking or archiving the login leaves the doctor and their visits untouched. **Schema PR first,** then the feature; test with a vet login on two clinics that it sees both clinics' residents and no others, can record for either, and is refused for a third (`scripts/` check like the 0108 / 0110 harnesses). Manual (Vets, Security), both dictionaries, a releases line and a decision file in `docs/decisions/`. **Schema half done on `claude/schema-doctor-multi-clinic` (migration 0125, 2026-10-02); the feature half remains. Build against this:** `vet_doctors` is now the person (`vet_id` kept, nullable and deprecated = first-listed clinic, so current pages still work; new `user_id` unique, set only by an admin at aal2, by trigger). `vet_doctor_clinics (vet_id, doctor_id, active, name_key)`, PK `(vet_id, doctor_id)`, is the link; "removed from a clinic" is `active = false` (a link a visit uses cannot be deleted); unique `(vet_id, name_key)` is the per-clinic spelling rule. The visit guarantee is the composite FK `vet_appointments (vet_id, doctor_id)` -> `vet_doctor_clinics (vet_id, doctor_id)`; moving a visit to another clinic re-resolves the typed name to that clinic's own doctor. No `user_vet_clinics`: a vet login's clinics = its linked doctor's active clinics, `current_user_vet_ids()` (uuid[]), UNION `user_roles.vet_id` until you move Settings -> Security onto the doctor link, backfill a doctor per login that has only `vet_id`, and drop that column. `current_user_vet_id()` still exists with no SQL callers; `src/lib/vets/scope.ts` still reads `user_roles.vet_id`, so switch it to the set and remove the function. Staff and management cannot edit the links of a doctor who has a login (admin only), and a vet writes links only for their own clinics, so the clinic page needs to handle those refusals. `merge_vet_doctors(from, into)` now merges across clinics (clinics, visits and login move; admin only if a login is involved; two logins refuse): call it for the "same person as..." merge. Not merged by name anywhere. Test with `scripts/check-doctor-multi-clinic.mjs`.

## Facility

- [x] **Put zones and enclosures in the shelter's own order, set in Settings.** Lutan, 2026-10-07: zones and enclosures do not appear in the order people want; let the order be set in Settings and used everywhere. **Why it happens:** everything sorts by **name** (`.order("name")` on `/enclosures`, an enclosure's page, Settings → Zones and Settings → Enclosures, and the zone → enclosure pickers), and alphabetical order is not how anyone walks the site; it also puts "Enclosure 10" before "Enclosure 2". **Build:** (1) **Schema PR first:** `sort_order integer` on `zones` and on `enclosures` (enclosures ordered within their zone), seeded from today's order with numbers sorted naturally (2 before 10) so nothing jumps on the day it lands; re-runnable. The Lifecycle pseudo-zone and its pseudo-enclosures keep their fixed place (the status cards at the top of `/enclosures`), so they take no order and the trigger that refuses them a map shape (`0142`) refuses an order too. (2) **Settings → Zones and Settings → Enclosures** get **Move up / Move down** buttons per row, the same pattern as Management → Shelter Friends' running order (`src/app/management/shelter-friends/FriendsOrder.tsx`, `moveFriend`), which works on a phone, where drag-and-drop is unreliable; the enclosures list is grouped by zone, and a move only reorders within the zone. A *Sort A-Z (numbers in order)* button per list as a starting point. (3) **Use the order everywhere** zones or enclosures are listed: `/enclosures` (its existing *By zone* sort becomes the shelter's order; keep *Name* and *Fullest* as alternatives), zone chips, an enclosure's page, every zone → enclosure picker (`EnclosurePicker`, `loadEnclosureOptions()`), the move, intake, hospital-return and rehome forms, the maintenance board's filters, the residents list's zone filter and sort, the medical jobs' resident lists grouped by zone, the facility map's lists, the zone figures and the Purchasing / special-diets lists where grouped by place. One shared ordering helper or a view column, so no screen goes back to sorting by name. (4) A new zone or enclosure goes **last** in its list. **Who:** whoever holds `facility.enclosures` (the Director today). No change to names or ids, so printed QR codes and name cards are unaffected. Manual (Zones and Enclosures topics), both dictionaries, a releases line, and a test plan: reorder on a phone, check two other screens follow, add a new enclosure, and confirm the Lifecycle cards stay where they are. **STATUS 2026-10-08: schema half done (0161 `zones.sort_order`, `enclosures.sort_order` within the zone, PR from `claude/order-and-units-schema`, see `docs/decisions/2026-10-08-order-and-unit-prices-schema.md`).** Seeded in natural name order, so nothing moves until a screen reads it; a new zone or enclosure already goes last (trigger), and the Lifecycle zone and its pseudo-enclosures refuse an order (0142's trigger), so build step (4) is done in the database. No view change was needed: every list reads the `zones` / `enclosures` tables, which every role but the vet can read; a screen grouping residents by place sorts the names from the views with the order read from the tables. Still to do, from the updated `main`: (2) Move up / Move down and Sort A-Z on Settings → Zones and Settings → Enclosures, (3) the shared ordering helper and every screen in the list above switched from `.order("name")` to it, and the manual, dictionaries, releases line and test plan. **DONE 2026-10-08, screen half (`claude/place-order-settings`):** Move up / Move down and Sort A-Z on Settings → Zones and Settings → Enclosures (grouped by zone; Lifecycle rows have no buttons and the actions refuse them before the database would); one shared helper, `src/lib/enclosures/order.ts`, now orders /enclosures (By zone, chips, map), `loadEnclosureOptions()` (every picker and the maintenance filters), the immunization form, the residents list's filters, the facility-map editor, and the medical pickers, medication list and special-diets list. An enclosure's own page lists one enclosure's residents, and Purchasing does not group by place, so neither needed a change. See `docs/decisions/2026-10-08-place-order-screens.md`.
- [x] **Enclosures page: a summary line under each zone's heading (place, enclosures, residents, free spaces).** Lutan, 2026-10-06: on `/enclosures` on a desktop, show each zone's figures beside its name, e.g. **Front Zone · White · On-site · 12 enclosures · 56 residents · 3 spaces free**. Today the zone heading (`src/app/enclosures/EnclosureGrid.tsx`, the `<h2>` per zone) shows only the name and a link to its zone-wide open maintenance jobs. **The figures:** (1) the zone's **colour** as its dot (from the zone colour item in Facility, so "White" is the dot and its name, not text in the zone name; until that lands, leave the colour out); (2) **On-site / Off-site** (`zones.internal`, the same place words as the filter); (3) **enclosures** in the zone; (4) **residents** in it now (the same headcount the cards use, `loadEnclosureOptions()` / the page's own query, so the heading and the cards can never disagree); (5) **spaces free**: the sum over the zone's enclosures of capacity minus residents, counting only enclosures that have a capacity and never going below zero per enclosure; if some enclosures have no capacity set, say "3 spaces free (2 enclosures have no capacity set)" rather than guess. **An over-capacity enclosure counts as zero** (Lutan, 2026-10-06): it adds no free spaces and takes none away from the others, and the heading says nothing about it. The enclosure's own card already shows red, and the shelter may decide that is fine; it must not reduce the free spaces shown for the rest of the zone, nor stop anyone placing a resident in an enclosure that has room. Add the zone-wide open jobs link to the same line as now. **Filters:** the figures describe what is shown, so with a search or the open-maintenance tick on, say so ("showing 4 of 12 enclosures") or count the whole zone and label it as such; decide which and keep it consistent. The Lifecycle cards (Hospital, Unassigned, Fostered) have no zone heading and no capacity: leave them out. **Phones:** Lutan asked for desktop; on a phone wrap the line under the name or show just residents and spaces free, rather than hide it entirely, since everyone but the Director is on a phone and "where is there room?" is a phone question. Also worth a total line above the zones ("All zones: 87 enclosures, 230 residents, 14 spaces free"). No schema. Both dictionaries (with Thai plurals handled by the dictionary functions, not string concatenation), Manual (Enclosures topic), a releases line, and a check with an over-capacity enclosure (zone total unchanged by it) and one with no capacity.
- [ ] **A colour for each zone, shown as a coloured dot beside its name, so the colour no longer has to be typed into the name.** Lutan, 2026-10-05. Zones are known on site by colour (the Main Zone plan is drawn blue, the House Zone sand, the Cat Zone pink), and today the only place to say so is the zone's name, so names carry text like "(Blue)". **Build:** (1) **Schema PR first:** `zones.colour` (nullable text, a `#rrggbb` hex checked by a constraint; null = no dot), re-runnable. Zones are read by everyone who reads enclosures, so no new grant; check `resident_list_view`, `resident_who_and_where` and any other view that carries zone names, and add the colour where the screens need it. Not on the public site (zones are not shown there). (2) **Settings → Zones** (`src/app/admin/zones`): a colour choice on create and in the table, as a **palette of about ten named swatches** (blue, green, yellow, orange, red, pink, purple, brown, grey, teal) rather than a free colour wheel, so the colours stay distinct from each other and readable in light and dark mode; each swatch has its name in English and Thai for screen readers and for staff who think of the zone as "the blue one". A *No colour* option. (3) **Show it everywhere a zone's name appears:** one small component (a filled dot with the zone name, colour never the only signal, `aria-hidden` dot) used wherever `placeName()` renders a zone today (about 34 files: the residents list and resident page, the enclosures list and zone chips, enclosure pages, the maintenance board, the move and other placement forms, the zone → enclosure pickers, the medical jobs' resident lists, the facility map's zone outlines if they are drawn). Enclosure names can borrow their zone's dot where that helps (the enclosure cards). (4) **Then rename the zones** to drop the colour text, done by Lutan in Settings once the dots show; nothing keys on zone names except the Lifecycle pseudo-zone, which takes no colour (and its trigger should refuse one, the same line `0119` and `0142` draw). **Check:** contrast of each swatch against both themes' backgrounds (the dot needs about 3:1 as a graphic), and that the enclosure capacity colours (`OccupancyIndicator`) cannot be confused with a zone colour on the same card; if they can, use a ring or a different shape for one of them. Manual (the Zones topic), both dictionaries for the swatch names, a releases line (staff will see the dots). **Schema half done 2026-10-08 on `claude/zone-colour-schema` (migration 0162; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-08-zone-colour-schema.md`); the palette on Settings → Zones and the dot remain. Build against this:** `zones.colour`, nullable text, `#rrggbb` in either case (constraint `zones_colour_hex` checks the form only, so any swatch the palette picks is accepted; null = no dot); the Lifecycle zone refuses a colour (`refuse_lifecycle_map`, message "takes no colour"). `zone_colour` is the last column of `resident_list_view`, `resident_who_and_where`, `medication_list_residents` and `special_diet_list`; screens reading the `zones` table see `colour` directly. The palette in this item has no white or sand, though the site uses both (the House Zone is sand; item above shows "Front Zone · White"): decide those two before building the swatches.
- [ ] **Facility map plans uploaded and replaced from the system, not committed to the repo — and two new plans to load (House Zone, revised Main Zone).** Lutan, 2026-10-05. **A reversal, stated as one:** `docs/decisions/2026-10-05-facility-map-editor.md` chose "committed files, no upload": a plan is a WebP dropped into `public/facility-maps/` and deployed, then registered in the editor's *Add a plan*. Lutan now wants plans **added and replaced through the system**, so a new drawing never needs a developer or a deploy. That decision file foresaw this ("only `planImageUrl` and `addPlan` change; the map and the editor read the URL, never the path"), so record it as a new decision that supersedes that section, not as a quiet edit. **The two new plans** (Lutan has the images; save the originals to Drive and into the PR's description, not into `public/`): (1) **House Zone**: the house, the Cat Zone, enclosures 2–5, the medical cages, washing machine and dish-washing area, the garden, the medical room / kitchen / storage block, the Front Zone along the bottom, and arrows towards the Main Zone. (2) **Main Zone, revised**: enclosures 1 (Main Room) to 13, the Medical room / Kitchen / Storage block, with the Cat Zone and House Zone drawn on its right and labels in English and Thai. Check with Lutan which plan is which zone's, and whether the revised Main Zone, which shows the whole site, should also become the **overview**. Several numbers repeat across zones (2, 3, 4, 5 exist in both), so placing shapes must be done against the right zone's enclosures, and the enclosure names in Settings → Enclosures should match what the drawings print. **Where the images live (decide in the PR, with the reasons the earlier file gave still standing):** it must **not** depend on Google Drive, since a lapsed token would make the map unopenable (the 2026-09-25 outage), and it must keep working when the Pi is down. The likely answer is **Supabase Storage**: a private bucket, a size and type gate (WebP, PNG, JPEG, SVG only after sanitising, ≤ about 5 MB, re-encoded or at least dimension-checked on upload), read by signed-in users only, served through the app with a long cache keyed on a version so a replaced plan shows at once. Rejected for the record, unless the build shows otherwise: base64 in the row (a megabyte in every `/enclosures` read) and Drive (above). Keep the three committed plans working until they are migrated into the new store, then delete them from `public/` (they are currently fetchable by anyone who knows the name, which this also fixes). **Replacing a plan is the hard part, not uploading one:** shapes are stored as percentages of the image (`0142`), so a new drawing with a different layout or crop puts every existing shape in the wrong place, silently. So *Replace this plan* must: show the new image with the existing shapes over it before saving; say how many enclosures are placed on it; offer to keep the shapes (same drawing, sharper copy) or clear them for re-placing (a new layout); and never leave the old image deleted until the new one is saved. Keep the previous image as the plan's history, so a mistaken replace can be undone. Record who replaced it (the audit log, `0121`). **Who:** the facility-map editor's activity (admin and management today, `facility.enclosures` in the catalogue); on a phone, since the Director's evenings are the only desk time (the phone-only note in the roles item), with the upload taking a photo or a file. **Then:** load the two new plans through the new screen (not by a migration), place the enclosures on each with the editor, and check the map on a phone. Manual (the Map and editor topics: replacing a plan), both dictionaries, a decision file superseding the storage section of the 2026-10-05 one, a releases line, and a test plan covering upload refused for a wrong type or size, replace keeping shapes, replace clearing shapes, undo a replace, and a signed-out request for a plan image refused.

A diet is mandatory at intake and defaults to the standard.** Today it is optional: `IntakeForm.tsx` offers "None yet — add from the hub later" and the hint says "Optional". ## Admin

- [x] **Bug: Settings → Security shows "Minified React error #441" instead of saying what went wrong.** Reported by Lutan 2026-09-26 with a screenshot on the dev-coloured site: an action on the archived `manager@gmail.com` row (Restore or Delete) failed and the row showed only "Minified React error #441; visit https://react.dev/errors/441…". **What #441 is:** React's production stand-in for *any* error thrown from a Server Action (`resolveErrorProd` in `react-server-dom-*-client.browser.production.js`). In a production build the real message is stripped before it reaches the browser, so `throw new Error(...)` in a `"use server"` file never shows its text to the user, **not even the friendly translated ones** (`t.admin.security.errors.cantDeleteOwnAccount` and the like). **So it is two fixes. (1) This page:** `src/app/admin/security/actions.ts` throws in every action (create, approve, change role, temporary password, archive, restore, delete). Make each return a result (`{ ok: true } | { ok: false, error: string }`) and have `UsersTable.tsx` / `AccessRequests.tsx` / `CreateUserForm.tsx` show `error`. Map known failures to plain words in both languages. Log the raw error server-side. **The failure in the screenshot is confirmed** (Lutan, 2026-09-26): it was **Delete** on a login with records against it. About 20 foreign keys to `auth.users` have no delete rule, so a delete is refused as soon as the person created anything: `created_by` on ten tables (from 0001 on), `uploaded_by`, `updated_by`, `user_id` on `assistant_actions` (0070), and others. **Don't fix it by changing them to `on delete set null`:** that would erase who recorded what, and that history is the point. Archive (0063) is the right tool for someone who has left. Instead, Delete is for accounts with no history (a typo, a test login). Before deleting, check the references (a small `security definer` function that counts them, or catch the error), and either hide Delete for such users or answer "This person has records in the system, so they can't be deleted. Archive them instead." **(2) The same pattern across the app:** at planning time 13 `"use server"` files under `src/app` held 98 `throw new Error` lines. Every one that can happen in normal use (validation, "not allowed", a clash) shows #441 in production. Sweep them to the return-a-result shape (the `upload-worker-error` fix already did this for uploads). Keep `throw` only for genuinely unexpected failures, and give those a generic "Something went wrong, please try again" plus the error's digest so it can be looked up in the Worker logs. Do the Security page first as its own small PR, then the sweep. The manual needs no change. Add a releases line (people will see real messages).
- [x] **Server Actions across the app: return a result instead of throwing (#441, part 2).** The Security page was converted on 2026-09-26 (`claude/security-action-errors`): its actions return `ActionResult` from `src/lib/action-result.ts` and use `runAction()` / `unexpectedFailure()` — see decisions.md, "Server Actions return a result, not a throw". The other `"use server"` files under `src/app` still `throw new Error(...)` for ordinary refusals (validation, not allowed, a clash), and every one of those shows "Minified React error #441" in production — including `assertAdminRole()` on the other `/admin` pages. Sweep them to the same type (and fold the existing near-duplicates, `FriendActionResult`, `ProjectActionResult`, `MaintenanceActionResult`, `TranslationActionResult`, into it). Verify against a production build (`next build` + `next start`); dev mode shows the real message and proves nothing. Probably one PR per area so they can run alongside other streams. Add a releases line. [`/admin` done 2026-09-28 (`claude/admin-actions-result-sweep`) `/residents` done 2026-09-29 (`claude/actions-result-sweep-residents`) contacts done 2026-09-29 (`claude/actions-result-sweep-contacts`) and management done 2026-09-30 (`claude/actions-result-sweep-management`) and projects done 2026-10-01 (`claude/actions-result-sweep-projects`) — see docs/decisions/; maintenance done 2026-10-01 (`claude/actions-result-sweep-maintenance`), which folds the last near-duplicate, `MaintenanceActionResult` — **item complete across all six areas**; a final grep found no `throw new Error` left in a `"use server"` file outside a `runAction` body (`ProjectActionResult`, `FriendActionResult` and `TranslationActionResult` are folded; translations lives under `/management`, so management took it). Management skipped `shelter-friends/actions.ts` (contacts' file) and `cashflow/fixed-outgoings` (already converted); `assertManagementRole()` is deleted. Residents had no near-duplicate type of its own. Shared helpers in `src/lib/placements/*` and `src/lib/archive/*` still return `{ error }` (the assistant uses them too); the residents actions translate at the boundary]
- [x] **Settings → System status: a health and usage page for admins.** Lutan, 2026-09-26. One read-only page (e.g. `/admin/status`, a card on the Settings landing page, admin only) showing whether the system is healthy, with a few plain usage figures. **Health, each a green/amber/red tile with the time it was checked and the error text when red:** database reachable (a trivial query, with its latency), Google Drive connected (a token refresh plus read access to `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID`; the token lapse of 2026-09-25 is exactly what this should have shown first), migrations in step with `main` (reuse what `scripts/apply-migrations.mjs --status` computes), the running release and when it was deployed (`src/lib/releases.ts` / the Worker version tag), release mail configured, the last weekly backup and its age (needs `scripts/backup.mjs` to record a run somewhere it can be read, e.g. a row or a Drive file timestamp), and the Pi origin once it is live (reachable, or "serving from the Worker fallback"). **Usage, with a period picker (7 / 30 / 90 days):** staff sign-ins and active users (from `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` or an audit of sign-ins), records added (residents, vet visits, weights, maintenance jobs), photos uploaded, assistant requests (`assistant_actions`), and public-site visitors. **Visitors need care:** `/privacy` promises no analytics tracking, so count them without cookies or trackers, from Cloudflare's own request analytics for the Worker (GraphQL Analytics API, aggregate counts only) or Cloudflare Web Analytics, and check the privacy text still holds. **Build notes:** run the checks server-side with short timeouts and cache them for a minute or so, so the page never hangs on a dead dependency. Never show secret values. Keep each check a small function so a later "alert me" (email or LINE when a tile goes red) can reuse them. **Overlap:** batch 1's `drive-upload-errors` stream is adding a Drive-token check; build this page on that check rather than writing a second one. Manual: a short Settings topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`.
- [x] **System status: alert me when a tile goes red.** Claude, 2026-09-26, follow-up to the System status page. Email (and later LINE) to admins when a health check in `src/lib/status/health.ts` turns `fail`, and again when it recovers, so nobody has to open the page to find out. Every check already returns `{ state, error, facts }` through `runCheck` (timeout, never throws, secrets scrubbed); alert on `fail` only, since `off` is deliberate (decisions.md 2026-09-26). Open questions: where it runs (a Worker cron trigger is the obvious home, but the checks import Next-side code), how to remember the last state so one outage is one mail rather than one per run, and whether release mail's Cloudflare send_email binding (verified addresses only) is enough or LINE is needed first.

The Website visitors tile on `/admin/status` is grey until the Worker has `CLOUDFLARE_ANALYTICS_TOKEN` (a Cloudflare API token with Zone → Analytics → Read on lannacare.org, created by Lutan in the Cloudflare dashboard) and `CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID`. Put both in each `.env.deploy.*` and run `deploy.mjs --secrets`, then confirm the tile turns green. Its GraphQL query (`httpRequests1dGroups`, src/lib/status/usage.ts) has never run against the live API, so fix it there if Cloudflare rejects it. The counts cover the whole zone, test site included; if that proves misleading, filter by host with `httpRequestsAdaptiveGroups` instead (check the free plan's range limit first).
- [x] **System status: a card showing access requests awaiting approval.** Lutan, 2026-09-27. Access requests are only visible on Settings → Security, and that page now needs a 2-step-verified session (`src/lib/auth/two-step.ts`, 0100) — so an admin has to step up just to find out whether anyone is waiting, and in practice nobody looks. Put a card on `/admin/status` (admin only, readable at a normal sign-in) that shows **how many** requests are waiting and how long the oldest has waited, with a link to Settings → Security that leads through the step-up. **Show the count, not the people:** names and emails stay behind 2-step verification, so the card says "2 access requests waiting, oldest 3 days" and nothing that identifies who. **Counting:** use the same definition as `src/app/admin/security/page.tsx` — an auth user with no `user_roles` row — so the card and the list can never disagree; ideally move that filter into one shared helper both call (service role, server side, short timeout like the other checks). Amber when any are waiting (it needs action, nothing is broken), neutral when there are none; not red, and so not an alert under the "alert me when a tile goes red" rule unless Lutan wants an email for new requests too — ask. Check archived users are not counted as requests. Manual: a sentence in the System status topic, en/th; a releases line.
- [ ] **A monitoring dashboard on Lutan's Home Assistant, with a walkthrough.** Lutan, 2026-10-02. Lutan runs a Home Assistant server and wants the system's health on a dashboard there, with alerts to his phone. **The deliverable is a working setup plus `docs/home-assistant-monitoring.md`,** written as a step-by-step walkthrough done together with Lutan, since he will be doing the Home Assistant side. **What exists to build on:** `src/lib/status/health.ts` already runs the System status checks (database, Drive, migrations in step, running release, release mail, last backup and its age, Pi origin or Worker fallback) with timeouts and secrets scrubbed, and `src/lib/status/alerts.ts` emails on a red tile. But the only machine-readable door is `POST /api/status/alerts`, guarded by the service-role key, which must never be handed to Home Assistant. **App side (small):** a read-only `GET /api/status/health` returning each check's `state` (ok / warn / fail / off), its checked-at time and a few safe numbers (backup age in days, release version, origin in use), guarded by its **own** bearer token (`STATUS_READ_TOKEN`, a Worker secret and in `.env.deploy.*`, rotatable without touching anything else), `no-store`, rate-limited, and never including error text that could leak internals. Let it through `src/proxy.ts` the way the alerts path is, plus a test in a `scripts/check-*.mjs` that a wrong or missing token is refused. A releases line is not needed (staff will not see it). **Home Assistant side (the walkthrough):** (1) a `rest` sensor polling that endpoint every few minutes, with template binary sensors per check; (2) plain uptime checks independent of the app, so a dead app is still noticed: the public site, the app's sign-in page and `test.lannacare.org` over HTTPS (HA's built-in HTTP/"uptime" checks, or the Uptime Kuma add-on if he prefers its history view), and the TLS certificate expiry; (3) the Pi itself, if it is on the same network: CPU temperature, disk space (the photo cache and backups live there), memory, and whether the `lanna-care` service and `cloudflared` are running (HA's System Monitor runs on HA's own host, so for the Pi use Glances or a small MQTT/exporter on the Pi; pick one with Lutan); (4) a dashboard: one row of green/red tiles, backup age, release version, Pi health, uptime history; (5) notifications to the HA phone app on any red, with a delay so a 30-second blip does not wake anyone, and a "recovered" message. **Answered by Lutan, 2026-10-02:** HA is **on the same network as the Pi**, and he wants **traffic as well as health** (database, Pi and the rest). So: **Pi health locally,** with nothing exposed to the internet: Glances on the Pi read by HA's Glances integration (CPU, temperature, memory, disk for the photo cache and backups, load), plus the `lanna-care` and `cloudflared` services up, and `cloudflared`'s own metrics (its local Prometheus port: tunnel connections up, requests and errors through the tunnel). **Database health,** added to the app's health endpoint as numbers, not just a state: response time, database size against the free tier's 500 MB, active connections, and the newest backup's age; computed server-side so HA never holds a database or service-role key. **Traffic** from Cloudflare's analytics (a read-only API token with Zone → Analytics → Read, the one the visitor-count item already needs, stored in HA's secrets, not the repo): visitors and requests per day, share served from Cloudflare's cache, 4xx/5xx counts, and Worker errors including the 1102 CPU-limit ones, so a spike shows on the dashboard. Either HA calls Cloudflare's GraphQL API directly with a `rest` sensor, or the app's endpoint relays it (one place for the token); pick with Lutan. Note the counts cover the whole zone (test site included) until filtered by host. **Also answered, 2026-10-02:** HA is a **Home Assistant Green** (HA OS, so add-ons are available), and **phone notifications are not set up yet**, so the walkthrough adds them as a step: install the Home Assistant Companion app on his phone and sign in to the Green, which gives a `notify.mobile_app_<phone>` service; its push messages arrive away from home too (they go through Home Assistant's push service), while opening the dashboard from outside the house needs remote access (Home Assistant Cloud, paid, or his own secure tunnel; decide with Lutan, and do not open HA to the internet directly). If he would rather not install the app, a Telegram bot is the common alternative; LINE Notify is closed, so LINE would need its Messaging API. Either way the app's own email alerts (`src/lib/status/alerts.ts`) stay as a second channel. Test an alert end to end (stop the `lanna-care` service on the Pi briefly; the phone gets "down" and then "recovered"). Keep the token and any HA config containing it out of the repo; the doc shows placeholders.
- [x] **A logo or photo upload that exhausts the Worker shows the user a minified React error.** Done 2026-09-25: it was not the Worker. Next caps a Server Action body at 1 MB and rejects a larger one before the action runs, so every logo or Website photo between 1 and 15 MB failed where no app code could catch it (reproduced on dev: HTTP 500, `E394 Body exceeded 1 MB limit`). `next.config.ts` now lets the app's one limit through (`src/lib/uploads/limits.ts`, 15 MB, also used by every upload route), the clients pre-check the size and turn any thrown failure into words (`runUploadAction`), and `proxy.ts`'s 10 MB body cap was raised with it. What a Worker can really process is still unmeasured — see the follow-up item. Found 2026-09-24 on production, uploading a Shelter Friend logo: the page printed `Minified React error #441` and a link to react.dev, which decodes to "An error occurred in the Server Components render" — the message itself is stripped in production builds. The same image failed twice and a smaller one succeeded, so it is size- or format-dependent. It is **not** the app's own validation: `MAX_FILE_BYTES` (15 MB) and `ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES` both `return { error }` with readable text before Drive is touched, and the Drive calls themselves sit in a `try/catch` that returns a friendly message. So a file that passes validation is breaking *during* the upload, where nothing catches it. The likely mechanism: `drive.uploadFile` (`src/lib/google/drive.ts`) builds a multipart `Blob` holding the whole file in memory inside the Worker, and on the free plan that can exhaust memory or the 10 ms CPU budget — the same `1102` family as the render pressure the Pi origin is meant to fix. **Two things to do, and they are independent.** (a) Catch it: wrap the action so a resource failure reaches the user as "that image is too large to process — try one under about 2 MB" rather than a React error code. A user cannot act on `#441`, and it reads as the app being broken. (b) Decide the real limit: 15 MB is inherited from `admin/website/actions.ts` and is almost certainly higher than a Worker can actually process, so the number should reflect what works rather than what the form accepts. **The same limits and upload path are used by `/admin/website` photos and blood-test attachments** (`src/app/api/blood-tests/[id]/attachments/route.ts`), so whatever is wrong is wrong in three places. Worth re-testing after the Pi origin lands, since rendering and uploads move to real CPU and memory then and the failure may simply go away — but the error message should be fixed regardless, because the next resource limit will produce the same unreadable page.
- [x] **Measure the largest upload a Worker actually completes, and set the limit from it.** Follow-up to the #441 fix (claude/upload-worker-error, 2026-09-25), which found the reported failure was Next's 1 MB Server Action body cap, not the Worker, and raised the framework limits to the app's 15 MB (`src/lib/uploads/limits.ts`). Whether a free-plan Worker can buffer, parse and re-send a 15 MB file inside its CPU and memory budget was never measured. On test.lannacare.org, upload real photos of about 3, 6, 10 and 14 MB through a Shelter Friend logo and a resident photo, and read each request's CPU time and outcome in the Worker's observability logs. If anything below 15 MB hits 1102, lower `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` to what works (the `fileTooLarge` strings name the number, in en and th). The user now sees a readable message either way, so this sets the right number rather than fixing a broken page. Moot for requests the Pi origin serves once it is live. **Done 2026-10-07 without a measurement campaign: the Pi is live, and on the Pi path (test, production) the Worker streams the body to it and never parses or re-sends it, holding at most one 16 MB copy for the 530 fallback; the one Worker-rendered environment (uat) is not deployed. `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` stays 15 MB. Unmeasured and named: an upload while the Pi is off the network (docs/decisions/2026-10-07-worker-upload-limit.md).**
- [x] **Refuse an "image" upload that isn't a real image.** Done 2026-09-26: `src/lib/uploads/file-signature.ts` checks the leading bytes on all seven Drive upload paths and refuses a mismatch in en/th before Drive is touched (a zero-filled `mid3.jpg` is now refused on every one); Website hero/gallery and Shelter Friend logos read the new file back before switching, and send the replaced or removed file to Drive's trash instead of deleting it. The dev hero had already been reset to a real photo by then; production's `hero_drive_file_id` was not checked from the worktree (handed to Lutan). Lutan, 2026-09-26. The dev (and test.lannacare.org) home page lost its hero photo: the hero is now `mid3.jpg` (Drive `1NAFh8O_pR5RBYTVjditj4nWo8qW1NL-2`), 3 MiB of zero bytes, uploaded on 2026-09-25 while testing the 15 MB upload limit (`docs/test-plans/upload-worker-error.md`, §4). It was accepted because every upload path trusts the browser's MIME type (`ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES`) and never looks at the bytes. Replacing the hero also permanently deleted the previous hero file from Drive, so the old photo can't be restored. **Fix:** check the file's leading bytes against the formats accepted (JPEG `FF D8 FF`, PNG `89 50 4E 47`, WebP `RIFF….WEBP`, GIF, HEIC `ftyp` box) in one helper beside `src/lib/uploads/limits.ts`. Call it from every image upload: Website hero and gallery, Shelter Friend logo, resident, project and maintenance photos. Refuse a mismatch with a readable en/th message before Drive is touched. Attachments that may be PDFs check `%PDF` the same way. **Also consider** not deleting the previous hero or gallery file until the new one is saved and confirmed readable, and moving it to Drive's trash rather than deleting it, so a bad upload can be undone. **Meanwhile:** set a real hero photo on dev under Settings → Website. If lannacare.org shows a broken hero too, check its `site_content.hero_drive_file_id` separately: that is the production database, which the upload test did not touch.

- [ ] **User preferences: pick a colour theme.** Requested 2026-09-23 by Lutan. **Zone colours (2026-10-08):** the twelve zone swatches in `src/lib/zones/palette.ts` were contrast-checked only against today's dark surfaces; a light theme must re-check them, and white (1.1:1 on white) and sand (1.7:1) will fail as dots, so give the dot a dark ring in a light theme (`ZoneDot`'s ring is the page background today) — see `docs/decisions/2026-10-08-zone-colour-palette.md`. Let each user choose the app's look and feel from a small set of colour themes (e.g. the current dark orange, a light theme, a high-contrast one, and one or two alternative accents), saved per user so it follows them between devices — a `user_preferences` row or `user_metadata` (preferences, not permissions, so user-editable metadata is fine here), with `localStorage` only as a no-flash cache. Put the picker in the account menu beside Change password. The groundwork is there: every component reads CSS tokens from `src/app/globals.css`, so a theme is a `[data-theme="…"]` block redefining `--background`, `--surface`, `--primary` etc., set on `<html>` by the layout. **The catch is the environment colouring:** `:root[data-env="dev"]` recolours the same tokens teal so dev is never mistaken for production, and a user theme must not erase that signal — decide whether the env marker moves to something themes don't touch (a header strip or badge, as the `uat` item plans) or each theme gets a dev variant. Check contrast (WCAG AA) for every theme, and the generated PDFs stay unthemed.

Same question for Messenger (username vs. a shared `m.me` link) — both are accepted as-is when pasted. - [x] **Settings → Website: break the page into tabs so it is manageable.** Done 2026-09-30 (see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-website-settings-tabs.md`): five tabs plus a Pages accordion. Lutan, 2026-09-27. `src/app/admin/website/page.tsx` stacks everything in one long column: hero photo, featured resident, site settings and contact details (`SiteSettingsForm`), the vet-visit estimate, one full editor per information page (`SitePageForm` × 5, each with English and Thai), the gallery and published projects. Finding one thing means scrolling past all the rest, and it will get longer: the International adoption page (which replaced Pet relocation, 2026-09-27) and Become a Shelter Friend each add another editor, and the Messenger/WhatsApp/X contacts add fields. **Suggested layout:** tabs across the top for the big groups, e.g. **Home page** (hero photo, featured resident, pet of the week), **Contact & settings** (site settings, contact channels, vet-visit estimate), **Pages** (the information pages), **Gallery**, **Projects**. Inside **Pages**, an accordion with one row per page (title, public link, last edited, "Thai missing" if the translation is empty), opening one editor at a time, so a new page just adds a row. Accordion-only for the whole page was considered: fewer clicks to see everything, but with this many sections it still ends up long once two are open; tabs plus one accordion is the recommendation, but the builder can show Lutan both before settling. **Behaviour:** the chosen tab is in the URL (`?tab=pages`, and `#our-story` opens that page's row), so a reload, the back button and links from elsewhere (e.g. the manual, or a System status tile) land in the right place. Every section already saves on its own, so switching tabs must not lose typing: warn before leaving a tab or closing a row with unsaved changes, as the stocktake sheet does. Use the same tab pattern as `/stocktake` (`StocktakeSheet.tsx`, `role="tab"`, keyboard arrows) rather than a new one, and keep it usable on a phone (tabs scroll sideways or become a select). No schema change. Manual: the Website section in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts` (where things are now), both dictionaries for the tab names, and a `src/lib/releases.ts` line. Best not to run in the same batch as another stream that edits this page.
- [ ] **A reviewable script for resident corrections, and a near-duplicate warning in the bulk loader.** Filed 2026-10-06 from a `.brief.md` written by a workstream created outside `/plan-day`; Lutan had the worktree removed, so the brief is preserved here rather than lost with the folder. **Why it came up:** bulk uploads (Brown, Blue, Left Zone) bring duplicates the loader's **exact-name** check misses, and fixing one needs whichever session happens to have access — on **2026-10-05 R-0240 was deleted by one chat and the same request for R-0220 was refused by another**, and Lutan had to hunt through chats. The miss that prompted it: Left Zone's *"Noon (Daeng)"* against the Blue upload's *"Noon"* — not an exact match, so the loader passed it. The fix is a fixed, reviewable script anyone can run, as `scripts/load-residents.mjs` was (`docs/decisions/2026-10-05-bulk-resident-loader.md`). **Two pieces. (1) A near-duplicate check in `load-residents.mjs`'s dry run — warn, do not refuse.** List each row matching an existing resident (**including archived and Lifecycle/Unassigned ones**) after stripping a bracket tag (`Noon` ~ `Noon (Daeng)`), by identical Thai name, or by close spelling; show the existing code, current enclosure and created date so a human can rule same-dog / different-dog. **Keep the exact-name refusal as it is.** **(2) `scripts/correct-resident.mjs`** — dry run by default, `--apply` to write, `--env` like the other scripts. `--remove R-nnnn` removes a resident with **no real history**, refusing if it has vet appointments, blood tests, prescriptions, weights beyond intake, immunizations, procedures, attachments/photos, adoption updates or a Drive folder; its seed placement and diet rows go with it inside one `begin…commit` (**the FKs have no cascade, and `0119` guards placement history — so check how the 2026-10-05 R-0240 delete got past it**, which is a finding in its own right). Print exactly what will be touched plus a before/after of the survivor; write the removed rows to `--receipt <path>` as JSON **before** deleting; refuse unless both codes exist and the names are similar, or `--force-names`. `--merge-into` only if it is clean to do, otherwise leave it out. **Constraints the brief recorded:** Lutan is non-technical here, so **output must be plain and the commands copy-paste**; **the script is the deliverable — no session runs it with `--apply` against any database**, dry runs against dev only, Lutan runs the apply; **no `process.exit()` after a `fetch`** (libuv assertion on Windows, see `load-residents.mjs`). No schema expected — if one is needed, stop and say so. Needs a decision file, the README scripts list, a `Bash(node scripts/correct-resident.mjs *)` permission rule for the main checkout, and `docs/test-plans/resident-corrections.md`. → *Admin* **SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-10-06 (Lutan): the duplicate residents themselves were already sorted out by another session, so the incident half is closed.** What is left is only the *prevention*: the near-duplicate warning in the loader so the next bulk upload does not pass `Noon` against `Noon (Daeng)`, and a reviewable script so a correction does not depend on finding a session willing to do it. **Lutan noted the inconsistency plainly — one chat deleted R-0240 and another refused the same request for R-0220.** The inconsistency is the real complaint, and a fixed script anyone can run is the answer to it. **Low priority now; drop it if the loader is not used again.**
  - **2026-10-07 (`resident-view-grants-0160`): the R-0240 question is answered; the rest of the item is untouched.** `0119` never guarded a delete: its triggers fire on `placement_history` UPDATE and INSERT, and only the deceased lock fires on DELETE. A session reaches the database as `postgres` or with the service key, both of which bypass RLS, so no policy was the path either. The delete was simply allowed. Production's `audit_log` (residents audited since `0121`) should hold it, and the script's `--remove` checks remain the only guard. `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-view-write-grants.md`.

## Management

- [ ] **Maps on contacts and Shelter Friends: fix the 404 when the map is tapped, and store the real location (a pin), not an address Google guesses.** Lutan, 2026-10-08: on a contact's page and on Shelter Friends, tapping the map thumbnail gives a **404**. The only thing that works is typing a street address, and **in Thailand Google often places a typed address at the wrong spot, or on a different business**. **Reproduce first, with real rows** (dev, and read-only on production when Lutan asks): note the exact address text and the URL the tap opens, for each failing contact and friend. **How it works today:** the address field holds either typed text or a pasted maps link, sometimes with text after it. The thumbnail is a keyless Google embed built from it (`addressMapEmbedSrc()`, `src/lib/contacts/map-preview.ts`, which follows `maps.app.goo.gl` short links server-side to find a place or coordinates). The tap opens `mapHref()` (`src/lib/contacts/contacts.ts`), which returns a pasted link **unchanged, including anything after it**, and `FriendCard` uses `map_location ?? address` the same way. **Likely causes to check, not conclusions:** (a) the tap opens the whole field (link plus trailing text, or a link with a space in it), which is not a valid URL; (b) **old-style `goo.gl/maps/…` short links stopped working in August 2025**, when Google shut down the Firebase Dynamic Links service behind `goo.gl`, so any contact saved with one now 404s at Google; `maps.app.goo.gl` links still work; (c) a short link that could not be resolved, so the thumbnail and the tap point at different things. **The fix:** (1) **Store a location, not just text.** Add `latitude` / `longitude` (or a place, e.g. Google's place ID plus coordinates) to contacts, and to Shelter Friends if they don't use the contact's (schema PR first). Get it **once, when the address is saved**, not on every view: from a pasted `maps.app.goo.gl` link (resolve it then, and refuse with a plain message if it cannot be resolved or is a dead `goo.gl/maps` link), from a pasted full Google Maps URL, from a **Plus Code** (common in Thai rural addresses), or from typed coordinates. Show **"Is this the right place?"** with a small map of the pin before saving, so a wrong location is caught when it is entered. (2) **Both the thumbnail and the tap use the stored coordinates** (`https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=<lat>,<lng>`, which opens the Maps app on a phone at that exact spot), never the address text. The typed address stays as the written address, for reading and for post. (3) **Guidance on the form,** in English and Thai: "In Google Maps, find the place, tap Share, copy the link and paste it here." That is what works for Thai addresses. (4) **Fix the existing rows:** a script that lists every contact and friend with an address that is a dead `goo.gl/maps` link, a link that does not resolve, or plain text, so Lutan or the Director can re-share the right link for each; the map is hidden for those (not a 404) until fixed. (5) **The website's contact map** (`site_content.contact_map_url`, Settings → Website) uses a similar link: check it the same way. Public Shelter Friend pages show the map only where the friend opted in (`show_map`, 0076); keep that. No paid Maps API; the keyless embed and links stay. Manual (Contacts, Shelter Friends: how to paste a location), both dictionaries, a releases line, and a test plan with a `maps.app.goo.gl` link, a dead `goo.gl/maps` link, a full Maps URL, a Plus Code, typed coordinates and a plain Thai address, each tapped on an iPhone and an Android phone.
- [ ] **Contacts: separate "Address" and "Map link" fields, so the written address is never a URL.** Lutan, 2026-10-08: "It may be beneficial to have an address field as well as a map field — currently they are one and the same." Seen on I Blue Paw (production): its address field holds only `https://maps.app.goo.gl/V5niTogBMv44An7k7`, so the contact page prints the raw link where the address should be and the public Friend card has no written address at all; Lutan: "I don't like the address". **Schema first, as its own PR:** a `map_url` (or the pin columns from the item above — decide which in the schema PR, and do not build both) on `contacts`, additive, plus `public_shelter_friends` exposing it under `show_map` instead of `address` (0076's `map_location`). **Then the feature:** two inputs on the contact forms (Management → Contacts create and edit, the Friend wizard) with the Share-link guidance (EN/TH) under the map one; the hub, `ContactActions`, `FriendCard` and the contacts list read the map from the new column through `addressMap()` (`src/lib/contacts/map-preview.ts`) and print `address` as text only. **Move the existing rows:** where `address` starts with a link, move the link to the new column and keep the text after it as the address (until then the stopgap is "link, space, written address" in the one field, which `splitAddress()` already reads that way). Overlaps parts (1) and (4) of the item above — do them together if that item is picked first. Manual (Contacts, Shelter Friends), both dictionaries, releases line, test plan.

If yes, grant the cell (it also opens the rest of Settings → Website, so check that is intended); if no, say so here and close this. Filed 2026-10-07. **ANSWERED 2026-10-08 (Lutan): yes, Management holds `website.content`** (`docs/decisions/2026-10-07-management-settings-split.md`: the Director runs the website by day as Management). **Not ticked, because it cannot happen yet:** no migration grants the cell. The grant is the cell plus those three policies rewritten to `has_permission('website.content')`.
