Accepted consequence, to say in that PR: anyone holding the Management role can change the public site.

- [x] **Purchasing: fold away the "Nothing to buy" rows so the page shows what to buy.** Lutan, 2026-10-05: on Management → Purchasing (`/management/purchasing`) the rows that read **Nothing to buy** create a lot of noise; remove them or put them behind an accordion. **Do the accordion, not removal:** under each table (Medicines, Food) show only the rows with something to buy, then one collapsed row, **"12 items need nothing for this period ▸"**, that opens the rest in place (native `<details>`, closed by default, the count in both languages). Removing them outright would hide the working ("counted 40 on …, need 25") someone uses to check why an item is not on the list. **Keep visible, never folded:** an item **never counted** or with a **stale count** (the page's `neverCounted` / `stale` warnings, `src/app/management/purchasing/page.tsx`), even when the sum says nothing to buy, because "nothing to buy" from a guessed stock figure is exactly what the warning exists for; sort those just after the items to buy. When every item needs nothing, the page keeps its existing "Nothing to buy for this period" message and the folded list. **Check the other views match:** the phone view (`PurchasingPhone.tsx`, which the 2IC uses, being phone-only) and the printable / CSV shopping list should already list only items to buy; confirm, and apply the same fold on the phone if it shows the zero rows. Remember the open state only for the visit (no stored preference). No schema. Manual (Purchasing topic: one line), both dictionaries, a releases line, and a check at 375 px with Thai labels.
- [ ] **One place to translate everything: bring every Thai field into Management → Translations, and add the ones that are missing (diets first).** Lutan, 2026-10-05: diets have no Thai option, and translating means hunting in different places. **Why it is scattered:** the 2026-09-21 decision split translation in two. **Prose** goes through the `translations` table and appears on `/management/translations`: resident bio, temperament, past story, hook line and ideal home; project stories and photo captions; maintenance job titles and descriptions; site page titles and bodies; Shelter Friend blurb, help kind and discount note (`translatable_fields`). **Labels** are paired `_th` columns edited wherever the English is: zone and enclosure names (Settings → Zones / Enclosures), project folder titles (under Rename, see the Our work item), the resident's Thai name, and the website's tagline, visiting hours and hero alt text (Settings → Website). So the translator has to know each of those screens exist. **And some things shown to staff have no Thai at all**, the diet list among them. Inventory before building, at least: diet type names and units (`diet_types`), medication names and units, frequency labels, immunization, procedure and blood-test type names, recurring job titles and descriptions, clinic names where they are descriptive, stocktake units of measure, and any staff-typed label that appears on a Thai reader's screen. For each, decide: needs Thai (a label a Thai reader sees, e.g. "Standard Kibble + Chicken"), or stays as typed (a brand or drug name that is the same in both languages, which is common for medications: say so rather than leaving it looking forgotten). **Build:** (1) **Translations page shows everything.** Keep the storage rule (labels as paired columns, prose in the table) if it suits, but the page lists **both**: every prose row as today, and every label whose Thai is empty or older than its English, grouped by kind (Website, Residents, Projects, Diets, Medications, Setup lists, Places, Maintenance, Recurring jobs), each with an inline editor and a link to where it is used. Filters: *missing*, *out of date*, *all*. A count of what is missing per group at the top. Alternatively, move labels into the `translations` table too, if one storage makes the page and the "out of date" tracking simpler; decide in the decision file, because a label in the table needs its English snapshot to know when it went stale, which paired columns do not have today. (2) **Add the missing Thai fields** found by the inventory, starting with **diets**: `diet_types.name_th` (or a translation row), shown to a Thai reader on the diet list, a resident's Diet tab, the Head of Medical's special-diets list, the stocktake and the purchasing page. Schema PR first for any new columns. (3) **Keep the field on its own screen as well**, so someone adding a diet can type the Thai there and then; the Translations page is the catch-all, not the only door. (4) **Who:** whoever holds `translations.manage` (Admin and Management today, and the Director's draft gives it to the Director only) translates everything from that page, including labels whose own screen they could not otherwise open (e.g. a setup list that is Admin's): translating a label is not editing the list. Note this in the permissions catalogue. Manual (Translations topic), both dictionaries, a releases line, a test plan per group, and a check that no Thai reader's screen shows an English label the inventory missed (walk the 2IC's and the two Heads' homes in Thai at 375 px).
- [x] **A step-by-step wizard to add a Shelter Friend, like intake [done 2026-10-02].** Lutan, 2026-10-02. Today it takes five separate places and actions, which a non-technical staff member will not find on their own: create a contact on Management → Contacts, open that contact, choose **Make a Shelter Friend** on its card (`src/app/contacts/[id]/ShelterFriendCard.tsx`), fill in the profile and **Save**, then **Publish**. Behind it are separate server actions: `createContact` (`src/app/management/contacts/actions.ts`), then `createFriend`, `updateFriend`, `uploadFriendLogo` and `setFriendPublished` (`src/app/management/shelter-friends/actions.ts`). **Shape:** one **Add a Shelter Friend** button on Management → Shelter Friends (and on Management → Contacts) opening a wizard at e.g. `/management/shelter-friends/new`, reusing the intake wizard's chrome and step model (`src/app/residents/new/WizardChrome.tsx`, `steps.ts`: progress header, Back / Next, a Review step with Edit links, `?step=` to survive a refresh, steps hidden rather than unmounted so typing survives). Suggested steps: (1) **Who** — pick an existing contact (search; Vendors today, gated through the same one place the card uses) or create a new one with just name, type and phone/LINE/email; (2) **How they help** — help kind, discount note, the short blurb (with the Thai version through the usual translation queue, so the wizard need not ask for it); (3) **Logo and links** — logo upload (optional), website and Facebook; (4) **What the public may see** — the opt-ins for phone, email, LINE, address and map, each **off by default** with the manual's "ask them first" in plain words beside them; (5) **Review** — a live preview of their card exactly as `/friends` will show it, then a choice: **Publish now** or **Save as a draft** (the draft state the `shelter-friend-draft-state` change made visible on the card). **Saving must not leave half a friend behind:** intake works because one RPC writes everything at the end; here a contact, a friend row, its text and the publish flag are separate writes, so give the wizard one server action that does them in order and returns `ActionResult`, and either a `security definer` RPC for the database part in one transaction (schema PR first if so) or clear clean-up if a later step fails. The logo goes to Drive after the rows exist (as today); a failed upload must not lose the rest, and should say "saved without a logo, add it from the friend's card". Re-check every rule the existing actions enforce (role checks, URL validation in `src/lib/links/validate.ts`, the logo's file-type check) by calling the same helpers rather than copying them. **Keep the existing card** for editing an existing friend; the wizard is for adding one. Same roles as today (management and admin). Manual: rewrite the Shelter Friends topic around the wizard in `src/lib/manual/en.ts`, both dictionaries, a releases line. Test as a management user on a phone width as well as desktop, including picking an existing contact, creating a new one, skipping the logo, saving as draft then publishing from the card, and a failed logo upload.
- [x] **Purchasing: should an item nobody has counted be assumed to be none on the shelf, and included in the buy list? (revisit)** Lutan, 2026-10-04. Today (#295) an uncounted item gets no recommendation: the page says "Never counted — count it before ordering" and leaves it off the list, because a guess at the cupboard is a number nobody can account for. For a medicine in use, leaving it off is the worse mistake (no one hears it is needed), so the proposal is to treat an uncounted item the period would use (`needed > 0`) as zero on the shelf and put it on the list. **Conditions:** (1) it keeps a flag, "Never counted, assuming none on the shelf", and sits in its own group on the supplier list, never mixed in with quantities worked out from a real count; (2) only items with `needed > 0`, as the banner uses now. **Cost:** it reverses #295's rule and changes `purchaseRow()` in `src/lib/management/purchasing.ts` (and `scripts/check-purchasing.mjs`), and until a shelter's first stocktake almost everything would show as "buy the period's whole need". Desk table and phone view change together. A decision file that says it supersedes `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-purchasing-page.md`'s no-guess rule. [Done 2026-10-04 in PR #334 (`2ic-purchasing-phone`): Lutan decided yes, a never-counted item is assumed none on the shelf and shown flagged (yellow on the phone, a "never counted" tag in the printed list); see `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-2ic-purchasing-phone.md`.]
- [x] **Management → Purchasing: how much of each medicine and food to buy, for a period you choose, with a safety stock.** Lutan, 2026-09-28. A Management page listing, per medication, a **recommended purchase quantity** so whoever does the ordering does not have to work it out. **The sum, per item, in its own unit:** *expected stock now* = last counted quantity (`stock_counts` / `medication.stock_on_hand`, 0093) − scheduled usage since that count (the prescription forecast, `doses_per_day` × `dose_quantity`) + receipts since that count (`stock_receipts`, 0096); *needed* = scheduled usage over the chosen period (from today) + **safety stock**; *recommended purchase* = needed − expected stock now, **never below 0**, rounded up to a whole pack if a pack size is known. **Period picker:** 1 week / 2 weeks (and perhaps 1 month / custom), reusing `src/lib/management/forecast-window.ts` as the forecasts already do. **Safety stock:** a new optional `medication.safety_stock` (numeric, the item's unit, null = none), set on the add/edit form in Management → Medications when an item is created or later, for items that are always in use whatever is prescribed today — IV/sub-Q fluids and the common antibiotics are Lutan's examples. It is a floor kept on the shelf, so it is added to *needed* on every period. Consider also adding the supplier lead time already stored (`reorder_lead_days`, 0083) to the period, so stock ordered today still covers the period once it arrives — say which in decisions.md. **Build on what exists, do not fork it:** `src/lib/management/stock.ts` already takes forecast usage off the last count to give days of stock; move the "expected stock now" part into one helper both pages call (and check whether it adds receipts since the count yet — if not, fix it there, so Medications' days-of-stock and this page agree). **Show the working** in each row (counted X on <date>, used ~Y since, received Z, need W for the period + S safety) so nobody has to trust a bare number, flag items **never counted** rather than guessing, and flag a count older than a few weeks as unreliable. A CSV / printable list of just the items to buy, grouped by supplier (the receipts' `supplier_contact_id` gives the usual supplier), is the thing someone takes to the vet wholesaler. **Cover food too (Lutan, 2026-09-28):** `diet_types` carry the same stock columns, counts and receipts, so the page covers every diet as well as every medicine — a Medicines / Food switch or two sections. **Special diets matter most**, so list them before the standard diet (the standard-diet flag from the Facility diet item tells them apart) and make the safety stock available on `diet_types` too. Diet usage comes from the residents on each diet (the diet forecast the Diets page already shows), not prescriptions. **Schema first:** `safety_stock` on both `medication` and `diet_types` (and pack size, if wanted) as a small `-schema` PR. Home: Management (or Shelter Operations if the Settings/Management review moves stock there). Manual topic en/th; a releases line. **Schema half done 2026-10-02 as `claude/schema-purchasing` (0128; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-safety-stock-schema.md`) — the feature half builds against this:** `safety_stock numeric` (nullable, `>= 0`, null = none; zero is a real value, distinct from null) on **both** `medication` and `diet_types`, stored in the item's **base unit** (`dose_unit` / `unit`) like `stock_on_hand`, counts and receipts. The form may take it in the purchase unit but must convert to base on save, as deliveries do. **No unit column and no pack-size column:** the pack is the item's purchase unit in `item_unit_conversions` (0118), so round up to its `base_units_per`. Nothing reads the column yet; write access is whoever already edits those tables. The item stays open for the page, the forms and the shared expected-stock helper.
- [x] **Stocktake in cupboard order.** *Done 2026-10-08 (`claude/stock-pages-finish`, batch 73): Cupboard order (`?view=order`) on Management → Medication stock and → Diet stock, Move up / Move down at 44 px, each press saved; `moveMedication` / `moveDietType` renumber the list 1…n (`src/lib/management/cupboard-order-server.ts`); `/stocktake` and both stock pages sort by `sort_order`, then name. Set on the Management half, not Settings, because the 2IC/Management phone is where the cupboard is walked. A shelf name can still be added later.* Claude, 2026-09-26, from the stocktake sheet. `/stocktake` lists items by name, which the backlog item accepted "for now"; staff walking the shelves want the list in the order the cupboard is laid out. Needs the shelter to describe the layout (shelf / box names), then a `shelf` or `sort_order` on `medication` and `diet_types` (schema PR first), a way for management to set it on the Management tables, and the sheet sorting by it with name as the tie-break. **STATUS 2026-10-08: schema half done (0161 `medication.sort_order`, `diet_types.sort_order`, both on the end of `stock_medications` / `stock_diet_types`, PR from `claude/order-and-units-schema`, see `docs/decisions/2026-10-08-order-and-unit-prices-schema.md`).** An order, not a shelf name: the shelter has not described its shelves, and the order is what the sheet needs whatever they are called; a shelf label can still be added later. Seeded in natural name order, new items go last (trigger). Still to do, from the updated `main`: Move up / Move down on Management → Medications and → Diets, and `/stocktake` sorting by `sort_order` with name as the tie-break.
- [x] **Saving a Shelter Friend does not publish it, and nothing says so.** Found 2026-09-24 on production, and it cost a round of "the release did not deploy properly" before the cause was clear. Making a Shelter Friend is two steps — **Save** the details, then **Publish** — implemented as two separate actions (`updateFriend` and `setFriendPublished` in `src/app/management/shelter-friends/actions.ts`). After Save the profile is a draft: `published` stays `false`, `public_shelter_friends` correctly filters it out (the view is `where sf.published and c.archived_at is null`, 0076), and so the public `/friends` page shows its empty state, the nav entry stays hidden and the home-page thank-you strip does not appear. All of that is correct behaviour, and from the outside it is indistinguishable from the feature being broken or not deployed. The card gives no indication after saving that the profile is still a draft. Options, cheapest first: a line on the card after Save — "Saved as a draft. Publish to show this on the website." — or a visible Draft badge on an unpublished profile, or a prompt on Save offering to publish straight away. Worth doing before anyone other than Lutan uses the feature, because the failure mode is silent: nothing errors, nothing is missing, the page simply looks empty. Related: the public page's empty state ("We're just getting started on thanking the businesses that help us") is written for a visitor and reads as intentional, which is right for the public but gives staff no hint that their own unpublished draft is the reason they cannot see it.

- [x] **Base the vet forecast on recent visit frequency, not a flat per-visit estimate.** Asked for by Lutan 2026-09-23 while testing PR #60: "can we set it to look at an average rolling number of visits recorded per week in the last quarter x the cost per vet visit, or number booked for that week if it's more." Today the vet line is a flat typical-visit estimate edited on `/admin/website` — it does not know how often the shelter actually goes. The proposed rule is, per week, `max(rolling average visits/week over the last quarter, visits actually booked that week) x cost per visit`, so a quiet week still carries the historical rate and a busy one is not understated. Decisions to make before building: (a) the page forecasts by **month**, so either the weekly figure is aggregated up or the rule is restated monthly; (b) whether "cost per vet visit" stays the flat estimate or becomes the mean of actual `vet_appointments.cost` over the same quarter, which would make the whole line self-maintaining; (c) what a booked visit with a **known** cost does — it should surely use its own cost rather than the estimate, which means the rule is really `max(estimated visits, booked visits)` for the *count* and actual cost where known; (d) the empty case, where a shelter with no history gets an average of zero and the line silently reads nothing rather than "unknown". Note this interacts with the defect PR #60 already fixed, where `coalesce(va.cost, estimate)` over a LEFT JOIN charged the estimate to months with no visits at all — any frequency-based rule needs the same care about distinguishing "no visits" from "no data". **Looked at 2026-09-24 and left as the flat figure on Lutan's call:** dev held 71 completed visits, none with a `cost`, and 20 in the last quarter (about 1.5 a week) — so the frequency half has data but the cost half does not, and the change needs a migration while 0073 was in flight. See `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-24). The dev query and the proposed rule are recorded there, so this is ready to pick up once invoices are being recorded.


- [x] **Archive contacts instead of deleting them.** Done 2026-09-24: schema in 0075 (#86); Archive / Restore on `/management/contacts` and `/contacts/[id]`, Show archived toggles on both lists, search still finds archived contacts, archived carers left out of `loadCarerOptions` and refused by `rehomeResident`. Customer request 2026-09-22. A carer who no longer fosters or adopts, a volunteer who has moved on, a supplier the shelter stopped buying from — none of them can be removed today: `deleteContact` in `src/app/management/contacts/actions.ts` refuses when `placement_history.carer_id` references them (rightly, that history must survive), and deleting the rest loses notes and contact details someone may need again. Add `archived_at timestamptz` (+ `archived_by`, optional `archive_reason` text) to `contacts` — a new migration, nullable, no RLS change. **Behaviour:** an Archive action (with reason prompt) on the contact row in `/management/contacts` and on `/contacts/[id]`; archived contacts drop out of the default `/management/contacts` and `/contacts` lists and out of every picker (`loadCarerOptions` in `src/lib/contacts/carers.ts`, the vendor/donor lookups) but stay reachable through a "Show archived" toggle, shown muted with an Archived badge and the reason, and can be Restored. **Where they still appear:** a resident's housing history keeps naming an archived carer (with the badge), and the foster/adopt form refuses to place a new animal with one unless the contact is restored first — that is the point of archiving. Keep `deleteContact` for contacts with no history at all (a typo entry); everything else archives. Reuse the same pattern the deceased-resident and hidden-deceased-list items use for "default hidden, toggle to show", and add `archived` to the contact search so a name lookup still finds them with the badge rather than reporting no match.
- [x] **Contacts: who should see a carer's phone and address?** **STATUS 2026-10-07 — option A is DONE (`claude/director-answers-schema`, `0155`); the data-level half is NOT: see "Carer contacts: close the data hole behind the pages" below.** *Waiting on the director* — deferred to them 2026-09-21; don't start until they've said which roles need which fields. Raised 2026-09-21 in the first staff walk-through: `/contacts` (list and hub, with phone, LINE, address and one-tap call / maps) is open to every signed-in role, and the RLS in 0001 gives staff read/write and volunteers/vets read on `contacts`. The concern is privacy — a volunteer shouldn't be able to look up a carer's home address. The counter-case, recorded in `src/app/contacts/page.tsx`, is that a volunteer doing a foster pick-up needs the carer's number. Options: (a) move the directory under Management (nav + `requireManagementUser()` on `/contacts` and `/contacts/[id]`) and leave the RLS alone so the carer pickers on intake / rehome keep working for staff — quick, but staff then have no way to phone a carer; (b) keep `/contacts` for staff and management, hide it from volunteers and vets, and drop the address from the list for everyone but management; (c) tighten RLS with a `contacts_directory` view that omits phone/address for non-management, which also protects the data from anyone poking the API. Decide with the user which roles need which fields, then do it in one pass with the manual and the README roles table. **DECIDED 2026-10-07 (the Director, with Lutan, from the decision brief q7, with q6): **ANSWERED after sixteen days. Management and the 2IC only.** Lutan chose option A (*"management only"*) and **added the 2IC** by name. Question 6 sets what she sees: **name and phone, no home address**. So: **Management — everything. The 2IC — name and phone. Staff and volunteers — nothing.** **The consequence was stated in the brief and accepted:** *"staff and volunteers can no longer phone a carer at all. They would have to ask you."* **Check that against the counter-case this item itself records** — `src/app/contacts/page.tsx` notes that *a volunteer doing a foster pick-up needs the carer's number*. **That is now a thing someone must ask Management for.** If it bites in practice, the fix is a narrower read (name and phone for a volunteer, address for nobody but Management), not a reversal. **Do not widen it without asking.** **TICKED 2026-10-07 (staleness sweep).** The question this item asks is answered and built: `0155` (`claude/director-answers-schema`, merged as #412, 2026-10-07) — `/contacts` opens on `requirePermission("contacts.browse")` (`src/app/contacts/page.tsx:19`), held by Management and the 2IC, the 2IC on name and phone only; `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-director-answers-schema.md`. **What is still open is its own item, immediately below:** *Carer contacts: close the data hole behind the pages* (staff still read `contacts` through the pickers and the RLS). The counter-case above (a volunteer on a foster pick-up must now ask Management for the number) is a watch-for, not open work.
- [ ] **Carer contacts: close the data hole behind the pages (option (c) of the contacts item).** Filed 2026-10-07 by `claude/director-answers-schema` (`0155`). The Director chose option A of the contacts item — management only, plus the 2IC on name and phone — and A is a **page-level** fix: `/contacts` and `/contacts/[id]` now ask a new `contacts.browse` cell, held by Management and the 2IC. **It gives screen privacy, not data privacy.** The `contacts` RLS was left alone on purpose, so the carer pickers on intake and rehome keep working for staff, which means **a staff login can still read every contact's phone and home address by sending the request by hand with their own token**, exactly like findings C9 and C10 (*the pages refuse both, so nothing on screen shows it*). The concern that started the item — someone looking up a carer's home address who should not — is therefore closed for what people can see and open for what the API will answer. **Option (c) is the data-level fix:** give the pickers a `picker_carers`-style fixed-column view (id, name, type, nothing private) in the `0136`/`0140`/`0143`/`0151` shape, move the pickers and embeds that read `contacts` for staff onto it (`src/lib/contacts/carers.ts`, `src/lib/placements/rehome.ts`, `src/app/residents/[id]/rehome/return/page.tsx`, `src/lib/adoption-updates/queries.ts`), then narrow `contacts_select_perm` to `contacts.browse` Read-or-better so staff read nothing off the table. `volunteer_contacts` (id, name, phone; `0155` re-granted it for the 2IC) and `vet_contacts` are the existing pattern. **Do not start it uninvited:** the Director chose A, and (c) is a view, a policy change and a pass over every reader. Raise it with Lutan when the next role-privacy batch is planned; the first thing to decide is whether staff should read *phone* off the picker (they cannot browse the page, so a staff member doing a pick-up still has to ask Management).

- [ ] **Machine-drafted translations (phase 2 of the free-text strategy).** The plumbing from 2026-09-21 (`0056_translations.sql`, `/management/translations`, `TranslationPanel`) has a human writing every draft; this adds a worker that fills `translations.text` with `status = 'draft'` so the managers check rather than write. Constraints agreed with the user: zero ongoing cost, open-weight models only, no PC on site. Shape: a Cloudflare Worker on a cron trigger in the same account the app deploys to (Workers AI binding, so no API key; Llama 3.x / Qwen / M2M100 all cover Thai and the free daily allowance dwarfs shelter volume), polling `translations where status = 'pending'` through Supabase REST with the service key as a Wrangler secret, writing back `text`, `engine` and `status = 'draft'`; a translate() function with one implementation per engine so a local Ollama box can replace it if one appears. Prompts differ by tier ("translate this adoption bio naturally; keep breed names" vs "translate this clinical note literally; keep drug names, doses, units and dates as written"). Then add the internal tier to `translatable_fields` (weight / vet-visit / prescription / intake notes, cause of death, behaviour notes) and attach the queueing trigger to those tables — shown to staff as a labelled machine translation under the original with **no** review step (the Weight tab finding from 2026-09-20). Before building, run twenty real bios and twenty real vet notes through two candidate models and let the two bilingual managers pick. Backfill in batches so the managers get ~20 Tier A drafts a day, not three hundred on Monday. **Status 2026-10-04: the worker is in, the item stays open** (`claude/translations-worker`, decisions `2026-10-04-translations-worker.md`): a cron on the existing Worker drafts pending rows through an engine seam (Workers AI, M2M100, or Ollama), `draft` only, with three-strike failure handling, a batch size and a 20-a-day reviewed-tier cap, no migration. **Still to do:** Lutan deploys to test and production and confirms Workers AI is on for the account (test plan, Left for manual verification); the two bilingual managers compare models on twenty real bios and twenty real vet notes (`TRANSLATE_MODEL` / `TRANSLATE_ENGINE`, drafts carry the model in `translations.engine`); then the schema PR that adds the `machine` tier fields and triggers.

- [x] **Fixed monthly outgoings in the cashflow forecast (salaries, rent and the like), as settings, not payroll.** Lutan, 2026-09-29, replacing the 2026-09-23 "Salaries: pay the people" item. **Payroll is out of scope for good:** no employee records, pay rates, pay periods, payslips or payment history, because per-person pay is the most sensitive data the app could hold, and the privacy risk grows again if the app ever serves several shelters. What the forecast does need is the shelter's regular fixed costs. So: a short list of **named fixed outgoings** managed by management/admin (e.g. "Salaries", "Rent", "Electricity", "Internet"): a label, a monthly amount in baht (`numeric(12,2)`, shown through `formatBaht`), an optional note, and an active flag. **Salaries is one total line** for the whole staff, never broken down by person. Nothing in it identifies anyone. **Where it lives:** a small table (schema PR first), readable and writable by admin and management only, with no anon or staff access (add it to `check-public-views.mjs`'s refusal checks). Not columns on `site_content`, whose `select using (true)` makes every column world-readable. Edited on a page under Management or Settings (whichever the Management/Settings split item settles; Management → Cashflow is the natural home). **Forecast:** `cashflow_forecast` gains one category per active outgoing, or one "Fixed outgoings" category with the lines as its breakdown. Either way it counts each active amount once per month in the window with `basis = 'priced'`, and the page's "no salaries" sentence comes off. Decide while building: a month that is only partly inside the window (pro-rata, or whole months only, matching how the page already groups by month), and whether an amount can change from a date (a start/end month per line is enough; no history table). Manual (Cashflow topic), both dictionaries, a releases line and a `docs/decisions.md` entry recording that payroll was deliberately dropped, and why. **Schema half done 2026-09-29 (0114, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-fixed-outgoings-not-payroll.md`):** `fixed_outgoings` (`label`, `monthly_amount numeric(12,2)`, `note`, `active`, plus optional `starts_on` / `ends_on` as the 1st of a month, `ends_on` inclusive, so a change of amount is one line ended and one started, no history table). Admin and management only via RLS, anon grants revoked, capped at 24 lines so it cannot hold one row per person; `check-public-views.mjs` refuses anon. **Feature half done 2026-09-29:** edit page at Management → Cashflow → Fixed outgoings, forecast category "Fixed outgoings" counted pro-rata by the day, manual, dictionaries and releases line (see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-fixed-outgoings-in-the-forecast.md`).
- [ ] **Dashboard follow-ups.** The first cut is deliberately rough; refine with the user once they've seen it against real data. Candidates: (a) **Export / print** — a "copy as text" or print stylesheet so the month section can go straight into the monthly report or a LINE message. (b) **Year view** — the same sections for a calendar year, and year-over-year comparison. (c) **Blood work location** — the report distinguishes "MW" from "vet visit" blood work; the dashboard splits on whether a test is linked to a vet appointment. If "MW" means a specific clinic rather than in-house, add a location/clinic field to `blood_tests` (or link every test to a visit) and split on that instead. (d) **Vet costs** — depends on the cost column in the Vet hub follow-ups item; spend per month belongs here. (e) **Time zone** — month boundaries for timestamp columns (`placement_history`, `vet_appointments`) are computed in the server's time zone (UTC on Cloudflare); a placement recorded before 07:00 on the 1st Thai time lands in the previous month. Decide on a shelter time zone constant and use it here and in the vet hub. (f) **Immunizations** — doses given this month and the stock forecast (decisions.md, open item 6) are the obvious next cards. (g) **Public "our impact"** — `public_shelter_stats` could grow lifetime totals (adopted since founding, sterilisations from project data) once the figures are trusted.
- [x] **Move frequency options to Admin, apart from medication management.** Done 2026-09-24: `/admin/frequencies` (Settings tile, manual topic, admin-gated actions), `/management/medications` is medications only. RLS left as it is on Lutan's call: `management_rw_frequency` is unchanged and there is no migration. See `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-24). Raised 2026-09-21: `/management/medications` currently carries two things — the medication list with its 7/30-day forecast (a manager's concern) and the "Frequency options" pick-list (label + schedule: times a day / every N days-weeks-months / as needed, 0044). The frequency list is system configuration, like immunization types and procedure types, and belongs under Admin. Split it out to `/admin/frequencies` (same shape as `/admin/immunization-types`; move `CreateFrequencyForm` / `FrequenciesTable` and the frequency actions, keep the shared `FrequencyScheduleFields` + `parseSchedule`), add it to the Admin nav, and leave `/management/medications` as medications only. RLS: `management_rw_frequency` (0043) would no longer be needed — decide whether to revoke it back to staff's read + insert twin, or leave management able to add one inline from the prescription form. The procedure-types page (`/admin/procedure-types`, done 2026-09-21) is the template — one page per list, same table shape.
- [ ] **Cashflow follow-ups.** Raised 2026-09-23 while building the forecast page, none of them blocking. (a) **Forecast against actuals** — once a few months have passed, show what a month was forecast to cost beside what it did, which is the only way to find out whether the flat vet estimate and the diet quantities are anywhere near right. (b) **A per-vet or per-reason vet average** to replace the single flat `site_content.vet_visit_estimate`, once `vet_appointments.cost` has enough invoice history to mean anything (see `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-23). (c) ~~**CSV export** for the monthly report~~ — done 2026-09-24: Download CSV beside the table, with a "not priced" count column per category so an exported 0 never hides a gap. (d) **The fixed windows have diverged:** Diets and Medications draw 7 and 30-day columns from `FIXED_FORECAST_DAYS` in `src/lib/management/forecast-window.ts`; Cashflow shows one window at a time and carries its own `CASHFLOW_FIXED_DAYS` of 30 and 90, because 7 days of outgoings is not a useful figure and changing the shared constant would have reshaped the other two pages mid-flight. Decide whether that is the permanent answer or whether the three pages should share one set. (e) **Immunization doses are counted once per resident per type per window** — `immunization_next_due` (0007) gives the next due date only, so a 90-day window does not see a second monthly dose. It understates rather than overstates, which is the safe direction, but a window longer than the shortest interval is wrong by construction and worth fixing if anyone forecasts a year. (f) **Salaries, rent and other fixed costs** are excluded only because nothing holds them; the "Fixed monthly outgoings" item above adds them as named monthly amounts (one total line for salaries, no payroll) and takes the "no salaries" sentence off the page when it lands.

- [x] **Recurring jobs: only offer people who can actually do the job.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. **Done 2026-09-27** (`claude/recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees`): vets are never assignable (Lutan: their work comes from vet appointments), and otherwise who can do a job is derived from its link path in `src/lib/recurring-jobs/eligibility.ts`, borrowing each page's own role predicate; the picker filters by it as the link changes, save and Hand over refuse anyone it excludes, and a job already with such a person is flagged on Management → Recurring jobs and loses its link on their My tasks. No migration. Pass 0 assigned a stocktake to a vet, because the assignee picker offers every user regardless of role. Pass 1 then tapped it and was thrown out of the app — the job's link goes to `/stocktake`, which vets cannot open. The job was impossible from the moment it was assigned, and nothing said so at the point where it could have been prevented. **Filter the assignee list by what the job's target screen needs**: a stocktake job offers only the roles `canStocktake` allows (admin, management, staff, volunteer); a maintenance job only those who can write maintenance; and so on. That means a recurring job has to know which capability it needs — today it knows a link, not a permission — so part of this is deciding how that is expressed (a capability on the job type, or derived from its target path). **Also handle the ones already assigned**, since a filter on the form does not fix a job created before it: either validate on save and on display, or show an assignee who can no longer do it as a problem on the Management page rather than leaving it to fail in someone's face. The bounce itself is the public-website item under Quick wins; this is the half that stops the situation arising.
- [x] **Cost per unit keeps only 2 decimals, so a per-gram price cannot be stored.** *Done 2026-10-08 (`claude/stock-pages-finish`, batch 73): the last part, the per-gram price display, ships here — each stock card shows the pack price beside the per-unit price (฿0.035 per g (฿35 per kg)). The production-order risk below is closed: **production checked 2026-10-08 at 0161 applied, no drift**, so nobody needs to re-check it before this release.* Found 2026-10-01 building units of measure: `diet_types.cost_per_unit` is `numeric(10,2)` and `medication.cost_per_unit` `numeric(12,2)`, so 35 baht a kg (0.035 a gram) would save as 0.04. The units feature refuses a price that rounds more than 1% off rather than saving it wrong, which blocks pricing a gram-based diet by the kilo. Widen both columns (schema PR, e.g. `numeric(12,4)`) and drop the refusal in `costPerBaseUnit` (`src/lib/units.ts`). **STATUS 2026-10-08: schema half done (0161 widens BOTH `diet_types.cost_per_unit` and `medication.cost_per_unit` to `numeric(12, 4)`, PR from `claude/order-and-units-schema`, see `docs/decisions/2026-10-08-order-and-unit-prices-schema.md`).** Still to do, from the updated `main` and only once 0161 is live on production: `costPerBaseUnit` (`src/lib/units.ts`) rounds to 4 places and drops the `tooCoarse` refusal, and the price inputs and `formatBaht` show a per-gram price with enough places to read (0.035, not 0.04). Code that saves 4 places into a 2-place column would round silently, which is why it waits. **STATUS 2026-10-08 (`claude/medications-diets-split`): most of the code half done.** `costPerBaseUnit` rounds to 4 places (still refuses only a price that 4 places cannot hold, e.g. 1 baht for 100 kg); the Medication stock and Diet stock cost inputs take 4 places (`parseUnitCost`) and show up to 4 (`formatUnitCost`, ฿0.0567 per g); the Units panel shows the per-base price to 4 places. **Left open:** the per-gram price *display* beside the pack price (batch 73), and the production order — **0161 must be on production before this release**, or 4-place prices save rounded to 2 there.
- [x] **Impact figures as a baseline plus a live count: enter a starting number and a date in Settings, and let the system add to it from then on (schema first).** **DECIDED 2026-10-07 (the Director and Lutan, decision brief q10):** asked for this instead of the brief's three options — *"can we have an option to add the baseline number in settings somewhere, then system calculates from that date onwards"*. **This is the right answer and it solves the general problem**, not just the website band: the shelter existed before the app, so every lifetime total is *a number nobody recorded* + *what the app has seen since*. **Shape:** a small table (one row per figure: key, label, label_th, baseline count, baseline date, who set it, when) edited on **Settings → Website** beside the other public-site fields; the public figure is **baseline + rows since baseline date**, computed, never stored. **Schema PR first**, as usual. **Which figures it serves on day one:** *animals rehomed* (adoptions in `placement_history` since the date, plus the baseline for everything before the app) and *sterilisations in local villages* **if** a project or a count field records them — check, and if nothing does, that figure is baseline-only until the item below exists. **Three things to get right:** (1) **say it is approximate where it is** — a hand-entered baseline is an estimate and the page should not imply a precise audit; (2) **both dictionaries**, since the labels are public; (3) **do not let a baseline be edited casually** — it changes a public claim, so Settings → Website is the right home and the change should be in the audit log. Replaces the "decide the source of each" half of the Impact band item. **Status 2026-10-07: schema half done (0156 `impact_baselines` + `public_impact_figures`, `claude/baseline-impact-schema`); app half open: Settings → Website editor, the "approximate" wording, both dictionaries (batch 64). Nothing records village sterilisations, so that figure is baseline-only. On dev only Admin holds `website.content`; Management needs the cell for the item's "→ Management" to be true.** **Status 2026-10-07 (later): app half done too** — Settings → Website → Home page editor, "About" wording on the band, both dictionaries, audit via the table trigger (`claude/baseline-impact-figures`). Admin-only until the Director rules on Management holding `website.content`. → *Management*

## Assistant

- [x] **Assistant demo: show it to the Director and record what they said.** The sketch is built and merged (2026-09-22): `/assistant` in the left nav with a *demo* badge, admin/management/staff, keyword parser in `src/lib/assistant/demo-parser.ts`, writes via the real move helper and vet-visit RPC after a preview card — see `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-22, "hard-codes the understanding, not the doing") for what was deliberately left out of the sketch (slide-over, `dryRun`, env flag, audit table). **What remains:** walk the Director through "move Panda to B1" and "book a vet visit for Panda tomorrow 10am", then record what they said in `docs/decisions.md` and let that steer the full assistant item below. Original brief, kept for the real build — raised 2026-09-22: No LLM, no API key: a chat panel whose "brain" is a small keyword matcher that recognises two requests and walks each through the real flow — (1) "move <resident> to <enclosure>" and (2) "book a vet visit for <resident>" — everything else gets a polite "I can only help with moves and vet visits in this demo." **Build the reusable parts properly, hard-code only the brain:** `src/components/assistant/AssistantPanel.tsx` (slide-over from a header button, message list, input, typing indicator, works on the phone), `src/lib/assistant/types.ts` (the message / tool-call / request-details / confirm-card shapes the real assistant will use), `PreviewCard` ("Move Panda (R-0042) from A3 → B1 today" with Confirm / Change / Cancel) and `RequestDetailsModal` (a small form built from a field spec — used when the resident name is ambiguous or the enclosure / vet / date is missing). The scripted brain lives in `src/lib/assistant/demo-brain.ts` behind the same `respond(messages) → AssistantTurn` interface the LLM adapter will implement later, so swapping it out is one import. **It should do real work:** resident and enclosure lookups hit `resident_list_view` / enclosures for real (so the Director can try it on Panda), the move preview calls `src/lib/placements/move.ts` with `dryRun` and, on Confirm, for real; the vet visit preview pre-fills date / vet / reason and on Confirm inserts the `vet_appointments` row the same way `/vet-visits/new` does. Both paths reuse the existing capacity warning and the existing confirm wording, and the resulting rows carry a "via assistant (demo)" note. **Guard rails:** behind a `NEXT_PUBLIC_ASSISTANT_DEMO=1` env flag so it is on for the customer showing and off otherwise; admin/management/staff only; English and Thai trigger phrases (a handful of each — the matcher is a list of regexes, not NLP). **Not in scope:** anything the two scripts don't cover, memory between messages, streaming. Afterwards, record what the customer said in `docs/decisions.md` and let that steer the full item.
- [ ] **Assistant v1: grow the keyword assistant into the production tool until an LLM takes over.** Customer direction 2026-09-22: the Director loved the demo, so the scripted assistant becomes version 1 in production, and the LLM item below becomes version 2 — same panel, same preview cards, a smarter brain later. First, close the demo item above: write the Director's reaction into `docs/decisions.md` and tick it. **Promote, don't rewrite:** keep `src/lib/assistant/demo-parser.ts`'s shape (a request → a `Draft` with nulls for whatever the text didn't say → an editable preview card → Confirm runs the app's own helper) but split it into one small parser per intent behind a `parse(text, context) → Draft | null` list tried in order, so each new function is a file, not a bigger regex. Drop the *demo* badge from `NavLinks.tsx` and `page.tsx` once at least the first batch below is in. **Functions to add, in this order** (each reuses an existing server action or RPC under the caller's session — never a new write path — and stamps notes with `via the assistant — "<request>"` as the demo does): (1) **hospital**: "send Panda to the vet hospital" / "Panda is back from hospital" → `sendToHospital` and the return path in `src/app/residents/[id]/hospital/actions.ts`; (2) **weight**: "Panda weighs 12.4 kg" / "log weight 12.4 for Panda" → the action in `src/app/weight/new/actions.ts`; (3) **death**: "Bella died last night, lung problems" → `recordDeath` in `residents/[id]/deceased/actions.ts` with the `ConfirmDialog` wording the deceased page uses, date left blank when "last night" straddles midnight; (4) **foster / adopt / return**: "Panda goes to foster with Khun Noi" / "Panda has been adopted by …" / "Panda came back from foster" → `rehome` in `residents/[id]/rehome/actions.ts`, carer matched against the same option list the form uses (`loadCarerOptions`); (5) **immunization**: "Panda had rabies today" → the `/immunizations/new` path; (6) **read-only lookups** that need no confirm: "where is Panda", "who is in B1", "what is due this week" — answered inline from `resident_list_view`, the enclosure page's query and the due-soon queries the dashboard already runs. **Thai** trigger phrases for every intent alongside English, as the demo has for move and vet. **Ambiguity:** when a name matches more than one resident the card offers the candidates with code, enclosure and photo — the demo's "pick one" count becomes a real picker. **Where it lives:** keep `/assistant` as the full page and add the slide-over `AssistantPanel` from a header button so it can be used from any screen, including the phone; the message list and input components should be the ones the LLM version will keep. **Roles:** admin / management / staff can write; volunteers get the lookups only. **Audit:** now that it is production, add the `assistant_actions` table from the LLM item (who, when, request text, intent, draft, result) in its own schema PR first — it is the eval corpus the LLM version will be measured against, so start collecting from day one. **Manual:** a new topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts` listing exactly what the assistant understands, phrased as the sentences to type — for a keyword assistant the manual *is* the grammar. Ship in batches (hospital + weight + lookups first, then death + rehome, then immunization) so each is a small PR and the Director sees progress; each batch is one workstream. Not in scope: any model, memory between messages, streaming — that is the item below, which should now be read as version 2 and reuse everything built here. **Progress: batch 1 shipped 2026-09-23** (hospital send + return, weight, and the read-only lookups — plus the slide-over, the audit writes, the manual topic and the demo-badge removal). `demo-parser.ts` is now one parser per intent under `src/lib/assistant/intents/`, tried in the order `parse.ts` lists, so the next batch is a new file there plus a card and an action. **Next: batch 2, death + rehome**; then batch 3, immunization.
- [x] **Doctor name through the assistant's vet booking.** **Done 2026-10-02** (`claude/doctor-name-assistant`; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-assistant-doctor-name.md`): the premise held — the form still passes free text to `schedule_bulk_appointments(p_doctor_name)` and the database links it to the clinic's roster, so the assistant does the same. Original: Raised 2026-09-24 when the doctor-name feature shipped: `/vet-visits/new` and Edit take an optional doctor (`vet_appointments.doctor_name`, 0074), but the assistant's vet intent (`src/lib/assistant/intents/vet.ts`) strips "dr", "dr." and "doctor" as `VET_STOPWORDS` when matching the vet, so "book a vet visit for Panda with Dr Somchai" books the visit and drops the name. Accept it, but don't ask for it: parse a trailing "with Dr X" / "หมอ X" into the draft, show it on the preview card as an editable, optional field, and write it the way `bookVetVisit` does (see `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-24). If a schema PR touches vet visits first, fold a `p_doctor_name` parameter into `schedule_bulk_appointments` then, so both callers write the doctor in the RPC's own transaction.
- [ ] **A volunteer turned away from an assistant write leaves no audit row.** Noticed building batch 1 (2026-09-23). Volunteers get the read-only lookups; if one types a write request the browser answers "that one is for staff and management" and nothing reaches the server, so `assistant_actions` (0070) never sees it. Those are worth having — they say what the people with the least access actually want the assistant to do, which is exactly what should steer whether volunteers get more of it. The status enum is created in `0070_assistant_actions.sql` as `('confirmed','cancelled','unmatched')` and none of them is true of a refusal, so a `refused` value has to be added before the client can record it. **Schema half in PR (2026-10-02, branch `claude/schema-assistant-refused`): `0130_assistant_action_refused.sql` adds `refused` to the enum.** It is one file, not two — the two-file rule (`docs/decisions.md`, PR #50) is `add value` plus a *use* of it in SQL, and nothing in SQL uses it (no check, view, function or policy reads `status`). Still to do, as its own feature branch from `main` once 0130 is applied: the client records a refusal — add `"refused"` to `AssistantActionStatus` in `src/lib/assistant/audit.ts` and have `AssistantConversation.tsx` send a row when it turns a volunteer away. (The old constraint that `claude/cashflow-schema` held the single migration slot is long gone; that branch merged.) Small, and not urgent — but the longer it waits the bigger the hole in the corpus. **Update 2026-10-06 (`perm-convert-settings`, `0150`):** the table policies now ask `assistant.record` on the caller's own row, so this is a one-policy change: insert asks `assistant.record` **or** (`assistant.ask`, `status = 'refused'`, own row). The volunteer holds `assistant.ask` and not `record`, so it is refused today. It widens who can write the table, so it is the item's own decision.
- [x] **`fetchAssistantContext()` has no role check of its own.** Noticed 2026-09-25 building `assistant-nav-link`. The server action in `src/app/assistant/actions.ts` calls `loadAssistantContext()` for whoever asks, so a vet — who has no Assistant button and whose `/assistant` page shows only "can't use the assistant" — can still call it directly and get the resident list, vets and enclosure options, limited only by RLS. Writes and lookups each re-check the role; this one does not. Return an empty context with `notAuthorized` unless `canUseAssistant(role)`, the way `lookups.ts` does, and check whether `page.tsx` should skip loading for the same roles.
- [ ] **In-app chat assistant that does things, not just answers.** Raised 2026-09-22: the Director's biggest pain point is time, and the ask is an experience like working with Claude here — type "move Panda from A3 to B1" or "Bella died last night, lung problems" and the system makes the changes, popping a modal only when it needs something it can't infer. **Hosting (user's direction 2026-09-22):** the production assistant should run on an LLM the shelter hosts itself — on the Raspberry Pi 5 at home or a cloud box — not on a metered Claude subscription. Claude is for the prototype and for teaching: build against the Claude API first because it gets tool use right with the least effort, and while doing so record every real exchange (request, tool calls, corrections) into an eval set that the self-hosted model is then measured against and, if needed, fine-tuned on. To keep that swap possible the model layer must be **provider-neutral from the first commit**: one `src/lib/assistant/llm.ts` adapter that takes messages + tool definitions and returns text / tool calls, with a Claude implementation (`@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `claude-opus-5`, streaming, adaptive thinking; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` as a Cloudflare secret) and an OpenAI-compatible implementation for whatever the shelter hosts (Ollama / llama.cpp / vLLM all speak that shape), selected by env var. Nothing outside the adapter may import a vendor SDK. **Honest sizing note for the Pi:** a Pi 5 with 8–16 GB runs a 3–8B quantised model at a few tokens/second; that is workable for a short reply but tool-calling accuracy in mixed Thai/English at that size is marginal and each turn will take tens of seconds. Expect the self-hosted target to be a small cloud GPU (or a hosted open-weight endpoint) and treat the Pi as the stretch goal — measure both against the eval set before deciding. **Shape:** a chat panel (slide-over from a header button, also a full page `/assistant`; works on the phone) that sends the conversation to a route handler which calls the adapter with a **fixed toolset that wraps the app's existing server-side operations** — never raw SQL. First tools: find resident (by name/Thai name/code, returning candidates), find enclosure, move resident (`src/lib/placements/move.ts`), send to / return from hospital (`hospital.ts`), record death (`deceased.ts`, with cause), foster/adopt/return (`rehome.ts`), log weight, log vet visit, log immunization, add prescription, and read-only lookups (resident hub summary, who is in an enclosure, what's due). Each tool runs under the caller's own Supabase session, so RLS and the role checks apply exactly as in the UI and the assistant can never do more than the person could by clicking. **Confirmation:** every write is a two-step — the model calls the tool with `dryRun: true`, the UI shows a preview card ("Move Panda (R-0042) from A3 → B1 today, no notes") with Confirm / Change / Cancel, and only a Confirm re-invokes the tool for real; irreversible ones (death) use the existing `ConfirmDialog` wording. This matters doubly for a smaller self-hosted model: the confirm step is what makes a mis-parsed request harmless. **Missing information:** a `request_details` tool the model calls when a required field is unknown (which of two residents named Panda; date of death when "last night" is ambiguous across midnight; the enclosure when only a zone was named) that renders a small modal form built from the tool's field spec; the answers go back as the tool result. **Ambiguity:** resident lookup always returns candidates with code, enclosure and photo so the model can ask "the Panda in A3 or the one in Foster?" instead of guessing. **Audit:** log each assistant conversation and every executed tool call (who, when, input, result, which model served it) to a new `assistant_actions` table, and stamp the resulting rows' notes with "via assistant" so the hub history shows it — this table doubles as the eval/training corpus. **Scope of first cut:** the placement and medical tools above, English + Thai input, admin/management/staff only (volunteers get read-only tools), no memory across conversations, Claude behind the adapter. **Second cut:** stand up the self-hosted model, run the eval set, switch the env var when it clears the bar. **Later:** voice input on the phone, "what happened this week" summaries from the management dashboard queries, drafting the Facebook post for a project. **Decide with the user before building:** whether low-risk logs (weight, immunization) may skip the confirm to keep it fast; the accuracy bar the self-hosted model must reach on the eval set before it replaces Claude; whether conversations are retained beyond the audit rows.

## Auth

- [x] **Name card taps under the new permissions: a signed-in person must never see less than a visitor, and what they see beyond that follows their role.** Lutan, 2026-10-07: with permissions now set per role (the roles build), does tapping a resident's name card still work? The intended rule: **signed in → the resident's page with medical; anonymous → the resident with general information only.** **What happens today (checked in the code, 2026-10-07):** `/r/<code>` (`src/app/r/[code]/page.tsx`) shows the **public card** to anyone signed out or signed in without app access (`public_viewer`), which is right. Anyone with app access is sent to `/residents/<id>`, and that page now depends on the role: (a) **Admin, Management, Staff:** the full page with medical. Right. (b) **The 2IC, Head of Medical, Head of Maintenance and volunteers:** these roles borrow `volunteer` underneath (`legacy_role`, `0136`, `0141`, `0143`), and a role without `resident.record` gets **`ResidentWhoAndWhere`** (`0134`): who the resident is and where it lives, **no medical, and less than the public card shows a stranger** (no bio, temperament, age or adoption details). So for them a tap is *worse* than not being signed in. (c) **A vet or doctor login:** the page is limited to residents their clinics treat (`0108`), so a card for any other resident gives **404**, where a visitor would have seen the public card. **The fix:** (1) **Never less than the public card.** When a signed-in person may not open the full record, `/r/<code>` shows them the public card's information (the same `public_resident_cards` view anyone could read) plus whatever their role adds, instead of the who-and-where page or a 404. The same rule on `/residents/<id>` itself. (2) **Medical follows permissions, not just being signed in.** Lutan's "signed in sees medical" is right for every role that can read medical records, but it is now a per-role choice: in the Director's draft (in Next up), the two Heads and volunteers have no "view residents and their records" tick, so they would **not** see medical; the Head of Medical's job is the medication list, not the record. **Ask Lutan whether that is what he and the Director want**, or whether everyone signed in should read a resident's medical history from a card, in which case it becomes a tick for those roles in her matrix, not a special case in the code. Record the answer in `docs/decisions/`. (3) **Keep the jobs landing** from the name card item (Medical records): a tap with a job waiting goes to that job, and a plain tap shows the resident with buttons for the person's own jobs. (4) **Make it provable:** add the card to the permissions parity check or a `scripts/check-*.mjs`: for each role (signed out, `public_viewer`, Admin, Management, Staff, 2IC, both Heads, volunteer, vet inside and outside their clinic), tap a card and assert it lands on the public card, the public card plus extras, or the full record, and never on no-access or 404. Both dictionaries, Manual ("Scanning a name card"), a releases line.
- [x] **Show "Signed in as Lutan · Admin" in the header instead of the email address — READY, unparked 2026-10-07.** Lutan, 2026-10-07. The header (`src/app/AppHeader.tsx`, `{user.email}`) shows the signed-in email; a person's name is friendlier, especially for shelter staff on a phone. **Unparked by Lutan, 2026-10-07:** he tests with three different `lutan.bennett…` email addresses, which names alone would all show as "Lutan". Point (4) below settles it, so this can be built now: **show the name with the role** ("Lutan · Admin", "Lutan · Vet"), and the admin-editable name lets a test account be named distinctly as well. **What exists:** a name is already worked out elsewhere: `display_name` in `private.app_users` (0126) is the Google account's `full_name` / `name`, and `appUserLabel()` (`src/lib/auth/app-users.ts`) falls back from it to the email, as the maintenance and recurring-job pickers do. Email-and-password accounts have no name at all, so the header would still show their email. **Build, when it is time:** (1) **a name every account can have:** a *Name* field set by an admin on Settings → Security (create user and the users table), and by the person themselves beside Change password, stored in `user_metadata` or a small profile table (a schema PR if a table), with Google's name only as the starting value; (2) the header shows that name, with the email in the account menu (tap the name to see which account it is, and Sign out); (3) **the same name everywhere one is shown** (maintenance assignees, recurring jobs, Recent changes, a doctor's linked login), through `appUserLabel()`, so it changes in one place; (4) **telling accounts apart** (Lutan's actual problem, and the shelter's later, e.g. two staff called Noi): show the role beside the name ("Lutan · Admin"), or use distinct names for test accounts ("Lutan (test vet)"), which the admin-editable name allows; **decided 2026-10-07: build the name · role form**, so it ships now. Both dictionaries, Manual (account menu, Security), a releases line.
- [x] **Decide who may see a Shelter Friend's card: today staff see it only because the contacts pages show it to everyone who reads contacts.** Found by `perm-convert-people` (`0147`, 2026-10-06). The paper gives `friends.manage` to Management only, and known tightening N3 says staff should not read `shelter_friends`; but `/contacts` and `/contacts/[id]` show the badge, the chip and a read-only card to every role that reads contacts, so converting the table strictly would have removed them from staff on screen. `0147` kept staff's read (the select policy is `friends.manage` OR the full address-book read), so **N3 is still open on purpose**. Pick one: staff keep seeing friends (then give them a Read cell and delete N3), or the app stops showing staff the card (then drop the second half of `shelter_friends_select_perm` and N3 closes). Small either way; the choice is Lutan's or the Director's. **DECIDED 2026-10-07 (Lutan, decision brief part 2 q12): **STAFF KEEP SEEING IT — and N3 goes.** Option A, with a reason that settles the question rather than just choosing: Lutan — *"staff can see it on the website anyway as anonymous, so no need to hide this."* **That is correct and it makes the tightening pointless:** the Shelter Friends band is public (the homepage build, 2026-09-26), so a staff member who cannot see a Friend's card in the app can read the same thing by opening the website signed out. Hiding it would have cost work to achieve nothing. **So:** give staff a **Read cell** for `shelter_friends` and **delete known tightening N3**, which is the first branch this item named. `0147` already kept staff's read on purpose, so the policy needs no widening — only the cell, so the policy can stop carrying the address-book read as its second half. **Small, and it closes a known entry on the parity board.**
- [x] **A role the Director's draft gives contacts to has no way to read them: the 2IC holds `contacts.directory` Edit and `contacts.add` and reaches the table through nothing.** Found by `perm-convert-people` (`0147`, 2026-10-06). The draft (`#380`) ticks "view and manage contacts" and "add a contact" for the 2IC, but her `scope_contacts` is `name_type` on the volunteer floor, and `0126` took that floor off the table in favour of views. `contacts` now asks `sees_all_contacts()` (`0147`), so the cells do nothing today, exactly as before. Either give the 2IC a view that fits what she may see (`name_type`, or a price-free `name_phone`, as `volunteer_contacts` does) and policies for it, or change her scope to `full` and say why. Also: a role holding `contacts.add` alone can insert but not read the row back, so `insert … select` fails for it; every real holder also holds the directory cell. **CLOSED by `0155_director_answers_schema.sql` (#412, 2026-10-07) — verified and ticked 2026-10-07 by `/plan-day`.** The item offered two fixes; `0155` took the second: **`update roles set scope_contacts = 'name_phone' where key = 'second_in_command';`** (line 105), with the reason in the file's own comments — *"name_phone is the scope the pages already honour for it (volunteer_contacts)"*. It also adds the new cell **`contacts.browse`** at level 2 for `management` and `second_in_command` (lines 93-99), and `/contacts` and `/contacts/[id]` ask that instead. So the 2IC now reaches the directory and sees name and phone, no address. **The item was found by `perm-convert-people` on 2026-10-06 and closed by the Director's answers the next day** — nobody connected the two, because the stream that shipped `0155` ticked its own items and not this one. **Still true and worth keeping:** the secondary note that a role holding `contacts.add` alone could insert but not read the row back — benign, because every real holder also holds the directory cell. Re-file it if a role ever holds `contacts.add` alone.

- [x] **The roles the shelter actually has, from the Director (2026-10-03): fit the system to them.** Lutan, 2026-10-03, after talking to the Director. This is the shelter's own description of who does what, and it should become the role set and the default permissions. **There are no PCs on site** (Lutan, 2026-10-03): the only person with a computer is the Director, at home. **So every role except the Director works on a phone, and only on a phone.** That changes several earlier assumptions: (i) the 2IC's stocktake, ordering, deliveries and Purchasing pages, and the Head of Maintenance's and Head of Medical's screens, must be complete on a phone, not merely viewable; (ii) the "Admin on mobile" decision (`docs/decisions/2026-09-24-admin-on-mobile-which-settings-and-management-pages-belong.md`) marked Management pages desktop-only with a "Best on a larger screen" notice, which is right only for what the Director alone does, so revisit every desktop-only row against the roles below and make anything a non-Director role needs phone-first; (iii) the desktop stocktake sheet is the Director's tool at most, and the card-by-card phone stocktake becomes the main one; (iv) the Mobile responsiveness sweep (Mobile) is now on the critical path for everyone but the Director, which Lutan should weigh when he next schedules it; (v) the acceptance matrix and the role walkthrough test these roles at 375 px, with desktop only for the Director. **The six roles:** (1) **Director → Admin.** Manages the website content and the projects pages, and books vet visits, among everything else. (2) **2IC / Manager → Management.** Management activities, exactly which ones to be settled by the Settings/Management split (see "Review which pages belong under Management and which under Settings", Admin); this explicitly **includes stocktakes and ordering medicine and food** (Purchasing, Stock deliveries, the stocktake). **This person is not comfortable with computers, so everything they use must be built for that:** one task per screen, wizard-style steps like intake and the Shelter Friend wizard, big buttons with an icon and a word, plain Thai first, no tables to edit in place, a confirmation that says in words what will happen, nothing that loses work if they go back, and the phone card-by-card stocktake from the medication-label item rather than the desktop sheet. Test each of their screens by watching them (or someone like them) use it, not by reading it. (3) **Head of Maintenance → a new Maintenance role.** Manages maintenance tasks: creates, assigns, progresses and completes jobs. (4) **Head of Medical → a new Medical role.** Manages giving medication to the residents. **Status 2026-10-03: the medication-round part of this item is superseded, not done.** The shelter ruled that medication given is not recorded; the Head of Medical gets a read-only list instead (shipped as the medication list, `docs/roles-and-permissions.md` §14, `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-lanna-roles-lutans-answers.md`). Do not file the round again as a gap. **Note there is no feature for this yet:** prescriptions record what *should* be given (dose, frequency, dates), but nothing records that a dose *was* given, by whom and when. Only immunizations have `date_administered` / `administered_by` (0001). So this role needs a **medication round** feature: today's doses due, grouped by zone or enclosure (the "giving medications to a zone of animals" idea from My dashboard), ticked off as given, skipped or refused with a reason, and a history per resident; schema PR first. (5) **Volunteers → read-only.** They can view the **map** and the **resident hub**, and nothing else changes data. **That is a narrowing of today's volunteer role**, which can add photos, move residents between enclosures (a `ChangeEnclosure` placement), run the stocktake and record deliveries (`canStocktake`, `canRecordDelivery`), and reads contacts' names and phones (0126). Confirm with Lutan that all of that goes, then remove it in RLS as well as the screens, since a volunteer's JWT is the real boundary. "The map" is the facility map, which is a prototype today (`/enclosures/map-prototype`, see the Facility item), so either that item is built first or volunteers get the enclosures list meanwhile. (6) **Vets → the doctor login.** Can add an appointment for a resident and add test results and the rest of the clinical record. That is roughly what the `vet` role does now, for residents its clinics treat (0108, 0110); the rename to Doctor and the open question of which residents a doctor login sees are in the parked clinics item (Medical records). **What this changes elsewhere:** (a) **There is no plain "Staff" in the list.** Ask whether `staff` still exists (other employees, kennel hands) or whether every employee is one of the heads above; if it goes, decide what today's staff logins become. (b) The **Senior Staff** item (Auth) is probably answered by this: Maintenance and Medical are the "staff with elevated control over some elements". Reconcile the two and close or rewrite Senior Staff. (c) The **per-shelter roles and permissions** item (Architecture) gets its first real input: these six are Lanna's default role set, and the matrix's activity list should be checked against them line by line. If the matrix is built first, Maintenance and Medical are configured roles; if not, they are two new `app_role` values, each a two-file enum migration. Decide which with Lutan rather than doing both. (d) The role walkthrough (`docs/role-walkthrough.md`) and the acceptance matrix (Documentation) gain a pass per role. **Deliverable first:** a one-page table, role by activity (Edit / Read / None), drafted from this list and the current role summaries in `src/lib/manual/en.ts`, with every gap marked, agreed with Lutan and the Director. Then the schema (new roles or matrix entries, the narrowed volunteer, the medication round), then the screens, the 2IC's first. Manual, both dictionaries, a decision file and a releases line. **Status 2026-10-04: F2 residents area swept in PR #336** (placements, register/edit, adoption news, microchip, resident photos, translations button; manual topics and matrix rows name activities). Still to sweep: medical (`canArchiveMedical`, medical sections), and the rest (contacts visibility, maintenance, projects, assistant, management, settings, `assertPhotoWriteAccess` for non-resident photo routes, `/residents/new` has no guard). **TICKED 2026-10-07 (staleness sweep). Volunteer narrowed `0134` (R1). Management needed no row (`docs/decisions/2026-10-05-management-role.md`, R5) and Admin needed nothing built (`docs/decisions/2026-10-07-admin-role.md` §1, R6). The 2IC's contacts scope is `0155`. **Nothing here is lost by the tick; what is left is owned by open items:** applying the agreed draft to **production** (*Apply the Director's first draft*, which says so), and watching each person use their role (*Roles build, then one role at a time* and *the maintenance board on a phone*). So Lanna needs three roles the system lacks (2IC, Maintenance, Medical), and Staff is unused there. **Also recorded:** `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-no-pcs-on-site-supersedes-admin-on-mobile.md`. **The fork is decided (Lutan, 2026-10-03, `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-configured-roles-not-enum-values.md`): configured roles, not enum values.** So Maintenance, Medical and the 2IC are rows in a `roles` table and no enum migration is written. **L2 answered by Lutan, 2026-10-04: one login for the Director, home follows the device.** That is §8’s recommended option in full — she is Admin, Admin already includes everything Management can do (§6), a phone lands on the Management home and a larger screen on Settings, and a switch at the top opens the other. **Nothing new in the data model**, so `home-screens` needs no schema. **That was the last thing holding `home-screens`, which is F2’s final piece** (§15), so once the medical and rest sweeps land the foundation is complete and the roles themselves can start. **Still proposed, awaiting Lutan (paper §17, L3 to L12):** `0132` as the permission tables; the order of the roles (Volunteer, Medical, Maintenance, 2IC, Management, Admin); and the order of the roles. **L3 answered by Lutan, 2026-10-04: there are no volunteer logins at all, on test or production.** That is the answer that makes R1 simple rather than delicate — §15 asked it because it *"decides whether R1 can simply be applied or has to be announced first"*, and with nobody holding the role the narrowing can just be applied: no announcement, no transition, nobody loses access they were using. It also means the three new roles can borrow the volunteer’s database rights without disturbing anyone while that is set up. **Two things follow that are worth saying out loud:** the volunteer role has never been exercised by a real user, so its acceptance pass will be against a test account rather than someone’s habits; and `volunteer-read-only`’s release note is about what a future volunteer will see, not a change anyone experiences — do not write it as though rights were taken away from people. **R1 is now unblocked, and `volunteer-schema` is a migration, so it takes the next free number ahead of `perm-convert-medical`: §12 puts R1 before R5, and R1 unblocks three roles while the convert unblocks none.** **Small points left open (P1 to P4):** whether the 2IC records deliveries, whether the Head of Maintenance sets recurring tasks up, how much of a resident the 2IC and the Head of Maintenance see. **Found on the way and filed on the `backlog` branch:** Management and the microchip (Auth); reads wider than the screens (Security). **Reported, not reopened:** the Mobile responsiveness sweep is now on the critical path for every role but Admin. **Next:** the paper's §15 names the pieces by role; `permissions-schema` (the permission tables) is the first schema piece now that the fork is decided, and the medication list and the phone maintenance board need nothing else and can start at once.
