Recurring-job eligibility keeps two stock role lists until a `role_can()` function exists (filed). Nothing else converted; not ticked. Pattern: `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-permissions-catalogue.md`. Next: the three sweeps, `home-screens`.
  **Status 2026-10-04 (`claude/home-screens`, PR to be linked): F2 IS NOW COMPLETE. Nothing else stands between the foundation and building the roles; not ticked.** The three sweeps (#336, #340, #342) moved every role list onto `can()`; this is the last piece: `/home`, the landing that follows the device, and a tile home per role built from the route registry (`src/lib/home/`, no migration, no policy). Admin (the Director, one login) lands on the Management home on a phone and on Settings on anything larger, and a switch at the top of both opens any other role's home, refused server-side for everyone but Admin. **What the switch lists today:** Settings, then Management, Staff, Vet and Volunteer, because those are the live roles; the 2IC, Maintenance and Medical appear the day their rows exist. **Not built, and not this item's:** the Director's curated daytime screen (`management-phone-home` and `recurring-jobs-phone`: Management's home is today every page Management can open, 19 tiles, not the whiteboard's five) and the volunteer's "Residents and Enclosures" home (R1). `docs/decisions/2026-10-04-home-screens.md` records the device rule and its tablet case.
- [ ] **A Senior Staff role: staff with elevated control over some parts of the system — decide what it can do, then schema PR, then build.** Lutan, 2026-10-03. Some staff members should be able to do more than a normal staff member can, without being made Management or Admin. **Today's roles** are the `app_role` enum: `admin`, `management`, `staff`, `vet`, `volunteer`, `public_viewer` (0001, 0038, 0085). Management already sits above staff, but it also gets the Management section (cashflow, contacts admin, recurring jobs and so on), which is more than "a trusted staff member" needs. **First, a decision with Lutan (the deliverable before any code):** a table of what Senior Staff can do that Staff cannot, and anything Management can do that Senior Staff still cannot. Candidates to put to him, drawn from what is gated above `staff` today (check each against the code before the table is final): withdraw a death recorded in error (admin only today); hand over or reassign a recurring job for a sick week; approve stocktake and delivery corrections; edit the reference lists (medications, diets, frequencies, types); publish residents and projects to the website; create or delete maintenance jobs for others; approve access requests (probably not: that is Settings → Security and needs 2-step). Also settle: **is Senior Staff "staff plus a list", or a rung between staff and management?** The first is easier to reason about and keeps Management's financial pages out of reach; the second means every `in ('admin','management')` check gains it. **How it is built matters more than the list:** role checks are scattered. In the app they are named predicates (`isShelterRole`, `canManage`, `canWriteMaintenance`, `canStocktake`, `canRecordDelivery`, `canArchiveMedical`, `canWriteProjects`, `canReadRecurringJobs`, `canDoJob`, `canUseAssistant`, among others), and in the database about 26 migrations name `'management'` in RLS policies and `security definer` guards. Every existing `staff` check must also let `senior_staff` through, or a Senior Staff member could do *less* than staff, so sweep for `'staff'` in both before choosing where to add the new rights. Prefer a single "role includes" helper in SQL and TypeScript (e.g. `role_at_least('staff')` or a capability function) over adding the new value to dozens of lists by hand, and decide whether this is the moment to introduce it. **Schema PR first, and it is two files:** `alter type app_role add value 'senior_staff'` alone in its own migration (an added enum value cannot be used in the same transaction, as 0085 records), then the policy and function changes in the next. Read `0085_public_viewer_role.sql` and the "enum migrations need two files" note in CLAUDE.md before numbering. **Then the feature:** the role in the Settings → Security picker and the `/admin/security` actions; the nav and route guards; `roleSummary` and per-topic `roles` in the manual (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`), both dictionaries, `/releases` role filtering (`isForRole`), the recurring-job eligibility rules, the assistant's role checks, the README roles table, and the role walkthrough (`docs/role-walkthrough.md`) gains a Senior Staff pass with its "must not" lines. A decision file in `docs/decisions/` and a releases line. **Test:** a Senior Staff login can do each thing in the agreed table, and is refused everything Management has that was not granted (pages and a direct Data API call with its own JWT), and a plain staff login is unchanged.
  **Status 2026-10-03: the roles paper recommends closing this as answered** (`docs/roles-and-permissions.md` §16, question L5). Under the matrix every role is "staff plus a list" and there are no rungs; each candidate power above is already a cell in the catalogue; and the 2IC, Maintenance and Medical are the Director's actual roles with more control over some elements. Not closed here: that is Lutan's call. If someone still fits none of the roles once the Director has answered, Senior Staff becomes the first role Lanna adds itself, not an enum value.
- [x] **Management cannot record a microchip, though the manual says it can — decide which is right, then fix the other.** Found 2026-10-03 by the roles-and-permissions audit (`docs/roles-and-permissions.md` §3 B, question L6). The manual's *Scanning a microchip* topic is tagged admin, management, staff and vet, so the acceptance matrix tells a Management tester to record a chip; `set_resident_microchip()` (0116) and `MICROCHIP_WRITE_ROLES` (`src/lib/residents/microchip.ts`) allow admin, staff and vet only, and Management is refused. The same shape as the blood-test fault `0131` fixed. The paper recommends giving it to Management, who can edit everything else about a resident: a re-runnable migration replacing the function's role list, the TypeScript set, and a line in `scripts/check-resident-microchip.mjs`. If Lutan rules the other way, the fix is the manual's `roles` tag and nothing else. **Also from the audit, for the acceptance matrix:** its row *Edit a job, record its cost, add before and after photos* says a volunteer must not, but the upload route and `volunteer_rw_maintenance_photos` let a volunteer add and remove a job's photos (moot if volunteers become read-only). **[done 2026-10-07 as `claude/director-answers-schema`, `0155`: `set_resident_microchip()` now asks `has_permission('resident.microchip')` and Management holds the cell; the TypeScript set the item names is long gone (the app already asked the cell), so the change was the cell and the function; decisions/…q8.]** **DECIDED 2026-10-07 (the Director, with Lutan, from the decision brief q8): **Management CAN record a microchip.** Option A, which is also what the paper recommended. So the handbook was right and the system was wrong. **The fix is the one this item already describes:** a re-runnable migration replacing `set_resident_microchip()`'s role list, the `MICROCHIP_WRITE_ROLES` TypeScript set (`src/lib/residents/microchip.ts`), and a line in `scripts/check-resident-microchip.mjs`. **Buildable now.**
- [x] **A vet can still write another clinic's records, and record a visit against any clinic, through the database.** Found 2026-09-28 building "A vet should see only the residents their own clinic treats" (0108). That item decided which residents a vet *sees*, and shows a visible resident's whole history, other clinics' visits included (docs/decisions.md, 2026-09-28). Writes were left as they were: a vet's JWT can still edit or delete another clinic's visit, prescription, procedure or blood test on a resident they can see, and can insert a visit with any `vet_id` — `src/lib/vets/scope.ts` holds "own clinic only" in the forms, not in RLS. Decide whether another clinic's rows are read-only to a vet (for `vet_appointments` it is a `vet_id = current_user_vet_id()` condition on update/delete; for prescriptions, procedures and blood tests it goes through their visit), and move the booking rule into `with check`, keeping the forms' "keep the clinic a visit already has" case working. Schema PR. **Done 2026-09-29** (`claude/vet-cross-clinic-writes`, 0110, see `docs/decisions.md`): another clinic's rows are read-only to a vet, and a visit can only be booked at the vet's own clinic.
- [x] **Should a vet read the shelter's address book at all?** Found 2026-09-28 building 0108, which left `vet_read_contacts` (and enclosures, zones, shelter_friends) as they were: the resident hub shows a carer's name from `contacts`, so dropping the policy breaks the hub for vets. But the Data API with a vet's JWT still returns every contact — adopters, donors, carers, with phone and email — though #182 took Contacts out of a vet's menu. Options: a view or definer function that gives the hub the carer name only, then drop the vet policy; or scope contacts to carers of residents the vet can see (`current_vet_resident_ids()`). Ask Lutan whether a vet ever needs an adopter's or carer's details before choosing. **Closed 2026-10-02** by the DB-5 decision: a vet reads id, name and type through `vet_contacts` (`0126`), not nothing and not everything.
- [x] **A visible "Request access" on the sign-in page — today nobody can tell how to ask.** Lutan, 2026-09-28: "I'm not sure how to request access right now." **How it works today, invisibly:** pressing **Continue with Google** on `/login` with an account that has no role creates the `auth.users` row, which *is* the access request on Settings → Security (`src/app/auth/callback/route.ts` refuses the session and redirects to `/login?error=no_role`). But nothing says so: the login page only offers sign-in, and the refusal reads "This account doesn't have access. Ask an administrator to assign you a role" — which sounds like a dead end, when the request has in fact just been made. **Build:** (1) a **Request access** link or button on `/login` (below the sign-in options) leading to a short explainer or step: "Continue with Google using the account you want to use; an administrator will be told and will approve you" — the button can simply run the same Google flow with a flag (e.g. `?intent=request`), so no new mechanism. Optionally ask for a name and a short note ("I'm the new volunteer vet") stored in `user_metadata`, shown beside the request on Settings → Security so the admin knows who it is. (2) **Reword the `no_role` message** to confirm what happened: "Your request has been sent. An administrator will approve it — try signing in again later." (en and th, `login.errors.noRole`). (3) Say what to do if they have no Google account: email login cannot self-register (admins create those users), so the explainer says to contact the shelter, with the shelter's email. (4) Tie-in: the System status card for waiting access requests (Admin section) and a possible email to admins on a new request — this is the other end of the same flow. Check the callback does not let a request intent do anything but create the unapproved row (no role, no session kept). Manual: the sign-in topic, en/th; a releases line.
- [x] **Settings → Security requires 2-step verification.** Done 2026-09-27 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/admin/security/verify` sets up an authenticator app and asks for the code once per sign-in; all eight Security actions check `aal2` themselves; Reset in the new 2-step column and `bootstrap-admin.mjs --reset-2step` are the recovery routes; no recovery codes. Lutan, 2026-09-26, replacing an earlier "admins and managers must sign in with Google" draft. **Scope, deliberately narrow:** only `/admin/security` and the actions behind it (create a login, give or change a role, approve or deny an access request, issue a temporary password, archive or restore a user) require a second step, because that page decides who can get in at all, which is the system's weakest point. Everything else stays as it is: the rest of the app changes shelter data, which can be corrected or reversed, so it keeps today's sign-in (password or Google). **Why 2-step and not "Google only":** the admins use personal Gmail accounts, so nothing enforces or reveals whether Google's own 2-step verification is on, and Google-only adds a lock-out if that sign-in ever breaks. Supabase Auth's built-in authenticator-app (TOTP) factor is free on the current plan, and a session that has passed it carries `aal2` in its JWT, so the rule can be checked in code and in the database. **Build:** (1) **Step-up, not a new login:** opening Security with an `aal1` session (`supabase.auth.mfa.getAuthenticatorAssuranceLevel()`) goes to a small page. The first time, it enrols an authenticator app (QR code + a manual key, verify one code, then show a few one-time recovery codes if Supabase supports them, otherwise explain the recovery route below). After that it asks for the 6-digit code and returns to Security. The same step-up works whether the admin signed in with a password or with Google. (2) **Enforce it where the power is, not just on the page:** every server action in `src/app/admin/security/` checks `aal2` itself before doing anything, since the service-role calls there bypass RLS. Add an RLS check (`auth.jwt()->>'aal' = 'aal2'`) on writes to `user_roles` too, so no path can change a role without it; that part is a small schema PR first. (3) **Settings landing page:** the Security tile says it needs your authenticator app. **Recovery:** a lost phone must not lock the shelter out. Another admin who has passed 2-step can reset a colleague's factor from Security, and `scripts/bootstrap-admin.mjs` (service role, run by Lutan) can remove a factor as the last resort. Write both down in README and `docs/decisions.md`. **Test on dev first:** confirm the `aal` claim after TOTP verification, what a Google session reports, how long `aal2` lasts in a session, and that a step-up survives the proxy's refresh. Manual (Security topic: set up the app, what to do if the phone is lost), both dictionaries, a releases line, a test plan covering each refused path, and the security review item should note it. **Not in scope:** requiring 2-step for management pages or for sign-in generally. Revisit if salaries (Management) land, since those figures cannot be "undone" once seen.

- [x] **Cut the vet's world down to residents and their own tasks.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. The vet menu today is the same as everyone's bar Stocktake: My tasks, Residents, Enclosures, Maintenance, Vets, Contacts, Projects. Lutan's call is that only **Residents** and **My tasks** belong there. Enclosures and Maintenance are shelter operations; Projects is the shelter's own programme of work; **Contacts** is the shelter's address book; and letting one clinic browse the list of **other clinics** the shelter uses is the one that is actively inappropriate rather than merely irrelevant. A vet is an external party with a clinical job, and the app should read that way. **Two halves, and the second is the one that matters.** The menu is `src/app/NavLinks.tsx`, where these five entries are ungated — easy. The routes are `/enclosures`, `/maintenance`, `/vets`, `/contacts`, `/projects` and their detail pages, each of which needs a guard, because hiding a link leaves the URL open and Pass 1 confirmed a vet reaches all five by typing them. Note `/maintenance` is already *effectively* shut — a vet has no maintenance RLS policy (`0001`, `0039`), so the board returns empty rather than forbidden — which is exactly the misleading state the walkthrough flagged, and the guard fixes the reading as well as the access. **Refuse properly, not with `redirect("/")`** — see the public-website bounce item. Manual and the roles topic need the same edit, and `roleSummary.vet` in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts` currently promises "Can read resident details", which stays true.
- [x] **A vet should see only the residents their own clinic treats.** **Done 2026-09-28** (`0108_vet_resident_scope.sql`; see `docs/decisions.md`): held in RLS through `current_vet_resident_ids()` — every vet policy on a resident's rows, the five owner-rights views and `record_attachment` — so a hand-typed `/residents/<id>` is a 404 and the Data API with a vet's JWT returns nothing outside the clinic. A cancelled visit counts; other clinics' rows on a visible resident are shown; a vet with no clinic sees no residents and `/residents` says why. Proved by `scripts/check-vet-resident-scope.mjs`. Closes Pass 1. Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. A vet signed in today sees the whole `/residents` list. Lutan expected it scoped: a doctor at Novel sees the residents Novel has records for — a vet appointment, and probably a procedure, prescription or blood test booked through them — and nobody else's. This is the largest item from Pass 1 and the one to think hardest about, because it is **data scoping, not menu gating**: it has to hold in RLS, not just in a page query, or the first `/residents/<id>` typed by hand walks straight through it. **What has to be decided first:** a vet account today has no link to a `vets` row at all — the role says "vet", not "which clinic" — so this needs that relationship to exist before any policy can reference it, which is a schema PR of its own and overlaps the doctor/clinic hierarchy item. Then: which records count as "treating" (appointments only, or prescriptions and procedures too); whether the scope is the clinic or the individual doctor; what a vet sees on a resident they treat but whose history includes another clinic's visits; and what happens to a resident whose only appointment is cancelled. Also whether this is even wanted before go-live — the shelter currently uses few clinics, and a scope that hides a resident from the vet about to treat them is worse than one that shows too much. **Decide the rule with Lutan before building.** **RULE DECIDED (Lutan, 2026-09-28): any clinical record, clinic-level.** A vet sees every resident their own clinic holds any clinical record for — a vet appointment, a prescription, a procedure or a blood test — and nobody else. Not appointments only: the broadest safe reading was chosen deliberately, because this item's own warning is that hiding a resident from the vet about to treat them is worse than showing too much. Clinic-level, not per-doctor, so a colleague covering a shift sees the same list. `user_roles.vet_id` and `current_user_vet_id()` (0102) are what the policy references. Still open for whoever builds it: what a vet sees on a resident they treat whose history includes another clinic's visits (the resident is visible either way — the question is the other clinic's rows), and what happens to a resident whose only appointment is cancelled. Both are narrower calls that can be made and recorded in `docs/decisions.md` rather than waiting on Lutan again.
- [x] **A vet can still edit the shelter's list of clinics through the database.** Found 2026-09-27 building "Cut the vet's world down to residents and their own tasks". That PR refuses a vet on `/vets`, `/contacts`, `/enclosures`, `/maintenance` and `/projects`, but the pages are only the UI: `vet_rw_vets` (`0001`) still gives a vet **full read/write on the `vets` table**, so a vet calling the Supabase API with their own session could rename or delete another clinic. Checked in dev's `pg_policies`. Reads are also still open — `vet_read_contacts`, `vet_read_enclosures`, `vet_read_zones` — so the shelter's address book is one API call away for a vet even though no page shows it. **The write is the one to fix**: drop `vet_rw_vets` for a read-only policy (the vet visit form needs to read clinic names), in a schema PR. Whether to narrow the reads belongs with the resident-level scope item above, which is where a vet's database access is being decided — `enclosures`/`zones` reads may be needed by resident pages a vet uses (current placement), so check before removing them. **Done 2026-09-27, `0105`**: `vet_rw_vets` replaced by `vet_read_vets` (select only), proved with a vet's own JWT by `scripts/check-vets-readonly.mjs`. The three reads were checked and left open: removing any would blank enclosure, zone and carer names on `/residents`, the resident hub, its housing section and adoption updates (`resident_list_view` is `security_invoker` and joins `enclosures`/`zones` directly) — see decisions.md, 2026-09-27, for what the resident-level scope item would have to move first.

- [x] **A vet's work comes from their appointments: My tasks and the menu built around vet visits.** **Done 2026-09-29** (`claude/vet-appointments-tasks`; see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-vet-appointments-page.md`): built as a new `/appointments` page rather than inside My tasks, per Lutan; the rest of the note below is the original draft. Lutan, 2026-09-27, reviewing `claude/recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees`: "vets should only be assigned tasks to perform actions related to a vet appointment, for example add a vaccination record — so even recurring tasks could come off navigation for vets, and they can just manage their appointments and the residents associated with the appointments." That PR already stops vets being *given* recurring jobs; this is the rest. **Draft user story, for Lutan to correct before it is built:** *As a visiting vet, I want to open the app and see my appointments at this shelter — upcoming and ones I still have to write up — so that I can get to the animal I am seeing and record what I did (vaccination, procedure, prescription, weight, notes) without finding my way through the shelter's own screens.* Acceptance, to be confirmed: (1) a vet's landing page is their appointments, not My tasks' recurring and maintenance sections, which a vet never has; (2) each appointment opens its resident, and the records a vet adds from there are pre-linked to that appointment; (3) the vet's menu is Appointments and Residents only — Recurring jobs never appears, and My tasks either goes or shows only appointment follow-ups; (4) a vet reaches only the residents on their appointments (this is the resident-level scope item above, order 4 — decide the rule there, not here); (5) nothing a vet has open is lost when the appointment is marked done. **Open questions for Lutan:** is an "appointment" the existing vet visit record, or something booked ahead that a visit is recorded against? Does a vet see the clinic's appointments or only their own (0102 links a vet account to a clinic and a doctor)? Depends on `vet-scope-navigation` (menu and route guards) landing first, and overlaps the batch 2 vet visit form item — sequence with both.
- [x] **A "see translations" cell, to replace `sees_all_translations()` (schema; found by `perm-convert-settings`, 2026-10-06).** **Done 2026-10-07** (`claude/see-translations-cell`; `0154`, `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-see-translations-cell.md`): `translations.view`, Yes/No, held by management and staff; the function is dropped and `public_viewer` is kept out by `has_permission()` itself. Staff read every `translations` row but hold no cell for it, so `0150` reads through a scope function (`sees_all_residents()` and the role opens the app). A catalogue cell (read-only, held by management and staff) lets the policy ask `has_permission()` and the function go. Belongs with "one place to translate everything"; it must not widen who may translate.
- [ ] **Who may read other people's assistant rows? (decision; found by `perm-convert-settings`, 2026-10-06).** Management could read every `assistant_actions` row and staff only their own; `0150` made it own-only for everyone but Admin, because no screen reads them and no activity names it. If managers should review what people ask the assistant, that is a read activity and one policy. Decide it together with the retention item (DB-11).
- [ ] **`facility_maps` writes when plans upload from the system (found by `perm-convert-settings`, 2026-10-06).** `0150` asks `facility.enclosures` Edit, which only Admin holds (management is Read). When the upload-a-plan item lands, the page writes this table, and the cell it asks is the one to decide again: management may need Edit on the map without Edit on every enclosure.
- [ ] **Scope functions must sit beside a cell: make the checker say so (schema/script; found by `perm-convert-settings`, 2026-10-06).** `sees_all_residents()`, `sees_all_contacts()`, `sees_all_translations()` and `has_shelter_floor()` are each true for `public_viewer`; every policy is safe only because `has_permission()` sits beside it, and `0150`'s first draft did not (`check-app-access-gate` caught 76 rows). A query in `check-policy-role-names.mjs` that fails on any policy using one of them without `has_permission()` would catch the next one at the migration, not at a different script.
- [x] **`0157_map_rooms` added a policy that names a role, and `check-policy-role-names.mjs` is RED on `main` because of it.** Found 2026-10-07 by `/plan-day`. `0157` (PR #422, `claude/map-nonresident-rooms`) created `management_rw_map_rooms` as `current_user_role() in ('admin', 'management')` — the **old enum pattern**, in the middle of the project to make every policy ask *what may you do* rather than *who are you*. The checker now reports **55 policies on 30 tables** where the 7 Oct baseline was 54 on 29, all the vet's, all owned; the new one is **the only unowned entry**, and it says: *"map_rooms names a role and nothing owns it (management_rw_map_rooms): add it to OWNERS and to §15"*. **Two defensible fixes, and this item does not prejudge which:** (a) **convert it to a permission cell**, which is the direction of travel and makes the check green honestly — note that *what cell* is entangled with the open item on `facility_maps` writes when plans upload from the system, so the two may want one answer; or (b) **declare an owner** in `OWNERS` and §15 if the conversion genuinely belongs with a later role slice, which keeps the check truthful without pretending the work is done. **Needs a migration either way, so it is a schema PR** — and it can fold into another `0158` schema PR rather than taking a number of its own. **Why it got through, worth fixing too:** `check-policy-role-names.mjs` is **not in `scripts/gates.mjs` or any CI workflow**, so nothing ran it on #422; and the `/plan-day` brief for that stream covered dictionaries, phone width, release lines and test plans and **never mentioned the permissions rule**, which is the most important standing rule in this repo right now. Consider adding the check to `gates.mjs`. → *Auth* **Done 2026-10-07 (`0158_map_rooms_permission`): converted (option a) to `facility.enclosures` Edit, the cell `facility_maps` writes already use; no new cell. `check-policy-role-names` GREEN. Gate wiring is `policy-enum-guard`'s.**

## Architecture

- [ ] **Discuss: keep the staff app on `lannacare.org` (later a Cooper `.org`) and move only the public website to `lannacareforanimals.org`.** Lutan, 2026-10-02. **The deliverable is a decision, not a build:** a `docs/decisions/` file agreed with Lutan, then a rewrite of the cutover item (Deployment) to match. **The idea:** instead of moving the whole of production to `lannacareforanimals.org`, the staff app (sign-in, residents, medical, Settings, everything behind a login) stays on `lannacare.org`, moving to a Cooper-named `.org` once the product has its name, and `lannacareforanimals.org` serves only the shelter's public pages (`/`, `/adopt`, `/our-work`, `/friends`, `/donate`, `/foster`, `/volunteer`, the public photo route). With several shelters that becomes one app domain for every shelter's staff, and one public domain per shelter. **What it buys:** (a) Google sign-in, the consent screen, Supabase redirect URLs, the 2-step setup and auth mail all live on one domain and never need re-reviewing per shelter, which is most of the cutover item's step 5; (b) a new shelter is a DNS record plus a public domain, not a new sign-in setup; (c) the QR and RFID tags already printed (`/r/`, `/e/` on `lannacare.org`) keep working; (d) the public site's cutover shrinks to DNS, a route and the public-page checks. **Things to settle, honestly:** (1) **It is still one app and one database.** "Front end and back end" here means one Next.js app answering on two hostnames and choosing what to serve by host (in `src/proxy.ts`): public routes on the public domain, app routes on the app domain, each redirecting to the other. Splitting into two deployments is possible later but not needed. (2) **The production database still has to be decided separately:** the cutover item's new Supabase project, and keeping today's one as UAT, are unaffected by which domain the app answers on. (3) **UAT needs a new home,** because the cutover item makes `lannacare.org` UAT: e.g. `uat.lannacare.org`, or `test.lannacare.org` doubling as it. (4) **Moving the app twice** (`lannacare.org` now, Cooper `.org` later) repeats the sign-in setup once; consider waiting and going straight to the Cooper domain at cutover if the name is close, and keep `lannacare.org` redirecting either way so tags never break. (5) **Printed tags for visitors:** a `/r/` tag scanned by a member of the public lands on the app domain today and shows the public profile there; it should redirect to that shelter's public domain instead, which needs a shelter → public-domain mapping (it is the same lookup the multi-shelter spike needs to pick a shelter from a hostname). (6) **Search engines and the lock:** the app domain is `noindex` everywhere; the public domain is indexable once the `PUBLIC_SITE` lock comes off for it; canonical URLs and Open Graph tags must use the public domain. (7) **Staff link from the public site** ("Staff login" in the footer) points at the app domain; a signed-in admin editing the website on the app domain previews it on the public domain. (8) **Photos:** public pages request images from their own domain (`/api/photos/` on the public host, edge and Pi cache as in the photo item), so the photo route must answer on both. (9) **Email:** sender domains (see "Send all auth mail from one sender domain") fit this naturally: auth mail from the app domain, any public mail from the shelter's. (10) **The Pi tunnel and Worker routes** gain a hostname each. **Read with:** the cutover item (Deployment), the multi-shelter spike and the per-shelter Drive item (Architecture), and the Chiang Mai regional-listing question, since a regional listing is one more public domain over several shelters. Lutan's view, to test in the paper: it should simplify going multi-tenant and make the move to `lannacareforanimals.org` easier because the public site needs little back-end setup. Both look right; the paper should confirm it against the cutover item's steps one by one.
- [x] **`/manual/pdf` on the Node origin fetches its screenshots unauthenticated and gets the login redirect.** Found 2026-10-01 while testing the Next 16.3.8 bump (#268), not caused by it. `src/app/manual/pdf/route.ts` `loadImage` falls back to `fetch(origin + src)` when there is no `ASSETS` binding (the Pi runs `next start`); `src/proxy.ts` redirects that request (307) to `/login`, so react-pdf receives HTML and logs `Incomplete or corrupt PNG file` once per image (~195 times for `?view=all`), and the request is slow. The Worker is unaffected. Fix: read the file from `public/` with `fs` when there is no binding, or forward the caller's cookie. Check that the PDF on the Pi actually has its pictures.

- [ ] **Promote the `test-plan` check from a red flag to a required check.** Requested 2026-09-23 by Lutan, alongside enlisting a test manager: every feature gets a test plan checklist, filled in during verification and signed off before the feature reaches production. The mechanism is built — `docs/test-plan-template.md`, copied per feature to `docs/test-plans/<feature>.md`, plus `scripts/check-test-plan.mjs` and a `test-plan` CI job that fails a PR which does not add or update a *completed* checklist (every line ticked or `n/a: <reason>`, no placeholders left, `Result:` one of three values, `fail` blocks). That is PR #55 — open, CI green, carrying its own completed checklist as the first one through the gate. **The part deliberately not enabled yet:** `test-plan` is not a required check in branch protection on `main`, so it reports red and the PR merges anyway. That is a GitHub settings change on the repo (Settings → Branches → `main` → require `CI` *and* `test-plan`). **No exemptions, including schema-only PRs** — ruled 2026-09-23: an exemption was granted and then reversed within minutes, on the grounds that a checklist which is mostly `n/a: no UI surface, no code reads these columns yet` costs about a minute and is exactly the record you want when that migration later turns out to matter. If it is ever revisited, derive the exemption from the diff (the PR touches nothing outside `supabase/migrations/`) rather than from a label: a label is a manual click that can land on a PR which is not schema-only, so it has to be verified against the file list anyway, at which point the file list is doing the work and the label only adds a way to get it wrong. Note also that the gate sits before merge, not before deploy — `main` moves several times a day (four merges on 2026-09-23 alone), so untested work accumulating on `main` would make every release all-or-nothing. Keep a thin release-time smoke list as well (sign in, residents list, one resident hub, `/adopt` signed out), because a per-feature gate cannot catch two features interacting badly. Everything merged up to `f32f2c1` (#51–#54) is the agreed baseline and is exempt; testing starts from the next feature. **Staged deliberately, 2026-09-23:** Lutan's call is to run `test-plan` as a red flag first and promote it to a required check once the process has visibly bedded in — "see how we go, and if it is working smoothly then we can change to a hard block". So red-without-blocking is a planned first stage, not an oversight, and this item is the scheduled revisit rather than a defect. The reasoning (a soft gate people follow beats a hard gate they resent, and nothing is live until the Pi lands) is recorded in `docs/decisions.md` on PR #55.

- [x] **`check-test-plan.mjs` can't tell a post-merge sign-off PR from a feature PR that forgot its release line.** Raised 2026-09-24 on PR #80. #77 (intake capacity warning) merged with its plan complete apart from a real-phone check; the phone pass was then recorded in a follow-up PR from the same branch that touched only `docs/test-plans/intake-capacity-warning.md`. The checker reads the plan's **Release notes.** line against *that PR's* diff, and a ticked line with no new `unreleased` entry fails ("gained no line in this PR"), even though #77 had added it. The workaround was to reword the line as `n/a: in this follow-up PR … went into unreleased with #77` — honest, but it rewrites the feature's permanent record to suit a later PR, and every sign-off recorded after merge will hit the same thing. This will be common: section 8's deployed smoke test can only happen after merge, so a manual check moved there (as the phone pass was) is always recorded post-merge. Fix options: (a) when a PR changes only files under `docs/test-plans/`, check the release-notes line against the diff that introduced the plan (`git log --diff-filter=A` on the file, or the first PR that touched it) rather than the current diff; (b) accept a ticked line when `unreleased` — or a cut release — already contains a line the plan quotes; (c) an explicit marker such as `release line: #77` that the checker verifies by looking at that PR's diff. (a) needs no new syntax and is probably the right default. Afterwards, restore #80's line to its ticked wording. Soft gate, so nothing is blocked meanwhile — but a false red on every sign-off PR is exactly what teaches people to ignore a red `test-plan`.

- [ ] **Top-to-bottom architectural review by Fable.** Requested 2026-09-22. Open a session on the Fable model (`claude-fable-5-1`) and ask for a read-only review of the whole system, not a feature: the Next.js app (`src/app` routes, `src/lib` services, `src/components`, `src/proxy.ts` auth/redirect handling), the Supabase schema, RLS policies and the migration history in `supabase/migrations/`, the Cloudflare Worker / OpenNext deployment and the environment split (`test.lannacare.org` vs `lannacare.org`, `scripts/deploy.mjs`), the operational scripts (`scripts/apply-migrations.mjs`, `scripts/backup.mjs`, `scripts/pi/`), the Google Drive integration, the public website vs the staff app, and the docs (`README.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/pi-hosting.md`, `docs/manual`). Ask for: (a) a plain description of the architecture as it actually is, to check against the README; (b) ranked findings — security and data-loss risks first, then coupling / duplication / anything that will hurt when the shelter grows or a second developer arrives, then nits — each with the file(s) and a concrete suggested change; (c) what is fine and should be left alone. Write the result to `docs/architecture-review-2026-09-22.md` (dated, so later reviews sit beside it) and turn each finding worth acting on into its own backlog item under the matching heading. No code changes in that session — the review is the deliverable.


- [x] **Full security review and cyber threat assessment of the platform.** Requested 2026-09-24 by Lutan. The deliverable is an **assessment report**: `docs/security-review-<date>.md`, dated so later reviews sit beside it. It maps every threat surface, says who could attack each one and how, rates each risk, and proposes mitigations. This is a separate item from the Fable architectural review above. That review ranks security first but covers the whole architecture. This one is only about security, and it asks "who would attack this, and what would they get?" not "is this well built?". Whichever runs second reads the other's report and does not repeat its findings. **Threat surfaces to cover, at minimum:** (a) **the public website and anonymous access**: `/adopt`, `/foster`, `/volunteer`, `/donate`, `/our-work`, `/r`, `/e`, `/privacy`; the anon key that ships in the client bundle by design; every table and view anon can reach through PostgREST, found by listing grants and `pg_policies`, not from `scripts/check-public-views.mjs`'s four-view allowlist (note `site_content`'s unqualified `using (true)`: every column of it is world-readable); and spam and abuse on any public form; (b) **authentication and sessions**: Google OAuth (`src/app/auth/callback`), email/password with temporary passwords and `must_change_password` in `app_metadata`, the access-request queue (a stranger's Google sign-in leaves an auth row), archived users, the `src/proxy.ts` guards, `bootstrap-admin.mjs`, whether the pages under `/admin` and `/management` each check the role or rely on the nav hiding them, and MFA for admins (since 2026-09-27 Settings → Security and its actions require `aal2` — an authenticator-app step-up; review the step-up page `/admin/security/verify`, the per-action check in `src/app/admin/security/actions.ts`, the reset routes including `bootstrap-admin.mjs --reset-2step`, that a reset reaches an open session only at its next refresh, and that nothing else asks for 2-step; `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-27); (c) **authorization and RLS**: role escalation (can a volunteer write `user_roles`?), `security definer` functions and triggers (0024, 0026 and later), write paths for each role, and service-role use in server actions; (d) **file handling**: the attachment and photo routes under `src/app/api/*` (upload type and size limits, who may read a `photos/[fileId]`, whether a Drive file ID can be guessed or enumerated) and the Google Drive service credentials, including what those credentials can reach beyond the shelter's folders; (e) **the assistant** (`/assistant`, `assistant_actions` 0070): prompt injection through resident notes, contact records or uploaded documents; what the assistant can read and write as the signed-in user; and whether its actions need confirmation; (f) **infrastructure and deployment**: the Cloudflare Worker (`worker/index.mjs`, including the Pi-origin fallback in `fetchFromOrigin`), `wrangler.jsonc`, the secrets in Workers and `.dev.vars`/`.env.local`, the dev/prod split (`test.lannacare.org` vs `lannacare.org`) and whether dev can ever reach prod data, `scripts/deploy.mjs`, the Pi host (`scripts/pi/`, `docs/pi-hosting.md`), security headers (CSP, HSTS, frame-ancestors) and Cloudflare WAF/rate-limit settings; (g) **the supply chain and repo**: npm dependencies (`npm audit`, lockfile integrity), GitHub Actions permissions in `ci.yml`, branch protection, the auto-push hooks, secrets in git history, and the Supabase Management API token that `apply-migrations.mjs` uses (who holds it, what it can do, and the 90-day token whose location is unknown); (h) **data protection and resilience**: what personal data is held (contacts, adopters, volunteers; payroll is deliberately out of scope, 2026-09-29), backups (`scripts/backup.mjs`, `backup-schedule.ps1`: where they go, whether they are encrypted, whether a restore has been tested), ransomware or account-takeover recovery, and PDPA (Thailand) obligations; (i) **people and process**: phishing of staff and admin Google accounts, shared logins, offboarding (archive vs delete), the lannacare.org email routing catch-all as a spoofing and impersonation risk (SPF, DKIM and DMARC on the domain), and how much depends on one developer with many AI sessions (bus factor, and what an AI session is permitted to do). **For each finding:** the surface, a plausible attacker (opportunist or bot, disgruntled ex-volunteer, targeted attacker), likelihood × impact → Critical / High / Medium / Low, evidence (file and line, or the query that showed it), and a concrete mitigation with a rough effort estimate. Also list what is already done well, so nobody "fixes" it. Open with a one-page executive summary and a top-5 list that a non-developer (the Director) can read. **Rules of engagement:** read-only, plus hands-on testing **against dev / `test.lannacare.org` only**, never production (production reads are off-limits from sessions). Use the anon key and a test token for each role to confirm what RLS actually allows, not what the policies appear to say. Do not load-test or fuzz Cloudflare. Do not write out real secrets in the report: name where a secret lives, never its value. **Afterwards:** turn every Critical/High finding into its own backlog item (on the `backlog` branch), note Medium/Low in the report only, and add a `docs/decisions.md` entry on any risk that is deliberately accepted. The report goes out as its own PR with a test-plan checklist, which is mostly `n/a: documentation only`. **TICKED 2026-10-07 (staleness sweep) — delivered 2026-09-30 under a different filename, and this item was never ticked.** `91c15c23` (#239, 2026-09-30): `docs/security/security-assessment-2026-09-30.pdf` and `.docx` (not `docs/security-review-<date>.md`), with its test plan `docs/test-plans/security-review.md`. It has what this item asks for: threat surfaces across database, code and front end; a severity scale and 33 rated findings (0 Critical, 1 High — the unencrypted backup — 13 Medium, 15 Low, 4 Info), each with mitigations; *"The five things to do first"* in plain words for the Director; a prioritised action plan; and an appendix of what it could not see. **Its findings are already the backlog:** every DB-n, WEB-n and CODE-n item under *Security* cites it, and most have since closed (DB-1 backups encrypted in 0.14.0, WEB-2 CSP enforced 2026-10-07). **One thing it did not do, now its own item below:** it was white-box only (*"Dynamic testing against a running instance"* is under *Not reviewed*), so this item's rule *"use the anon key and a test token for each role to confirm what RLS actually allows"* was not met by it.
- [ ] **The security assessment's dynamic half: ask the running dev database what each login can actually read and write.** Split out 2026-10-07 by the backlog staleness sweep from *Full security review* (ticked above), whose report (`docs/security/security-assessment-2026-09-30.pdf`) was a white-box read of the code and lists *"Dynamic testing against a running instance"* under *Not reviewed*. That item's rule was to use the anon key and a test token for each role and record what RLS answers, not what the policies appear to say. **Check what already covers it before starting, because a lot does now:** `check-permission-parity.mjs` probes every activity under each role's own JWT, `check-public-views.mjs` the anon tier, `check-app-access-gate` the `public_viewer`, and the per-role `check-*-role.mjs` scripts each configured role. What none of them does is *discover*: each asserts a list someone wrote. **The gap is the reverse question.** For anon and each role, list every table, view and function that answers at all (grants plus `pg_policies`, then a probe), and compare that with what the scripts expect. Run against dev / test only, never production. Write the result beside the assessment as a dated addendum, and file each surprise as its own item. → *Security*

- [x] **Enforce migration numbering with a check, not prose.** Raised 2026-09-22 after two in-flight branches both shipped `0069` (`0069_seed_standard_diet.sql` on the diet branch and `0069_assistant_actions.sql` on `claude/assistant-v1-schema`) and both were applied to dev before anyone noticed — the same clash as the two `0067` PRs earlier. CLAUDE.md already says the next number is one past the highest on `main` and that only one in-flight branch may carry a migration, but it is prose that nothing enforces, and `apply-migrations.mjs --status` reports `0 pending` while dev holds rows main has no file for, so the divergence is invisible. Add a check that fails at commit time (`.githooks/pre-commit`) and in CI: a new file under `supabase/migrations/` must be numbered exactly one past the highest on `origin/main`, and no two files may share a number. Worth pairing with a `--drift` mode on `apply-migrations.mjs` that lists rows in `schema_migrations` with no matching file — dev currently has **two**, not three: `--status` reported 72 applied against 70 files on `main` with `0 pending` (checked 2026-09-23), so exactly two recorded rows have no file. They are the two renumberings — `0067_public_resident_cards.sql` (now `0068`) and `0069_assistant_actions.sql` (now `0070`, commit `34bf49e`). `0069_seed_standard_diet.sql` kept its number and is still a file on `main`, so it is not an orphan. Both leftovers are harmless, but note that the count in this item was wrong until someone did the arithmetic by hand — which is the whole argument for the flag: an orphan count nobody can read off a command is an orphan count that drifts in the notes as well as the database.

- [x] **Make production drift visible: nothing records what production has actually run.** Raised 2026-09-22, alongside the numbering item above and arguably the larger half of it. `schema_migrations` lives inside each database, so the repo has no idea what production has applied and `apply-migrations.mjs --status` only ever answers for the database it is pointed at. `0069_seed_standard_diet.sql` was applied to **production** while `main` had no file for it at all, and sat that way until PR #46 merged; dev meanwhile held three rows with no file on `main`, and `--status` cheerfully reported `0 pending` throughout. Drift is silent in both directions and nobody finds out until someone rebuilds a database or renumbers a file. Note the only thing that prevented a second production apply was accidental: `scripts/worktree.mjs new` copies `.env.local` but not `.env.deploy.production`, so a feature worktree physically cannot reach production — while the main checkout has the file and can. That is luck, not design. Worth: a `--drift` mode that diffs `schema_migrations` against the files on `origin/main` for a named environment and names both halves (applied-but-missing, present-but-unapplied); a `--status` that says which files on `origin/main` production has **not** run, so the pre-deploy gap is one command; and a loud refusal (or at least a prompt) when `--env production` is used from a branch whose migration is not yet on `origin/main`, since schema is supposed to reach `main` before any database. See `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-22 for the 0069 clash this came out of.

- [x] **`deploy.mjs` ships code without ever asking whether the database has the schema it needs.** Raised 2026-09-30 after it went wrong twice in one day. `deploy.mjs` guards a lot before it builds — branch is `main`, working tree clean, `main` level with `origin/main`, `package.json` matching `latestRelease`, `unreleased` empty — and then deploys without checking a single migration. `apply-migrations.mjs --drift <env>` answers exactly that question and already shares its implementation with Settings → System status (`src/lib/migration-drift.ts`), so the work is wiring, not building. **Two consecutive releases, both on 2026-09-30, and the difference between them was luck rather than process:** on `0.10.0` the deploy was started before `0113`–`0116` were applied, and got away with it only because a deploy spends its first minutes building — the migrations were applied alongside it and reached `No drift` at 116 files while the deploy was 134 seconds in and still compiling, so the schema landed before the Worker upload. It was noticed only because an unrelated `--drift` could not write to `deploy.log` (`Device or resource busy`). On `0.10.1` the luck ran out: `0117` went on about an hour *after* the release was live, and its consumer had shipped in that release (`17c7f0d`, the Microchipped tick on `/adopt/[id]`, with `src/app/adopt/[id]/page.tsx` and `src/lib/residents/public.ts` both selecting `is_microchipped`), so the **public adoption profile was querying a column that did not exist**. How long that lasted cannot be established, because that deploy was run without `>> deploy.log` and its start time is unrecorded. Both are written up in `docs/releases/2026-09-30.md`. **What to build:** in the `GUARDED` block (`uat` and `production` only — leave `test` alone, where running ahead is normal and wanted), refuse the deploy when the target database is missing any migration, listing the pending filenames and the `apply-migrations.mjs` command to fix it, and exit `2` like the sibling guards. **Get the comparison right, because the obvious one is wrong:** the rule is *every migration file in the commit being deployed must be applied to the target*, **not** "the target matches `origin/main`". Extra applied rows are fine — schema ahead of code is the safe direction this project deliberately uses, and a migration belonging to the *next* release can legitimately sit on `main` already. `0118` was in exactly that position during `0.10.1`: on `main`, belonging to `#236` which merged after the cut, and correctly not applied. A naive equality check would have refused that deploy for a file it did not need. In practice the two formulations coincide, because the existing guard already requires `HEAD` to equal `origin/main` — but they should not be *written* as the same thing, or the check breaks the first time someone deploys a tag or a hotfix. **And distinguish "the database says it is behind" from "could not ask":** refuse on the first, warn loudly and continue on the second. A deploy already depends on the network, so this is not the `release-prs.mjs` situation where failing closed on a blip was the thing to avoid; but a Supabase timeout should not be able to block a release either. Worth pairing with the reverse check at the other end: `apply-migrations.mjs` could warn when it applies a file whose consumer is not yet deployed, which is the same information read the other way round.

- [x] **Every guard on the deploy is on the copy nobody is served by.** Raised 2026-10-01, and it is the sibling of the item above: that one is now done, which is exactly what makes this one visible. `deploy.mjs` refuses a `uat`/`production` deploy unless the branch is `main`, the tree is clean, `main` is level with `origin/main`, `package.json` matches `latestRelease`, `unreleased` is empty — "ships only a written-down release" — and, since the item above, unless the target database holds every migration the commit carries. **`scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh` has none of that.** It is 51 lines: `git fetch origin main` → `git checkout main` → `git reset --hard origin/main` → `npm ci` → `npm run build` → `systemctl restart`. It does run `apply-migrations.mjs --status | tail -1`, but only *prints* it, with a comment saying a build against a database missing a migration is still a valid build — which was a reasonable position before `deploy.mjs` started refusing in that exact case, and is now an asymmetry rather than a decision. **And the Pi is the copy that serves users**: `ORIGIN_HOST` is `pi.lannacare.org` in the production block, and `lannacare.org` answers `x-lanna-served-by: pi`. The Worker is the fallback. So the whole release discipline — cut, record, version, schema check — is enforced on the path that answers only when the Pi times out. **What it costs, all three observed rather than theorised:** (a) **code reaches users without a release cut.** Every note in `0.11.0` was already live before it was cut, because the Pi had been reset to `origin/main` by earlier deploys; release notes now describe what people already have, and `/releases` can show an older version than the app's behaviour. (b) **The release-time gate never runs for it.** `docs/release-smoke-test.md`'s "every PR in this release has a completed test plan — check this by hand" is a pre-*deploy* step, and there is no deploy step here to hang it on. (c) **`npx wrangler rollback --env production` is no longer the production rollback.** It reverts the Worker, which is not serving. Rolling the Pi back means `git reset --hard <sha>` and a rebuild, and that is written down nowhere as a rollback path — while four release records in `docs/releases/` state the `wrangler rollback` position as though it were the whole answer. That documentation is wrong from 2026-09-30 onward and should be corrected with this item. **What to build:** the cheap version is to make `deploy-pi.sh` run the same two checks `deploy.mjs` already has, reusing them rather than restating them — `unreleased` empty and `package.json` matching `latestRelease` for a production or uat deploy, and `missingFromDatabase(deployedMigrations(git), applied)` for the schema, refusing with the same messages and keeping "could not ask" a warning. A `--force` for a genuine emergency is fine *if* it prints loudly and the reason ends up in a record; the sign-in outage of 30 September is exactly the case for one, and it was handled properly through a PR anyway. Keep `--env test` unguarded, as `deploy.mjs` does. **The harder question to settle first, because the fix depends on it:** is the Pi meant to be continuously deployed from `main`, or to be a release target like the Worker? If continuous deployment is wanted — and it has its merits, since it is how the 30 September fix reached users fast — then the honest change is the opposite one: stop pretending a cut release gates production, say so in `docs/release-smoke-test.md`, and move the test-plan gate to merge time where it would actually bite. What cannot hold is the present state, where the documents describe a gate that the serving path does not have. Worth pairing with a version endpoint served by the app rather than the Worker: `/api/releases/current` is answered by `worker/index.mjs:158` before the origin is consulted, so there is currently **no way to ask what the Pi is running** without signing in — the `0.11.0` record could only state it by quoting a terminal someone pasted into chat.

- [x] **A `.env.*.local` for the wrong environment silently bakes that environment's host into the build, and nothing can see it.** Found 2026-10-01 by Lutan: Google sign-in on `test.lannacare.org` returned him to `http://localhost:3000`. **The chain, proven rather than inferred:** `.env.production.local` existed on the dev machine — generated by `scripts/pi/write-env.mjs` for the *production* environment, header and all, presumably left from the Pi setup — and it sets `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL=https://lannacare.org`. `next build` is always a production build, so Next loads `.env.production.local` for **every** deploy including `deploy:test`. `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` values are inlined into the compiled output at build time, so the test build carried `https://lannacare.org` in **24 places across 19 files**. On test, `getSiteOrigin()` therefore returned the production host, `signInWithGoogle` sent `redirectTo=https://lannacare.org/auth/callback`, the **dev** Supabase project rejected it as off-allow-list, and Supabase fell back to that project's Site URL — `http://localhost:3000`. Moving the file out of the repo and redeploying fixed it: `strip-baked-env` went from 12 vars to 10, and the inlined host went from 24 occurrences to 0. **Three things made it invisible, which is the real point.** (a) The file is gitignored (`.env.*.local`), so it appears in no diff and no review. (b) `strip-baked-env` is the mechanism that exists to stop dev values shipping, and it **cannot** help here — its own header says "NEXT_PUBLIC_* values are inlined by `next build` separately and don't depend on this file", because it only empties `.open-next/cloudflare/next-env.mjs`. (c) The only visible symptom in the deploy output was the var count going from 10 to 12, which means nothing unless someone knows what it was yesterday. **And it is worse in the other direction than the one we hit.** Here a production host leaked into test, which breaks sign-in loudly. Run `write-env.mjs --env test` on this machine once and the *next production deploy* bakes `https://test.lannacare.org` into production — where `getSiteOrigin()` would then hand the test host to OAuth redirects, `metadataBase`, and anything else that asks for the site origin, on the real site, with no error anywhere. **Two fixes, and the second is the better one:** (a) make `deploy.mjs` refuse when a `.env.*.local` naming a different environment is present, printing the filename — cheap, and it sits beside the check already there that refuses when the Supabase project does not match the target (`appEnvForSupabaseUrl`, which is the same class of mistake caught one layer down); (b) stop relying on env-file precedence at all and have the deploy set `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` explicitly for the environment being built, so there is nothing to leak and no file ordering to reason about. (b) also fixes the case where the file is absent: today an absent value falls back to the request host, which is right for the Worker and wrong for the Pi, and that distinction is currently carried by a generated file rather than by the deploy. Either way, also worth asking why `write-env.mjs` — documented as a Pi script — had been run on the dev machine, and whether it should refuse to write outside the Pi.




- [x] **A release's PR list is built from `git log --first-parent`, which cannot see every PR.** Found 2026-09-29 preparing `0.9.1`. #213 (`claude/stock-edit-history`, which brought migration `0112`) reached `main` through a plain `Merge remote-tracking branch 'origin/main'` rather than a merge commit naming the PR, so it does **not** appear in `git log --oneline --first-parent <last-release>..HEAD` — the listing every cut plan and every release record uses to enumerate a release's PRs. It was found only by tracing `0112`'s author commit back to its branch, and the release was nearly recorded as eight PRs rather than nine. **Nothing was lost this time**: #213's test plan existed and its release-notes line was a sound `n/a: a shelter user sees no change; the history it now feeds is not shown anywhere`. The failure mode is what matters — a PR the audit cannot see is a PR whose test plan nobody checks and whose *missing* release note nobody notices. Note which gate this defeats: `docs/release-smoke-test.md`'s pre-deploy item is "Every PR in this release has a completed `docs/test-plans/<feature>.md` — **check this by hand**", explicitly by hand because `test-plan` reports red without blocking a merge. A hand check against an incomplete list passes silently, which is worse than no check, because it produces a tick. **How it happens:** any route by which a PR's commits join `main`'s first-parent line without their own merge commit — a branch that merges `origin/main` into itself and is then fast-forwarded, a squash or rebase merge, or a second branch that carried the first one's commits along with it. So it is not a one-off mistake to be avoided by discipline; it is a property of the merge shape, and three of the four shapes are ones GitHub offers by default. **Fix options:** (a) enumerate from GitHub rather than git — `gh pr list --state merged` filtered to PRs whose merge commit is an ancestor of `HEAD` but not of the previous release's SHA; authoritative, but needs network and `gh` auth; (b) drop `--first-parent` and read every commit in the range, grouping by branch name from the merge subjects; catches more, but lists nothing for a branch whose name is gone; (c) **cross-check against the two artifacts every feature must have** — for each file added under `supabase/migrations/` and each `docs/test-plans/*.md` in the range, assert its introducing PR is in the list. This is the cheap safety net and it would have caught this exact case: `0112` was in the range with no PR in the list; (d) a `scripts/release-prs.mjs <since-sha>` doing (a) with (b) as fallback and (c) as an assertion, printing the list a cut plan pastes and exiting non-zero when they disagree. (c) alone is worth having even without the rest, since it needs no network and no new syntax. Worth doing before the next release rather than after: the by-hand check is the only thing standing between an unverified feature and production, and it has now been shown to be checkable against the wrong list.




- [x] **The photo proxy serves any known Drive file to a signed-out visitor, internal attachments included.** Found 2026-09-25 while closing anon's function EXECUTE (`0082`, see `docs/decisions.md`, "Anon loses the functions"). `/api/photos/<fileId>` asks `is_known_drive_file`, which says yes for *every* `attachments` row (blood-test and procedure files, not just resident photos), `project_photos`, `maintenance_photos`, the site photos and Shelter Friend logos, and then streams the bytes with no session check. The only barrier is that a Drive file id is hard to guess; anon cannot list them, but an id that leaks (a shared link, a screenshot of a URL, browser history on a shared shelter PC) opens that file to anyone. And the edge cache (`caches.default`) is keyed by URL alone, so a file a signed-in user viewed is served from cache to anyone with the URL for 24h whatever the database says. Decide what the public side needs: probably the files behind `public_resident_photos`, `public_project_photos`, `site_content(_photos)` and `public_shelter_friends` logos. Then either split the check (`is_public_drive_file` for anon, the current one for signed-in users) or require a session for anything else, and make the cache key or `Cache-Control` distinguish the two (`private` for non-public files). Not a schema-only PR: the route changes with it.
- [x] **Take `is_known_drive_file` back from anon.** Since `claude/photo-proxy-session-check` the photo proxy asks `is_public_drive_file`, and for anything else the caller's own RLS on `attachments`, `project_photos`, `maintenance_photos` and `shelter_friends`; nothing anon-facing calls `is_known_drive_file` any more. `0082` still grants anon EXECUTE on it, and it answers yes for any internal file id, so a signed-out caller can confirm that an internal file exists. A small schema PR: revoke from anon (and probably authenticated: the app no longer calls it), and move it out of `check-public-views.mjs`'s allow-list in the same PR, or that check fails. `check-shelter-friends.mjs` and `check-public-drive-file.mjs` call it as the owner and should be unaffected. **Only after the route change is deployed to production**: until then production's proxy still calls it as anon, and revoking would break every public photo.

- [ ] **Roles and permissions each shelter configures: a matrix of activities × roles in Settings, and roles defined at setup — design first.** Lutan, 2026-10-03, thinking about multi-tenancy. Shelters are organised differently: one has a staff member who handles purchasing, another leaves that to Management. Today's roles are a fixed enum (`admin`, `management`, `staff`, `vet`, `volunteer`, `public_viewer`) with their rights hard-coded in about a dozen app predicates (`isShelterRole`, `canManage`, `canStocktake`, `canRecordDelivery`, `canArchiveMedical`, `canWriteMaintenance`, `canWriteProjects`, `canDoJob`, `canUseAssistant`, …) and in RLS across roughly 26 migrations. That cannot vary per shelter. **What is wanted:** (1) **A catalogue of activities with no grey areas.** Every action in the system is one named activity, each set per role to **Edit / Read / None** (or **Yes / No** where reading makes no sense, e.g. "record a death"). Where one screen hides two different powers (create vs delete, record vs approve, see a contact's name vs their phone), split it into two activities rather than adding levels, so every cell means exactly one thing. (2) **A matrix in Settings:** activities down the side, the shelter's roles across the top, admin-editable. (3) **Roles defined per shelter at setup:** a shelter can add a role ("Purchasing", "Senior Staff", "Kennel lead") and set its column, or keep the defaults. **Design points to settle in the paper:** (a) **Defaults reproduce today exactly.** Ship the six current roles as the starting template, filled in so that switching to the matrix changes nothing for Lanna, proved by a check that compares each default cell with today's behaviour. (b) **Some things are not configurable.** Admin always has everything and cannot be edited away; no change may leave a shelter with no one able to edit the matrix; Settings → Security keeps its 2-step rule; `public_viewer` and signed-out visitors stay fixed. (c) **Data scope is separate from capability.** A vet seeing only their clinic's residents (0108) is a *scope*, not a cell, so model scopes apart (or as a small fixed set of scope options per role) rather than forcing them into Edit/Read/None. (d) **Enforcement must be in the database, not only the menu.** One SQL function such as `has_permission('stock.delivery', 'edit')` used by every policy and `security definer` guard, and one `can()` in TypeScript replacing the predicates, both reading the same tables. Decide how it stays fast under RLS (a `stable` function over a small cached table, or the role's permissions placed in the JWT by Supabase's custom access token hook, which means a change applies at the next token refresh — say so in the UI). (e) **Everything that keys on a role name today** has to move to activities: the manual's and `/releases`' per-role filtering (`isForRole`, topic `roles`), recurring-job eligibility, the assistant, nav and route guards, the role walkthrough. (f) **Changes are audited** (who changed which cell, when) through `audit_log` (0121), and the matrix can be exported, since it is also what a shelter's acceptance sign-off is checked against. **Define the catalogue once:** the role-by-activity acceptance matrix (Documentation) needs the same list of activities, so it should be generated from this catalogue rather than kept as a second list. **Where it sits:** it is part of the multi-shelter work (the spike just below): `roles` and the matrix are per `shelter_id` once tenancy lands, but the catalogue and enforcement can be built for one shelter first, and it is cheaper before seven shelters' data exists than after. **The Senior Staff item (Auth) should be decided against this:** if the matrix is coming soon, Senior Staff is better as the first shelter-defined role than as a new enum value that later has to be migrated out. Settle the order with Lutan before either is built. **Deliverable:** `docs/roles-and-permissions.md` with the full activity catalogue (every page and action, from the manual's topics and the current predicates and policies), the default matrix, the non-configurable rules, the data model (`roles`, `role_permissions`, `user_roles.role_id`, tenancy columns), the enforcement and caching choice and a migration path from the enum. Agree it with Lutan, record it in `docs/decisions/`, then schema PRs and the build in stages: catalogue and enforcement first, behaviour unchanged; then the Settings matrix; then custom roles.
- [x] **Recurring-job eligibility asks about another person's role, which `can()` cannot answer: add `role_can(role_key, activity, level)` (schema), then convert `canDoJob`.** Found by `permissions-catalogue`, 2026-10-03. `src/lib/recurring-jobs/eligibility.ts` decides whether an *assignee* can do a job that links to `/stocktake` or `/deliveries`; `can()` reads the caller's own cells and `role_permissions` is admin-read-only (`0132`), so those two rules keep `STOCK_COUNT_ROLES` / `STOCK_DELIVERY_ROLES`, equal to the seed and held there by `scripts/check-permission-catalogue.mjs`. A `security definer` function that answers for any role (readable by Management, who set up rotas), an RPC the form and `my-tasks` can call, and `canDoJob` taking its answer instead of a role string would remove the last stock role list and is needed before any sweep touches `eligibility.ts` (its `/management`, `/admin` and `/maintenance` rules are the same shape). Two units: the function is a schema PR; the app side follows it. See `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-permissions-catalogue.md`.
  **Status 2026-10-03 (`claude/schema-role-can`): the function is in (`0133_role_can.sql`, applied to dev, `scripts/check-role-can.mjs`), not ticked.** Callable by a role holding `recurring.manage` (so Management and Admin), by anyone about their own role, and by the service role; anyone else is refused. Left: the app half, `role-can-app` (RPC wiring, `canDoJob`, the form picker), with or before the first sweep that touches `eligibility.ts`. See `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-role-can.md`.
  **Done 2026-10-04 (`claude/role-can-app`): the app half.** `canDoJob` takes an `Eligibility` built from `role_can()` by `loadEligibility`; the form, the save check, the stranded warning and My tasks read it; `STOCK_COUNT_ROLES` / `STOCK_DELIVERY_ROLES` are gone and the stock rules come from the route registry. Both units are in, nothing else in the item remains. The shape the other three rules copy is in `docs/decisions/2026-10-04-role-can-app.md`.
  **Status 2026-10-03 (`claude/roles-and-permissions-design`, PR #323): `docs/roles-and-permissions.md` is written; the design is proposed and not agreed; not ticked.** It has every part the deliverable lists: the catalogue (55 activities, seeded from the acceptance matrix's 93 rows and audited against the 243 policies, 15 functions and 10 views that name a role on dev, and every page guard), the default cells for today's roles, the rules nobody can configure, scopes kept apart from cells (now including "who and where" for a resident), the data model with its tenancy column, the enforcement choice (a live `has_permission()` lookup, not the token hook, because archiving a person must stay immediate), and Lutan's page-per-role steer answered (single pages with access or no access, keyed to a task rather than a role name). **The migration from the enum is role by role, as Lutan asked on 2026-10-03** (§12): a foundation, then the volunteer narrowed, then each new role borrowing that narrowest set of rights and granted only its own job through `has_permission()`, then today's roles converted, then the enum dropped, then the Settings matrix and roles a shelter adds. **The parity check is specified, not built** (§11): it needs the catalogue file and tables that belong to the next stream. **The audit found** eight powers the 93 rows do not describe, one the manual promises and the system refuses, and twelve groups of cases where the database allows more than any screen. **Decided by Lutan, 2026-10-03: configured roles** (`docs/decisions/2026-10-03-configured-roles-not-enum-values.md`), so this item's matrix goes ahead in its stages: enforcement first, then the Settings screen, then roles a shelter adds. **Asks of Lutan:** L8 (default cells are what the screens offer today, and the wider database grants are closed as tables are converted), L9 (live lookup), L10 (`0132`), L12 (the order of the roles). **No decision file records the remaining details** (L8, L9, L12), because nobody has agreed them; the fork and Lutan's answers on who does what at Lanna each have their own. Pieces for `/plan-day` are in §15.
- [ ] **Spike: can the app hold several shelters in one database? Measure it, do not estimate it.** Lutan, 2026-09-28: seven shelters have shown interest — five in Chiang Mai, two elsewhere in Thailand — and the system would be **a gift to all of them**, so the ongoing cost must be as close to zero as possible (Lutan carries his own time and Claude costs, the shelters carry their own domains). **That constraint decides the architecture.** A silo (one Supabase project per shelter) cannot meet it: the free tier allows two projects per organisation and pauses a project after a week idle, so seven shelters means seven paid projects, forever, for a gift. Pooling — one database, a `shelter_id` on every table — keeps the marginal cost of each shelter at zero, because it adds no new services: the same two projects, the same Worker, the same Pi origin, the same weekly backup. **So the decision is effectively made; what is not known is the cost of getting there, and that is what this item buys.** Do it on a throwaway branch and do not merge it: add `shelter_id`, a `current_user_shelter_id()` helper (shaped like `current_user_vet_id()` from `0102`), and tenancy as **restrictive** policies — the pattern `0100` already uses for the `aal2` check on `user_roles`, where an `as restrictive` policy is AND-ed with every existing permissive one. That means roughly **one restrictive policy per table (~45), not a rewrite of all 181**. Then push it through the parts restrictive policies do not save you: **53 `security definer` functions**, which bypass RLS by design and each need their own tenant check, and **58 views**. Report a real number of hours and a list of the places that fought back. **Design the spike around the anonymous tier specifically** — the `public_*` views are served to signed-out visitors, so a tenancy miss there publishes another shelter's animals to the internet. That exact class of miss has already happened twice in this codebase at a smaller scale: `0101` closed the public gallery and the photo proxy and profile photos still leaked through three other views until `0103`, and #182 gated five route trees while a vet could still write the `vets` table through the API until `0105`. **Not before Lanna is live** — go-live is still gated on the Pi origin, the domain cutover and the AppSheet cutover, and one running reference shelter is what turns seven interested into seven committed. But **pre-production is the cheapest this will ever be**: adding a tenant column when one shelter has data is far cheaper than when seven do. Record the outcome in `docs/decisions.md` whichever way it goes. Note what is being given up: one database means one blast radius, so a bad migration takes all seven down at once where a silo could roll shelter by shelter. **The product needs its own name and domain (Lutan, 2026-09-28).** Once more than one shelter uses it, the app is no longer Lanna's, so it should not live on `lannacare.org` — that stays Lanna's public site, and each shelter keeps its own domain for its own public pages. Choose a name for the application itself and register a matching **`.org`** to host the staff app on (e.g. `app.<name>.org`, with the shelters' domains or subdomains pointing at the same Worker). **The whole project is in honour of Cooper, Lutan's dog who died recently**, so the name should carry him — shortlist a few Cooper-based `.org` names, check which are free (Cloudflare Registrar, where `lannacare.org` already is, sells at cost), and let Lutan choose. *Shortlist checked 2026-09-28 against the .org registry (RDAP), all unregistered that day: cooperscare.org, coopercare.org, coopershaven.org, cooperskeeper.org, coopersheart.org, coopersden.org, cooperspaws.org, friendsofcooper.org, cooperslegacy.org, cooperscircle.org. Already taken: coopercares, coopersfriends, coopersrescue, coopersplace, teamcooper. Re-check at checkout; a registry premium price shows only there.* Register it through Cloudflare so DNS, Email Routing and the Worker routes sit alongside the existing domain. Decide the name before the tenancy build, because it goes into the Worker routes, auth redirect URLs (Supabase and Google OAuth), release mail's sender and the manual.
- [ ] **Per-shelter Drive: each shelter keeps its photos in its own Google account.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28. Do **not** pool the photos even if the database is pooled. Each shelter uses their own Google account — 15GB free each — so storage cost never lands on Lutan and never grows without bound, and the photos live in an account the shelter owns, which matters if they ever leave or the arrangement ends. That makes Drive **per-shelter configuration, not per-shelter infrastructure**: a folder id and OAuth credentials keyed by `shelter_id` rather than the single set of env vars `src/lib/google/drive.ts` reads today. **The risk this creates is the one that has already bitten twice, multiplied by seven.** A Drive refresh token dying breaks that shelter's uploads silently until someone tries to add a photo: on 2026-09-25 the `lanna-care-dev` consent screen was still in *Testing*, so the token expired after 7 days and every upload on lannacare.org failed; a 90-day expiry is set somewhere and still unlocated (see Deployment). With seven shelters this stops being an incident and becomes a recurring support load. **Most of the fix already exists** — `scripts/check-drive-token.mjs`, the Drive-connected indicator on `/admin`, and the status-alert mailer from `0098` (#174). Extend the alerting to check each shelter's token and mail before a shelter notices, and treat that as a **prerequisite of onboarding shelter #2, not a follow-up**. Also decide where per-shelter OAuth credentials live: Worker secrets do not scale to seven, so most likely the database, and then how they are protected.

Two groups: tables that already have a `_perm` policy (just drop `admin_all_*`; `has_permission()` admits Admin, parity unchanged) and the Admin-only tables with no cell (`user_roles`, `roles`, `role_permissions`, `permission_activities`, `audit_log`, `site_content*`, `site_pages`, `translatable_fields`), which need an enum-free admin rule (§6). Extend the checker to count `'vet'`, `'admin'`, `'volunteer'` and bare `current_user_role()` in policy text, one `OWNERS` entry per table, so "done" cannot drift again. Parity unchanged, 1,926 / 24 / 21.]
