- [x] **`perm-convert-vet` (schema; BLOCKED on a decision of Lutan's).** 71 `vet_*` policies on 29 tables (counted in `pg_policies` on dev, 2026-10-07; the migrations show 62) plus the vet-reading functions and views. Converting makes the Director's draft real: today the vet's cells are cosmetic because the `vet_*` policies never ask them. **The question: do vets keep their reference-data cells while on hold (the paper's 13), or is the paper rewritten to the draft's one (the microchip)?** The 21 mismatch lines on the parity board are exactly this, and `check-permission-tables` stays `HARNESS-KNOWN-RED` until it is answered. Also decide whether a vet's clinic limits become the scopes of §5 one for one. `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-admin-role.md` §3. **CLOSED 2026-10-07 — DECLINED, not built (Lutan, in chat, confirming the decision brief's q4).** The question this item was blocked on is answered: *do not convert; leave vets exactly as they are* (option B, the Director with Lutan; recorded in full under *Roles build, foundation 3*). So the `vet_*` policies keep naming the role, the vet's cells stay cosmetic, and the 21 vet lines on the parity board and `check-permission-tables`'s `HARNESS-KNOWN-RED` are a recorded decision. **Do not tidy them.** **The cost, so it is not forgotten:** `perm-drop-enum` needs every table converted, so it, `settings-permission-matrix` and `custom-roles` stay parked behind this. **To reopen:** on the day vets are wanted in the system, untick this, convert, and the chain unblocks. Start from `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-admin-role.md` §3 rather than re-deriving it.
- [ ] **Ask the Chiang Mai five whether they want one regional adoption listing or five separate sites.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28. This is a product question that decides architecture more firmly than hosting cost does, and it is cheap to ask now and expensive to discover later. Five of the seven interested shelters are in Chiang Mai. Either they want **five separate sites** — each its own brand, adoption page and donors — or they want **one regional network**: a combined "dogs available in Chiang Mai" listing, Shelter Friends shared across shelters, and vet clinics entered once rather than five times (they will largely use the same clinics). The second is the stronger mission outcome — more animals seen by more people — and under a pooled database it is **nearly free to build**, where under a silo it would need a data warehouse on top. If even two of the five want the combined listing, pooling wins outright and the spike above becomes urgent rather than exploratory. Ask before the spike reports, so its answer can shape what the spike measures. Record what they say in `docs/decisions.md`.

- [x] **Public website photos load slowly: serve sized copies, cached on the Pi, not the originals.** Lutan, 2026-10-01. **Done 2026-10-01** by `claude/public-photo-sizing` (decisions/2026-10-01-public-photo-sizing.md): widths 160/400/1200, Pi disk cache capped at 1 GiB; the phone-from-Thailand timing is Lutan's. Seen on project pages (`/our-work`, `/our-work/[id]`); **scope is every public-facing image** (Lutan, same day): project cards and galleries, resident photos on `/adopt`, `/adopt/[id]`, `/r/` and the home page cards, pet of the week, the hero and site gallery, recent adoptions, adoption updates shown publicly, and Shelter Friend logos. Staff-only photos inside the app stay as they are for now. **Likely cause, from the code:** every image is the full-size original. `driveImageUrl(fileId)` (`src/lib/google/drive-client.ts`) is just `/api/photos/<fileId>` with no size, the photo route streams Drive's `alt=media` bytes as uploaded, and `next.config.ts` sets `images.unoptimized: true`, so `next/image` does no resizing either. A project card (`ProjectCard.tsx`), the story gallery's main image and **every thumbnail in its strip** (`StoryGallery.tsx` → `ThumbnailStrip`) each download a phone-camera original, often 3–10 MB, so a story with twenty photos can be over 100 MB before anyone taps anything. Every other public image uses the same route, so the same fix covers them all. **Measure first** (a real phone, from Thailand): bytes and time for `/our-work` and one story, cold and warm, so the fix can be shown to work. **Fix:** let the photo route take a width (e.g. `/api/photos/<id>?w=400`, only from a small fixed set such as 160 / 400 / 1200 so the cache cannot be flooded with sizes), served from Drive's own rendition through the existing `drive.downloadThumbnail(fileId, size)` (`src/lib/google/drive.ts`, today used only by the deceased-archive PDF), which also turns phone HEIC into JPEG. Fall back to the original if Drive has no rendition yet. **Keep the sized copies on the Pi (Lutan, 2026-10-01):** now that the Pi serves the site, the route writes each generated size to a disk cache on its SSD (e.g. `<cache dir>/<fileId>/<width>.jpg`) and serves it from there next time, so Drive is asked for a given size once rather than on every edge-cache miss. Small files make the Pi's home upload a non-issue for thumbnails and cards. Rules: Cloudflare's edge cache stays in front (the Pi is the origin, not a replacement); only files `is_public_drive_file` approves are written to disk, so internal attachments never land there; the cache is disposable (no backup, safe to empty, size-capped with oldest-first eviction), and Drive stays the source of truth; when a photo is replaced or deleted in the app, its cached sizes are removed (or the cache key includes something that changes, such as Drive's `modifiedTime`). When the Worker is serving without the Pi (fallback), it skips the disk and goes to Drive as today. Cache each size under its own key with the same public/private rules the route already applies (`is_public_drive_file`, `Cache-Control`). Then give `driveImageUrl` a size argument and use it on every public page listed above: thumbnails ~160, cards ~400, the gallery's main image ~1200 with a `srcset`, and a "view original" only where someone actually wants it. Keep `loading="lazy"` off-screen and `priority` only on the first image. Check the edge and Pi caching still apply per size, and that a staff-only photo is still refused when signed out at every size. **This takes in most of the Pi spike below:** it answers the spike's question (2) and builds its pull-through cache, for sized copies. Afterwards the spike only needs to measure whether full-size originals are worth caching on the Pi too. A releases line (visitors and staff will notice faster pages). No schema.
- [x] **Spike: would serving the published photos from the Pi make them load faster? Measure before building.** **Subsumed 2026-10-01** by the sized-photos item above (sized copies + the Pi disk cache are built; no one should run this spike). Lutan, 2026-09-30, once the Pi was serving the site. Today `/api/photos/<fileId>` (`src/app/api/photos/[fileId]/route.ts`) fetches a public photo from Drive through the authenticated API and caches it 24 hours at Cloudflare's edge and in the browser; only a cold miss costs a Drive round trip. Under `next start` on the Pi there is no edge Cache API (the route feature-detects it), so the Worker's own caching is what covers repeat views. The idea is to hold public photos on the Pi's SSD so a miss never waits on Drive. **Questions the spike answers first:** (1) where the time goes today, on a cold photo and a warm one, from Thailand and from elsewhere: Drive fetch, the tunnel hop, or the image size itself (are photos resized, or served at full upload size?); (2) whether a smaller image or a resized variant fixes most of it without touching hosting. **The caution:** a photo served from the Pi leaves through the home connection's upload, which is likely slower than Cloudflare's edge, so the Pi should be the origin behind the edge cache and never a replacement for it. **If it goes ahead, keep the design small:** a disposable pull-through cache on the SSD (Drive stays the source of truth, nothing is uploaded to the Pi), only for files `is_public_drive_file` approves so internal attachments never land on disk, no backup of it, and safe to empty at any time. It should also fit the per-shelter Drive item above rather than assume one Drive. Success is a measured drop in photo load time on a real phone connection, not a Drive request count.
## Documentation

- [x] **A role-by-activity matrix that becomes the acceptance checklist the shelter signs.** [done 2026-10-02 as `claude/acceptance-matrix`: `node scripts/acceptance-matrix.mjs` generates it from the manual, the role walkthrough and the Admin-on-mobile decision (93 rows from 67 topics, 7 role columns, EN/TH × phone/desktop boxes per row, a sheet per role, failures list and sign-off lines) and `npm run lint` fails when a manual topic has no entry; the generated copy is not committed, the signed edition is; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-acceptance-matrix.md`. **Not built: the printable A4 PDF with cover page and Thai fonts, and the "agree who tests which role / blocker vs accepted" step before the first run** — follow-up filed on the `backlog` branch.] Lutan, 2026-10-02. **Purpose:** user acceptance testing where every feature is tried by a real user **in the role that uses it**, in **Thai and English**, on **phone and desktop** as each activity is really done, ending in one document the shelter signs to say the system does what they need. **Build it from what already exists, so it stays true as features land:** (a) the manual already lists every activity and which roles it is for (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`: 81 topics, 55 tagged with `roles`, the rest for everyone, read through `isForRole`), so **generate** the matrix from it with a script (e.g. `scripts/acceptance-matrix.mjs`) rather than typing it, and fail loudly when a topic has no matrix entry; (b) the role walkthrough (`docs/role-walkthrough.md`, Passes 0-6) supplies the "must **not** be able to" lines per role, which matter as much as the positive ones; (c) the "Admin on mobile" decision (`docs/decisions/2026-09-24-admin-on-mobile-which-settings-and-management-pages-belong.md`: field-needed / nice-to-have / desktop-only per screen) says which device each activity is meant for. The roles are the app's own: admin, management, staff, vet, volunteer, public viewer, and signed-out visitor for the public site. **Shape of the matrix:** one row per activity (e.g. "register a new resident", "record a vet visit", "count stock", "add a Shelter Friend"), one column per role marked **does** / **must not** / **n/a**, and for each "does" cell the cases to test: **language** (each activity once in the tester's working language, plus a full Thai pass and a full English pass across the whole matrix, so untranslated text and Thai layout problems are caught) and **device** (phone for field activities, desktop for setup and reporting, both where the decision says both). Each case has: what to do, the expected result in plain words, pass / fail, the tester's name and date, and a notes column for what they saw. Keep each case to one line a shelter worker can follow without a developer. **The sign-off document:** printable (a PDF generated from the same source, A4, Thai and English side by side or one edition each; the summary PDF's Thai fonts in `src/lib/archive/fonts/` already cover Thai), with a cover page (system version from `/releases`, environment tested on, dates), one section per role signed by the shelter person who tested it, a list of failures and how each was resolved or accepted, and a final sign-off line for the Director. Version it: a signed edition is kept as `docs/uat/acceptance-<date>.md` plus the PDF, and any later change to a signed activity starts a new edition rather than editing the old one. Distinct from `docs/test-plans/` (per feature, developer-facing) and `docs/release-smoke-test.md` (per release, ten minutes): this is per milestone, shelter-facing, and is what go-live waits on. **Before the first run:** agree with Lutan and the Director who tests which role (the walkthrough's two-account role-switching works for one tester; real UAT wants a real person per role where possible), give each tester an account in that role on the test or UAT site with disposable data, and decide what counts as a blocker versus an accepted issue. Ties in with the Thai edition of the manual (Documentation), since Thai testers will read it, and with the multi-shelter work, where a new shelter would sign the same checklist.

Not needed by `fable-dry-run`, which walks the Markdown.
- [ ] **Fable dry run of the acceptance checklist: functionality and ease of use, ending in a report.** Lutan, 2026-10-02. **Depends on** the acceptance matrix (the item just above): run it once the matrix exists, before real shelter users do, so they meet fewer problems and their time goes on judging the system rather than tripping over bugs. **Who and where:** a Claude session on the Fable model (`claude-fable-5-1`, as the architecture review item does), driving the app in the browser on **`test.lannacare.org` or a local dev server only** (dev database, disposable data; never production), signing in as each role in turn with test accounts (the walkthrough's role-switching mechanic: one admin account changes a test account's role between passes, then a hard refresh). Best done after test is served from the Pi (Deployment), since 1102 errors on the Worker would make the run report the platform rather than the app. **Two lenses on every checklist line:** (1) **Functionality:** did the activity work as the expected result says, did each "must not" stay refused (and refused politely, inside the app), did the data persist after a reload, in Thai and English, on a phone width and on desktop. Pass / fail with what happened. (2) **Ease of use:** could a non-technical shelter worker do it without help: how many steps and screens it took, whether they had to know where to look first (the Shelter Friend setup is the known example), whether labels and messages are plain (in both languages), whether the next step was obvious, whether anything on a phone was too small to tap or needed sideways scrolling, and whether errors said what to do. Rate each on a short scale (e.g. easy / some friction / hard) with one sentence of why. **The report:** `docs/uat/dry-run-<date>.md` (under `docs/uat/`, not `docs/test-plans/`), with an executive summary Lutan and the Director can read in five minutes, the matrix with each line's functionality result and ease-of-use rating, screenshots of every failure and every "hard" rating, and a **findings table** ranked by severity (blocks a role / wrong result / friction / polish), each finding written as a ready-to-paste backlog item (title, where, what happened, suggested fix). It also notes lines the run could not test (needs a real phone, real scanner, real Thai reader) so the shelter's run covers them. **After it:** Lutan reviews the report; the findings he accepts go onto the `backlog` branch as items under their headings (not raised automatically), and the matrix is corrected wherever the dry run shows an expected result was wrong. **The dry run never signs anything:** it is a rehearsal, the shelter's sign-off stays with the people who use the system.
  **Status 2026-10-03 (staff role only, `claude/dry-run-staff`, PR #314):** the staff sheet was run phone-first in English and Thai with a desktop sweep; report at `docs/uat/dry-run-2026-10-03-staff.md` with 22 findings ranked by severity (none raised yet; Lutan reviews) and the corrections the checklist needs. Volunteer, vet, management, admin, public viewer and signed-out visitor remain, one run each; item stays open.

the Residents list now names the clinic and shows only that clinic's residents (`0108`); another clinic's vet visits, prescriptions, procedures and blood tests are visible but **read-only**, and Edit says so (`0110`); the vet-visit form offers only the vet's own clinic and a doctor from the managed roster (`0102`, `claude/vet-visit-form-scoping`); a prescription cannot link to a future visit (`0107`) and a weight cannot attach to a visit that already has one (`0106`); the photo upload has no folder picker for a vet and the route answers 403 to anything but Medical; `/manual` and `/releases` now open on the reader's role. **Pass 0 — two changes, both blocking:** (a) add a step to **set the vet account's clinic** at Settings → Security (`user_roles.vet_id`, `0102`) and to check the test resident has a visit, prescription, procedure or blood test from that clinic — without both, `0108` means the vet sees no residents at all and the pass dead-ends on its first line; (b) the "create a recurring job assigned to the test account" step can no longer be done for a vet — `claude/recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees` removed vets from the picker, so the seed that exposed findings 5 and 6 is impossible by design. Rewrite it to seed the job for the Staff and Volunteer passes and to confirm, as a vet line, that the vet is *not* offered. **Not in scope:** Passes 2–6 were never run, so leave them as they are; this is the Vet pass and the Pass 0 lines it depends on. **Also worth doing while here:** every vet fix's `docs/test-plans/*.md` reads `Manual verification by: pending`, and pending on precisely these lines, so the rewritten pass should name which plans a completed run lets someone sign (`vet-scope-navigation`, `vet-clinic-resident-scope`, `vet-cross-clinic-writes`, `vet-visit-form-scoping`, `vet-doctors-roster-screen`, `recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees`, `prescription-no-future-visit`, `one-weight-per-visit`, `role-based-manual`, `release-notes-by-role`, `medical-photos-profile`, `mobile-date-clear`, `file-type-icon`). Docs only, no schema, no `src/` change. **Still not fixed and not this item's job:** finding 17, Cloudflare 1102 on the test site — `ORIGIN_HOST` is still `""` in all three environments of `wrangler.jsonc` and the Pi is deferred to 2026-10-02, so a full run may still be cut short. Finding 6's fix reduces the load (a refusal no longer renders the public home page) but does not remove the mechanism. **Done 2026-09-30** (`claude/walkthrough-vet-pass-update`): most of it landed in #225; this stream added what #227 changed (Appointments replaces My tasks, `/my` redirects) and a marked-pending note for the microchip feature.

- [ ] **Review the manual against real use.** Have a staff member and a volunteer follow it on a phone for a day and note every step that didn't match, was missing (e.g. what to do when an upload fails, how to update a vet visit's status), or used a word the shelter doesn't. Fold the answers back into `en.ts` and re-run the screenshot script.
- [ ] **Thai edition of the manual.** A second content file (`src/lib/manual/th.ts`, same `Manual` shape) picked by locale in `src/app/manual/page.tsx`, with screenshots captured with the app switched to Thai (the script would need a locale flag and a `public/manual/th/` output folder). Wait until the English text has been through the review above so it's translated once.
- [x] **Manual as a printable PDF.** Volunteers without a login (and the office wall) want a paper copy. `@react-pdf/renderer` is already a dependency for the resident summary, so a `/manual/pdf` route that renders the same `Manual` data is the natural route; the Thai fonts under `src/lib/archive/fonts/` cover the Thai edition.
- [x] **Give the manual's table of contents its own scroll bar.** Customer request 2026-09-24: on `/manual` the Contents list and the text scroll together, so on a long page the contents are out of reach and anything below the fold of the list can only be reached by scrolling the whole page. The desktop `<aside>` in `src/app/manual/page.tsx` is already `lg:sticky lg:top-6` but has no height limit, so once the list is taller than the window it just scrolls away with the page. Cap it to the viewport (e.g. `lg:max-h-[calc(100dvh-…)]` allowing for the app header, plus `lg:overflow-y-auto` and `overscroll-contain`) so the contents stay put with their own scroll bar while the sections scroll beside them. Check sticky actually engages — if the app shell scrolls an inner container rather than the window, the offset and height must be measured against that — and consider scrolling the active entry into view in the list. The phone `<details>` version is unaffected. Update the manual's own description of the Contents panel in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` if it has one.
- [x] **Deep links into the manual land short of their topic.** Found 2026-09-24 while verifying `claude/manual-toc-scroll`: a cold load of `/manual#getting-help` on dev (1400×900) ended with the section 1279px below the top of the window. The manual's screenshots are `loading="lazy"` `<img>`s with no `width`/`height` (`Screenshot` in `src/app/manual/page.tsx`), so they load after the browser's jump to the anchor and push the text down. Give each screenshot its intrinsic size (store `width`/`height` in `ManualScreenshot`, or read them in `scripts/manual-screenshots.mjs` when capturing) so the space is reserved, then recheck a deep link cold. Pre-existing; the Contents list itself highlights the right entry.
- [x] **Four manual screenshots are missing from `public/manual/`.** Found 2026-09-25 by `claude/manual-deep-links`: `diet-new.png`, `management-diets.png`, `management-cashflow.png` and `admin-blood-test-types.png` are referenced in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` but 404, so those topics show a broken image. `scripts/manual-screenshots.mjs` has no entry at all for `diet-new` or `management-cashflow`, so the planned full re-run will not fix those two by itself — add them to `SIGNED_IN` first (`diet-new` needs a resident id, like `prescription-new`).
- [x] **The last topics in the manual cannot scroll to the top of the window.** Found 2026-09-25 by `claude/manual-deep-links`: `/manual#getting-help` (the last topic) lands about 800px down a 900px window because the page ends 100px below it and is already at maximum scroll. Closing it means blank space under the manual (e.g. bottom padding of about a screen height minus the last topic), which is a design call. The image-shift half of the old deep-link item is fixed.
- [x] **Release notes as an accordion: version and title first, details on click.** Done 2026-09-24: each release is a `<details>`, newest open; `OpenReleaseFromHash` opens the release a `#v<version>` link targets; no Expand all yet (three releases; `docs/decisions.md`). Customer request 2026-09-24. `/releases` (`src/app/releases/page.tsx`) renders every release in full — version, title, date, Major and environment badges, then every note — so it grows by a screenful with each release (0.2.0 alone has seven long notes) and older releases are only reached by scrolling past everything newer. Make each release a collapsed row showing **version · title**, the date and the badges, which expands to its notes on click. Native `<details>`/`<summary>` is enough — keyboard and screen-reader accessible with no client JavaScript, so the page stays a server component; style the marker as a chevron that turns when open. Decide while building: (a) whether the **newest release starts expanded** (probably yes — it is the one people come to read) with the rest collapsed; (b) the existing `id="v<version>"` anchors must still land on an **open** release, since links such as `/releases#v0.2.0` would otherwise point at a closed `<details>` with its notes hidden — open the targeted one (a small client effect reading `location.hash`, including on `hashchange`); (c) the dev-only "Not released yet" block stays expanded as it is; (d) optionally an Expand all / Collapse all control once there are enough releases to want it. Check it on a phone, where the badges already wrap under the title. The manual's release-notes topic gets a line saying to click a release to see what changed, and the PR adds its own line to `unreleased`.

- [x] **A role-based manual: show each role the manual for what it can do.** Done 2026-09-27 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/manual` opens on the reader's role; other roles' topics are hidden until found (Find on page and #anchors reveal them, greyed), a section with none of the reader's topics goes with its intro, and Show everything (`?view=all`) brings the rest back greyed. Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. The manual already knows the answer — every topic carries `roles` in `src/lib/manual/en.ts`, and the page shows them as badges — but it shows everybody everything, so a vet reads intake, stocktake, cashflow and Settings to find the seven medical topics that apply to them. Filter `/manual` to the signed-in role by default. **Keep a way to see the rest**, a "show everything" toggle, because "can I do this?" is a real question and an empty search result is a worse answer than a greyed one. Two things to get right: topics with no `roles` mean *everyone* and must not vanish; and the section intros describe whole sections ("For the management and admin roles…") so a filtered view needs the section to disappear too when nothing in it survives. Worth pairing with the vet-scope item — as the vet's menu shrinks, a manual that still explains Enclosures is actively wrong.
- [x] **Release notes segmented by role.** Done 2026-09-28 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/releases` opens on the reader's role with the manual's `isForRole`; every release stays listed with its number, and one with nothing for the role carries a "Nothing for Vet" badge and says so; `?view=all` shows the rest greyed. A note may now be `{ text, roles }`; released history stays untagged, which reads as everyone. The release mail stays unfiltered. Raised by Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. Nice to have, and Lutan flagged the difficulty in raising it. A vet reading `/releases` gets stocktake, cashflow and deploy-tooling entries that will never mean anything to them. Tagging each note with the roles it affects and filtering the page the same way the manual would is the obvious shape. **The hard part is versions, not tags:** releases are numbered whole-app, so a vet filtering the list would see `0.7.0` followed by `0.4.0` with gaps where nothing touched them, which reads like a broken page. Options are showing every version with "nothing for you in this one", or dropping version numbers from the filtered view and showing dated changes. Also the release mail: it goes to admins, so it is not affected, but any per-role notes work should decide whether that stays true. Low priority — do the manual first, it has the same shape and more value.
- [x] **Dependabot PR #264 is stale enough to fail CI for reasons that are not its own — bring it up to date or close it.** Noticed 2026-10-07. *"Bump the actions group across 1 directory with 2 updates"*, `dependabot/github_actions/actions-901392d03b`, open long enough that its branch predates several checks that now exist on `main`. Concretely it does **not** contain the `public_impact_figures` allowlist entry from #420, so `public-views` will fail on it the moment anything triggers a run, for a reason that has nothing to do with bumping action versions. **Why it matters more than one stale PR:** a red check on a dependency PR is how a security update gets ignored, and 2026-10-07 already showed what a red check nobody can explain costs — the release manager lost 45 minutes to one. **What to do:** rebase it on `main` (or comment `@dependabot rebase`), see whether CI is then genuinely green, and either merge it or close it with a reason. The actions in `.github/workflows/` are currently pinned by SHA with a version comment, so check what the bump actually changes and keep the pinning style. If it is closed rather than merged, say why here, because Dependabot will raise it again. → *Documentation* **Done 2026-10-07 (`claude/dependabot-264`):** the bump (`actions/checkout` v4 → v7.0.1, `actions/setup-node` v4 → v7.0.0, still pinned by SHA, both SHAs checked against the upstream tags) is carried on this branch with a test plan, and #264 is closed as superseded: Dependabot closes its own PR once `main` has the new versions. The item's prediction was wrong. PR CI runs on the merge commit with `main`, so #264's age never mattered and `public-views` passed. The only red was `test-plan`, because a bot PR cannot carry a plan, and every future Dependabot PR will hit the same thing. See `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-dependabot-prs-carried-on-a-branch.md`.

## Public website

- [x] **Our work: make a project's Thai title easy to find and hard to forget.** *(Done 2026-10-06, `claude/project-thai-title`: the create form already had the Thai field; the folder's About panel now shows the title and Edit details edits it, a published folder without one says so, Settings → Website marks it, and Management → Translations carries a note listing them instead of rows. Category chips and photo captions confirmed already fine. Not driven in a browser; see the test plan.)* Lutan, 2026-10-05: on the public `/our-work` pages a project folder's name shows only in English, and he asked for a Thai option. **It already exists but is hidden:** `project_folders.name_th` (0034) is published as `title_th` through `public_projects` (0042, 0056), and `/our-work` and `/our-work/[id]` already show it to a visitor reading Thai (`src/lib/projects/public.ts`, `localized(locale, title, title_th)`), falling back to English when it is empty. The only place to type it is the folder's **Rename** form on Projects → *the folder* (`src/app/projects/[id]/FolderView.tsx`, "Thai name"), which nobody would think to open to add a translation, and nothing says it is missing. So the English shows because the Thai was never entered. **Build:** (1) put the **Thai title** beside the English one wherever a project is described, not only under Rename: the folder's info panel with the story, date and location, and the create-folder form; (2) when a folder is **published** (`is_public`) with no Thai title, say so on the folder ("Thai visitors see the English title") and in Settings → Website's list of published projects; (3) add missing Thai project titles to Management → **Translations** as their own rows, so the people who translate the story see the title too, even though the title stays a paired column rather than a `translations` row (the 2026-09-21 rule: labels are paired columns, prose goes through the table); if that mixes two kinds of thing badly, a "titles missing Thai" note at the top of that page is enough; (4) check the same gap for **category names** on `/our-work`'s chips (they come from the dictionaries, so they should already be Thai; confirm) and for **photo captions** on a story (prose, so they should already be in the translation queue; confirm). No schema. Manual (Projects topic: the Thai title, and the warning), both dictionaries, a releases line. **Meanwhile**, Lutan can add Thai titles today: Projects → open the folder → **Rename** → *Thai name* → Save.
- [x] **Clickable links in the text of the website's pages (e.g. DonorBox on Donate).** Lutan, 2026-10-04. The shelter takes donations through **DonorBox** (donorbox.org) among other methods, and the Donate page's text names the DonorBox address, but it shows as plain text a visitor has to copy into the address bar. On a phone, which is how most visitors arrive, that usually means they don't. **Why it happens:** page bodies typed on Settings → Website are rendered by `SiteBody` (`src/components/SiteBody.tsx`) through `parseBody()` (`src/lib/site/body.ts`), which understands paragraphs, `## ` sub-headings and `- ` bullet lists only. Everything else, a URL included, is printed as text. **Build:** (1) **Bare URLs become links on their own.** Any `https://…` or `www.…` in a body is linked, so what is already typed (the DonorBox address) starts working without anyone re-editing it. Strip trailing punctuation the way a reader expects ("…donorbox.org/lanna." links without the full stop). (2) **Links with words**, so the page can say "Give monthly on DonorBox" rather than show the raw address. Use Markdown's `[Give monthly on DonorBox](https://donorbox.org/…)`, since `parseBody` already borrows Markdown's `## ` and `- `. Also allow an email address (`mailto:`) and a phone number (`tel:`), which are just as useful on a phone. (3) **Safe by construction:** build the link as a React element from the parsed parts, never as HTML from the text; accept only `https:`, `http:`, `mailto:`, `tel:` and paths on this site (`/adopt`, `/friends/join`), and print anything else (`javascript:`, `data:`) as plain text. A link to another site opens in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"` and a visible "opens in a new tab" cue for screen readers. Style it so it reads as a link on the cream background, with enough contrast in both themes and a tap target of at least 44 px on a phone. (4) **Everywhere `SiteBody` is used:** the information pages (Donate, Foster, Volunteer, How to adopt, International adoption, Become a Shelter Friend) and the home page's story, so one change covers them all. Decide while building whether the same rule should reach other public text that staff type: project stories on `/our-work/[id]`, resident bios on `/adopt/[id]` and Shelter Friend blurbs. Probably yes for project stories and friends, which are where an outside link is natural, but check each renderer and say which in the decision file. (5) **Translations:** a Thai version goes through the translation queue, so a translator must keep the address part of `[…](…)` unchanged. Say so in the translation editor's hint, and have the translation check refuse a Thai version whose links differ from the English. (6) **The editor:** a one-line hint under each body field on Settings → Website ("Paste a web address and it becomes a link. For your own words: [words](https://…)"), and a preview that shows the link working before Save. **Also, separately and worth asking:** a proper **Donate on DonorBox** button on `/donate` (and perhaps the header's Donate button pointing there), with the address as a Website setting rather than typed in the text, so the most important donation link is a big button and not a line in a paragraph. That overlaps the Fundraising items ("Make `/donate` ask for money", the per-donation QR paper), which should mention DonorBox now that the shelter is known to use it. No schema for the links themselves. Manual (the Website topic), both dictionaries for the hints, a decision file, a releases line (visitors will notice links that work), and a test plan covering a bare link, a worded link, an email, a phone number, a refused `javascript:` link and a Thai page.
- [x] **Settings → Website: let the shelter choose its preferred contact channel for the public site.** Lutan, 2026-09-28. The public pages lead with **LINE** wherever they offer one way to get in touch: the sticky "Ask on LINE" bar at the bottom of each animal's profile (`src/app/adopt/[id]/page.tsx`), the "get in touch" card on site pages (`SitePageView.tsx`, "Email us or message us on LINE"), the footer's Contact us (`PublicFooter.tsx`) and the order of the phone menu's "Talk to us" buttons (`PublicHeader.tsx` / `PublicNav.tsx`, "LINE first"). But Lanna actually use **Facebook Messenger, Instagram and WhatsApp**, and with the multi-shelter plan each shelter will have its own habits, so this belongs in a setting, not the code. **The links are already settings:** `site_content` holds `contact_line`, `messenger_url`, `whatsapp_number` and `instagram_url` (0080), edited and validated on Settings → Website (`SiteSettingsForm.tsx`, `src/lib/links/validate.ts`). What is missing is the choice. **Add:** a "Preferred way to contact us" picker on Settings → Website (LINE / Messenger / WhatsApp / Instagram / Phone / Email), offering only channels that have a value, stored as e.g. `site_content.preferred_channel` (a small `-schema` PR first; default `line` so nothing changes until someone picks). Consider an ordered list instead of one choice, so the second and third buttons follow the shelter's order too. **Use it everywhere LINE is hard-coded first:** the sticky bar's label and link ("Message us on Messenger", `m.me` link; WhatsApp opens `wa.me/<number>` with the message pre-filled as the email subject is today; Instagram opens the profile, since it has no pre-filled DM link), the get-in-touch card and its hint text, the footer and the Talk to us order. One helper (e.g. `preferredChannel(content)` returning label, href and icon) so every page asks the same question. Fall back to the next channel set, then phone, then email, if the chosen one is cleared. Text in en and th for each channel's call to action. **Multi-tenancy:** `site_content` is one row today; the tenancy spike's `shelter_id` makes it one per shelter, so a setting here is already per-tenant — no extra design needed, but list it among the tables the spike covers. Manual: the Website settings topic; a releases line (visitors see the button change). **Schema half done 2026-09-29 (migration `0111`, `claude/schema-contact-channel`):** an ordered list, `site_content.preferred_channels text[]` default `{line}` (first = preferred, the rest = button order and fallback), not a single `preferred_channel`; see `docs/decisions.md`. **Feature half done 2026-09-29 (`claude/contact-channel-picker`):** the picker on Settings → Website, `preferredChannels(content)` in `src/lib/site/channels.ts`, and the four public surfaces all converted; see `docs/decisions.md`.
- [x] **A "Pet relocation" information page on the public site.** Done 2026-09-27 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/relocation` is a `SitePageView` page with `PuppyFlying` (sketch B, crate, in pyjamas) beside the heading, under a new **Services** menu group (Lutan: services is its own tree, desexing drives to follow) and in the footer, with a line on `/adopt` for adopters abroad. Ships with generic starter text in English and Thai, shown until an admin saves a body and prefilled in the editor; no licence, timeline or price claims. Original item: Lutan, 2026-09-26: the Director is licensed to manage pet relocations, both international and within Thailand, and offers them as a service. The website should say so. **An information page, not a booking system:** no enquiry form, no case tracking, no payments. People get in touch through the contact channels the site already shows (LINE, phone, email, and Messenger / WhatsApp once that item lands). **Build it the way `/foster`, `/volunteer` and `/donate` are built:** a new `site_pages` slug (0059) rendered by `SitePageView`, with title and body typed by an admin on `/admin/website` and the Thai version going through the translation queue, so the Director can change the wording herself. `site_pages.slug` has a check constraint listing the five slugs (0059), so adding `relocation` (and seeding its empty row) is a tiny schema PR first. **Where it links from:** the redesign's menu (under "Get involved" or "About & contact"; agree which with Lutan) and the footer, plus a line on `/adopt` for overseas adopters ("Adopting from abroad? We can arrange the journey"). **Content to get from the Director** (the page ships with her text, not placeholder copy): what she offers (international and domestic moves, and which parts she handles: paperwork, vet checks and vaccinations, export permit, flights, road transport), her licence details (licence number and issuing body, and any wording the licence requires), typical timelines, whether it is for shelter adopters, the public or both, and how to get in touch. `generateMetadata` with Open Graph tags like the other public pages. A releases line. **Unblocked 2026-09-27 (Lutan): ship generic copy now, modelled on what a Thai pet-relocation service page covers** — e.g. Boonma (boonma.com), Thailand's oldest mover, whose pet service is one-stop: documents, vet checks and vaccinations, microchip, export permit, crate, flight booking, customs clearance at both ends, and door-to-door delivery, international and domestic. So the page no longer waits on the Director's copy, and the body is admin-editable anyway — she edits it herself when she has time. **Two things that do NOT become generic.** (a) **Write original text.** Do not copy Boonma's wording or anyone else's; describe what the service involves in our own words. (b) **Licence claims still come from her.** The generic "what's involved" content is safe, but a statement that *this* shelter or Director is licensed — licence number, issuing body, any wording the licence requires — is a factual claim about a real person and must not be invented or implied. Ship the page without a licence block, or with a placeholder only she fills, and leave "Licensed by…" out until she supplies it. Same for typical timelines and prices: omit rather than guess. Still a tiny schema PR first for the slug. **A flying caped puppy in the page heading** (Lutan, 2026-09-27): the same character as the loading animation — the apricot poodle-cross from `src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx` (#148) — in a cape, flying, as the hero illustration. It suits the subject: the dog that carries pets across the world. Add it as a third drawing beside `PuppyAtLaptop` and `PuppyGlyph` in that file, same approach (plain SVG, CSS keyframes in `globals.css`, redesign palette, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected — a still pose rather than a hover/flap loop), and it is then reusable anywhere else a "journey" illustration is wanted. **Keep it our own character, not a Superman pastiche.** A cape and a flying pose are generic and fine; Superman's costume (the S-shield, the red/blue/yellow scheme) and Krypto the Superdog (DC's white dog in a red cape) are DC's, so no shield, no chest emblem, and not that colourway — use our terracotta and forest green. Agree the drawing with Lutan the way the loader was agreed: two or three sketches first.
- [x] **A "Become a Shelter Friend" page for businesses that want to help.** Done 2026-09-27 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/friends/join` is a `SitePageView` page with generic starter text in both languages, under **Get involved** in the header and footer (Lutan: it is a way to help, not a service), linked from both homepage tiles, a line on `/friends` and the `/donate` Give in kind card. Its contact card adds Call, a prefilled email, and a prefilled LINE message when the LINE id is an Official Account. It explains what a Shelter Friend is, the ways to help (donating goods such as food, litter, medicine or building materials, giving supporters a discount, offering a service), what the business gets in return (logo and profile on `/friends` and in the homepage band, and which contact details are shown only with their agreement, per the opt-ins from 0076), and how to get in touch (LINE, phone, email, with an optional prefilled LINE or email message). **Link it from:** both homepage tiles, a line at the top of `/friends`, and the `/donate` "Give in kind" mention. **Schema:** `site_pages.slug` has a check constraint (0059), so the new slug needs a tiny schema PR. **Fold it into the same schema PR as the relocation page's slug.** Starter text from the Shelter Friends item's own description, then reworded by the Director. A releases line.

Icons are inline SVGs (`src/components/FacebookIcon.tsx`, `InstagramIcon.tsx`) because lucide-react 1.x has no brand icons, and there was no JSON-LD block, so no `sameAs`. Both fields ship empty until the customer supplies the links. Customer request 2026-09-22. The public footer (`src/app/adopt/PublicFooter.tsx`) already shows address, email, phone and a LINE link from `site_content`, but nothing points at the shelter's Facebook page, which is where most of their supporters actually are. Add `facebook_url` (and while there `instagram_url`, both nullable text) to `site_content` in a new migration, fields for them on the admin Website form (`/admin/website`, `StoryForm.tsx` / `actions.ts` — validate they are `https://` URLs on the right hosts), and in the footer render a small row of social icons (lucide `Facebook` / `Instagram`, `target="_blank" rel="noreferrer"`, `aria-label` from the dictionaries) after the LINE link, shown only for the URLs that are set. Distinct from the low-priority "Publish to Facebook from the app" item below, which is about posting, not linking.
- [x] **"Shelter Friends" — thank the businesses that help, on the public site.** Done 2026-09-24 (schema 0076 in #89, feature in `claude/shelter-friends`; see `docs/decisions.md`): `/friends` reads only `public_shelter_friends`; a Shelter Friend card on the contact hub (make / edit / opt-ins / publish / live preview), badge and filter chip on both contact lists, Management → Shelter Friends for the order; header/footer link, home logo strip and `/donate` mention appear once someone is published. The link validator is `src/lib/links/validate.ts` for the Facebook-link item to reuse. Thai title is a placeholder until the customer confirms it. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-24. Local companies help the shelter by donating goods (food, litter, medicine, building materials) or giving discounts (a vet clinic, a feed shop, a hardware store). The shelter wants to feature them publicly: who they are, what they do for the shelter, links to their website / Facebook page, and a map and contact details — both as a thank-you and so supporters can give them custom. **Build on contacts, don't duplicate them:** the business is already (or should be) a row in `contacts` — the Vendor it buys from, with phone, email, LINE, address and the resolved maps link from the contact-hub tidy-up — so a Friend is a contact with a public profile, not a second address book. Keep the curated public face in its own 1:1 table rather than more columns on `contacts`, so nothing private leaks by accident and the public view has an obvious boundary: **schema PR first** — `shelter_friends` (`contact_id` unique FK → `contacts`, `blurb` text through the translations table/queue like `site_pages`, `help_kind` (Donates goods / Discount / Services / Sponsorship — or free text, ask), `discount_note` e.g. "10% off for adopters, show your adoption card", `website_url`, `facebook_url`, `logo_drive_file_id` (Drive-hosted like the hero photo), `friend_since date`, `sort_order`, `published boolean default false`, and per-field opt-ins `show_phone` / `show_email` / `show_line` / `show_address` / `show_map`, all default false), admin/management RLS, and a `public_shelter_friends` view granted to `anon` that returns only published rows and only the opted-in contact fields — add it to `scripts/check-public-views.mjs`. A friend whose contact is archived (see "Archive contacts" under Management) drops out of the view. **Staff side:** a "Shelter Friend" card on `/contacts/[id]` (Make a Shelter Friend / edit profile / publish toggle / preview link), a Friend badge and filter chip on `/contacts` and `/management/contacts`, and a list on `/admin/website` (or Management → Shelter Friends) to reorder and unpublish. Validate URLs as `https://`, Facebook on facebook.com / fb.com — share the validator with the Facebook-link item above. **Contact type: stays `Vendor`** (decided 2026-09-24) — any business, including one that only donates, is entered as a Vendor; no enum change in this item. Keep the Friend profile keyed on `contact_id`, not on the type, so a type added later (e.g. `Business` / `Supporter`) can become a Shelter Friend with no further schema work — the Make a Shelter Friend action may be offered on Vendors only for now, but gate it in one place so widening it is a one-line change. **Public side:** `/friends` (title "Shelter Friends" / Thai to confirm with the customer) — a card per friend: logo, name, what they do for the shelter, discount note, website / Facebook icon links (`target="_blank" rel="noreferrer"`), phone / LINE tap-to-contact and the lazy-loaded map embed reused from the contact hub, only for what they opted into; linked from `PublicHeader` / `PublicFooter`, a small logo strip on `/` ("Thank you to our Shelter Friends"), and a mention on `/donate` ("give in kind — our friends' discounts go further"). `generateMetadata` with OG tags like the other public pages. **Consent:** the opt-in flags are the point — staff tick only what the business agreed to show, so the manual topic should say to ask first. **Link to Fundraising:** if donations are recorded later, an in-kind gift from a friend should be able to point at the same contact, so the two meet on `contact_id`. Manual topic, dictionary strings in both languages, a releases line and a `docs/decisions.md` entry (separate table vs. contact columns; why opt-in per field).

- [ ] **Publish to Facebook from the app (low priority, discuss first).** Raised 2026-09-22: could a resident profile, a project story or a "pet of the week" be posted to the shelter's Facebook Page (feed post, photo, or a 24-hour Story) from inside the app? Technically yes — the Meta Graph API publishes to a Page the app has been granted (`pages_manage_posts` + a long-lived Page token held as a Worker secret; Page Stories have their own photo/video endpoint; Instagram Business publishing is a sibling if the Page has an IG account). Personal profiles are not publishable by API. The cost is not code but Meta's App Review (screencast, business verification of the Page; `/privacy` already exists) and a token that needs refreshing. Before building anything, decide between: (a) no API at all — a **Share on Facebook** button on `/adopt/[id]` and `/our-work/[id]` that opens the share dialog pre-filled with the public URL (the OG tags are already there, so the card renders well) and staff post from their own account; (b) Meta Business Suite scheduling straight from the Page, no app changes; (c) the full integration — an Admin → Website action that composes a post from a resident or project (photo, bilingual caption from `translations`, link back) and publishes to the Page, with a log of what was posted. Start with (a) if anything; only go to (c) if staff are posting several times a week and the copy-paste is the pain.
- [x] **A deliberate delay on the way to the available dogs, to show off the puppy loader.** [done 2026-09-28: `SHOWCASE_LOADER_MS` env var, on (1800ms) in test/uat/production per Lutan — see `docs/decisions.md`] Lutan, 2026-09-27. Going from the home page to the dogs up for adoption (`/adopt`, linked from `src/app/page.tsx`) is usually fast enough that the puppy-at-a-laptop loader (`src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx`, root `src/app/loading.tsx`) never shows: it waits 300 ms before appearing so quick loads don't flash it. Add a short, deliberate pause on that page so people see the animation, e.g. 1.5–2 s. **How:** an `await` pause at the top of the `/adopt` server page (before its data fetch), so the route's `loading.tsx` streams the puppy during it. Only on that page's first load from navigation, not when changing filters or paging within it. **Keep it switchable, not hard-coded:** read the length from an env var (e.g. `SHOWCASE_LOADER_MS`, `0` or unset = no pause), set in `wrangler.jsonc` per environment, so it can be on for demos on test/UAT and turned off (or shortened) for real visitors in production without a code change — a slower page costs adopters and search ranking. Clamp it (say, at most 3 s) so a typo cannot hang the page. Decide with Lutan whether production gets it at all at launch; record the choice and the reason in `docs/decisions.md`. Check it on a phone: the animation plays through, the page arrives cleanly after it, and the back button does not replay the pause. No manual change (public page); a `src/lib/releases.ts` line only if production gets the pause.

Today the only way home is the logo in `src/app/adopt/PublicHeader.tsx` (it does link to `/`, but visitors don't know that), the menu has no Home entry, and detail pages such as a resident (`/adopt/[id]`) and a project (`/our-work/[id]`) have no link back to their list, so on a phone the only way out is the browser's back gesture. **Suggested:** (1) a **Home** entry at the start of the menu, at least in the mobile menu (`PublicNav.tsx`), where the logo is least likely to be tried; on desktop, decide with Lutan whether the logo plus a "Home" word is enough. (2) A clear **"← All dogs and cats"** link at the top of `/adopt/[id]` and **"← All projects"** on `/our-work/[id]`: if the visitor came from the list, go back in history so their filters and scroll position are kept; if they arrived from outside (a shared link, Facebook, search), link to the list instead of leaving the site. (3) **Breadcrumbs, at least on desktop (Lutan, 2026-09-27):** a breadcrumb line under the header on every sub page — Home › Adopt › Panda, Home › Our work › <project>, Home › Get involved › Foster — each part a link, the last one plain text for the page you are on. On a phone, decide whether the breadcrumb shows too (shortened, e.g. only the parent: "‹ Adopt") or whether (2) does that job there. Mark it up as a `nav` with `aria-label="Breadcrumb"` and `aria-current="page"`, and add schema.org `BreadcrumbList` data so search results show the path. Also a Home link in the footer. The information pages (Foster, Volunteer, Donate, Friends, Our story) get the Home entry from the menu; they have no list to go back to. Both languages (`src/lib/i18n/dictionaries/en.ts` / `th.ts`), big enough tap targets on a phone, and check the look against the redesign mockups (Next up) so this doesn't fight them — if the redesign stream is still open, fold this into it. No schema change. A `src/lib/releases.ts` line.
- [x] **Homepage polish: centre the impact figures, and rethink the Shelter Friends logo tiles.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, from a screenshot of the live homepage. Both are in `src/app/page.tsx`. **(1) Impact band:** the four figures (in our care today / adopted this year / with foster families / in vet care) sit left-aligned in their columns, which leaves the right of each column empty and the band looking lopsided.
