Original item: All in `src/app/contacts/[id]/ContactHub.tsx` unless noted; one branch, one PR. (a) **Drop the Donor and Other contact types.** `CONTACT_TYPES` in `src/lib/contacts/contacts.ts` and the `contact_type` enum (`0001`) both carry `Donor` and `Other`; remove them from the constant, the Management → Contacts form and the `t.contacts.types` labels (`en.ts` ~2550 and `th.ts`). Check the dev and production `contacts` tables for rows of those types first (`select type, count(*) from contacts group by 1`) and decide with the user what they become (Volunteer? Vendor?) before writing the migration — Postgres can't drop an enum value, so either leave the values in the enum and stop offering them (simplest) or recreate the type; say which in `docs/decisions.md`. `scripts/import-appsheet.mjs` maps AppSheet's Donor too. (b) **Phone and WhatsApp appear twice on the card**: `ContactActions size="lg"` already renders each channel as an orange tile with its value, and the `<dl>` beneath repeats the same values as text. Remove the duplicated text rows — keep the `<dl>` only for details that have no action tile (today that's none, since every detail has a matching action), or fold the value into the tile and drop the list; whichever, the address must stay readable in full (long Thai addresses are truncated in a tile). (c) **Map preview for the address.** `mapHref()` treats an `http(s)` address as a pasted maps link and everything else as a search query. When the address is a link (or, cheaper, always), show a small embedded map under the address: a keyless `<iframe src="https://www.google.com/maps?q=<query>&output=embed">` works without an API key for a search query; a shared `maps.app.goo.gl` short link can't be embedded directly (it redirects, and iframes don't follow it into a usable embed) — either resolve it server-side once and store the coordinates, or fall back to the search embed on the text after the link. Lazy-load the iframe, keep it ~200px tall, and don't render it in the `/contacts` list. (d) **Alignment between Residents in care and Past placements.** The two sections sit in a `lg:grid-cols-2`; the left one has the `residentsInCareDetail` helper paragraph under its heading and the right one doesn't, so the two lists start at different heights. Either give both sections a helper line (e.g. "Residents who used to live with this carer.") or move the helper below the list next to `assignFromResident`, so the headings and the first card line up.
- [x] **Pin User manual, Change password and Security to the bottom of the left nav.** Done 2026-09-22: `NavLinks` splits its list into `mainItems` and a `footerItems` group (User manual, Change password, and Security for admins) rendered after `mt-auto` and made `sticky bottom-4` — the sidebar is stretched to the height of the page, not the window, so `mt-auto` alone parked the group thousands of pixels below the fold on a long list. It now sits on the bottom of the window above a divider, and comes last in the phone drawer. Security left the Admin accordion, which made two knock-ons: `/admin` redirects to `/admin/website` (the first page the group still lists) instead of `/admin/security`, and a group counts as “inside” for auto-expand when the route matches one of its own children rather than the group href, so opening Security no longer springs Admin open. Manual’s navigation topic and the Security topic’s path updated. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. In `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` the `/manual` and `/account/password` entries sit in the middle of `items`, between the Assistant demo and the Management group; Security is a child of the Admin accordion. Split the list into a main group and a footer group rendered after a `mt-auto` spacer so the footer sits at the bottom of the sidebar (the drawer on mobile can just list them last with a divider). Footer contents: User manual, Change password and — for admins — Security, which then leaves the Admin accordion (keep `/admin` itself listing it on its landing page). Update the manual's navigation topic (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`, `th.ts`).
- [x] **The pinned nav footer group hides the last Admin links when both accordions are open.** Closed 2026-09-23 by the nav rework rather than fixed separately: the Management and Settings children are gone, and the pinned group is now pinned only on windows at least 44rem tall (`[@media(min-height:44rem)]:sticky`). Even the flat list plus the group needs ~690px for an admin, so it still covered Settings on a 640px window when first built; below that height the group now follows the list behind its divider, as in the phone drawer. Bounding the sidebar with its own scrollbar (below) was not needed. Original brief: Noticed 2026-09-22 while building the footer group. `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` renders it `mt-auto sticky bottom-4 bg-surface`, which holds it against the bottom of the window — but the sidebar is a flex item stretched to the height of the *document*, so the links stay in the flow at the top while the group floats over them. An admin with Management *and* Admin expanded on a window under ~950px has 21 links plus the group: at the top of the page `/admin/zones`, `/admin/immunization-types` and `/admin/procedure-types` sit behind it, and since the divider looks like the end of the menu they read as missing rather than hidden. Scrolling ~200px clears them; one group expanded never overlaps. The proper fix is to bound the sidebar to the window and give it its own scrollbar (`md:sticky md:top-14 md:h-[calc(100dvh-3.5rem)] md:overflow-y-auto`), which needs `AppHeader` pinned at a fixed height and made sticky — a visible change on every page, so decide with the user first. Making the two accordions mutually exclusive would also do it without any layout change.
- [x] **Nav rework: rename Admin to Settings, drop the chevrons, reorder, add icons.** Done 2026-09-23: Management and Settings are single links to their tile-grid landing pages, so the ~13 child links, both chevrons, the `nav-expanded` sessionStorage and the auto-expand rules are gone from `src/app/NavLinks.tsx`. Order agreed with Lutan: Residents, Enclosures, Maintenance | Vets, Contacts, Projects | Assistant | Management, Settings, with a thin rule between groups, then the pinned footer. Every link has the icon its page already uses (`NAV_ICONS` in `src/components/hub-icons.ts`, which the Settings Security tile now shares). The active link is the longest matching href, so `/admin/security` lights Security and not Settings too. The rename is label-only — `/admin/*` URLs and the `admin` role keep their names (`docs/decisions.md`); nav, Settings landing heading, menu paths in `en.ts` / `th.ts` and the manual changed. Original brief:
  Requested 2026-09-23 by Lutan; this absorbs the earlier "collapse the accordions to plain links" follow-up, which was waiting on exactly this decision. Four parts, one stream:
  1. **Drop the chevrons.** `/management` and `/admin` are tile-grid landing pages now (built 2026-09-23), so the sidebar lists Management and Settings as ordinary links and the landing page is the menu. That removes ~13 child links, the two chevron buttons, the `sessionStorage` (`nav-expanded`) choices and the auto-expand rules in `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` — and closes the pinned-footer overlap item above as a side effect, so tick that one too.
  2. **Rename "Admin" to "Settings"** in the nav label, the landing page heading, the i18n dictionaries (`en.ts` / `th.ts`) and the manual. Decide whether the URL moves too: `/settings` with `/admin/*` redirects (as `/admin/vets` → `/management/vets` was done) is cleaner, but a label-only rename is much smaller — ask. The `admin` *role* keeps its name; this is the menu, not the permission.
  3. **Reorder the nav.** Propose an order grouped by how often each link is used (daily field work first — Residents, medical, Facility — then Management, Settings, and the pinned footer) and **agree it with the user before building**; a quick sketch of two orders is enough.
  4. **Icons beside every nav item.** The landing-page tiles already carry an icon each — reuse the same icon set (and the same icon per page) so the nav and the tiles match. Icons should also make a collapsed/narrow sidebar feasible later, but don't build that here.

  `NavLinks.tsx` is a conflict magnet, so give it a stream of its own with nothing else in it, and update the manual's navigation topic (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`, `th.ts`).
- [x] **The assistant still carries its own copy of the "not deceased" filter.** Done 2026-09-24: `src/lib/assistant/data.ts` (where the assistant query had moved) now imports `NOT_DECEASED`; it was the only literal copy under `src/lib/assistant/` and `src/app/assistant/`. Original item: Noticed 2026-09-23 while doing the hide-deceased list item, which moved that PostgREST expression into `src/lib/residents/status.ts` as `NOT_DECEASED` and pointed the residents list and the vet-visit and immunization pickers at it. `src/app/assistant/page.tsx` was being rewritten on another branch at the time, so its literal `.or("current_status.neq.Deceased,current_status.is.null")` was left alone. Swap it for the import once the assistant work has landed, so there is one definition of which residents are still with us.

### Mobile

- [x] **Collapsible mobile navigation.** Replace the fixed `w-48` sidebar in `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` / `layout.tsx` with a hamburger-toggled drawer below the `md` breakpoint so phones don't show only the nav bar.
- [x] **Icon-based mobile hub and residents list.** Hub stat cards, tabs and action buttons use lucide icons (`src/components/hub-icons.ts`, `ActionLink.tsx`); the phone residents list shows only ID + name with no checkboxes or zone/enclosure filters.
- [x] **Browse residents by enclosure.** New mobile-friendly views to navigate zone → enclosure → residents (replacing the desktop-only zone/enclosure filters and extra columns that are hidden on phones in `src/app/residents/`). Lives at `/enclosures` (zone chips, name search, sort by zone/name/fullest; capacity indicator per card) and `/enclosures/[id]` (occupancy bar, notes, resident thumbnails, disabled Maintenance placeholder).

### Resident operations

- [x] **Summary PDF: a stray hyphen where a long name wraps before a Thai word.** Done 2026-09-24: textkit hands the hyphenation callback one *run* at a time and starts a new run where the script changes, so `Valley (เจ้าหญิง…)` arrived as `(` and `เจ้าหญิง…)`; with no space between them the line breaker counted the join as a hyphenation point and printed `(-` when it broke there. Every `Text` in `src/lib/archive/resident-summary-pdf.tsx` now carries `hyphenationPenalty` of textkit's infinity (10000), which makes such points unbreakable, so lines break only at spaces. Verified by rendering long Thai and long Latin names before and after (`docs/test-plans/summary-pdf-hyphen.md`).
- [x] **Intake warns when the chosen enclosure is nearly full or full.** Done 2026-09-24: the wizard's Arrival step uses `EnclosurePicker` (idPrefix `intake`, with placeholders that keep "blank means Unassigned"), fed by `loadEnclosureOptions()` — the same loader as Move — so the headcount and thresholds cannot disagree between screens. Register opens `CapacityWarningDialog` (intake wording via a new `copy` prop) and, as everywhere else, only warns: Register anyway still registers. The warning is on Register rather than on Arrival's Next (see `docs/decisions.md`). Correction to the brief: `record_intake` never rejected a full enclosure, so before this a volunteer got no warning at all rather than a late error. Original brief: Noticed 2026-09-22 while turning `/residents/new` into a wizard. Every other placement screen — move, return from hospital, return to shelter, the edit form's housing section, maintenance — uses `EnclosurePicker` (`src/components/EnclosurePicker.tsx`), which shows the chosen enclosure's occupancy underneath and, with `CapacityWarningDialog`, asks before putting one more resident into a full or nearly-full one. Intake is the exception: it has its own plain Zone and Enclosure selects, so a volunteer registering an arrival at the gate — the person least likely to know which kennels are full — gets no warning, and the first they hear of it is an error from `record_intake`. Swap the two selects on the wizard's Arrival step for `EnclosurePicker`. It needs `residentCount` and `capacity` per enclosure (`src/lib/enclosures/options.ts`), which `/residents/new`'s page query doesn't select yet — copy the query the move page uses. Note the picker names its own controls (`idPrefix`), so the wizard's Review step, which reads answers back out of the form by control id, needs its zone/enclosure lookups updated to match.
- [x] **Deceased Resident Summary PDF: the name overlaps the subtitle, and the footer never renders.** Done 2026-09-24: one cause for both — the page style's unitless `lineHeight: 1.4`, which @react-pdf resolves to an absolute 12.6pt and hands down to every child (see `docs/decisions.md`). The 20pt name got a 12.6pt line box; the footer's page-number text, re-laid-out after its render prop runs, read the inherited 12.6 as a multiplier on each pass and came out ~5,300pt tall, so the bottom-anchored footer sat thousands of points above the page. Line height is now set per text style in `src/lib/archive/resident-summary-pdf.tsx`. Checked by rendering the real component against stub records (Latin, Thai, Thai-only, a name that wraps, a four-page DEV render) and reading every page. The six archives already in production keep the broken layout until each is regenerated — a separate backlog item. Original brief: Noticed 2026-09-23 while rendering the PDF for the UAT watermark. The 20pt name and the `R-0042 · Dog · …` subtitle print on top of each other, and the fixed footer (name, code, generated date, page N / M) is missing from every page. Production output shows both, so they predate the watermark. A render of the component against a stub record is enough to check a fix; no Drive or database needed.
- [x] **Hide deceased residents from the residents list by default, with a "Show all" toggle.** Done 2026-09-23: `/residents` filters `resident_list_view` with the shared `NOT_DECEASED` expression (`src/lib/residents/status.ts`, now also used by the vet-visit and immunization pickers), and `?all=1` — the Show all / Hide deceased chip beside the filters, on phones too — brings them back, dimmed, with Deceased in the Status column. The line under the heading counts the mode: "212 residents · 38 deceased hidden", or "including 38 deceased" with the toggle on. A name search that matches a deceased animal says so rather than switching the list out from under you — "1 deceased resident matches — show" (see `docs/decisions.md`). The deceased count respects the name / zone / enclosure filters, so it always counts the animals that list would have shown. `/enclosures/[id]` is unchanged by design.
- [x] **Step-by-step intake instead of one long form.** Done 2026-09-22: `/residents/new` now asks the same questions over five steps (Who, Arrival, Health, For adopters, Story) and a Review that lists every answer with an Edit link back to its step. The write is untouched — one `recordIntake` call to the `record_intake` RPC on the single Register press (see `docs/decisions.md`). Steps are hidden rather than unmounted so values and `required` survive; every forward move runs `checkValidity()` over the steps it passes so the browser's own message lands on the field at fault, and Register validates before `requestSubmit()`. `?step=` keeps the place across a refresh, never the answers. The adoption recommendation fields got a step of their own (the brief named four field groups but five steps); `EditResidentForm` stays flat. Manual's intake topic rewritten. *Still to do:* re-capture `public/manual/resident-intake.png` — it needs a signed-in browser against a dev database, so it is a run of `node scripts/manual-screenshots.mjs` rather than a code change. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22: `/residents/new` is intimidating — `src/app/residents/new/IntakeForm.tsx` shows all four fieldsets (identity with name / species / sex / size / age / weight / blood-test interval / diet; adoption profile; arrival with date / origin / zone + enclosure / notes / ready-for-adoption; bio with temperament / past story / behaviour) on one page, ~25 fields before the Register button. Turn it into a wizard: one section per step, a progress header ("Step 2 of 4 · Arrival", steps clickable once visited), Back / Next, and a final **Review** step that lists every answer with an Edit link back to its step before the single Register press. **Keep the write exactly as it is:** one `recordIntake` call to the `record_intake` RPC at the end (`actions.ts`), so nothing half-registered is ever created — the steps are a client-side view over one `<form>`, not separate submissions. Implementation shape: keep everything in the one `<form action={formAction}>` and hide the inactive steps with `hidden` (not unmounted, so their values and the `required` attributes survive), and run `checkValidity()` on the visible step's fields on Next so a step can't be left with its required fields empty — the browser's own validation messages then point at the right control. Order the steps so the required things come first and the optional prose last: 1 *Who* (name, Thai name, other names, species, breed, sex, size), 2 *Arrival* (date, origin incl. the add-origin toggle, zone → enclosure with the existing capacity note, ready for adoption), 3 *Health* (age, weight, blood-test interval, diet), 4 *Story* (bio, temperament, past story, behaviour — all optional, say so, and note they can be filled later from Edit resident), 5 *Review*. Keep the URL at `/residents/new` (a `?step=` param is fine so a refresh doesn't lose the place; the values themselves live in the form, not the URL). Phone first — this is the form a volunteer fills at the gate — so one column, big Next button pinned at the bottom, and check it in the phone drawer's width. The `IntakeState` error from the server (name taken, enclosure full, RPC failure) should land the user on the Review step with the message, not on step 1. Reuse `AdoptionProfileFields` unchanged. Leave `EditResidentForm` as the flat form it is — editing one field on a wizard is worse, and the two forms already share the field components. Update the manual's intake topic (`src/lib/manual/en.ts` `id: "intake"`, and `th.ts`) and re-capture `resident-intake.png` (or whatever the screenshot script names it) since the page looks different. Record in `docs/decisions.md` that the wizard is presentation only and the RPC is unchanged.
- [x] **Decide which fields stay editable after death.** Decided 2026-09-21: the bio group (bio, temperament, past story, behaviour notes) and photos (add, remove, choose the profile photo) — nothing else. `0052_deceased_editable_fields.sql` relaxes `enforce_deceased_lock()` for a `residents` UPDATE that changes only those columns and `enforce_deceased_lock_attachment()` for `'resident'` attachments; blood test and procedure files stay locked. The edit page shows only the photo and bio sections for a deceased resident, the Photos tab keeps its uploader, and `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()` regenerates the summary PDF and offline index after each such change (best effort — the hub's Retry still covers a Drive failure). Original brief: The deceased lock in `0026_deceased_workflow.sql` currently allows nothing, pending that decision. Once the list exists, relax `enforce_deceased_lock()` for those columns (and re-generate the archive when one of them changes).
- [x] **Edit resident details.** Add `/residents/[id]/edit` with a form + server action to update identity/bio fields (name, Thai name, other names, animal code, species, breed, sex, age, notes, adoption/public flags). Housing is not edited here.
- [x] **Move resident between enclosures.** `/residents/[id]/move` (linked from the hub's Housing card and the housing section) and a Housing section on the edit form both insert a `ChangeEnclosure` row into `placement_history` via `src/lib/placements/move.ts` (zone → enclosure picker, date, notes; Lifecycle pseudo-enclosures excluded; capacity warning before moving into a nearly-full/full/over-capacity enclosure). Existing trigger closes the prior placement (0024 makes it `BEFORE INSERT` — it never could have fired past the one-active-placement index as `AFTER` — and `security definer` so volunteers' moves work).
- [x] **Send to hospital.** `/residents/[id]/hospital` (from the hub's Housing card, the housing section, and each vet visit record via `?vetAppointmentId=` which pre-fills date and notes) inserts a `SendToHospital` placement into the Lifecycle/Hospital enclosure via `src/lib/placements/hospital.ts`, storing `previous_enclosure_id` so the resident can return later. Admin/staff only. The Housing card reads "In hospital · Off-site in medical care · Returns to X" while they're there.
- [x] **Return from hospital.** `/residents/[id]/hospital/return` (from the hub's Housing card and the housing section while hospitalised — it and "Send to hospital" are opposites, only one is ever shown) inserts a `ReturnFromHospital` placement via `returnResidentFromHospital()` in `src/lib/placements/hospital.ts`. The zone → enclosure picker defaults to the stored `previous_enclosure_id` but any physical enclosure can be chosen (e.g. isolation first), with a notice when the previous one is gone or wasn't physical. Admin/staff only. "Move enclosure" is hidden (and rejected server-side) while in hospital.
- [x] **Foster and adopt.** `/residents/[id]/rehome` (from the hub's Housing card and the housing section; `?type=foster|adopt` picks the tab) inserts a `Foster` or `Adopt` placement into the Lifecycle/Fostered or /Adopted enclosure with `carer_id` via `rehomeResident()` in `src/lib/placements/rehome.ts`. The carer is picked from contacts of type Carer, or added inline (name, phone, email, LINE ID — saved as a Carer contact) until Contacts management exists. A fostered resident can be adopted, moved to another carer, or sent to hospital; an adopted one can only be returned. `/residents/[id]/rehome/return` inserts a `ReturnToShelter` placement into a physical enclosure (`returnResidentToShelter()`), defaulting to the enclosure they left from. Admin/staff only. Which actions show for each status lives in `src/lib/placements/available.ts`.
- [x] **Hide adopted residents from the public adoption pages.** `0025_public_views_exclude_adopted.sql` filters `public_resident_profiles` and `public_resident_photos` on `current_status not in ('Deceased', 'Adopted')`; fostered residents stay listed (foster-to-adopt is common). Same migration revokes everything but SELECT on both views from `anon` / `authenticated` — Supabase's default privileges had granted INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE, and `public_resident_profiles` is auto-updatable, so anonymous writes through it were being accepted.
- [x] **Undo a death recorded by mistake.** Done 2026-09-21 (see `docs/decisions.md`): the deceased banner offers an admin **Withdraw this death** → `/residents/[id]/deceased/undo` (reason required, confirmation dialog). `undo_deceased_placement()` (0049) appends a `DeceasedInError` placement (0048) back into whatever the death closed — the resident's history keeps both the death and the reversal, dated when it was made — and restores exactly what the cascade did from the snapshot the Deceased row now records in `placement_history.deceased_cascade`: the appointments it cancelled, the prescriptions it ended (with the end dates they had) and `ready_for_adoption`. The lock lifts on its own. The Drive folder moves back under `Residents/` and the generated summary PDF and index page are deleted (`src/lib/archive/restore-deceased-resident.ts`), with a retry on the hub if Drive failed. The dashboard no longer counts a withdrawn death. Original brief: Today a `Deceased` placement can only be reversed in SQL: the record is locked database-side and the cascade (cancelled appointments, ended prescriptions) isn't reversible row by row. Decide whether admins get a proper "recorded in error" path, and what it should restore.
- [x] **Record deceased.** `/residents/[id]/deceased` (from the icon beside the edit pencil on the hub's details card, admin/staff only) records the death (date, cause, notes) behind a confirmation dialog via `src/lib/placements/deceased.ts`. The resident becomes Deceased, drops off the public pages, and their whole record turns read-only — enforced by triggers in `0026_deceased_workflow.sql`, not just hidden buttons. The same migration fixes `current_status` never having returned `'Deceased'` (it tested the zone name, so `0025`'s public-view filter wasn't actually hiding deceased residents) and makes the 7.2 cascade `security definer` so staff's appointment/prescription cleanup isn't silently filtered out by RLS. Afterwards the Drive folder moves to `Residents/Deceased/`, and a summary PDF plus an offline `index.html` index are written into it (`src/lib/archive/`); the hub shows the archive state with a retry when Drive was down.

### Medical records

- [x] **Optional doctor name on a vet appointment.** Done 2026-09-24 in two PRs. #76 added `vet_appointments.doctor_name` (0074: free text; a trigger trims it and stores blank as NULL, and a check constraint states the same). The feature PR adds an optional **Doctor** field to `/vet-visits/new` and `/vet-visits/[id]/edit`. As decided 2026-09-23 it is free text with a `<datalist>` of the names already recorded against the chosen vet, loaded once with the page and following the vet select. The name shows after the vet on the resident's Vet Appointments tab and in the deceased archive (summary PDF and offline index), and after the reason on the vet hub's visit list. A visit with no doctor renders exactly as before. `schedule_bulk_appointments` did not gain the `p_doctor_name` parameter this item planned; the booking action sets the name on the rows the RPC returns instead (see `docs/decisions.md`). The assistant's vet booking neither asks for nor accepts a doctor yet. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-23: some clinics put a particular doctor on a particular animal, and staff want the record to say who actually saw the resident. `vet_appointments` today records the clinic (`vet_id`), date, status, reason, notes and cost (0053) — there is nowhere to put a person.
- [x] **Medication requirement page.** Closed 2026-09-21 with the window picker: `/management/medications` (and `/management/diets`, same shape) shows each row's forecast for the next 7 and 30 days and, when From/To dates are entered above the table, a third column for that period (`ForecastWindowPicker`, a GET form; `src/lib/management/forecast-window.ts` parses and bounds it to a year). The days-of-stock projection is its own open item now, since nothing records stock on hand. Original brief: `medication_daily_requirement` (0027) already totals each medication's daily consumption across current prescriptions for living, non-adopted residents. Surface it — probably beside the immunization forecast once that exists — with a days-of-stock projection if a stock-on-hand figure is ever recorded.
- [x] **Dietary requirements.** Done 2026-09-21 as dated records, not a field (see `docs/decisions.md`): `0051_diets.sql` adds `residents.size` (Small / Medium / Large — required on the intake and edit forms, nullable in the database for residents intaken before it, shown on the hub and the public adoption pages), `diet_types` (Management → Diets: unit, cost per unit in baht, daily quantity per size) and `resident_diets` (diet, start / end date, meals a day, optional per-resident quantity override, notes; several may run at once; locked with the record on death). The hub gets a Diet card and a Diet tab (Current / Past, Add / Edit / End today like prescriptions), `/diets/new` and `/diets/[id]/edit`, intake takes the size and an optional starting diet, and `diet_forecast(from, to)` feeds a 7 / 30-day quantity and cost forecast with a total on the Management → Diets page, excluding fostered, adopted and deceased residents. Still open: a feeding sheet / enclosure view for volunteers on rounds, and Thai for free-text notes per the Quick Wins item. Original brief: Add a dietary requirements field/section to residents (free text or structured), editable from the hub and visible on the resident info tab.
- [x] **Blood test types.** Done 2026-09-21: `0050_blood_test_types.sql` adds the table (read for every role, write for admin), seeds the seven panels, adds `blood_tests.blood_test_type_id` NOT NULL with every existing row backfilled to CBC (see `docs/decisions.md`), and a `merge_blood_test_type` helper. `/admin/blood-test-types` (Admin nav, manual entry) is the procedure-types page with the names changed — add / rename / merge, delete blocked while any blood test references the row. The blood test form has a required Test type dropdown defaulting to CBC (no inline add — the list is short and the admin page exists from day one); the Blood Tests tab, the archive PDF and offline index show the type. Original brief: New `blood_test_types` table + admin CRUD page, `blood_test_type_id` on `blood_tests`, and a type dropdown on the blood test form defaulting to CBC. Seed: CBC (Complete Blood Count), Blood Chemistry Panel, Thyroid Panel, Heartworm Test, Tick Borne Disease Panel, Cortisol Test, Urinary Analysis.
- [x] **End or edit a prescription.** Done 2026-09-21: every row on the Prescriptions tab has an **Edit** link to `/prescriptions/[id]/edit` (the same `PrescriptionForm`, now in `src/app/prescriptions/`, in edit mode with a Cancel link), and current rows whose course has started have an **End today** button that sets `end_date` to today in place. No migration — 0027 already gave staff, vets and admins update on `prescriptions`, and its end-after-start check is why End today is withheld from a row dated to start later (edit it instead). Original brief: A vet stopping a course early, or a typo in the dose, currently means a new row: there's no edit page and no "end today" action on the Prescriptions tab. Add both (an `end_date = today` shortcut on current rows, and an edit form reusing `PrescriptionForm`), keeping the end-after-start check in mind.
- [x] **Prescriptions.** `/prescriptions/new?residentId=…[&vetAppointmentId=…]` (from the hub's prescriptions card, the Prescriptions tab, or an "Add prescription" link on each row of the Vet Appointments tab) records medication, dose, frequency, start/end date, linked visit and notes; the tab lists them as Current vs Expired. `0027_prescriptions.sql` adds what the AppSheet shape was missing — a `dose_unit` per medication (tablet, ml, mg, drop, …, so IV fluids and suspensions fit), `dose_quantity` and `vet_appointment_id` on the prescription, `doses_per_day` on frequency, an end-after-start check, staff write access, and a `medication_daily_requirement` view that excludes deceased/adopted residents. The deceased cascade already ended open prescriptions on the date of death; it now ends a future-dated one on its own start date instead so the new check can't roll a death back.
- [x] **Medication management.** Done 2026-09-21 under Management, not Admin (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/management/medications` lists medications (name, unit, doses and quantity due in the next 7 / 30 days from `medication_forecast`, prescription count) and the frequency options (label, schedule, prescription count) with add / rename / fix-unit / fix-doses-per-day inline, delete blocked while any prescription references the row, and **Merge…** to fold a duplicate into the one to keep (`merge_medication` / `merge_frequency`, 0043 — one transaction; medications only merge within the same unit). 0043 gives `management` read/write on both tables; 0044 turns frequencies into whole-number schedules (times a day, or every N days/weeks/months from the start date) so weekly and monthly tablets forecast as whole doses on the days they fall. Original brief: Admin page for medications and frequencies. Both can be added inline from the prescription form (vet/staff), but only an admin can rename one, fix a unit or `doses_per_day`, or merge a duplicate, and today that's SQL. Same shape as `/admin/immunization-types`.
- [x] **Weight.** Form + hub section to log and list weight entries (date, kg) against the existing `weight` table, newest first. Done with a trend chart, entry from the hub, vet visits and intake, and `vet_appointment_id` on `weight` (0028/0029).
- [x] **Procedures.** `/procedures/new?residentId=…[&vetAppointmentId=…]` (from the hub's Procedures card, the Procedures tab, or a "Log procedure" link on each vet appointment row) records type, date, linked visit and notes; the tab lists them with their files. `0031_procedure_types.sql` replaces the free-text type with a seeded `procedure_types` lookup (extendable inline), lets staff log procedures (on-site jobs like nail clipping), and adds `'procedure'` to `attachment_owner_type`; `0032` teaches the deceased lock to resolve those files. X-rays, scans and PDFs upload via `/api/procedures/[id]/attachments` into `Residents/<Name> (<ID>)/Procedures/<Type> <YYYYMMDD>/`, right after saving or later from the tab, and appear in the deceased archive.
- [x] **Single form for details + file upload.** Done 2026-09-21 for blood tests and procedures (see `docs/decisions.md`): the queue/progress/retry logic that `MaintenanceForm` had is now `useDeferredUploads()` + `FileDropZone` / `PendingFileList` / `UploadProgressPanel` in `src/components/DeferredUploads.tsx`, and `BloodTestForm` and `ProcedureForm` pick files alongside the details, upload them on Save and land on the tab. Still to do: **intake photos** — `IntakeForm` (`src/app/residents/new/IntakeForm.tsx`) has no picker; the photo route needs a folder category and a date-taken per file, and `recordIntake` redirects server-side rather than returning the id, so it's a different shape from the medical forms. Any future record-with-files form (projects, vet visits if they ever take files) should use the same hook. The sequential-upload constraint (Drive folder check-then-create isn't safe under concurrency) lives in the hook.
- [x] **Attach files to an existing blood test.** Done 2026-09-21: `BloodTestList` has the per-row "Attach files" toggle the Procedures tab has.
- [x] **Admin page for procedure types.** Done 2026-09-21: `/admin/procedure-types` (Admin nav, manual entry) lists each type with its procedure count and offers add / rename inline, delete blocked while any procedure references the row, and **Merge…** to fold a duplicate into the one to keep (`merge_procedure_type`, 0047 — one transaction, same shape as 0043's). Its own page rather than a combined one, see `docs/decisions.md`. Original brief: Staff and vets can add a type inline from the procedure form, but renaming one or merging a duplicate is SQL today. Same shape as `/admin/immunization-types`; could share a page with the medications/frequencies admin item below.

### Facility

- [x] **Copyable links for enclosure QR codes and resident RFID cards.** Done 2026-09-22 (see `docs/decisions.md`): the enclosure page and resident hub show the tag address with a **Copy link** button, and the enclosure cards and resident rows carry a copy icon for batch programming. The address is a short redirect — `/e/<id>`, `/r/<R-code>` — and a resident's serves visitors too: signed in → the hub, signed out → the public profile on `/adopt` if the resident is shown on the public site, otherwise sign-in and back. The proxy now remembers where a signed-out visitor was going (`?next=`) through both sign-in routes. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. The shelter will put a QR code on every enclosure and a credit-card-sized RFID card outside each one showing the resident’s photo, name, age, sex and temperament; both are programmed with an external tool, so the system only needs to hand over the right URL. Add a **Copy link** control (with the URL shown next to it so it can also be selected by hand) on the enclosure page (`/enclosures/[id]`) and the resident hub (`/residents/[id]`), building the absolute URL with `getSiteOrigin()` from `src/lib/site-origin.ts` (it already resolves the real host behind Cloudflare and honours `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`) so the link is always `https://lannacare.org/...` in production and never a localhost / IP address. Also add a **Links** column or per-row copy button on the enclosure list (`/enclosures`) and the residents list so a batch of tags can be programmed without opening each page. Decide (and record in `docs/decisions.md`) whether the link should be the plain hub URL or a dedicated short route (e.g. `/e/<id>`, `/r/<id>`) that redirects — a short route keeps the QR code low-density and survives a later page restructure. A scanner who is not signed in should land on the sign-in page and come back to the same URL afterwards (the redirect in `src/proxy.ts` and the `next` handling in `src/app/auth/callback/route.ts`). The card print itself (photo, name, age, sex, temperament) is out of scope here; if staff later want the system to produce the card artwork, that is a separate item.
- [x] **Staff feedback, round 1 (desktop look and feel).** Done 2026-09-21 from the first walk-through as a staff member (see `docs/decisions.md`). **Vet hub:** a 1-month period joins 3 / 6 / 12 / all time; the Residents seen card now says "Different residents · each seen once" or "· N repeat visits", since it only differs from Visits when a resident went more than once. **Maintenance:** staff and volunteers open the board on their own jobs (Assigned to: Me / Everyone; management and admin start on Everyone; a link naming a zone or enclosure shows everything there; `?assignee=me|all`); a job goes to a **team** — `0063_maintenance_teams_archived_users.sql` replaces `maintenance.assigned_user_id` with a `maintenance_assignees` join table, the form ticks people instead of picking one, cards read "Ann, Bo" or "Ann +2"; people who leave are **archived** from Admin → Security (`user_roles.archived_at`: `current_user_role()` returns null for them so nothing lets them in, the picker leaves them out, their name stays on past jobs with "(no longer here)" and the edit form keeps them ticked so they can be taken off; Restore undoes it). **Enclosures:** the three status buckets that matter for housing — Hospital, Unassigned, Fostered — sit at the top of the browser as cards without a zone heading (Adopted and Deceased are off it); each enclosure card's corner shows its open maintenance count in place of the zone name, and a zone heading shows its zone-wide open jobs as a link to the filtered board. **Residents list:** the Zone and Location cells are blank for a resident in a Lifecycle bucket instead of "Lifecycle · Internal". **Projects:** folder cards are a 16:9 strip with a smaller caption, six to a row on a wide screen. Contacts privacy (6a) is a separate open item under Management.
- [x] **Thai names for zones and enclosures.** Done 2026-09-21 as the paired-column pattern (`0058_place_names_th.sql`: `name_th` on `zones` and `enclosures`, carried through `resident_list_view` as `zone_name_th` / `enclosure_name_th`; Lifecycle pseudo-rows seeded with Thai names). `placeName(locale, name, name_th)` in `src/lib/enclosures/names.ts` is used at every render site — residents list and hub, placement forms and history, enclosure browser and hub, maintenance board / job page / forms, every zone → enclosure picker — with the English as fallback; Admin → Zones and → Enclosures edit the Thai name in place and take it on create. English stays the key for Drive, sorting and the status logic. Original brief: Asked for 2026-09-21 right after the translations feature landed: the staff who read the maintenance board and the resident hub in Thai see zone and enclosure names in English. These are labels, not prose — `zones.name` / `enclosures.name` are unique keys, Drive folder path components (`Projects/…/Enclosure Maintenance/<Zone>/<Enclosure>/`, resident folders), and the `Lifecycle` zone and its `Hospital` / `Fostered` / `Adopted` pseudo-enclosures are matched by name in code — so this is the paired-column pattern (`name_th`, as on `project_folders` and `residents.thai_name`), **not** the `translations` table. Migration: `name_th` on both tables (nullable), carried through `resident_list_view` (`zone_name_th`, `enclosure_name_th`) and every query that embeds `zones(name)` / `enclosures(name)`; Admin → Zones and Admin → Enclosures get a Thai name column edited in place. Display: one helper (say `placeName(locale, name, name_th)` in `src/lib/enclosures/`) used at every render site — 58 across 35 files as of today (`grep -rn "zone_name|enclosure_name" src`): the resident list and hub, enclosure browser and hub, pickers (`EnclosurePicker`, `loadEnclosureOptions`), maintenance board and job page, placement forms. English stays the key everywhere (sorting, Drive, matching); Thai is display only. Not on the public site (enclosures aren't shown there).
- [x] **Migrate the legacy AppSheet maintenance folders.** Closed 2026-09-21 without a migration: the user confirmed the only folders under the legacy `Enclosure Maintenance/<Enclosure>/Active|Completed/<date> (<id>)/Work to be Done/` tree are test folders, so there are no real jobs to read into `maintenance` rows and nothing to move. Original brief: read those folders into `maintenance` rows and move their files into the new layout, agreeing the mapping (enclosure names, what counts as Completed, the date) at a screen with the user and running once with a dry run first. Was (c) of the Maintenance follow-ups item; (a) and (b) closed the same day.
- [x] **Maintenance follow-ups (a) delete a job, (b) assign to a contact.** Done 2026-09-21. (a) **Delete job** on the job page (staff and above; confirm first): removes the attachment rows and the job, then the Drive folder — which takes the files with it — or, for a job that never had a folder, each file. Database first, Drive best effort, redirects to the board. (b) **Assigned to** on the log/edit form — corrected the same day to a **login**, not a contact (`0055_maintenance_assigned_user.sql`: `assigned_user_id` → auth.users replaces `assigned_to`; `app_users` view for the picker and names): the person who actions a job is always staff, a volunteer or management, never a carer or supplier. The assignee shows on the job page and on board cards. Original brief: (a) Deleting a job — none today, a mistaken job can only be edited; needs to remove its attachments and Drive folder. (b) Assigning a job to a contact — `maintenance.assigned_to` exists but isn't on the form; contacts now exist at `/contacts`, so the form can offer a picker (all types, not just carers).
- [x] **Enclosure maintenance.** Done 2026-09-20 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `0033_enclosure_maintenance.sql` adds `enclosure_id` (nullable — zone-wide jobs), estimated/actual cost, due date, `job_code` (M-0001), a cached Drive folder id and a before/after `phase` on `attachments`, and renames the statuses to Not Started / In Progress / Blocked / Completed. `/maintenance` is a drag-and-drop Kanban (desktop) / status list (phone), `/maintenance/[id]` has status buttons and Before / After photo sections, `/maintenance/new` uploads photos on the same form as the details. Files live in `Projects/Shelter Projects/Enclosure Maintenance/<Zone>/<Enclosure>/<Status>/<M-0001 Title>/` and the folder moves with the status.

### Admin

- [x] **`worktree.mjs list` should say WHY a folder is HELD, so `/clean-streams` can clear the common case.** [done 2026-10-07, see `docs/decisions/2026-10-07-worktree-held-reason.md`: the column reads `HELD by a dev server on port N` when a next dev server is the only holder, a named session still wins, and `done` stays opt-in via `--stop-servers`.] Lutan, 2026-10-06, after asking what could possibly have `volunteer-read-only` open when nothing — not even he — goes into those folders. **The answer was a `next dev` server nobody stopped**, pid 15544, running since **2026-10-04 13:19**, serving a worktree whose PR had merged long before. It held `.next/` inside the folder, so `list` could not rename it and reported `HELD`, and **three cleanup rounds in a row could not touch it**. It was not alone: **all nine HELD folders had exactly one such server each** (ports 3004, 3006, 3011, 3015, 3017, 3018, 3019, 3020, 3023), **3.2 GB of working set between them**, out of 46 `node.exe` on the machine. **Why it accumulates:** in ordinary work you run one dev server from a terminal and closing the terminal ends it. Here a server is started **per worktree** by a session, it **detaches and outlives that session**, and the folder it serves is **meant to be deleted** afterwards. None of those is true of normal work, so it is a cost of the worktree-per-stream design rather than anything anyone is doing wrong. **The capability already exists and is well built:** `done <slug> --stop-servers` ends them and retries, and its `holders()` matches only a process *running from* the folder (an executable inside it, or `node.exe` running a script under its `node_modules`), **deliberately not one that merely mentions the path**, because *"a shell whose command line merely names the folder is somebody's session … and --stop-servers must never end one"*. It also found a **child process** a hand-written `Stop-Process` had missed. **So this item is not about building the ability — it is about surfacing the reason.** **Build:** `list`'s `held` column should read **`HELD by a dev server on port 3006`** (or the process name it found) instead of a bare `HELD` — **`holders()` already computes exactly this and throws it away**. Then `/clean-streams`, which is currently told *"do not propose `--stop-servers` unless the user says so for that slug"*, can safely offer it for folders held **only** by a dev server, while keeping that caution for anything else. **Worth considering alongside:** whether `done` should stop a dev server on the worktree's own `.port` without the flag at all, since by then the branch is merged and the folder is being deleted. **Why it matters beyond tidiness:** a bare `HELD` was read for three weeks as "someone is still working in there", including by `/plan-day`, which repeatedly told Lutan not to tear those folders down on that basis. A reason in the column answers the question without anyone investigating. No schema. → *Admin*
- [x] **Release notes register, with an email to admins on each major release.** Done 2026-09-23 (see `docs/decisions.md`, "Release notes register"): `src/lib/releases.ts` seeded with `0.0.1` "Current Baseline Build", `/releases` for every signed-in role (nav footer, manual topic), `package.json` at `0.0.1`; `scripts/deploy.mjs` refuses a production deploy with unreleased notes or a version mismatch, tags the Worker version, and mails a major release new to the site to that environment’s admins through the Worker’s free Cloudflare `send_email` binding (`worker/release-mail.mjs`, verified addresses only, `[UAT]`/`[Production]` in the subject, dev never sends). One-off Cloudflare setup in `docs/email-sending.md` §1; Resend SMTP for password reset stays under the Deployment item (§2). Original brief: Requested 2026-09-23 by Lutan. A page in the app (admin-visible; readable by everyone is worth asking) listing each release — version, date, **environment (UAT or Production)**, and short plain-language notes of what changed for users. Today nothing records a release: `package.json` is still `0.1.0` and deploys go through `scripts/deploy.mjs --env`, so the design needs to settle (a) **where notes live** — a checked-in file (e.g. `docs/releases.md` or a typed `src/lib/releases.ts`, written as part of the PR) is simplest and reviewable; a DB table (needs a schema PR first) allows editing in the app; (b) **how a release is recorded** — most naturally by `scripts/deploy.mjs`, which already knows the target env, stamping the version + env + date at deploy time; (c) **major vs minor** — a flag per release; only major releases send email. **Email:** on a major release, send the notes to every user with the `admin` role in that environment's database, with the environment named in the subject (`[UAT]` / `[Production]`) so a UAT test release is never mistaken for a live one. There is no app mailer yet — the only email today is Supabase Auth's (and production SMTP is still an open Deployment item), so pick a sender (a transactional API such as Resend, or the SMTP account set up for Auth) and note that `lannacare.org` Email Routing is receive-only. Depends on the `uat` environment item in Deployment for the UAT/Production distinction; until it lands, "dev" stands in for UAT. Record the choices in `docs/decisions.md`.
- [x] **Contacts follow-ups (a) notes, (c) maintenance link.** Done 2026-09-21: `0054_contact_notes.sql` adds `contacts.notes`, on the Management → Contacts add form and table (edit in place), shown on the contact hub under the details and included in the contact list's search. (c) moot since jobs are assigned to logins, not contacts (0055). Original brief: (a) A `notes` field (what a supplier sells, a volunteer's availability, a carer's home set-up) — the vets item has the same gap. (c) `maintenance.assigned_to` now has a contact page to link to once the maintenance form can set it.
- [x] **Vet hub follow-ups.** Done 2026-09-21: `0053_vet_cost_notes.sql` adds `vet_appointments.cost` (baht, ≥ 0) and `vets.notes`. Cost is entered on the new `/vet-visits/[id]/edit` page — the first way to change a visit after booking at all: status (now including Cancelled), date/time, vet, reason, notes, cost — reached from an Edit link on each row of the resident's Vet Appointments tab; the row shows the cost, and the vet hub gains a Spend card (total for the period, with how many of the visits carry a cost) and the cost on each visit row. Notes are on the Management → Vets form/table and under the contact details on the hub. Spend per resident and per month on the dashboard remain under Dashboard follow-ups (d). Original brief: (a) Visit cost — there is no cost column on `vet_appointments`, so the hub can't show spend per vet or per resident; add one (baht, like maintenance) and a total to the hub once invoices are being recorded. (b) A vet's `created_at` is the only "since when" we have; a `notes` field (specialities, opening hours, emergency line) would be more useful on the hub than in `contact_info` free text.
- [x] **Vets management.** Done 2026-09-20 (see `docs/decisions.md`); moved to `/management/vets` 2026-09-21 (`/admin/vets` redirects): the CRUD page for the existing `vets` table (name, clinic, contact info; delete is blocked while a vet has logged visits), and `/vets` → `/vets/[id]` is a read-only vet hub for every role — per-vet visit counts, residents seen, upcoming/overdue, linked procedures / blood tests / prescriptions, a visits-per-month chart and the visit list, over a 3 / 6 / 12 month / all-time window. Both in the nav. No migration.
- [x] **Contacts management.** Done 2026-09-20 (see `docs/decisions.md`); moved to `/management/contacts` 2026-09-21 (`/admin/contacts` redirects): the CRUD page for the `contacts` table (name, type, phone, email, LINE ID, Messenger, WhatsApp, address — 0036/0037; delete and a type change away from Carer are blocked while the contact has placements), and `/contacts` → `/contacts/[id]` is the phone-first contact list for every role — search, type chips, one-tap call / LINE / Messenger / WhatsApp / email / maps, and on the hub the residents currently with a carer plus their past placements. Assigning stays on the resident hub. Both in the nav.
- [x] **Project work (outreach projects) — file-system style.** Done 2026-09-20 (see `docs/decisions.md`): 0034/0035, `/projects` and `/projects/[id]`, categories seeded as locked root rows, photos on `attachments` (`owner_type = project`) with captions and a cover, Drive tree mirrored via `ensureProjectFolderPath()`. Original brief: The shelter does a lot of outreach — sterilisation drives, community feeding, school visits, rescue call-outs — and needs somewhere to keep photos and a short story about each one, for its own records and for the public site (see the "Our work" item under Public website). `project_folders` / `project_photos` (0001) exist and already model a tree (`parent_folder_id`, `drive_folder_id`, `top_level_category`) but carry no info: no title, date, story or public flag, and the RLS lets any volunteer write photos. **Shape:** the UI is a file browser. Root is `/Projects/`; the second level is a *fixed* set of categories — Shelter Projects, Community Projects, Community Outreach, Visitors and Volunteers, Social Media, Puppies, Sterilisations, Donations, Fundraising Campaigns, Events, Rescues, Miscellaneous (store as `top_level_category` with a check constraint or lookup; Shelter Projects already holds Enclosure Maintenance, see 0033, so the existing Drive folder must be reused, not recreated). Every level below that is user-defined through the UI: staff create nested folders to any depth, and any folder can hold photos *and* its own info card. **Migration:** add to `project_folders` `title_th`, `summary` / `summary_th` (short story, markdown), `project_date`, `location`, `is_public` (default false), `cover_photo_id` (FK to `project_photos`, nullable); add `caption` / `caption_th` and `sort_order` to `project_photos`; tighten RLS so volunteers can add photos to an existing folder but not create, rename, move or publish folders. **Drive:** mirror the tree exactly — `Projects/<Category>/<user folders…>/` — via `ensureProjectFolder()` in `src/lib/google/drive.ts` (create on demand, rename/move folder with the record), uploads through `/api/projects/[id]/photos` following the resident-photo route, registered in the photo-proxy lookup (0015/0019 pattern) so `driveImageUrl` serves them. **Pages:** `/projects` shows the category level; `/projects/[id]` shows a folder — breadcrumb path, subfolders first then a photo grid with captions, plus an info panel (title, date, location, story, cover-photo picker, "Show on website" toggle) and actions to new-subfolder / rename / move / upload. Newest-first and name sort, search across folder titles. Nav entry beside Enclosures. Bilingual fields follow the free-text strategy item under Quick wins — until that is decided, at least the public-facing title/summary/captions get Thai columns.

### Management


- [x] **Management role and dashboard.** Done 2026-09-21 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `management` is a fifth `app_role` (0038) with staff's operational access mirrored by RLS (0039) plus the Management section in the nav — `/management/dashboard`, `/management/contacts` and `/management/vets` (both moved from Admin). The dashboard covers the monthly report's figures (in care, adopted, fostered new/continuing, died, blood work in-house vs at a vet visit, initial vs follow-up vet visits, procedures by type — each with the names) for any month, a "right now" strip (in shelter / hospital / fostered / outreach, ready for adoption, vet visits due and overdue, open maintenance) and a 12-month intakes / adoptions / deaths chart. Admin sees everything management does.
- [x] **Move Vets management under Management.** Done 2026-09-21 on the user's call: `/management/vets` (`/admin/vets` redirects), with 0040 giving management read/write on `vets` — the one place management has more than staff. Website content stayed under Admin because only admins edit `site_content` (0018).
- [x] **Cashflow forecast: what the shelter is about to spend, in one place.** Done 2026-09-23 in two PRs. Schema first (`0071_cashflow_prices.sql`): `medication.cost_per_unit`, `immunization_types.cost` and `site_content.vet_visit_estimate`, all nullable so "not priced yet" is representable, edited in place on `/management/medications`, `/admin/immunization-types` and `/admin/website`. Then the feature: `0072_cashflow_forecast.sql` adds `cashflow_forecast(p_from, p_to)` returning one row per category per month — `category`, `month`, `amount`, `basis` (`priced` / `estimated` / `actual`) and `missing_prices` — built by calling `diet_forecast` (0051) and `medication_forecast` (0044) once per month rather than restating them (verified lossless against the whole-window figures). `/management/cashflow` is management + admin only: 30 / 90-day buttons plus the shared From/To picker, window total / average month / unpriced count, a stacked column chart (a sibling to `TrendChart.tsx`, no chart library) and the table it is drawn from, with a category toggle driving chart, table and totals together. Unpriced categories read "not priced yet" and link to where the price is entered; the page says on its face that it is committed outgoings, not a budget. See `docs/decisions.md` for the vet estimate, the gap-not-zero rule and the series colours. Follow-ups left open below: forecast vs actuals, the shared time-zone constant (Dashboard follow-ups (e)), CSV export, and 30/90 as the shared fixed windows.
- [x] **Make the "not priced yet" card on /management/cashflow actionable.** Done 2026-09-24. The card links straight to the pricing page when every missing price is in one category (`notPricedTarget()` in `src/lib/management/cashflow.ts`), and otherwise jumps to the table's "Not priced yet" row, which is highlighted on arrival and already links each category to its own page. With nothing missing it stays a plain read-out. Went out with CSV export (Cashflow follow-ups (c)).

### Auth

- [x] **Brand the Google sign-in screen.** Done 2026-09-22, as part of publishing the production sign-in app (Completed → Deployment): app name `Lanna Care for Animals`, home page `https://lannacare.org`, privacy `https://lannacare.org/privacy`, authorised domains `lannacare.org` + the Supabase host, published to *In production*, brand-verified the same day via the Search Console TXT record. Not recorded: whether the LCA logo was uploaded on the Branding page — if the account chooser still shows no logo, that is the one thing left. The Supabase Custom Domain add-on (paid) was not taken; the small-print domain still reads `supabase.co`. Original prompt: Google's account chooser currently says "to continue to qxkmhwybjggxvsfxsxbd.supabase.co". To show "Lanna Care for Animals" + logo instead: (1) Google Cloud console → Google Auth Platform → Branding: app name, LCA logo, support/developer emails, app home page, privacy policy URL, authorised domain(s) — the site domain plus `supabase.co`; (2) Audience → publish to *In production*; (3) submit for brand verification (basic `email`/`profile`/`openid` scopes only, typically a few days). Prerequisite on our side: a live `/privacy` page on the public website (and optionally `/terms`) that the Branding form can link to. Optional extra: Supabase *Custom Domain* add-on (Pro plan, paid) so the small-print domain reads e.g. `auth.<our domain>` instead of `supabase.co`. Remember to also add the production `https://<domain>/auth/callback` to Supabase → Authentication → URL Configuration → Redirect URLs, and the production login page won't work until that's done.
- [x] **Forgot password.** Done 2026-09-21: **Forgot password?** under the Sign in button → `/login/forgot` (public) → `resetPasswordForEmail` with a `redirectTo` of `/auth/callback?next=/account/password?reset=1`; the same "if that email has an account…" reply whether or not it exists. The callback now honours a same-origin `next` after the code exchange, and `/account/password?reset=1` is the existing change-password page in a third mode (heading says it came from a reset link; goes on to `/residents` after saving). **Production prerequisite** (README deploy step 5): a real SMTP provider in Supabase and the production callback in Redirect URLs — the built-in mailer is dev-only and rate-limited. Original brief: Add a "Forgot password?" link on login, a request page using Supabase `resetPasswordForEmail`, and a reset page that consumes the recovery link and sets a new password. Needs a production SMTP provider configured in Supabase.
- [x] **Access request queue on Admin → Security.** Asked for 2026-09-21 and done the same day: accounts with no `user_roles` row — a Google sign-in that was turned away, or a created login whose role insert failed — are listed under **Access requests** at the top of the page (name from Google where there is one, how they signed in, first seen, last attempt) with a role picker + **Approve** and a **Deny** that removes the account; the users table below shows only accounts with a role. No migration; the callback already kept the auth row. A public "request access" form for people without a Google account was considered and not built.
- [x] **Self-managed passwords: generated temp password + forced change on first login.** Done 2026-09-21 (see `docs/decisions.md`): Admin → Security no longer has a password field — creating a user mints a temporary password (`src/lib/auth/temp-password.ts`, 12 chars, no look-alikes) shown once with a Copy button, and the row's Reset became **Issue temporary password** (same, with a confirm; not for your own account). The account carries `app_metadata.must_change_password`; the request proxy sends such a user to `/account/password` and nowhere else while their session was opened with a password (the JWT `amr` claim) — a Google sign-in carries on. `/account/password` (also in the nav as Change password, for anyone) sets the new password as the user and then clears the flag via the service role. Google-only accounts just never use the temporary password. Original brief: Instead of the admin typing a password on Admin → Security, the app generates a temporary one (shown once to the admin to pass on), and the user must set their own password on first email/password login before reaching the app (flag on the account, e.g. `user_metadata.must_change_password`, checked in the session proxy / a `/account/password` page). Google sign-in bypasses the forced change since no password is used. Pairs with the Forgot-password item below so people manage their own passwords end-to-end and admins stop being in the password loop; the admin "reset password" action on the Security page then becomes "issue new temporary password". Consider making the password optional at creation for Google-only accounts.
- [x] **Sign in with Google.** Done 2026-09-20 (see `docs/decisions.md`); Supabase provider configured and verified working.

### Architecture

- [x] **`worktree.mjs done` refuses to remove free worktrees, blaming "a process that cannot be identified".** Done 2026-09-26 (see `docs/decisions.md`, "A failed rename is not a session"). `held()` now retries the rename probe (four tries over ~450 ms) and only a folder that refuses every time counts as held; `done` samples it immediately before removal and gives an unnamed refusal one longer wait (six tries over ~2 s). When nobody can be named, `done` no longer asserts an unidentifiable process: it says Windows refused the rename but no session or dev server was found, that this is usually transient and `list` may show the folder free, to re-run rather than `--force`, and what to look for if it persists. A named holder is refused exactly as before. The rename back is retried too, so a transient handle can never strand a worktree under its probe name. The false-positive case is now in `docs/test-plans/worktree-tooling.md` and `docs/test-plans/worktree-done-fix.md`: under a holder that flickers on and off, the old script refused 3/3 with the "cannot be identified" message and the new one got past the probe 3/3.
- [x] **Security-definer read helpers answer anon for any row: review function EXECUTE.** Done 2026-09-25 on dev (see `docs/decisions.md`, "Anon loses the functions"); **production apply awaits Lutan's go**. Reproduced first, and it was worse than the item said: besides `approved_translations` returning the approved Thai bio of residents who are not public (3 of the 4 on dev), four `security definer` writers let anon through because `current_user_role() not in (...)` is NULL, not true, for a NULL role. As anon, in a rolled-back transaction, `delete_resident_photo` deleted a public resident photo (its id is readable from `public_resident_photos`) and `record_attachment` added an attachment to a hidden resident and made it the profile photo. `0082_anon_function_execute.sql`: guards made null-safe (`is null or … not in`), which also covers a signed-in user with no role; the unused 5-argument `record_attachment` overload dropped; `approved_translations` moved to a new `private` schema the Data API does not expose (the views follow it by oid and keep working signed out); EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and anon on every function in `public`, then granted back to anon on `current_user_role`, `is_known_drive_file` and the three `shelter_*` date helpers only; `postgres`'s default privileges closed so a new function starts unexecutable by anon. `scripts/check-public-views.mjs` now calls every RPC the schema root lists as anon and fails unless the allow-listed five answer and every other one is refused as a function (42501 naming it): 23 FAIL before, 0 after.
- [x] **Anon can read four internal views that bypass RLS. Revoke it.** Done 2026-09-25 on dev (see `docs/decisions.md`, "Anon loses the internal views"); **production apply awaits Lutan's go**. Reproduced first: with only the anon key, `current_placement` (81 rows), `resident_current_state` (81) and `immunization_compliance` (220) answered, `immunization_duplicate_check` 200 empty. `0081_anon_view_grants.sql` goes further than the four: it revokes everything `anon` holds on every table, view and sequence in `public`, grants back SELECT on the ten `public_*` views and `site_content` / `site_content_photos` / `site_pages`, and closes the `postgres` role's default privileges for anon so a future object does not start out readable. All four views now return 401 `permission denied`; so do the other 38 internal objects, `schema_migrations` included. TRUNCATE: PostgREST has no verb that issues it and no `public` function contains it, so it was never reachable; anon now holds it on nothing. `security_invoker` deliberately not turned on (a signed-in behaviour change, not needed to close the anon hole). `scripts/check-public-views.mjs` now lists every Data API table and view with the service-role key and fails if anon can read any that is not on its public list, so a new object is covered without anyone adding it.
- [x] **`prescriptions` and `resident_diets` have no `updated_at`, so a wrong `end_date` is permanently undetectable.** Done 2026-09-24 (see `docs/decisions.md`). `0078_prescriptions_diets_updated_at.sql` adds `updated_at timestamptz not null default now()` to both, kept current by a generic `touch_updated_at()` BEFORE trigger: `now()` on insert and on any real change, kept on a no-op update, never settable by hand. Existing rows were back-filled with the apply time, so the audit value starts at 0078 (the column comments say so). Asserted on dev by `scripts/check-prescriptions-updated-at.mjs`. Original brief: Raised 2026-09-23 by the `utc-date-audit` stream. The audit could check `date` columns against `created_at` — a timestamptz, and therefore correct whatever the session timezone was — and that is how it established what the UTC "today" bug actually touched. `end_date` cannot be audited the same way: it is written days or weeks after the row is created, so `created_at` says nothing about when it was set, and neither table records when a row was last changed. Any `end_date` corrupted by the seven-hour window (from "End today", or from the deceased cascade above) is therefore unrecoverable after the fact — there is no reference timestamp to compare against. Add `updated_at timestamptz not null default now()` to both tables with a touch trigger, so the same audit is possible going forward. Additive and harmless to existing code; goes in the same migration PR as the `current_date` item above if that one is done first, since both are small.
- [x] **Nothing catches a user-visible PR that forgot its `unreleased` line.** Done 2026-09-24 (see `docs/decisions.md`, "What counts as user-visible"). `docs/test-plan-template.md` §7 has a release-notes line, ticked or `n/a: <reason>`, and `scripts/check-test-plan.mjs` holds it to the diff: a tick with no new `unreleased` line fails, and a PR touching `src/app/`, `src/components/`, `src/lib/manual/`, `src/lib/i18n/` or `worker/` with no new line fails unless a plan answers the line; an `n/a` reason is printed in the CI output for the reviewer. Red flag, not a hard block, like the rest of `test-plan`. Original brief: Found 2026-09-24 while cutting `0.1.0`: `unreleased` in `src/lib/releases.ts` was **empty**, so the release notes for ten merged PRs had to be reconstructed from PR titles and diffs rather than written by the people who made the changes — which is the guesswork the register exists to prevent. #60 (cashflow forecast) and #67 (nav rework) both merged *after* the register landed in #62 and neither added a line, and both are plainly user-visible. `scripts/deploy.mjs` cannot catch it: it refuses production while `unreleased` is **non-empty** (so nothing ships undescribed), but an empty list is indistinguishable from "nothing user-visible shipped in this batch". The gap is one-directional and silent, and it gets worse the longer a release goes uncut, because the reconstruction is done by whoever cuts it from titles rather than intent. Fix: a line in `docs/test-plan-template.md` under Documentation — *"does this change something a shelter user would notice? if so, `src/lib/releases.ts`'s `unreleased` has a line for it, written for a user and not as a commit message"* — ticked or `n/a: <reason>` like everything else, so a PR that genuinely changes nothing visible says so out loud. Worth pairing with a check in `scripts/check-test-plan.mjs`: when a PR touches `src/app/` or `src/components/` and `unreleased` is unchanged, require that line to carry a reason rather than a bare tick, since that is exactly the combination that produced this. Not a hard gate on the diff — plenty of `src/app/` changes are genuinely invisible — but a prompt at the moment the author still remembers what they changed.
- [x] **Postgres has its own copy of the UTC "today" bug — `current_date` in a UTC session.** Done 2026-09-24 (see `docs/decisions.md`). `0073_shelter_today.sql` adds `shelter_time_zone()` / `shelter_today()` / `shelter_date(timestamptz)` and moves eight sites onto them: the `maintenance.date_created` default, the `date_completed` stamp, both lines of the death cascade, `public_shelter_stats` (`in_treatment` and both "this year" counts) and `public_recent_adoptions.adopted_on`. A death at 01:26 Bangkok now closes open courses on its own day, asserted in a rollback harness on dev. `medication_daily_requirement` had already been dropped by 0044; existing rows not repaired (dev: 0 affected). Cashflow month boundaries stay with Dashboard follow-ups (e).
- [x] **One command to run the three gates, printing each exit code.** Done 2026-09-24. `node scripts/gates.mjs` runs `npm run typecheck`, `lint` and `build` — the same three as CI's `check` job — every one of them even after a failure, and ends with `gates: typecheck=0 lint=0 build=0`, exiting 1 if any failed. It refuses to start (exit 2, nothing run) when `node_modules/.bin` lacks `next`, `tsc` or `eslint`. `--plan` adds `check-test-plan.mjs` as a fourth, opt-in because a correct plan is red until someone signs. Section 2 of the test-plan template now asks for its output pasted as-is.
- [x] **`docs/decisions.md` merges by union locally but not on GitHub, so parallel PRs read CONFLICTING.** Done 2026-09-29. Split by decision: from 2026-09-24 on, each decision is its own file, `docs/decisions/<date>-<slug>.md`, so two streams add different paths and GitHub has nothing to conflict on. `docs/decisions.md` is a frozen baseline and the `merge=union` line is gone from `.gitattributes`. CLAUDE.md, README and the test-plan template say to add a file. Old citations by path still resolve, since the date names the file. `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-decisions-one-file-per-decision.md`.
- [x] **`worktree.mjs done` reports success when it has not removed the folder.** Done 2026-09-23 (see `docs/decisions.md`). `done` now checks, before touching anything, whether any process has the folder open (rename it to a sibling name and back: Windows refuses exactly when something holds it) and refuses, naming the holder. It removes the folder, prunes git's registry and deletes the branches, then verifies all four and prints `not done:` with what survived rather than `done`. `--stop-servers` ends dev servers running from the folder's `node_modules` and retries. `list` survives a stale or broken worktree (a `STALE` row instead of `fatal: not a git repository`) and names husks, which `done <name>` clears (build output only unless `--force`).
- [x] **`worktree.mjs list` should show which worktrees have a live session, and `done` should refuse one that does.** Done 2026-09-23. `list` has a `held` column (`HELD`/`free`, plus the Claude session's name from `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`, checked against the live process's start time) and an `unpushed` column. `done` hard-refuses a branch with commits no origin ref has, with no override. It also *refuses*, rather than warns about, a held folder. The item asked for a warning because the check races. But Windows cannot delete a held folder anyway, so carrying on would only pull git out from under the session and leave a husk. The race (a session attaching in the milliseconds after the check) is the one case left.
- [x] **`sync` leaves the branch unpushed — the post-commit hook does not fire for merges.** Done 2026-09-23. `sync` pushes as its last step, and `.githooks/post-merge` pushes after every other merge or pull (including the daily `backlog` fast-forward). CLAUDE.md's "always matches" sentence is corrected and points at `list`'s `unpushed` column.
- [x] **`.claude/launch.json` hardcodes port 3000, so the browser pane is unusable from any worktree.** Done 2026-09-23. The file is gitignored and generated per checkout from `.port` (`dev` runs `npm run dev -- --port <port>`) by `new`, `dev`, `sync`, the post-merge hook and `node scripts/worktree.mjs launch`. Verified: `preview_start` with `name: "dev"` in the worktree-tooling worktree started `next dev --port 3002` and rendered the home page.

Done 2026-10-01: `0120_contact_map_url_check.sql`; the one existing row was NULL, so a plain validated check, no `not valid` step.

- [x] **`apply-migrations.mjs` should warn when it applies a file whose consumer is not yet deployed.** Split from the `deploy.mjs` schema check (2026-10-01), which guards the deploy end; this is the same information read from the apply end. Needs a way to know a migration's consumer (e.g. a `-- consumer: <path>` header line, or checking whether the column is selected under `src/` on a commit newer than the live release); the repo holds no such map, so it is not a few lines. **Done:** header-line version (`-- consumer:`), warn-only, live release assumed from `package.json`; inference rejected — see `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-migration-consumer-header.md`.

- [x] **The app should answer "what version am I?" without a sign-in, from the origin rather than the Worker.** Raised 2026-10-02 by `apply-migrations-consumer-warning`: `/api/releases/current` is answered by `worker/index.mjs` before the origin is consulted, so nothing can ask what the Pi is running. `apply-migrations.mjs` has to *assume* the newest cut release is live, so an undeployed cut reads as live. An unauthenticated version route served by the app itself (version plus git sha) would let that check, and `deploy.mjs`, be exact.



### Moved out of test plans, 2026-10-04

These were written as rows in `Left for manual verification` tables and sat there undone — some for over a week, one written twice in two days. They are tasks, not checks, and `docs/manual-verification-queue.md` is the argument for why that is how they stayed undone. Moved here on Lutan's instruction; the plans they came from keep a pointer to this section rather than the row.
